# The app's API

Everything the app's pages do goes through its HTTP API, and agents use the same API (with the app_api tool) to read the app's state and change it for you. This topic lists the endpoints by area, with what each takes and returns. It is written for agents and developers; you don't need it to use the app.

## How agents use it

- An agent calls an endpoint with **app_api** (`method`, `path`, `body`), as the signed-in user, inside the app itself. Reading (`GET`) runs at once.
- A change shows the user a card first, with what changes (before → after for a setting) and the call itself. Give `summary`, the change in a few plain words, as its title. The user can allow it once, allow such changes for the rest of the session, or say no. Deleting, starting agent work, adding MCP servers or machines, changing what agents may do and similar changes ask every time.
- To have the user fill in or check details on that card, give `form`: fields as a JSON schema's properties (`type`, `title`, `description`, `enum`, `default`, `format`…), each named by its address in `body` (`name`, `settings.results`), filled in from `body`; the user's answers go into `body` before the call.
- Every option of Settings, by its id: `GET /api/settings` gives each one's value now (`?section=` or `?q=` with what each takes and how it changes), `PUT /api/settings/:id` changes one, and `POST /api/settings/:id/run` presses a button ([Every setting](#every-setting)). The card asks as the calls the change makes would.
- Read the current state before changing it: most settings are read with `GET` at the same address their `PUT` or `PATCH` writes.
- In Plan mode (and Codex's Read only) an agent only reads, except its own session's settings (below), which it changes on a card the user answers. With Claude Code's Bypass or Codex's or Custom's Full access no approval card shows, except for a session's permissions and spending caps. Forms still show when details are needed.
- A session's permissions mode (`mode` in `POST /api/sessions/:key/config`) and spending caps (`capUsd` in `PATCH /api/sessions/:key/extra`, and `/api/budget/…`) change only on a card the user answers each time, in every mode, Full access included, with no **Allow for this session**.
- An agent's own session is `this` in a path: `POST /api/sessions/this/config` changes its own model, effort or permissions when the user asks for that.
- Never through app_api: the password and signing in, keys' values (ask with request_secret), a signature's picture or any change to one (ask_signature and place_signature), making access tokens and API keys, answering approval cards and the cards that ask the user to decide, files, terminals, notebooks and git (agents use their own tools), the live browser (the browser tools), the Mac's screen, mouse and keyboard and macOS's permissions for them (the computer tools; the user allows those in Settings).
- Links for the user: the app's address plus the page: `/s/<session key>`, `/projects/<id>` (the project's page; followed from another page, it opens the project's details in a window over that page; `/projects/<id>/files` opens its **Files**), `/apps/<id>`, `/pages/<id>`, `/settings?s=<section>`, `/guide/<topic>#<section>`.

## Settings kept on each device

These live in each browser, one set per device: the theme, the language, motion and text size, the notification sound, what Enter does, terminal suggestions, the sidebar and its groups, and voice. Every open page tells the server of the machine it shows what its browser keeps, when it opens and whenever one changes, so an agent reads them as it changes them. A change goes to the pages showing its session, with no card:

- `GET /device` — the settings of each open page's browser, the pages showing this session first. Returns: `{ devices: [{ id — the page's own (one for each open page), device — which browser and system ("Chrome on Mac", "Hydor for Windows", "Safari on iPhone (Home Screen)"), showsThisSession: boolean, settings: { theme, darkTheme, lightTheme — the themes shown in dark and in light mode, by name, motion, textSize, zoom — the conversation’s zoom in percent, language, sound, enter, terminalSuggestions, wordSuggestions, technicalDetails, sidebar, groupSessions, voiceLanguage, dictation, readingVoice, readingSpeed }, notifications?: 'on'|'off'|'blocked'|'unsupported'|'app' — read only: each device turns notices on for itself, as its browser asks the person's permission ('app': the desktop app shows them itself), voices?: string[] — read only: the voices it reads aloud in, those for the language spoken first (at most 40), toldAt }] }`; with no page open, it says the values cannot be read now.
- `PUT /device` — changes them on the pages showing this session, which switch at once. Body: `{ theme?: 'dark'|'light'|'system', themeName?: string — a theme's name ("Hydor Dark", "Midnight", "Sage", or one of the user's): it shows in dark or light mode, as the theme's base is; a name no theme has is refused with the list, motion?: 'full'|'calm'|'off' — how much of the app moves (the system's Reduce motion always wins), textSize?: 'default'|'large'|'larger' — how big messages, the Help pages and the message box are, zoom?: number — a session’s conversation zoom in percent, 67 to 200 (100 is normal; Ctrl or ⌘ with + − 0 in a session), language?: 'en'|'de'|'es'|'fr'|'pt'|'el'|'ja'|'zh' — reloads the page, sound?: boolean — a chime with each notice, enter?: 'join'|'queue' — what Enter does while an agent works, terminalSuggestions?: boolean, wordSuggestions?: boolean — the grey word suggestions in this browser (Settings → Capabilities → Word suggestions), technicalDetails?: boolean — web apps show files, commands and logs beside the plain words (Settings → General → Web apps), sidebar?: boolean — shown on a computer, groupSessions?: boolean — the sidebar's groups for projects and folders (false: the sessions by day only), voiceLanguage?: string — the language spoken, for dictation (a tag such as "de-DE"; "" follows the browser's), dictation?: 'browser'|'server' — the browser's own recognition or the app's (Settings → Voice), readingVoice?: string — one of the voices GET lists ("" lets the browser pick), readingSpeed?: number — 0.5 to 2, device?: string — another open page's id from GET, or "all" for every open page, instead of the pages showing this session }`. When no page shows the session (the person writes from a chat app or the terminal), it is refused with the pages open now, to name one.

## Every setting

Every option of Settings, by an id made of its section and its words (`general.tips`, `memory.search-by-meaning`), with what it holds now and how it changes: the same table `hydor settings` reads and changes in the terminal. An option the table leaves to a page or to endpoints of its own names them in `change`; one only the person can change says why in `only`.

- `GET /api/settings` — every option's value now. Query: `section?` (a section's id, as in `/settings?s=`, or several with commas), `q?` (words: the options they describe, at most 25), `values?` (`0`: without reading the values), `key?` (a session: the device showing it gives the settings kept on each device; app_api adds this session's). Returns, for all: `{ how, device: { label, showsThisSession } | null, sections: [{ id, title, tab, values: { [option id]: value }, errors?: { [option id]: why it could not be read }, more?: [option id] — buttons, pages and lists with endpoints of their own }] }`; for a section or words: `{ how, device, sections: [{ id, title, tab, options: [Option] }] }`. **Option**: `{ id, label — the words the page shows, kind: 'switch'|'choice'|'number'|'text'|'list'|'secret'|'action'|'page'|'device'|'command', value?, error?, takes? — what PUT takes, choices?: [{ value, label }], change? — how it changes otherwise (PUT /device, POST …/run, its own endpoints or your tools), only? — what only the person can do, and where, note?, link — its place in Settings }`. A secret reads as true or false (set or not), never its value.
- `GET /api/settings/:id` — one option, as above, with `section` and `device`. Its id may be words that name one option (`tips`, `general.resume`).
- `PUT /api/settings/:id` — changes one option through its own endpoint, as its page does. Body: `{ value — as takes says: true or false, a number, a choice's value, a text, a list (an array, or items with commas); "" removes a key, confirm?: true — goes ahead with a change that asks first (a list that deletes the items it leaves out) }`. Returns: `{ id, label, changed, value, said: string[] }`. 400 for a value it does not take (it says what it takes), or for an option changed elsewhere (it says how); 409 with `question` for a change that asks first. Through app_api the card shows the option before → after and the calls it makes, and asks as those calls would (a key's value is refused; removing one is fine); a device's own option goes to the pages as `PUT /device` does.
- `POST /api/settings/:id/run` — presses a button (Check now, Send a test, Tidy up now…). Body: `{ arg? — what it takes (a name), confirm?: true — for a button that asks first }`. Returns: `{ id, label, said: string[] }`. Through app_api it asks as the calls it makes would.

## The guide and Agent Guidelines

- `GET /api/guide` — the whole guide. Returns: `{ topics: [{ id, title, summary, body (Markdown), sections: [{ id, title, text }] }] }`. Agents read it with app_guide instead.
- `GET /api/guide/images/:name` — a picture in the guide (docs/guide/images), such as `projects.webp`. Query: `theme?` (`light`: its light version, when there is one). Returns: the image.
- `GET /api/guidelines` — Agent Guidelines: for every session, and a project's or a session's. Query: `key?` (a session key: its own guidelines, and its project's), `space?` (a project's id). Returns: `{ global: string, space?: { id, name, text }, session?: { key, text } }`.
- `PUT /api/guidelines` — changes the levels given, leaving the others. Body: `{ global?: string, space?: { id: string, text: string }, session?: { key: string, text: string } }` (each at most 20,000 characters; an empty text clears that level). Returns: the same as GET for that session and project. A changed session's agent follows the new guidelines from the next message.
- `GET /api/setup/instructions` — the instruction files Claude Code or Codex reads on its own for a session in a folder (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md and the like), in the order it reads them. Query: `agent: 'claude'|'codex'`, `cwd?` (the folder; the app's default when left out). Returns: `{ files: [{ path, scope: 'user'|'parent'|'project'|'local'|'rules', exists: boolean, size?, primary? }] }`. A file's text is read and changed with an agent's own file tools.
- `POST /api/setup/instructions` — creates one of those files where it is missing (with a title line). Body: `{ agent: 'claude'|'codex', path: string — one the GET lists, cwd?: string }`. Returns: `{ path }`.

## Conventions

- Session keys are `claude:<id>`, `codex:<id>` or `acp:<id>` (after the colon, 4 to 80 letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`); URL-encode them in paths (`claude%3A…`). Other keys get 400 "Bad session key".
- Times are Unix milliseconds. Errors come back as `{ error: string }` with an HTTP status (400 bad input, 404 not found, 409 not possible right now).
- Attachments, where a body takes them: `images?: [{ mediaType: 'image/png'|'image/jpeg'|'image/gif'|'image/webp', data: string (base64), name?: string }]` (at most 8 are kept) and `files?: [{ name: string, data: string (base64) }]` (at most 20; saved in the app's uploads folder and listed by path at the end of the message as "Attached files:").
- A `SessionSummary` has: `key, agent ('claude'|'codex'|'acp'), id, title, cwd, gitBranch, model, createdAt, updatedAt, firstPrompt, lastPrompt, stats { userMessages, turns, toolCalls, commands, filesChanged, linesAdded, linesRemoved, tokensIn, tokensOut, tokensCached, costUsd }, live ({ status: 'starting'|'idle'|'working'|'waiting'|'stopping'|'exited'|'error', model, effort, mode, pendingApprovals, contextTokens, contextWindow, backgroundTasks, endedTasks, goal } or null when the agent is not running), external ({ status, client, pid } when it runs in VS Code or a terminal), archived, pinned, order, snoozedUntil, doneAt, seenAt (unread when doneAt > seenAt), queue [{ id, text, at, att }], queueHeld, goal { objective, status: 'active'|'paused'|'blocked'|'limited', app?: { state, round?, rounds, holds, of } — set for a goal the app's own loop drives and is not over (then status is 'paused' while paused, 'blocked' while the checks or the user's help wait for the user, else 'active'; state is the goal's own status, holds of of the checks hold where the files stand), none once the goal is over }, worktree { path, branch, base, repo }, pr { url, number, title, state, checks }, resumeAt, account, acpAgent, acpName, extra { memory, assistant, capUsd, parent, space, swarm, research, arena, confidential }, prefs { model, effort, mode }, forkFrom, handoffFrom, handoffTo`.
- **Careful** marks a route that, used on a session other than the one the agent is in, does something the user would not expect (spends money, sends words in their name, stops work, deletes, changes files or publishes). Use it on another session only when the user asked for exactly that.

## The app and the start page

- `GET /api/bootstrap` — what the pages load first. Returns: `{ version, home, dataDir, defaultCwd (the default folder), autoFolders: string[] (folders no one picks: where the app makes task folders, apps' runs and worktrees, its data folder and the temporary folder; the sidebar groups no sessions by them), folderNames: { [folder]: name } (folders Codex keeps projects in, by the project's name, such as a ChatGPT project's folder), folderOrigins: { [folder]: [{ agent, account?, sources: string[], sessions }] } (each folder two or more sessions worked in: empty when its first session was started in Hydor; else where its sessions from outside came from, by agent and added account, with the apps they were started in as the agent wrote them down, such as `claude-vscode` or `cli`, the most sessions first), host (machine name), start (the start page's last picks, as PUT /api/start), prefs { claude, codex } (each agent's last model/effort/mode/account), accounts [{ id, agent, name }] (added accounts), acpAgents [{ id, name, command, builtin }] (Custom is id 'console'), tiers and team (as GET /api/models/tiers: the start page offers Automatic by them), themes, update, tourSeen, indexing }`.
- `PUT /api/start` — changes what the start page opens with next time, on every device (merges: send only what changes; `null` clears a field). Body: `{ agent?: 'claude'|'codex'|'acp:<id>' (Custom: 'acp:console'), runs?: { '<agent key as above>': { model?: string|null, effort?: string|null, mode?: string|null, account?: string ('' = the agent's own sign-in) } } (joins that agent's earlier run; null = the agent's default), assistant?: string (assistant id: general, researcher, writer, coder, tutor, planner, analyst or your own), space?: string (a project's id), task?: boolean (true = a new task folder), cwd?: string (absolute path; left out on read if the folder is gone), worktree?: boolean, memory?: false|null (false = start without memory), capUsd?: number|null (> 0, ≤ 1,000,000), starters?: false|null (false = no starter ideas under the empty message box; null shows them again), places?: { '<place>': { agent?: string, runs?: { '<agent key as above>': { model?: string|null, effort?: string|null, mode?: string|null } }, assistant?: string, worktree?: boolean, memory?: false, capUsd?: number, confidential?: true, at: number (when something was last picked or started there, in milliseconds) } | null } (what was picked in each place, where `<place>` is `task` for the new task folder, `project:<id>` or `folder:<absolute path>`; a place you send is replaced as a whole, `null` forgets it, and the 60 used last are kept; a change that sends no `places` is also kept as the picks of the place picked last) }`. Returns: the whole start settings after the change. Open start pages update at once.
- `GET /api/catalog/:agent` — an agent's models, permissions modes and commands. `:agent` is `claude`, `codex` or `acp` (then `?acp=<id>`, `console` for Custom; another ACP agent's lists are known only after it has run once, except Grok Build's models and the efforts each takes, which the app asks Grok for as soon as it is added; a model's `efforts` and `defaultEffort` are what its picker offers). Returns: `{ models [{ value, label, isDefault, efforts: string[], defaultEffort, description, local? (Custom's: it runs on this computer or your private network) }], modes [{ value, label, description }], defaultMode, commands? [{ name, description }] (Claude Code's slash commands and skills), modeNote?, prefs }`. Mode values: Claude Code `default` (Ask), `acceptEdits`, `auto`, `plan`, `bypassPermissions`; Codex `plan`, `read-only`, `auto`, `auto-review`, `full`; Custom `ask`, `edits`, `auto`, `full`, `plan`.
- `GET /api/fs/recent?picked=1` — the folders the start page offers: up to 12 folders sessions worked in lately, newest first, without the ones Hydor made (task folders, app runs, worktrees). Without `picked=1`: up to 30 folders, those included. Returns: `{ dirs: string[] }`.
- `GET /api/fs/made?dir=<folder>&since=<ms>&until=<ms>` — the documents (PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OpenDocument, CSV and TSV, LaTeX, notebooks, EPUB, Markdown) under a folder that were last changed within a span of time, newest first: what a conversation shows as cards under a turn's reply. Query: `dir` (a folder, absolute or from `~`), `since` and `until` (times in milliseconds since 1970, at most 400 days apart: a longer span keeps its last 400 days). Returns: `{ files: [{ path, size, mtime, born? (when the file was made, where the file system says) }] }`, at most 300. It looks at most 6 folders deep and at 8000 entries, never follows links, and leaves out folders whose names start with a dot, and node_modules, __pycache__, venv, site-packages and bower_components. 400 when `since` or `until` is missing or not a number.
- `GET /api/fs/thumb?path=<file>&w=<pixels>&t=<time>` — the small picture of a file, as a project's **Files** tab shows it on a tile: a picture scaled down, a video's frame, a PDF's first page, the first page, slide or cells of a Word, PowerPoint, Excel or OpenDocument file (the picture the file carries, or one Hydor draws of it), an HTML file or a notebook as it opens. It follows the same rules for the path as the file itself (a file, as `~` and relative paths resolve), and uses what the computer has to make the picture (ImageMagick, ffmpeg, poppler, a Mac's own tools, Hydor's browser); nothing from outside the file is in it. Query: `path` (a file, absolute or from `~`), `w` (the width in pixels, from 120 to 800, 360 when left out), `t` (the file's change time: send it and the answer may be kept for good by the browser). Returns: the picture itself (WebP, JPEG or PNG), kept on this computer for a month. 404 `{ error }` when there is no picture for the file: a kind it draws nothing for (text, code and the like), no tool for it on this computer, a file too big, or one that is not what its name says.
- `GET /api/git/repo-info?path=<folder>` — whether a folder is in a git repository (the start page then offers a worktree). Returns: `{ info: { repo, branch, sub } | null }`.

## Finding sessions

- `GET /api/sessions` — the session list, newest first, pinned first. Query: `agent?=claude|codex|acp`, `archived?=1` (only archived ones; default: only not archived), `limit?` (1–1000, default 300), `offset?`, `cwd?` (exact folder). Returns: `{ sessions: SessionSummary[], total }`. Hydor subagents are listed too; their parent is `extra.parent`.
- `GET /api/search?q=<words>` — search through sessions: titles, first messages and folders, then the words said in messages. Archived sessions are included. Returns: `{ hits: [{ key, agent, title, cwd, updatedAt, kind ('title' or where the words were found), snippet (matches between \u0001 and \u0002), ts }] }`.
- `GET /api/find?q=<words>` — what the search box (Ctrl+K) asks the server (up to 300 characters). Query: `in?: string — places to look in, comma-separated (sessions, messages, spaces, pages, templates (apps), files, memory, tasks, assistants, skills, connections, settings, actions, guide, browsing); none: everywhere`; `browsing?: "1" — also look in the user's own browser (history, bookmarks, downloads) when the place is in scope: only for the person using the search box in the desktop app, never for an agent (the answer then has no `browsing`)`. Returns: `{ meaning (search by meaning answered), sessions [{ key, agent, title, firstPrompt, cwd, updatedAt, score }] (by title, and by meaning when on), messages [as /api/search hits] (up to 10), notes [{ id, scope, scopeKey, kind, text, updatedAt }] (memory notes, when memory is on), alike [{ id, score }] (other things alike in meaning, by the search box's ids: go:<page>, sec:<section>, opt:<section>:<words>, app:…, task:…, asst:…, skill:…, page:…, conn:…, space:…), browsing [{ kind (history, bookmark, download), id, title, url?, path?, at, visits?, failed?, folder? }] (only when asked for as above, never in an agent's call) }`.
- `GET /api/find/scope` — where the search box looks: its **Search in** menu, the same on every device. Returns: `{ in: string[] }` (the places picked; empty: everywhere).
- `PUT /api/find/scope` — sets it; open search boxes change at once. Body: `{ in: string[] — the places to look in, from: sessions, messages (words said in sessions), spaces, pages, templates (apps), files, memory, tasks (scheduled tasks), assistants, skills, connections, settings (with themes), actions (the app's pages and actions: Go to, Actions), guide (Help), browsing (the user's own browser: history, bookmarks, downloads; agents never get it); [] or all of them: everywhere }`. Returns: `{ in }` (in the menu's order). A name it does not know is refused (400).
- `GET /api/sessions/:key` — one session. Returns: `{ summary: SessionSummary|null, live, overlay { items, approvals [{ id, title, kind, detail, options [{ id, label }], questions? }] } }`. The approvals are what the session waits on the user for; agents must leave them to the user.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/timeline` — the conversation, a page at a time. Query: `before?` (an item index; default the end), `limit?` (default 300, at most 2000), `whole?=1` (start the page at a turn's start). Returns: `{ items [{ id, seq, kind: 'user'|'assistant'|'tool'|'thinking'|'system'|'turn', ts, text?, tool? { name, category, title, status, output (shortened), files } }], total, start }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/items/:id` — one timeline item in full (output and diffs not shortened). Returns: the item.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/subagents` — the subagents the session's own program ran (Claude Code's Agent tool, Codex's spawn_agent; Hydor switches these off in the sessions it runs, so only a session run in VS Code or a terminal, or an earlier one, has any), oldest first, each as a session of its own: you open it to read its conversation, but it runs inside its lead, so it cannot be sent a message, resumed, forked, handed off or deleted (those answer with a 400 saying so). One that is working has `live` set; `subagentOf` is its lead's key. Returns: `{ sessions: SessionSummary[] }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/last-reply` — the last thing the agent wrote in the session, whole (empty when it has written nothing): what a Codex subagent said when it came back, which its lead's conversation does not carry. Returns: `{ text: string }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/find?q=<text>` — your messages and the agent's that contain the text, any case (at least 2 characters; up to 500 matches), oldest first. Returns: `{ matches [{ seq, kind: 'user'|'assistant', snippet (match between \u0001 and \u0002) }] }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/export?format=md` — the conversation as a Markdown file (what Export as Markdown downloads): the title and date, each of your messages and each reply in order, a turn's steps as one line ("Worked for 2 min · 5 steps"), handed-over files by name, answer cards as text and tables, Mermaid and maths as written. `md` is the only format; `download=0` shows it inline instead of as an attachment. Returns: the Markdown (`text/markdown`), named `<title>.md`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/export/doc` — keeps the conversation as a document (the conversation's menu → **Keep as a document**): the same Markdown with your messages' pictures and the handed-over pictures in it; a confidential session's web pictures stay links. Body: `{ title?: string — its name (the session's title unless given), space?: string — a project (the session's unless given), folder?: string — an older way to group kept things, not shown in the app }`. Returns: `{ page, notes: string[] — what could not be taken in }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/changes` — every file the session changed, the most changed first. Returns: `{ files [{ path, added, removed, edits [{ itemId, ts, change { path, op: 'edit'|'create'|'write'|'delete'|'move', added, removed, diff (cut at 60,000 characters: diffTruncated) } }] }] }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/breakdown` — what a turn's **Time and cost** needs beyond the session's items: how long each card waited for the user (approvals, questions and forms; the latest 2,000), and the price of the models asked for. Query: `models?: string — model names, comma-separated (at most 10)`. Returns: `{ waits: [{ kind, at, until }] (oldest first), rates: { [model]: { input, output, cacheRead } (dollars a million tokens) | null (no price known) } }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/resources` — what each live session's programs use now (its agent's program, the commands it runs, the servers it started; measured on Linux and a Mac), the limits sessions start under, and whether limits work here. Returns: `{ sessions: [{ key, title, agent, cpu: number|null — percent of one processor (null on the first look), memory: number — bytes, procs: number, limits?: { memoryMb?, cpus? } — those it runs under, stopped?: number — programs stopped at its memory limit }] (the most memory first), limits: { memoryMb?, cpus? }, support: { ok: boolean, why? }, measured: boolean }`.
- `PUT /api/sessions/limits` — Settings → General → Limit each session: the most memory and processors one session may use, from its agent's next start (Linux with the user's systemd). Body: `{ memoryMb?: 2048|4096|8192|16384|null, cpus?: 1|2|4|8|null }` (null: no limit). Returns: `{ limits, support }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/commands` — every shell command the agent ran, oldest first. Returns: `{ commands: [timeline tool items] }` (tool.title is the command, tool.status, tool.exitCode, tool.output shortened).
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/notes` — what the app added under turns (memory updates, second opinions, spending cap notes, Hydor subagent cards, a line of kind "tier" when the session moved to the Max tier's model by itself). Returns: `{ notes [{ id, kind, at, anchor, data }] }`.
- `GET /api/commits/:key/show?hash=` — one commit an agent made in session `:key` (from a session note of kind "commits", whose `data.commits` lists `{ hash, subject, branch?, repo, own?, author?, at, files, added, removed }`). Returns: `{ hash, author, email?, ts, message, parents, refs, head, pushed, files: [{ path, added, removed, binary?, status?, from?, diff }] }`. 404 when the commit is not in one of the session's notes.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/extra` — the session's settings beyond its agent's own. Returns: `{ extra { memory?, assistant?, capUsd?, parent?, space?, swarm?, research?, arena?, connections?, confidential?, browserOnly? } }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/tasks/:taskId/log` — a background task's latest output (Claude Code sessions only; task ids are in `live.backgroundTasks`, and in `live.endedTasks` once they end). Returns: `{ kind: 'output'|'agent', text?, entries?, size?, truncated?, updatedAt }`.

## The inbox (read only)

- `GET /api/inbox` — everything waiting on the user on this machine, newest first: approvals, questions and forms in sessions, a browser that needs them, a key an agent asked for, a skill to review, sessions waiting in VS Code or a terminal, calls held in the Outbox. Returns: `{ items [{ id, kind: 'approval'|'question'|'form'|'browser'|'key'|'skill'|'elsewhere'|'comment'|'outbox', title, detail?, key? (session), session? (its title), agent?, at, path (where it is answered in the app), approval?, outbox? (a held call, as GET /api/outbox lists them) }] }`. Report these to the user; answering them is theirs to do.

## While you were away (the start page)

- `GET /api/away?until=<ms>` — what happened since the user's last visit, as the start page's **While you were away** shows it: what waits on them (the inbox now, less what waited already when they last marked all as seen), sessions whose last turn ended Done or Failed since then and that they have not opened since (not archived, not a helper of another session, not a run's, not one still at work: in a turn, or with subagents or background tasks it started still running), scheduled tasks', agents' and apps' runs that ended since then (not tests, not steps of another run), and the scheduled tasks due before `until` (the end of the user's day; the server's own midnight when left out). The last visit is the end of the last time the app was open, in front and in use for a minute or more, on any device; one day back before the first. Returns: `{ now, since, rows: [{ id: 'session:<key>'|'run:<id>'|'app:<id>'|<inbox item id>, group: 'needs'|'finished'|'ran', outcome: 'waiting'|'done'|'failed'|'quiet' (quiet: a run that found nothing new), title, line? (the start of the last reply, the run's summary or error, or what is asked), at, path (where it opens in the app), agent?, acpName?, session? (for what waits: the session's title), files?: string[] (paths of files the session made, the first five), fileCount? (made or changed, all told), costUsd?, by?: 'task'|'agent'|'app', byName?, item? (the inbox item, as GET /api/inbox lists it) }] (most important first: what waits, the longest waiting first; then what failed; sessions done; runs done; runs with nothing new), counts: { needs, done, failed, quiet }, next: [{ id (the task), name, at, path, by }] (three at most, soonest first) }`. Report it to the user; answering what waits is theirs to do.
- `POST /api/away/seen` — **Mark all as seen**: what the start page lists up to `at` counts as seen, and the sessions in it as opened, on every device (their dots in the sidebar go too); what happens after shows again. Body: `{ at?: number — the summary's now; now when left out }`. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## The Outbox (calls held for the user's OK)

A connected account's tool set to Ask first is not called: the call is held here until the user sends or drops it (Inbox → Outbox). Agents can list held calls and drop them; only the user sends one.

- `GET /api/outbox` — held calls, newest first: those waiting for the user's OK, and those that failed when sent (they wait again). Query: `{ key?: string — one session's, run?: string — one scheduled run's, all?: '1' — also those sent, dropped, and kept by a test that sends nothing }`. Returns: `{ calls: [{ id, status: 'held'|'sending'|'sent'|'failed'|'dropped'|'dry' — dry: kept by a test that sends nothing, never made, key? — the session, session? — its title, agent?, run? — the scheduled task's run, task? — its name, app — the connected account's id, appName, tool, description?, input — the call's input, as the agent gave it or as changed, schema?: { properties, required } — the tool's input fields, shape: 'email'|'message'|'post'|'fields' — how the Inbox shows it, result? — what the account answered once sent, at, decidedAt? }], held — how many wait }`.
- `POST /api/outbox/:id/send` — makes a held call now (Hydor calls the account's tool itself) and keeps what it answered; the session's line says so and its agent hears it with the next message. The user's alone: agents can't send held calls. Body: `{ input?: object — the call's input as changed; the agent's when left out }`. Returns: `{ call }` (status 'sent', or 'failed' with the reason in result).
- `POST /api/outbox/:id/drop` — drops a held call: it is never made. Returns: `{ call }`.
- `POST /api/outbox/send-all` — sends one session's (or one run's) held calls as they are, in the order they were held. The user's alone. Body: `{ key?: string — the session, run?: string — the scheduled run }`. Returns: `{ calls }`.

## Starting sessions

- `POST /api/sessions` — starts a session, as the start page does; the agent begins on `prompt` at once. Body: `{ agent: 'claude'|'codex'|'acp', acpAgent?: string (required for 'acp': 'console' for Custom, or an id from Settings → More agents), prompt?: string, cwd?: string (an existing folder; `~` works; empty = the default folder), model?: string (one of the agent's models from /api/catalog, or `"auto"` for Automatic: the Best tier's model for the agent, whose main agent may step itself up to the Max tier's (step_up_session); left out = the agent's own default), effort?: string, mode?: string (a mode value from /api/catalog; default Claude Code 'default', Codex 'auto'), account?: string (an added account's id), worktree?: boolean (a new branch and folder; cwd must be in a git repository), title?: string, extra?: { assistant?: string, memory?: false, capUsd?: number, space?: string (a project's or a group's id: the session gets its guidelines; pass its folder as cwd and its assistant), connections?: { '<connection id>': true }, research?: { breadth (1–8), depth (1–5), queries (10–500), format: 'page'|'document' }, swarm?: true | 'campaign' (a swarm on a hard problem, or a campaign: one big change in parts, each member in a worktree; cwd must be in a git repository), arena?: { with: [{ agent, acpAgent?, model? }] } (up to 2 more models: each gets the same prompt in a session of its own), confidential?: true (a confidential session: agent 'acp' with acpAgent 'console', and a model `GET /api/confidential` lists, or none for the best of them; memory goes off; not with research, swarm or arena: 400), help?: true | string (a question asked on the Help page, or about the guide topic with this id: the agent answers from the guide, and the session's model, effort and mode don't become the agent's remembered defaults), browserHandoff?: { tab: string } (Do, in Ask on the Browser page: the id of an open tab of the user's own browser, which the session gets as a tab of its own, signed in as the user, kept to that site and to the browser tools alone; Claude Code or Custom only, 400 for the others, and not with confidential, swarm, research or arena. Only the user's page has a tab to give: agents leave it out) }, images?, files?, paths?: string[] (files and folders already on this computer, up to 20: the message lists them by path under "Attached files", a folder with a slash at its end, and PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP pictures up to 5 MB also go to the model to look at; paths that aren't there are left out), askMemory?: true (the start page sets it for a session a person starts; agents leave it out: in a project whose wiki memory is new (fewer than 10 articles, no build from history run) the session then first asks the person whether to build it, holding the first message, and the person answers on a card in the session), startId?: string (your own id for this start: 'new-' then 8–40 lowercase letters or digits; sent again with the same id, as when its answer was lost on the way, it returns the session that start made instead of making another) }`. Returns: `{ key }`. To start in a new task folder as the start page does, first `POST /api/assistants/task-folder { prompt }` → `{ path }` and pass that as cwd. The model, effort, mode and account also become that agent's remembered defaults. **Careful**: research, swarm and arena run several agents and cost several times as much; say so before using them. Antigravity CLI (`acpAgent: 'antigravity'`) runs only in sessions a person starts, because Google's terms for its plans rule out work an agent or the app starts: when an agent (not a person) asks, it is refused with 'Antigravity CLI runs only in sessions you start and drive yourself…'; pick another agent.
- `GET /api/sessions/start/:id` — what came of a start sent with `startId` whose answer never arrived: the session it made, or whether it is still being made. Returns: `{ key: string | null, starting: boolean }` (no key and not starting: it never arrived, so send it again with the same `startId`).
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/fork` — a new session that starts with this conversation (all of it, or up to `at`), then `text`; the original stays. Claude Code, Codex and Custom only; not for a session open in another app (409). Body: `{ text: string (required, unless attachments), at?: string (Claude Code: a reply's `entry` id from the timeline; Custom: a turn id), images?, files? }`. Returns: `{ key }` (titled "<title> · fork").
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/handoff` — "Continue in …": a new session on another agent in the same folder, starting with this conversation (first request plus the latest messages within about 60,000 characters) and `text`. From Claude Code or Codex sessions only, to the other one or to Custom; never from a confidential session (400). Body: `{ agent: 'claude'|'codex'|'acp', acpAgent?: 'console' (required with 'acp'), text?: string (default "Continue where the conversation left off."), model?, effort?, mode?, account?, images?, files? }`. Returns: `{ key }`. The two sessions link to each other.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/rewind` — "Edit from here": continues from just before one of your messages with new text, in a copy that takes the title; **the original is archived**. Claude Code, Codex and Custom; 409 "Stop the agent first" while a turn runs. Body: `{ itemId: string (a user message's id from the timeline), text: string (required), restoreFiles?: boolean (put the files back as they were before that message: Claude Code, Custom, or any session in a worktree; Codex needs a worktree) }`. 409 "This session’s goal is checking its files right now: …" or "…is putting its files back: …" with restoreFiles while its goal is in the middle of a step. Returns: `{ key }`. **Careful**: restoreFiles changes files on disk.

## Talking to a session

- `POST /api/sessions/:key/message` — sends a message now: into the running turn for Claude Code and Codex, or as a new turn; ACP agents (Custom included) queue it while they work. Starts the agent first if it is stopped (409 if the session runs in another app: use takeover). A reached spending cap refuses it. Body: `{ text: string, images?, files?, sendId?: string (your own id for this message: 'send-' then 8–40 lowercase letters or digits; sent again with the same id within ten minutes, as when its answer was lost on the way, it is not sent twice) }` (text or an attachment required). Returns: `{ ok, queued?: true (it went to the queue instead, as for an ACP agent at work) }`. **Careful**: on another session this speaks in the user's name and makes that agent work.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/queue` — adds a message to send when the running turn ends (sent at once if the agent is running, idle, and the queue is not paused; a stopped agent's queue waits until it runs again). Body: `{ text: string, images?, files?, sendId?: string (as for message: the same id queues it once) }`. Returns: `{ ok }`. **Careful** as for message.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/queue/:id/send` — sends one queued message now, into the running turn (starts the agent if needed). Returns: `{ ok }` (404 if it already went out). **Careful** as for message.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/queue/:id/edit` — changes a queued message. Body: `{ text: string, keep?: string[] (refs of attachments to keep, 'img:<n>'/'file:<n>' from queue[].att; leave out to keep all), images?, files? (new ones, with keep) }`. Returns: `{ ok }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/queue/order` — reorders the queue. Body: `{ ids: string[] }` (ids missing keep their place after these). Returns: `{ ok }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/queue/:id/delete` — takes a message off the queue; its attachments are deleted. Returns: `{ ok }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/queue/resume` — lets a paused queue go out again (after a stop or a restart). Returns: `{ ok }`.

## Running and stopping

- `POST /api/sessions/:key/start` — resumes a stopped session's agent with its whole conversation (nothing is sent). Body: `{ model?, effort?, mode? }` (default: what it last used). Returns: `{ ok }`. 409 if it runs in VS Code or a terminal.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/interrupt` — stops the running turn (as Stop or Esc); the agent stays ready and the queue pauses. Returns: `{ ok }` (409 if the agent is not running). **Careful**: stops another session's work.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/stop` — ends the agent's process; the session and its history stay; the queue pauses. Returns: `{ ok }`. **Careful** as for interrupt.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/stop-all` — stops everything the session started, as the Stop button by the message box does: its running turn (the queue pauses), each background task still running, and each running Hydor subagent, theirs in turn. A Hydor subagent stopped this way does not report back to the session, and a report on its way waits in the paused queue. Returns: `{ ok: true, turn: boolean — a turn was running, tasks: number — background tasks stopped, children: number — the session's own running Hydor subagents stopped }`. A goal whose round is at work (working or checking) in a session that has Hydor subagents, or that leads a swarm, pauses with "You stopped the agent and its subagents. Resume checks what they did." (a swarm: "You stopped the swarm. Resume checks what it did."); what its subagents still send waits in the session's held queue. **Careful**: stops another session's work, and its subagents'.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/tasks/:taskId/stop` — stops one background task (ids in `live.backgroundTasks`). Returns: `{ ok }`. **Careful**: its work ends unfinished.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/takeover` — continues here a Claude Code session open in VS Code or a terminal: **ends that process** (stopping what it does), then resumes it here, with a message if given. Not for Codex (409). Body: `{ text?, images?, files? }`. Returns: `{ ok, queued?: true (as for message) }`. **Careful**: always ask the user first; they may be working in that app.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/resume-at` — "Resume at reset": continues a Claude Code or Codex session stopped by a usage limit (refused for an Antigravity CLI session, which never continues by itself) by sending "Continue where you left off." (the page uses the reset time plus a minute); replaces an earlier one. Only for limits: the app keeps the time on the account's reset as it moves, and continues at once when the account is not at a limit. Body: `{ at: number (ms, not more than a minute in the past) }`. Returns: `{ ok }`. Not a way to schedule messages (use a scheduled task), and not snooze (that is `/flags`).
- `DELETE /api/sessions/:key/resume-at` — cancels that. Returns: `{ ok }`.

## Session settings

- `POST /api/sessions/:key/config` — changes the model, effort or permissions mode; applies to the running agent at once and is kept for the session (and as that agent's remembered default). Body: `{ model?: string|null, effort?: string|null, mode?: string|null }` (send only what changes; values from /api/catalog; a confidential session takes only another model on this computer). Returns: `{ ok }`. **Careful**: a mode like `bypassPermissions` or `full` lets the agent act without asking; never raise permissions without the user's explicit word. Through app_api a change of `mode` always asks the user on a card, and a session in Plan mode can change its own settings this way (on a card) when the user asks it to leave Plan mode.
- `PATCH /api/sessions/:key/extra` — merges into the session's settings beyond its agent's own; `null` removes a field. The page uses it for memory: `{ memory: false }` turns memory off for this session, `{ memory: null }` back on. Returns: `{ extra }`. `confidential` is set only when a session starts and never removed, and a confidential session keeps memory off and takes no swarm, research or arena (400). While the session has a goal that is not over, turning `swarm: 'campaign'`, `research` or `arena` on is refused (409, "This session has a goal: stop the goal first, or lead a swarm with it.", also for a goal that stopped at its limit, which Give it more… can still carry on); `swarm: true`, or the swarm off, is allowed at any time and counts from the loop's next message. The spending cap has its own route (`PUT /api/budget/:key { capUsd }`, from $0.01 to $100,000), and connected accounts theirs.
- `GET /api/goals/:key` — the session's goal as the app's own goal loop keeps it, whether it is over or not (the goal of a program's own, typed as `/goal`, is not here). Returns: `{ goal: GoalState | null }`, where a `GoalState` has `id`; `status` (drafting: the agent works out the checks; agreeing: the checks wait for the user's Start; measuring; working: a round runs; checking: the app runs the checks; asking: a question waits; paused; needs-you; reached; stopped; limit); `reason` (why it paused, which limit, what it needs, or the note that the money limit cannot count this session's spending); `contract { objective, title?, checks [{ id, kind: 'command'|'measure'|'score'|'review'|'you', label, run?, read?, target? { op: '>='|'<=', value } — a score's is set by the app from its baseline, unit?, slack?, evaluation? — a score's evaluation id, gain? — how much better than the baseline a score must be, in its own units (0: beyond the noise of its seeds), baseline? { score, mean, seal, name, metric } — the baseline a score was agreed against, fixed by the app, claim?, evidence? { diff?, files? — files or folders in the session's folder that the reviewer reads (a folder shows its first 24 files, a file its first 30,000 characters), run? }, timeoutS? — for a score, the most seconds it waits for its score (6 hours unless set, 48 at most) }], protect: string[] — paths the agent may not change, limits { rounds, usd?, minutes? } }`; `settings { asks: 'own'|'forks'|'every', noAnswerMin }`; `rounds [{ n — 0 is the measurement before any round, did?, remains?, way?, results [{ id, holds, value? — a score's mean, verdict?: 'better'|'worse'|'same' — a score against its baseline, score? — the score's id, closeness, detail? — kept only for the start, the best round and the last three rounds; a review's may end with the files read at the reviewer's request, error?, since? — in round 0 only: the round after which a check the user added or changed while the goal ran was first measured, so it has no value "at the start", ms }], score, outcome: 'start'|'kept'|'undone'|'stays' (stays: not undone, because the session does not work in a worktree, or another session worked in the folder or a commit was made there meanwhile, or a limit stopped the round midway: `why` says which), why?, cause?: 'protected'|'rule'|'broke'|'behind'|'none' — what kept an undone round from being kept, by?: 'you', reason?, usd, members? — in a session that leads a swarm: how many of its members worked in the round, left out when none did, startedAt, endedAt, checkpoint? }]`; `best` (the round the files stand at); `round` (the one in progress); `pausing?` (true while a pause waits for the round that runs to be checked); `undo` (the session works in a worktree, so a round can be undone); `rules [{ id, text, at }]`; `pending?` (`{ checks?, protect? }`: a change the user made while a round ran, which takes effect once that round has been judged); `next` (the ways it will follow); `dropped` (what was tried and dropped); `question?` (`{ id, kind: 'way'|'yours'|'blocked', question, context?, ways [{ name, why?, expects?, cost? }], pick, why?, askedAt, expiresAt? }`) and `answered?`; `spentUsd`, `activeMs`, `startedAt`, `endedAt?`; `scoring?` (`{ check, runs, done, waiting, why?, since }`: how a score's runs stand while the goal waits for it); and `report?` once it is over (with `finals? [{ evaluation, name }]` when a reached goal's score checks have an evaluation with a final test, which a goal never runs). The page also gets every change as the feature event `goal`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/goal` — set a goal, or steer the one the session has: the app's goal loop drives the session round after round and decides itself, by running the checks, when it is done; it works for every agent. Body: one of `{ action: 'set', objective: string — up to 4,000 characters, what is true when the work is done, settings?: { asks?: 'own'|'forks'|'every', noAnswerMin?: whole number 0–1440 — minutes before an unanswered question goes on with the agent's pick, 0 waits } }` (replaces a goal that is not over; the agent first proposes the checks, and nothing runs before the user agrees; 400 in a Hydor subagent, a session outside the app, Plan mode, or a session that leads a campaign ("This session leads a campaign: a goal works with a swarm, not with a campaign yet."), a deep research ("This session leads a deep research: a goal does not go with it.") or an arena match ("This session is in an arena match: a goal does not go with it."); a session that leads a swarm may have a goal, and a round is then the whole swarm's work; 409 when the session has a goal of its program's own, which `/goal clear` removes first); `{ action: 'agree', contract?: { checks?, protect?, limits?, title? } }` (the user's Start, with the checks as changed; a score check's target is fixed then from its evaluation's baseline, the files the evaluation protects join `protect`, and it is scored for the first time, as the start (a score is never run before this, in any mode); 400 with a sentence when its evaluation is not there, cannot score the session's folder or has no baseline ("“Beats the baseline”: the baseline of “Relevance” is not measured yet: only you can measure it, on the evaluation’s page."); 409 unless the checks wait for it); `{ action: 'change', contract: { checks?, protect?, limits? } }` (limits count at once; checks and protected paths take effect with the next round, or once the running round has been judged, and show as `pending` meanwhile; a check keeps its `id` to be changed, one without is new; a check changed only in `target`, `slack`, `unit`, `timeoutS` or `label` is judged again from its stored values against the new target, not measured again, and the start keeps its value; a changed command, pattern, kind, claim or evidence is measured again; at most 12 checks, and at most 4 wildcards in a protected path, else 400; 409 while the goal is drafting, measuring or checking; on a goal that stopped at a limit only new `limits` are accepted, and they give it more and it goes on, with 409 and the team's sentence ("This session leads a campaign: a goal works with a swarm, not with a campaign yet.", and the same for a deep research and an arena match) when the session now leads a campaign, a deep research or an arena match, with 409 naming what it has used when a limit is still hit ("It has run 4 rounds: give it more than that.", and the same for money and time), and 409 saying what is in the way while the round it stopped is not yet saved and its files put back; a goal that was reached or stopped stays over, 409); `{ action: 'answer', ways: number[] — indexes of the ways ticked, in the order to follow them (for a check only the user can confirm: 0 holds, 1 not yet; for the question the goal asks when a score's evaluation was sealed again or given another baseline, 0 fixes the check against the evaluation as it is now and scores the round that waited, and while the evaluation has no baseline the question stays, saying so), other?: string — a way of their own, note?: string }`; `{ action: 'wait' }` (the open question waits instead of going on with the pick; 409 without an open question); `{ action: 'pause' }` (while a round runs, or its checks do, the goal does not pause at once: `pausing` is set, that round is finished and checked as any round, and then the goal pauses; while a score is waited for, its runs are stopped and the goal pauses at once with the round unchecked, which Resume scores again; in every other state it pauses at once); `{ action: 'resume' }` (also takes back a pause that waits for the round that runs: the goal carries straight on); `{ action: 'stop' }` (`clear` is the older name; a score being waited for has its runs stopped; a goal that is over is deleted, which dismisses its report); `{ action: 'settings', settings: { asks?, noAnswerMin? } }`; `{ action: 'rule', text: string — up to 2,000 characters, kept for every round (at most 12) }`; `{ action: 'unrule', id: string }` (`rule`, `unrule` and `settings` are also accepted on a goal that stopped at a limit: they count when it goes on); `{ action: 'round', n: number, do: 'keep'|'undo'|'from', reason?: string, relax?: { [checkId]: number — a new target } }` (the user's say on a round; only in a worktree and between rounds, else 409; 409 "The goal pauses once this round has been checked: give your say then." while a pause waits for the round that runs; also 409, with a sentence that says what is in the way and nothing applied, while this session's agent is at work ("This session’s agent is at work: wait for its turn to end, then try again."), while another session is at work in the folder, after a commit made there since the goal last left the files, and when files were changed outside the goal since then: it names up to five of them and gives the commands, to run in the worktree's top folder, that put the user's changes to them aside in a stash and put back the goal's own version; if putting the files back does not finish the goal pauses once, with everything saved in the round's files). Pause, resume and clear in a session without such a goal act on the program's own goal (`/goal` in Claude Code, a Codex thread's goal; 400 to pause Claude Code's, 409 for other agents). Returns: `{ goal: GoalState | null }`; 400 with a sentence when something is off, 409 when the goal is not in a state for the action (for example "Wait for the round to end, or pause the goal first"). At a money or time limit in the middle of a round, the session's Hydor subagents at work (a swarm's members, or its own, also those between turns with a command still running and one waiting to carry on after a usage limit) are stopped as well as its agent, and what they send after that waits in its held queue until the goal goes on (Resume and Give it more… send it first when the queue holds only agents' messages). **Careful**: a goal keeps an agent working (and spending) until its checks hold or a limit is reached.
- `GET /api/goal-settings` — what a new goal starts from (Settings → Capabilities → Goals). Returns: `{ rounds: number — the rounds a new goal gets before it stops at its limit, which the agreement card or the agent's proposal may change (default 30; until set here, the old Custom setting's number); asks: 'own'|'forks'|'every' — how much a new goal asks (default 'forks'); noAnswerMin: number — minutes before an unanswered question goes on with the agent's pick, 0 waits (default 10) }`.
- `PUT /api/goal-settings` — changes any of the three; merges. Body: `{ rounds?: whole number 1–500, asks?: 'own'|'forks'|'every', noAnswerMin?: whole number 0–1440 }`. Returns: as GET; 400 with a sentence ("Rounds a goal may run: a whole number from 1 to 500.") when one is not valid, and nothing changes.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/compact` — replaces the conversation so far with a summary to free context (Claude Code and Codex; 400 for ACP agents; 409 while a turn runs). Starts the agent if stopped. Returns: `{ ok }`.

- `GET /api/sessions/:key/wiki-checkpoint` — what became of the last checkpoint Wiki memory took of this session before an automatic compaction. Returns: `{ status: "saved"|"failed", event: "PreCompact"|"PostCompact"|"PostToolUse"|"Stop", at: string (ISO time), content: "summary"|"metadata"|null }`, or `{ status: "disabled" }` (Wiki memory or Memory is off for the chat, or it is confidential: nothing else is said), `{ status: "unavailable" }` (none yet) or `{ status: "error" }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/wiki-codex-hooks` — whether a Codex session has the compaction hook the app registers. Returns: `{ coverage: null | { complete: boolean, events: { sessionStart|preCompact|postCompact|stop: { trusted: number, needsReview: number, disabled: number } } } }`; `null` for other agents, when it is off for the chat, or when this Codex cannot say. Counts only: they do not show that a checkpoint was saved, and never name a command.

## Organizing sessions

- `POST /api/sessions/:key/account` — moves a Claude Code, Codex or Grok Build session to another of its agent's accounts (Settings → Accounts): its history moves there (a Grok Build session's Grok history too: when Grok cannot reopen it there, it starts afresh with a note), and its agent starts there with the next message. Body: `{ account: string — an added account's id, or '' for the CLI's own sign-in }`. Returns: `{ ok }`. 404 for an account of another agent or none; 409 while a turn is running, or when the session is open in another program.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/rename` — sets the title. Body: `{ title: string }` (trimmed, up to 200 characters; empty gives back the agent's own title). Returns: `{ ok }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/flags` — pin, archive or snooze (send only what changes). Body: `{ pinned?: boolean, archived?: boolean, snoozedUntil?: number|null (ms in the future; null unsnoozes) }`. Returns: `{ ok }`. A snoozed session comes back at that time, or as soon as it needs the user.
- `POST /api/sessions/order` — the order of a sidebar group (Active or Pinned) after a drag. Body: `{ keys: string[] }` (top to bottom, up to 1,000 session keys). Returns: `{ ok }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/folder` — puts the session in one of the sidebar's folders, or takes it out of its folder. A session is in one folder at most, and the folder must be in the session's own project (a session in no project goes in a top-level folder). Body: `{ folder: string|null — a folder's id from GET /api/session-folders; null takes the session out of its folder }`. Returns: `{ ok }`. 404 for a session or folder that is not there; 400 for a folder of another project. A session's folder shows as `extra.folder` in its entry of `GET /api/sessions`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/settle` — "Settle: done for now": stops the agent (409 "It is still working; stop it first" while a turn runs) and marks the session read; history and worktree stay. Returns: `{ ok }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/seen` — marks the session read (it finished since the user last looked). Returns: `{ ok }`.
- `DELETE /api/sessions/:key` — stops the session and deletes its history from disk (Claude Code's and Codex's own files too), its queued messages and its notes. Cannot be undone. Returns: `{ ok }`. **Careful**: confirm with the user, naming the session; prefer archiving.

## Worktrees

- `GET /api/sessions/:key/worktree` — the session's worktree (404 if it has none). Returns: `{ path, branch, base, repo, exists, dirty (uncommitted files), ahead, behind (commits against base) }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/worktree/remove` — removes the worktree's folder (stops the agent first); the session keeps its history; other sessions in the same worktree lose it too. The branch stays unless asked, or when it has nothing new. Body: `{ deleteBranch?: boolean, force?: boolean (needed when there are uncommitted changes, which are then lost) }`. Returns: `{ branchKept: boolean }`. **Careful**: deletes files; force and deleteBranch can lose work.

## Pull requests and review (from a session's branch)

- `GET /api/sessions/:key/pr/draft` — what a pull request would hold. Returns: `{ head, base, remote, host? { provider, owner, repo } (missing when no account in Settings → Source control covers it), commits [{ sha, subject }], stat { files, added, removed }, dirty, pushed, existing? }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/pr/describe` — a model drafts a title and description from the commits and diff (needs commits). Returns: `{ title, body }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/pr` — commits (if asked), **pushes the branch** and opens the pull request on GitHub or Gitea, linked to the session. Body: `{ title: string (required), body?: string (Markdown), draft?: boolean, commitAll?: boolean (commit uncommitted changes first, with the title as message) }`. Returns: `{ pr { url, number, title, state, checks?, repo } }`. **Careful**: publishes to the remote; only when the user asked.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/pr/link` — links an existing pull request by its address. Body: `{ url: string }`. Returns: `{ pr }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/pr/refresh` — reads its state and checks now. Returns: `{ pr }`.
- `DELETE /api/sessions/:key/pr` — unlinks it (it stays on its host). Returns: `{ ok }`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/review` — everything the branch changes since its base, uncommitted work included. Returns: `{ head, base, files [{ path, status, added, removed, diff, binary? }], uncommitted, guide? }`.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/review/guide` — a model writes a reading order with a note per file. Returns: `{ guide { summary, steps [{ path, note }] } }`.

## Read-only links (listed only; documented with sharing)

- `GET /api/sessions/:key/guest-links` — the links that work now.
- `POST /api/sessions/:key/guest-links` — makes a link (`{ days?: number|null }`, at most 365; null = until turned off); returns `{ id, token }`, the address being `/g/<token>`. 409 for a confidential session. **Careful**: anyone with it can read the session.
- `DELETE /api/guest-links/:id` — turns one off.

## Projects

A project: `{ id ("sp…"), name, color, description?, folder (where its sessions work; fixed once made), instructions? (its guidelines, told to every session in it), assistant? (the id of the assistant its sessions start as), sharedKnowledge?: false (present only when its sessions leave the shared knowledge out), wikiMemory?: boolean (present only when the project has its own Wiki memory switch: true on, false off; absent, it follows **Settings → Memory → Wiki memory**), memoryShared?: false (present only when the project keeps itself to itself: agents in your other projects cannot search or read its notes, wiki, documents and conversations; absent, they can), wikiDocumentLimit?: number (present only when the project has its own number for **Most documents to read into its wiki**, 0 to 100000; absent, it follows **Settings → Memory → Wiki memory → Most documents to read into the wiki**), members? (project ids: it is a group), createdAt }`.

Elsewhere a project's id is in fields named `space`, and lists of projects in `spaces`: the name projects had before. Their old addresses, `/api/spaces/…`, still work.

- `GET /api/projects` — every project, oldest first, with counts. Returns: `{ spaces: [{ …space, sessions: number (every session in it: started in it, or working in its folder), lastAt?: ms (when one of them was last at work, by their own activity), found?: ('claude' | 'codex')[] (the programs whose sessions the app found the project in: only on a project the app made by itself), docs: number (documents in its own knowledge bases), skills: number (skills of its own), library: number (the things kept in it: pages, decks, documents, spreadsheets, LaTeX documents, pictures and videos, which show in its **Files**), people?: number (how many people it is shared with now, by links that work; absent when none) }] }`.
- `GET /api/projects/activity?days=28&tz=Europe/Athens` — the projects' work: how many messages each project's sessions were sent on each of the last `days` days (7 to 90, default 28), the days counted in the time zone `tz` (an IANA name such as `Europe/Athens`; UTC without one, or when it is not a time zone). A session counts for the project it is in, and a subagent for its lead's. Returns: `{ days: [{ key: string — the date, "2026-11-18"; start: ms — that date as UTC midnight }] (oldest first, the last one today), projects: [{ id, sessions: number — how many of its sessions were at work in those days, prompts: number — the messages they were sent, byDay: number[] — the messages of each day, one for each of `days` }] (the busiest first; a project with no work in those days is left out) }`.
- `GET /api/projects/suggestions` — folders worth a project of their own: those sessions worked in, or changed files in, in the last 90 days, that no project has yet (neither its folder nor one of the folders it works with), the most used and most recent first, at most 6. A session counts for the folder it ran in and, less, for each other folder whose files it changed; a folder inside a git repository counts as the repository; sessions in a project don't count. The home folder, hidden folders, temporary ones, the app's own (task folders, apps' runs, worktrees) and those hidden with the route below are left out. Returns: `{ folders: [{ path, sessions: number — sessions that worked in it or changed its files, changed: number — of those, how many only changed its files, lastAt: ms }] }`.
- `POST /api/projects/suggestions/hide` — "Not a project": stops suggesting a folder and stops the app making it a project by itself (the folder itself is untouched; a project can still be made for it with `POST /api/projects`). Body: `{ folder: string — its full path }` (400 "Which folder?" otherwise). Returns: `{ folders }` as above.
- `GET /api/projects/:id/wiki` — the project's Wiki memory: its articles and sources, read-only. Looking never makes the wiki's folder. Returns `{ status: "off"|"empty"|"ready"|"error", on: boolean (whether Wiki memory is on for the project now), inherited: boolean (true when the project follows **Settings → Memory → Wiki memory** and has no switch of its own), root?, articles?, sources?, plans? (the project's plans, `output/projects/`, which only the plan tools change, and the evidence of carrying them out, `output/evidence/`), truncated?, error? }`: `off` when it is off for the project, `empty` when it is on and nothing is kept yet, `ready` with articles and sources as `{ path, title, updated?: number — when its file last changed (ms since 1970), summary?: string — its `summary:` line, at most 200 characters, left out when it has none }` entries, `error` when the folder cannot be read safely. Hidden, archived, retired and linked files are left out, and so are redirects (articles merged into another). Once the wiki has areas it also has `areas: [{ id, name, about, parent: id|null (a sub-area's area), hub?: path (its hub, `wiki/areas/<id>.md`), articles: path[] (in its hub's reading order) }]`, top-level areas in order, each followed by its sub-areas, Other (`id: "other"`) last; an article in two areas is in both, and `articles` then leaves the hubs out. 404 when the project does not exist.
- `GET /api/projects/:id/memory/search` — one search of a project's memory, for the Memory tab's search box: its notes, its Wiki memory and its own documents at once. Read-only; it never makes the wiki's folder and never waits for search by meaning to start (it takes part when its model is already running). Query: `q` (what to look for), `limit` (how many of each kind, default 20, at most 50). Returns: `{ query: string, notes: entry[] — the project's own notes, its topics and its profile, best first (never another project's notes or your own profile), articles: [{ path: string — wiki/… or raw/…, title: string, excerpt?: string — the passage that matched, or the article's summary when only its title did, by: ("words"|"meaning"|"exact"|"title")[] }] — the wiki's articles and sources, best first: those whose title holds the whole question, then those whose passages the wiki's index found, then those whose title or summary holds every word (none while Wiki memory is off for the project; area hubs are left out), documents: KnowledgeHit[] — passages of the project's own knowledge bases (not its wiki's index, not the shared knowledge, not another project's), best first, meaning: boolean — search by meaning took part }`. 404 when the project does not exist; 400 without `q`.
- `GET /api/projects/:id/wiki/file?path=…` — one active Markdown article, source, plan or piece of evidence under `wiki/`, `raw/`, `output/projects/` or `output/evidence/` of this project's wiki. An article that was merged into another (a redirect) is followed one step. Returns `{ path, content, sha256, redirectedFrom?: string — the path asked for, when it was a redirect, note?: string }`. 404 when Wiki memory is off for the project, the wiki or the file is not there, or the path is not allowed (hidden folders, `..`, links, retired documents).
- `GET /api/projects/:id/wiki/updates` — what new and changed documents in the project's knowledge did to its Wiki memory and memory notes, newest first, at most 20. Returns `{ updates: [{ id: number, at: number — when, document: string — its title, path: string — the file, or `session:<key>` for a conversation, `commits:…` for a day's commit messages, `code:…` for articles brought up to date after the project's files changed (**Code changes**), `overview:<folder>` for an overview of documents the wiki does not read, `areas:<date>` for a reorganisation of the wiki's areas (**Areas**: `document` says what changed, such as "Split Crawling into Fetching and Index; moved 6 articles", and `articles` the articles it moved), `merge:<path>` for articles merged into the one at that path (**Merged**: `document` says "A into B"), `rebuild:<time>` for a rebuild from the wiki's sources (**Rebuild**: one row, its `document` saying how far it has got, then what it made) and `restore:<time>` for an archived wiki put back, articles: string[] — the wiki articles it added or changed, notes: number — memory notes it added, error?: string — why it did nothing or what went wrong }], documents: { count: number — indexed documents in the project's own knowledge (not its wiki's files), limit: number — the most its wiki reads (the project's own `wikiDocumentLimit`, else **Most documents to read into the wiki** in Settings), over: boolean — count is more than limit: its wiki reads only `limit` of them (the pinned, the most used by agents, then the newest) and agents search all of them } }`. 404 when the project does not exist.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/wiki/reorganise` — looks at the project's wiki areas again now, whatever its triggers say, and waits for it (a minute or two; one at a time in the app): the stronger model proposes the areas, the helper checks where each moved article goes, and Hydor applies the rules (every article in an area or Other, two levels at most, areas of two or more, at most one area per two articles, and but for the first time at most a third of the articles moved). Body: none. Returns: `{ ran: true, first: boolean — the wiki's first organisation, summary: string — what changed, as **Wiki updates…** shows it, areas: [{ id, name, parent, articles: number }] (Other last), added: string[], renamed: [{ from, to }], split: [{ from, into: string[] }], merged: [{ from: string[], into }], removed: string[] — area names, moved: number — articles whose areas it changed, skipped: number — articles changed while it ran, placed next time, waiting: number — moves held back for the next pass, queued: number — folds and duplicates queued (merged later, at most two a day), fitChanged: number — placements the check changed }`. The hubs it makes out of date are written afterwards, in the background. 404 when the project does not exist; 409 when Memory or the project's Wiki memory is off, the wiki is empty or has fewer than two articles, or a reorganisation is running; 502 when the helper model gave no usable answer.
- `POST /api/wiki/reorganise` — the same for the shared wiki. Body: none. Returns: as above; 409 when the shared wiki is off.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/wiki/rebuild` — rebuilds the project's wiki from the sources it keeps in `raw/`. Without `confirm` it only estimates: Body: `{}`. Returns: `{ sources: number — the sources it would read: the ones it keeps in `raw/` (an article that cites no source counts as one; commit messages are left out, they are read again from the repository) and the history it makes fresh, reads: { kept: number, files: string[] — what was found (README.md, AGENTS.md, docs/), notes: number, commits: number, commitSources: number, documents: number, sessions: number — the documents and conversations the wiki never read }, minutes: number — about how long, workers: number — how many sources are read at once, calls: number — helper calls, about two a source plus the areas and one a hub, callsMax: number — the most it may take, two more for each article now (an earlier article no new one covers is read again), chars: number — what it reads, articles: number — the wiki's articles now, helper: { model: "auto" or a Custom model's id, label, priced: boolean, usd?: number — the price from the app's model prices, $0 for a model on this computer, usdMax?: number — the price at callsMax, note?: string — with Automatic, that it uses the Claude account's usage }, archives: [{ at, articles }] }`. With `{ confirm: true }` it archives the wiki (its `wiki/`, `index.md`, `log.md` and areas, into a hidden folder; the 3 latest kept; `raw/` and `output/` never move), then reads in the background, a few at once (`workers`): the project's files, made fresh; the kept sources oldest first, except commit messages, which it reads fresh from the repository; the documents and conversations the wiki never read; the project's memory notes, made fresh; and then each article of the earlier wiki that no new article covers (about two more calls each), organises the areas, writes the hubs, does the queued folds and makes every old article path that is gone a redirect to the new article that matches it best (else to `index.md`); memory notes are never touched. Returns: `{ started: true, sources: number, archive: string — when the earlier wiki was archived }`. 404 when the project does not exist; 409 when its Wiki memory is off, the wiki is empty (build it from its history instead) or has nothing to read, or it is building, rebuilding, updating or reorganising already.
- `GET /api/projects/:id/wiki/rebuild` — what the wiki is doing at a person's request (a build from history, a rebuild, an update or a reorganisation), or did last, with its progress, and its archives. Returns: `{ state: "idle"|"running"|"done"|"failed"|"stopped", kind?: "build"|"rebuild"|"update"|"reorganise", phase?: string — while it runs: "sources", "organise", "hubs" for a build; the same and "redirects" for a rebuild; "documents", "conversations", "commits", "drift", "hubs", "merges", "areas" for an update, done: number — sources read, or parts done, total: number, startedAt?, endedAt?, error?: string, parts?: string[] — an update's, did?: string[] — what it did, a line each, progress?: { steps: [{ id: "files"|"notes"|"sessions"|"commits"|"documents"|"sources"|"organise"|"hubs", label: string, done: number, total: number, essential: boolean — what a build reads first }], current: string[] — what is being read now, minutesLeft: number — worked out from the time recent sources took, divided by the workers, minutesLeftEssentials?: number, essentials?: number — how many of the sources a build reads first, essentialsAt?: number — when they had all been read } — not for a reorganisation, archives: [{ at: string — when it was archived, articles: number }] — newest first }`. A build or rebuild the app's restart cut short goes on where it stopped; one a person stopped stays stopped. 404 when the project does not exist.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/wiki/rebuild/restore` — puts an archived wiki back; the wiki as it is now is archived first. Body: `{ at: string — an archive's time, as GET lists it }`. Returns: `{ restored: string, archived: string — when the wiki it replaced was archived, articles: number }`. 400 without `at`; 404 when there is no such archive; 409 while the wiki is rebuilding, updating or reorganising.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/wiki/update` — Update now: does at once, in the background, what the wiki would do over the next hours. Body: `{ parts?: ("sources"|"articles"|"areas")[] — default all three }`: `sources` reads the project's documents waiting in the queue, its conversations not read yet (the daily pass for this project alone, with its limits and exclusions) and its new commits; `articles` brings up to date up to 10 articles marked out of date by the code, writes every stale hub (no hourly limit) and does up to 5 queued merges; `areas` is the reorganising pass (as `…/wiki/reorganise`). Returns: `{ started: true, parts }`; its progress and what it did are in `GET …/wiki/rebuild`. 400 for other parts; 404 when the project does not exist; 409 when its Wiki memory is off or it is busy.
- `GET /api/projects/:id/wiki/build` — what building the project's wiki from its history would read and take (nothing is prepared; cheap enough for a session's start). Returns: `{ empty: boolean — the wiki has no article yet (the index, log, hubs and redirects do not count), fresh: boolean — the wiki is new: fewer than 10 articles and no build from history has run for the project (a build starting, or a rebuild finishing, ends it); this is what a session's question and **Build from history…** go by, counts: { sessions: number — conversations the wiki has not read, commits: number — the newest 1,200 without merges, documents: number, notes: number, files: string[] — what was found, such as README.md, AGENTS.md, docs/ }, worth: boolean — there is anything to read, essentials: number — how many sources are read first, sources: number — all of them, minutesEssentials: number, minutesAll: number — about how long, helper: { label, priced: boolean, usd?: number } }`. 404 when it is not a project; 409 when memory or the project's Wiki memory is off.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/wiki/build` — Build from history, for a project whose wiki is new (reads only what the wiki has not read). Without `confirm` it answers with the offer, as the GET does. Body: `{ confirm?: boolean, exclude?: string — a session key left out of what is read }`. With `{ confirm: true }` it reads, in the background and a few sources at once, the project's files, memory notes (only read: no note is made or changed), conversations (as the daily pass may read them, up to 300, the most signal first), commit messages (the newest 1,200) and documents the wiki has not read; what matters most first (the files, the notes and the 10 conversations with the most signal), then the rest; then it organises the areas, writes the hubs and merges duplicates. Returns: `{ started: true, sources: number, essentials: number }`; its progress is in `GET …/wiki/rebuild`. 404 when it is not a project; 409 when memory or Wiki memory is off, the wiki is not new (10 articles or more, or a build from history has run: "use Update now or Rebuild"), there is nothing to read, or the wiki is busy.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/wiki/stop` — stops the wiki's running build, rebuild or update: the loop ends between sources and the helper calls in flight are aborted. What was written stays; the job's state becomes `"stopped"` with where it stopped, and a stopped rebuild keeps its archive (Restore still works).
