Returns: `{ stopping: true, kind }`. 404 when it is not a project; 409 when nothing runs, or a reorganisation does (it cannot be stopped).
- `POST /api/projects/:id/wiki/continue` — goes on with a stopped build or rebuild from where it stopped, or after a failed one reads again what the helper could not read. Returns: `{ started: true, kind, done, total }`. 404 when it is not a project; 409 when there is no stopped or failed build or rebuild, or the wiki is busy.
- `POST /api/wiki/rebuild`, `GET /api/wiki/rebuild`, `POST /api/wiki/rebuild/restore`, `POST /api/wiki/update`, `POST /api/wiki/stop`, `POST /api/wiki/continue` — the same for the shared wiki (its `sources` part reads only the shared knowledge's waiting documents).
- `GET /api/wiki/updates` — the same for the shared wiki, from documents in shared knowledge (which add no memory notes). Returns `{ updates: [...], documents: { count, limit, over } }` for the shared knowledge, against the number in Settings.
- `GET /api/projects/:id` — one project and its sessions. Returns: `{ space, sessions: string[] — session keys of every session in it (started in it, or working in its folder), the last at work first, at most 100, memoryKey: string — where memory keeps the project's notes (`scopeKey` of its "repo" notes: its Git repository's root, the main repository's for a worktree, else its folder) }`. 404 "No such project".
- `POST /api/projects` — makes a project, or a group when `members` is given. Body: `{ name: string — required, cut at 60 chars (400 "Give the project a name" when empty); color?: "amber"|"blue"|"green"|"pink"|"violet"|"teal"|"orange"|"slate" — default: the next in turn, any other value becomes blue; description?: string — what it is about, cut at 300; folder?: string — an existing folder to make the project around (a full path or ~/…; 400 "No folder at …" when it isn't one); left out, the app makes a new one: <task folders>/Projects/<name in lowercase with dashes> (default ~/Hydor/Projects/…, with -2, -3… when taken); folders?: string[] — other folders of the user's it works with, where they are (full paths or ~/…; 400 "No folder at …" when one isn't a folder): each once, at most 12, its own folder and folders inside it left out; its sessions are told of them, and Claude Code works in them as in its own; instructions?: string — its guidelines (Agent Guidelines), cut at 20,000; assistant?: string — an assistant id (GET /api/assistants), cut at 80; sharedKnowledge?: boolean — false: its sessions search only its own documents (default: the shared knowledge too); wikiMemory?: boolean|null — true or false: Wiki memory on or off for this project; null or left out: it follows **Settings → Memory → Wiki memory** (400 for anything else); memoryShared?: boolean — false: it keeps itself to itself, so agents in other projects cannot search or read its notes, wiki, documents and conversations (default: they can, when they look for them; 400 for anything but true or false); wikiDocumentLimit?: number|null — the most documents of its knowledge its wiki reads, a whole number from 0 to 100000: a project with more keeps them all out of its wiki and agents search them instead; null or left out: it follows **Settings → Memory → Wiki memory → Most documents to read into the wiki** (400 "wikiDocumentLimit: a whole number from 0 to 100000, or null" for anything else); members?: string[] — project ids to bring together: existing projects that aren't groups, each once, at most 12 (others are silently dropped) }`. Sessions already working in its folder (or a folder inside it) join it. A `folder` that is a project's own which the app made by itself (one with `found`) takes that project over: the fields given are set, `found` is dropped, and it returns that project. Returns: the project.
- `PATCH /api/projects/:id` — changes a project. It merges: only the fields given change, the rest are kept. Body: the same fields as POST except `folder`, which can't change. `""` clears description, instructions or assistant; `folders` replaces the list of folders it works with (`[]` leaves none; the folders themselves are untouched); `sharedKnowledge: true` (or anything but false) brings the shared knowledge back; `wikiMemory: true` or `false` sets the project's own Wiki memory switch, `null` removes it so the project follows Settings again, and leaving it out keeps it; `memoryShared: false` makes the project keep itself to itself (agents in your other projects can't search or read its notes, wiki, documents and conversations), `true` lets them again (400 "memoryShared: true or false" for anything else), and leaving it out keeps it; `wikiDocumentLimit: 300` gives the project its own number of documents its wiki reads, `null` removes it so the project follows Settings again, and leaving it out keeps it; `members: []` turns a group back into a plain project; giving members to a project that is itself in a group fails with 400 ("… is in a group already…"). Returns: the project. 404 "No such project".
- `DELETE /api/projects/:id` — deletes a project. Its folder, files, sessions and memory notes stay, and its folder goes on the Not a project list (neither suggested nor made a project again by the app; a project can still be made for it with `POST /api/projects`); what it kept stays, in no project, and the files those things had in its folder stay as plain files; its knowledge bases become shared; its skills join the skill library (one whose name the library has already gets the project's after it: research → research-thesis); it leaves every group it was in (a group left with no projects becomes a plain project). Returns: `{ ok: true, skills: string[] — the names its skills have in the skill library now }`.
- `GET /api/projects/:id/skills` — a project's own skills (only its sessions get them, with the skill library; a group's sessions get its projects' too), the ones removed that can come back, and the skills it switches on or off for its own sessions. Returns: `{ skills: [{ name, description (when to use it), triggers: string[] (keywords for Custom), overrides?: true (the library has a skill of that name, which this one takes the place of in the project), path, updatedAt: ms, uses: number (times agents loaded it in the project or a group that brings it in), lastUsed?: ms }], removed: [{ name, at }], switches: { [key]: boolean } (key: a skill of the library or of the project by its name, Claude Code's or Codex's own as "claude:<name>" or "codex:<name>"; true: on for the project's sessions, false: off; a skill not named is as Settings → Skills has it, the project's own on), members?: [{ id, name, skills: [as above] }] (a group: the skills of the projects it brings together, which its sessions get too) }`. 404 "No such project".
- `PUT /api/projects/:id/skill-switches` — turns a skill on or off for the project's sessions alone, whatever Settings → Skills says for every other session, or back to that. Body: `{ name: string — the skill's name as the skills list shows it, agent?: 'claude'|'codex' — for one of that agent's own skills (GET /api/setup/skills?cwd=<the project's folder>); left out, a skill of the library or of the project, on: boolean | null — null: as Settings → Skills has it again }`. A project's sessions get it when they start (Custom from its next turn): the library's and the project's skills in their skill list, Claude Code's own as its settings for the session, Codex's own as its thread's settings; the agents' own settings files are not changed. At most 400 switches. Returns: `{ switches }` as in GET /api/projects/:id/skills. 400 for a name that isn't a skill's, another agent, or an `on` that is not true, false or null; 404 "No such project".
- `POST /api/projects/:id/skill-switches/bulk` — turns a snapshot of skills on or off for this project's sessions in one write. Body: `{ skills: [{ name, agent?: 'claude'|'codex'|null, usual: boolean }], expected: { [key]: boolean }, on: boolean }`, where `usual` is the skill's setting without a project override and `expected` is the project's switches as last read. A skill normally matching the requested state follows its setting; otherwise it gets an override. Unlisted switches stay unchanged. Returns `{ switches }`. 400 for an invalid snapshot or more than 400 switches; 409 when the switches changed or duplicate keys disagree. A new skill follows its existing setting. Existing sessions get changes as described for the single switch above.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/skill-switches/off` — the same as `/bulk` with `on: false` (the route of 0.71.0, kept for apps and machines on that version). Body: `{ skills, expected }` as for `/bulk`. Returns: `{ switches }`.
- `DELETE /api/projects/:id/skill-switches` — every skill back to what Settings → Skills says, for the project's sessions too. Returns: `{ switches: {} }`. 404 "No such project".
- `GET /api/projects/:id/skills/:name` — one of a project's skills, whole, with its history of changes. Returns: `{ name, text (the whole SKILL.md), source: 'space', space: { id, name }, file, history: [{ id, name, at, action: 'create'|'update'|'remove'|'restore', key?, diff, added, removed }] }`. 404 when the project has no skill of that name.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/skills` — adds a skill to the project, or copies one of the skill library into it to change for the project alone. Body: `{ name: string — lowercase words with dashes (other text is turned into that: "Citation style" → citation-style; 400 when it ends up shorter than 2 characters), description: string — when to use it, one line of at least 10 characters ("Use when citing a source in the thesis"), body: string — the steps, checks and mistakes to avoid, in Markdown (more than a few words; the whole skill at most 20,000 characters), triggers?: string[] — up to 12 keywords that bring it into Custom's turn }`, or `{ from: string — a library skill's name: copied with its other files }`. A skill that looks like it holds a secret is refused (400). Returns: the skill, as in the list. 409 when the project has a skill of that name already; 404 when `from` names no library skill.
- `PUT /api/projects/:id/skills/:name` — changes one of the project's skills. It merges: only the fields given change (its keywords stay unless `triggers` is given). Body: `{ name?, description?, body?, triggers? }` as for POST; a new `name` renames it, its other files, history and switch going along (409 when the project has a skill of that name). Returns: the skill. 404 when the project has no skill of that name.
- `DELETE /api/projects/:id/skills/:name` — removes one of the project's skills: its sessions stop seeing it (or get the library's skill of that name again, as Settings → Skills has it: the removed skill's switch goes with it). Its text stays in its history. Returns: `{ ok: true }`. 404 when the project has no skill of that name.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/skills/:name/restore` — brings back a skill the project had, as it was when removed. No body. Returns: `{ ok: true }`. 404 when there is nothing to bring back; 409 when the project has a skill of that name.
To start a session in a project as the start page does, call `POST /api/sessions` (Starting sessions, above) with `cwd`: the project's `folder`, and `extra: { space: <space id>, assistant?: <its assistant> }`. The server then adds the project's guidelines, what its folder holds now and the other folders it works with (in a group, each member project's name, folders and guidelines too; Claude Code gets access to all those folders) and scopes the session's document search to the project; it doesn't fill in the folder or the assistant from the project. To add a file to a project as the Files page does, copy it into the project's `folder` with your own tools; to add a folder, append it to the project's `folders` with PATCH.

## Shared knowledge and a project's documents

A knowledge base: `{ id ("kb…"), name, description?, space? (the project it belongs to: only that project's sessions search it; none: shared), createdAt, sources: [{ id, kind: "files"|"folder", path, name, addedAt }] (kind "files" is the base's own "Files you added" folder, made with it), filesDir (where dropped files are kept, in the app's data folder), docs, passages, embedded (passages ready for search by meaning), errors: [{ path, error }] (documents that could not be read, at most 20), indexing?: { done, total } (being read now), indexedAt? }`.

- `GET /api/knowledge` — every knowledge base, and whether search by meaning is on (Settings → Memory). Returns: `{ bases: KnowledgeBase[], meaning: boolean, meaningHeld?: { why: "setting-up"|"starting"|"memory"|"failed", detail?: string — what it is doing, or why it stopped, freeMb?: number, needMb?: number — memory free and needed, for "memory" } — while some passages wait for their vectors and search by meaning cannot make them now: setting itself up, its model starting, too little memory free (it tries again every few minutes), or stopped (PUT /api/memory with { retry: true } starts it again) }`. Each base's `embedded` counts the passages ready to search by meaning, of its `passages`. A base whose `space` no longer exists counts as shared. A base that is the index of a project's or the shared Wiki memory ("Project wiki", "Shared wiki") carries `wiki: true`; the others carry no `wiki`.
- `GET /api/knowledge/:id/docs` — the documents a base holds, by path, at most 2,000. Returns: `{ docs: [{ id, base, path, title (the file's name), format, size, passages, status: "indexed"|"empty" (no text in it)|"error", error?, indexedAt, wikiPin?: true (pinned: wiki memory always reads it) }] }`. 404 "No such knowledge base".
- `PUT /api/knowledge/documents/:id/wiki-pin` — pins a document to be read into wiki memory, or lets it go: a pinned document is read first when a project has more documents than its wiki reads (**Memory** tab → click the document → the switch **Read into wiki memory**), and is read at once. Body: `{ pin: boolean — true to pin, false to let go }`. Returns: `{ id, pinned }`. 404 "No such document" (also for the wiki's own files); 400 "A wiki’s own articles are not read into the wiki" and "Say pin: true or false".
- `POST /api/knowledge/search` — searches the documents as agents do. Body: `{ query: string — a question; "exact words" in quotes or a /pattern/ is searched for exactly; mode?: "auto" (words and meaning, the default)|"words"|"meaning"|"exact"; space?: string|null — search only what a session in that project searches (null or "": a session in no project, so the shared knowledge alone); left out: every base; bases?: string[] — only these bases, by id or name; limit?: number — default 10, at most 30 }`. Returns: `{ hits: [{ ref ("kb:<n>"), base, baseName, docId, path, title, part? (the page, slide, sheet or heading), text (an excerpt, ≤900 chars), score, by: ("words"|"meaning"|"exact")[] }], meaning: boolean — whether search by meaning took part }`. Exact hits come in reading order, the others best first.
- `POST /api/knowledge` — makes an empty knowledge base. Body: `{ name?: string — cut at 60, default "My documents"; description?: string — cut at 300; space?: string — a project's id to make it that project's own; left out or "": shared }`. Returns: the knowledge base.
- `PATCH /api/knowledge/:id` — renames a base or moves it. It merges: only the fields given change. Body: `{ name?: string — cut at 60, not empty (400 "Give it a name"); description?: string — "" clears it; space?: string|null — a project's id to move it into, null or "" to make it shared }`. Returns: the knowledge base.
- `POST /api/knowledge/:id/folders` — adds a folder on this computer to a base. It is read where it is (nothing copied), at once, then again a minute after the app starts and every hour: new and changed files are read, deleted ones leave (and after a start, the documents that could not be read are tried again). Hidden files and folders, tool folders (node_modules, .git, dist, build, venv…) and pictures are skipped; at most 20,000 files, 12 levels deep. Body: `{ path: string — a full path or ~/… (400 "Which folder?" when empty, "No folder at …" or "… is not a folder") }`. Adding a folder the base already has changes nothing. Returns: the source `{ id, kind: "folder", path, name, addedAt }`.
- `POST /api/knowledge/:id/scan` — reads the base again now: files just dropped in, folders that changed, and the documents that could not be read before. No body. Returns: `{ ok: true }`; `GET /api/knowledge` shows `indexing` while it reads.
- `DELETE /api/knowledge/:id/sources/:sid` — stops reading a folder: its documents leave the base; the folder itself is untouched. 400 for the "files" source ("Remove the files themselves instead"). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `DELETE /api/knowledge/:id/docs/:doc` — removes a document that was dropped into the base, deleting the base's copy of it. 400 for a document in a folder on this computer (remove it there, or stop reading the folder). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `DELETE /api/knowledge/:id` — deletes a base: its passages, their vectors and the files dropped into it. Folders on this computer stay as they are. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
Files from the user's device are added by uploading them into the base's `filesDir` (the files routes, as the page does) and then calling `POST /api/knowledge/:id/scan`. An agent adds documents that are already on this computer with `POST /api/knowledge/:id/folders`.

## Agents

Agents are called assistants in the API. An agent object: `{ id, name, icon, description, instructions, agent?, acpAgent?, model?, tools?, connections?, skills?, mode?, folder?, projects?, tier?, helperMax?, stepUp?, canAsk?, askable?, budgetUsd?, standing?, output?, builtin?, source? }`. The eight built-ins (ids `general`, `researcher`, `writer`, `coder`, `tutor`, `planner`, `analyst`, `app-builder`) come first, have `builtin: true`, and can be duplicated (read one, then POST it under a new name) but not changed or deleted.

- `GET /api/assistants` — every assistant (built-ins first, then the user's own) and the settings beside them. Returns: `{ assistants: Assistant[], taskRoot: string — where new task folders go, defaultTaskRoot: string — ~/Hydor (~/Naget where an older install made it), cleanUp: boolean — agents clean up after a task }`.
- `GET /api/assistants/import?cwd=` — Claude Code agent files that can be imported: those in `~/.claude/agents` and, with `cwd` (optional, a folder path), in `<cwd>/.claude/agents` (up to 200 per folder). Returns: `{ files: [{ path, scope: "user"|"project", name, description, imported?: string — the id of the assistant already made from it }] }`.
- `POST /api/assistants` — makes a new assistant of the user's own (at most 200; its id is made from the name, like `bookkeeper-3f9a`). Body: `{ name: string — required, ≤60 chars; icon?: "sparkles"|"telescope"|"pen-line"|"code"|"graduation-cap"|"calendar-check"|"chart-column"|"bot"|"briefcase"|"book-open"|"calculator"|"chef-hat"|"dumbbell"|"heart"|"languages"|"lightbulb"|"megaphone"|"music"|"palette"|"plane"|"house"|"mail"|"scale"|"shield"|"wallet"|"stethoscope"|"leaf"|"newspaper"|"wrench"|"users" — any other value becomes "bot"; description?: string — one line, ≤160; instructions?: string — who it is and how it works, ≤20,000; agent?: "claude"|"codex"|"acp" — the agent it suggests, left out = keep the start page's; acpAgent?: string — required with agent "acp": "console" is Custom, else an agent id from Settings → More agents; model?: string — the model it suggests, in that agent's own names (kept only with an agent); tools?: ("read"|"edit"|"run"|"web"|"browser"|"computer"|"mcp")[] — what it may use (read files, create and change files, run commands, use the web, use a browser, use the computer, other tools = MCP servers and plugins); left out or all seven = everything, [] = none of them (the app's own tools stay on); connections?: string[] — connection ids (Settings → Connections) turned on in its sessions, ≤50; skills?: string[] — skill names to favor, ≤30; mode?: "ask"|"edits"|"full"|"plan" — its permission mode, left out = keep the start page's; folder?: "task"|"project" — where its sessions start when no folder is picked: a new task folder (default) or the folder on the start page; projects?: string[] — project ids it works in, ≤50; left out = everywhere; tier?: "quick"|"standard"|"best"|"max" — the tier it thinks with (its model from GET /api/models/tiers), left out = Automatic; helperMax?: "quick"|"standard"|"best"|"max" — the highest tier of the subagents it starts (never above its own, whatever this says); stepUp?: false — a subagent it starts on a tier whose reply falls short, and a scheduled run of it that fails, do not run again a tier up (left out = they do); canAsk?: string[] — ids of the agents it may hand work to, ≤30 (never itself); askable?: false — other agents may not hand it work; budgetUsd?: number — what its sessions may spend in a month, in dollars (≤100,000; 0 or left out = no limit); standing?: true — it keeps one conversation of its own, with an inbox (see the inbox routes below); output?: { fields?: [{ id?, label, type: "text"|"markdown"|"number"|"yesno"|"date"|"url"|"list"|"table", required?: boolean, columns?: [{ id?, label, type }] — a table's }] — the fields of each result it hands back with its hand_back tool, checked (≤30; none = its reply), to?: — where each result goes besides the conversation (≤8; one of each kind but file and webhook): { kind: "file", path: string — inside the folder it works in, like reports/{date}.md ({date}, {time} and {agent} fill in; .json for the fields as JSON, else Markdown; 400 for an absolute path or one with ..) } | { kind: "library", each?: "new" — a new page each run, named after the agent and the day; left out: the same page each run, as a new version of it, page?: string — that page's id (left out: the one its first run made, named after the agent) } | { kind: "sheet", page?: string — a kept spreadsheet's id (left out: one its first run makes, named after the agent), tab?: string — its tab, by name (left out: the first; one not there is added) } (a row each run: when it ran, then a column per field, found by their headings; new fields get columns at the end) | { kind: "email", to: string[] — 1–20 addresses ("a@b.com" or "Name <a@b.com>"; a comma-separated string works too; 400 for one that is not an address), subject?: string — {date}, {time} and {agent} fill in (left out: "{agent} · {date}"), account?: string — the connected mail account it is sent from, by address (left out: the first) } (sent at once, without the Outbox) | { kind: "webhook", url: string — https:// only, secret?: string — a vault key's name, in capitals: it signs the call, and the call goes only to the hosts the key allows } | { kind: "notify" } (a notification with its first line) } }; examples?: [{ id?: string, name?: string — ≤80, message: string — required, ≤8,000: the message it is tried with, checks?: string[] — ≤8, each ≤400: a sentence the helper model reads its answer against, or a rule as an app's check is, on {{reply}} and {{result.<field id>}} (400 for a rule that does not read) }] — ≤10, tried with POST /api/assistants/:id/examples/try; why?: string — what you changed and why, in a sentence: it becomes the version's line (left out: what changed, in sentences); drafted?: true — it was drafted (POST /api/assistants/draft): its first version says so }`. Each save that changes what it is keeps a version (see the versions routes below). Returns: `{ assistant: Assistant, assistants, taskRoot, defaultTaskRoot, cleanUp }`.
- `PUT /api/assistants/:id` — changes one of the user's own assistants. It replaces the assistant whole with the body (same fields as POST; any field left out is cleared, so send the full object back with your change; send `source` back to keep an imported one linked to its file), except `examples`: left out, they stay as they were. Give `why` to say what you changed. When what it is changed (anything but its examples), a version is kept, by "agent" when you change it. 400 for a built-in, 404 for an unknown id. Returns: `{ assistant, assistants, taskRoot, defaultTaskRoot, cleanUp }`.
- `GET /api/assistants/:id/versions` — its versions, newest first (the latest 50 are kept): each save that changed what it is. Returns: `{ versions: [{ n: number, at: number, by: "you"|"agent"|"draft"|"install"|"import"|"before" — who saved it ("before": what it was before versions were kept), line: string — what changed, in sentences, or what the one who saved it said, examples?: { tried: number, passed: number } — how its examples went with this version (the latest try of each) }] }`. 404 for an unknown agent.
- `GET /api/assistants/:id/versions/:n` — one version, with what the agent was then (its settings and instructions; never its examples, which are not part of a version). Returns: `{ version: { n, at, by, line, agent: Assistant without id } }`. 404 for a version no longer kept.
- `POST /api/assistants/:id/versions/:n/restore` — Bring it back: version `n` becomes the current one, saved as a new version whose line says "Brought back version n." and what changed. Its examples stay as they are now; agents it handed work to and projects gone since are left out. Body: none. Returns: `{ assistant, assistants, taskRoot, defaultTaskRoot, cleanUp }`. 404 for a built-in, an unknown agent or a version no longer kept.
- `GET /api/assistants/:id/examples` — its examples' latest tries and what a try costs. Returns: `{ runs: [{ id, example — its example's id, version: number — the agent's version it ran with, key?: string — its session, status: "running"|"waiting" (its session waits for the user)|"judging" (its checks being read)|"passed"|"failed"|"error" (it did not finish), reply?, result?: { <field id>: value } — what it handed back, checks?: [{ check, ok: boolean, why? }], error?, costUsd?, startedAt, endedAt? }] — the latest try of each example, version: number — the agent's version now, changedSince?: number — the version they were last tried with, when it changed since, estimate: { count — how many examples, tier?: string, perRun?: number, total?: number — about what a try and all of them cost, in dollars, from?: "tries"|"sessions" — from its last tries or its sessions this month (none before anything ran), budgetLeft?: number — what is left of its monthly budget } }`.
- `POST /api/assistants/:id/examples/try` — tries its examples (or those named), each in a session of its own started as the agent on its tier, in an empty folder of its own (removed once its result is kept; leftovers are swept at start-up), archived; it sends nothing (its connected apps' calls that act are kept as ones it would have sent, and what it hands back stays with the try); when its turn ends its checks are read (sentences by the helper model). Examples already being tried are left to finish. It costs what those sessions cost and counts towards its monthly budget: say so, with `estimate` from GET …/examples, before you call it. Body: `{ only?: string[] — example ids, example?: string — one example's id, agent?: "claude"|"codex"|"acp", acpAgent?: string — the program for an agent that suggests none (default Claude Code) }`. Returns: `{ started: [the tries started, as in runs], runs, version, changedSince?, estimate }`. 400 for a built-in agent or one without examples.
- `GET /api/assistants/:id/export` — the agent as a `.hydor-agent` file (JSON, as a download): `{ hydor: "agent", format: 1, app: string — the Hydor version, exportedAt, agent: its settings and instructions, without its id, projects, connections, where it came from, the kept pages its results went to, email recipients and account, and webhook address and key, team?: string[] — the agents it hands work to, by name, schedules?: [{ name, schedule: TaskSchedule, task }] — its schedules by the clock (daily, every, cron), examples?, leftOut?: string[] — what was left out, in words }`. Built-in agents export too.
- `POST /api/assistants/install/check` — reads an agent's file and says what installing it would do; nothing is saved. Body: `{ file: string|object — the file's text, or its JSON }`. Returns: `{ agent — as it would be saved, brings: string[], needs: string[], notes: string[] — in words, team: [{ name, found?: string — the id of your agent by that name }], schedules: [{ name, schedule, task }], examples, clash?: { id, name } — your agent of the same name, beside: string — the name it gets beside yours ("Name (2)"), newer?: true — from a newer Hydor (what this one does not know is left out) }`. 400, saying what is wrong, for a file that is not an agent's (an app's file, a Claude Code .md, broken JSON, no format or no agent).
- `POST /api/assistants/install` — installs an agent from its file: beside yours of the same name (as `beside`), or in its place when `replace` (a new version of it, by "install"; its own examples stay, after the file's). Its projects, connections and the places its results go that need yours (email, webhook) are left out; Full access becomes Accept edits; a program not set up here is dropped. Body: `{ file: string|object, replace?: boolean, schedules?: boolean — add its suggested schedules, paused, agent?, acpAgent? — the program they run on, for an agent that suggests none }`. Returns: `{ assistant, schedules: number — how many were added, assistants, taskRoot, defaultTaskRoot, cleanUp }`.
- `DELETE /api/assistants/:id` — deletes one of the user's own assistants (400 for a built-in). Sessions started with it carry on without its instructions; its scheduled tasks (those that run as it, `extra.assistant`) are deleted with it; other agents' `canAsk` no longer lists it. Its history of results goes; the pages and spreadsheets it filled stay where they were kept. Returns: `{ assistants, taskRoot, defaultTaskRoot, cleanUp, schedules: number — how many of its scheduled tasks were deleted }`.
- `GET /api/assistants/activity` — what the Agents page shows of every agent, by id. Returns: `{ activity: { <id>: { schedules: [{ id, name, schedule: TaskSchedule, enabled: boolean, nextAt?: number, lastStatus?: string }] — the scheduled tasks that run as it (extra.assistant), working: number, waiting: number — its sessions at work, and waiting for the user, lastAt?: number — when a session last started as it, monthUsd: number, monthSessions: number — what its sessions spent since the month began (its own and its plain subagents'), and how many started, inbox?: { queued: number, sent: number } — for an agent that keeps a conversation of its own: messages waiting, and the one with it now } } }`.
- `GET /api/assistants/:id/inbox` — the inbox of an agent that keeps a conversation of its own, newest first (up to 50). Returns: `{ key: string|null — its conversation's session key, when it has one yet, items: [{ id, from: { kind: "user"|"agent"|"task", key?, name }, text, status: "queued"|"sent"|"answered"|"failed", reply?, createdAt, sentAt?, answeredAt? }] }`.
- `POST /api/assistants/:id/inbox` — puts a message from the user in its inbox: it goes into its conversation in turn, and its reply comes back as a notification. Body: `{ text: string — required, ≤40,000 }`. Returns: `{ item }`. 400 when the agent does not keep a conversation of its own, 404 for an unknown id.
- `POST /api/assistants/:id/standing` — its conversation: the one it has, or a new one made now (in its project's folder when it works in one project, else in Agents/<its name> under the task folders). Body: none. Returns: `{ key }`. 400 when it does not keep a conversation of its own.
- `GET /api/assistants/:id/results?limit=&before=` — its history: the results it handed back, newest first, a page at a time (kept a year, up to 500 an agent). `limit`: how many (1–200, default 50); `before`: the `id` of the last one of the page before, for older ones. 404 for an unknown agent. Returns: `{ results: [{ id: number, at, key — the session it came from, line — its first line, value: { <field id>: value } (a field too long to keep is "Too long to keep here: see where it went."), sent: [{ kind: "file"|"library"|"sheet"|"email"|"webhook"|"notify", ok: boolean, detail, page?: string — the kept page or spreadsheet it went to }] }], total: number — how many it keeps, more: boolean — older ones wait, made: { library?: { id, title }, sheet?: { id, title } } — the kept page and spreadsheet its first run made for its results }`.
- `POST /api/assistants/draft` — drafts an agent from a description with the helper model; nothing is saved. Body: `{ text: string — what it should do, and when it should work by itself, ≤4,000 }`. Returns: `{ agent: { name, icon, description, instructions, tools?, tier?, budgetUsd? } — checked as POST /api/assistants would, schedule?: TaskSchedule — when it asked to run by itself (daily or every), task?: string — the message each scheduled run starts with }`. To make it, POST the agent, then POST /api/tasks with the schedule, the task as its prompt and `extra: { assistant: <its id> }`.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/agent` — makes an agent for a project and saves it: drafted by the helper model to fit the project when it says enough about itself (its description and guidelines, or a README or agent notes in its folder), starting from the template for its kind; else its kind's template (software, web, ml, data, paper, research, writing, media, slides) from what its folder holds or the words it was described with; else the default project agent. It works only in the project (`projects: [<id>]`, `folder: "project"`), and becomes the agent the project's sessions start as unless the project names one. Body: none. Returns: `{ assistant: Assistant, how: "drafted"|"template"|"default", kind: string, assistants, taskRoot, defaultTaskRoot, cleanUp }`. 404 "No such project".
- `GET /api/models/tiers` — Settings → Models → Tiers: the model and effort each tier means for each program, and how sessions use the tiers. Returns: `{ tiers: { claude|codex|console: { quick|standard|best|max: { model?: string, effort?: string } } } — console is Custom; left out = the program's default, defaults: the same shape — what they are until changed (the newest model of each tier's kind that the program offers now; for Custom, the newest of its own models of that kind, from your Claude plan, else your ChatGPT plan, else the models not on this computer, and empty when none fits), team: { on: boolean — a session's main agent leads subagents on cheaper tiers, and the start page and the task form offer Automatic, hard: boolean — a main agent on Automatic may step itself up to Max, and a main agent may move itself there } }`.
- `PUT /api/models/tiers` — sets the tiers, how sessions use them, or both. Body: `{ tiers?: { claude?|codex?|console?: { quick?|standard?|best?|max?: { model?: string — in that program's own names (Custom: one of its model ids), effort?: string } } } — the whole table as it should be: a tier left out, or the same as its default, follows its default from then on, team?: { on?: boolean, hard?: boolean } — what is left out stays }`. Returns: `{ tiers, defaults, team }` as GET.
- `PUT /api/assistants/session/:key` — switches a session to another assistant, as its message box does: its agent works as that one from the next message on (it is told of the switch with that message; an idle Claude Code or Codex agent starts afresh first, with the new instructions and limits, and the conversation carries on). The connections the assistant turns on replace the previous one's in the session (the session's other choices stay); its permission mode is set on the session, and its model when it suggests the session's own agent. Body: `{ id: string — an assistant id (GET /api/assistants) }`. Returns: `{ extra: SessionExtra — the session's settings now, model?: string, mode?: string — what it set, in the agent's own names }`. 404 "No such assistant" or "No such session".
- `PUT /api/assistants/settings` — changes the settings beside the list; only the fields given change. Body: `{ taskRoot?: string — the folder new task folders go in (new projects' own folders go in its Projects subfolder): a full path or one starting with ~/, "" for the default ~/Hydor; cleanUp?: boolean — every agent cleans up after a task (on by default) }`. Returns: `{ assistants, taskRoot, defaultTaskRoot, cleanUp }`.
- `POST /api/assistants/import` — makes an assistant from a Claude Code agent file (name, description, model, tools, permissionMode and skills from its frontmatter; the text below as its instructions; it works in the folder on the start page). Importing the same file path again updates the assistant made from it (its icon kept). Body: `{ path?: string — an .md agent file on this computer, ≤256 KB; text?: string — the file's text instead of a path, ≤256 KB; name?: string — the file name, used for pasted text whose frontmatter has no name }` (one of path or text). Returns: `{ assistant, assistants, taskRoot, defaultTaskRoot, cleanUp }`.
- `POST /api/assistants/task-folder` — makes a new, empty task folder now, the way the start page does for a session without a folder: under `taskRoot`, named by today's date in the user's time zone and a few words of the prompt (`2026-10-31-plan-trip-lisbon`, `-2` when taken). Body: `{ prompt?: string — the task, for the name }`. Returns: `{ path: string }`.
To start a session as an assistant the way the start page does, call `POST /api/sessions` (Starting sessions, above) with `extra: { assistant: <id>, connections?: { <connection id>: true, … } }` (its connections), the agent, model and mode it suggests (the mode in that agent's names: ask/edits/full/plan are default/acceptEdits/bypassPermissions/plan for Claude Code, read-only/auto/full/plan for Codex, ask/edits/full/plan for Custom), and `cwd` from `POST /api/assistants/task-folder` when its `folder` is "task" and the user named no folder. The server adds the assistant's instructions, skills and tool limits by itself, and, when no model is given, the model of the tier it thinks with; an agent with a monthly budget gets what is left of it as the session's spending cap (400 once it is spent). It does not fill in the agent, mode, connections or folder.

## Memory

- `GET /api/wiki/settings` — the Wiki memory settings (**Settings → Memory → Wiki memory**). Returns `{ enabled: boolean (Wiki memory for the shared wiki and for every project that has no switch of its own; on unless turned off), fromDocuments: boolean (new and changed documents in knowledge add memory notes and update the wiki; default on), documentLimit: number (the most documents a project's knowledge may hold for its wiki to read them, a whole number from 0 to 100000, default 100; a project with more has its wiki read that many (the pinned, the most used by agents, then the newest) and agents search all of them; a project's own `wikiDocumentLimit` wins, and the shared wiki uses this), hubPath: string (the shared wiki's folder, an absolute path; default <task folders>/.wiki, and project wikis are kept inside it, in `projects/`), projectWikis: "hub"|"folder" (where each project's wiki is kept: "hub", the default, inside the shared wiki's folder, so nothing is written into the project's own folder; "folder", inside the project's folder), projectPath: string (with "folder": the wiki's folder, relative to the project's folder; default ".wiki"), gitIgnore: boolean (with "folder": add that folder to the project's .gitignore, only in Git repositories; default off) }`.
- `PUT /api/wiki/settings` — change the Wiki memory settings. Body: any of `{ enabled?: boolean, fromDocuments?: boolean, documentLimit?: number, hubPath?: string, projectWikis?: "hub"|"folder", projectPath?: string, gitIgnore?: boolean }`; returns the settings. 400 when a value is not allowed: `documentLimit` is a whole number from 0 to 100000, the hub is an absolute path (the only absolute path a wiki can have; never the root of a disk), the project path is relative and stays inside the project (not `.git`). Changing a nonempty hub needs the reviewed move below (409 otherwise). A wiki's folder is always inside the hub or inside its project's folder: an entry in the hub's `wikis.json` or a `- Wiki:` line in a project's AGENTS.md that points elsewhere is not followed. Already tracked Git files remain tracked.
- `POST /api/wiki/migration/preview` — review a central hub folder change with `{ hubPath }`; returns file counts, conflicts and a short-lived token. Hashing a big wiki happens in the background, so the app keeps answering meanwhile; session checkpoints and Hydor's own lock and backup folders are not counted.
- `POST /api/wiki/migration/apply` — apply the reviewed choice with `{ hubPath, planToken, action, conflict? }`. Actions are `keep`, `merge` and `replace`; conflict choices are `source`, `target` and `oldest`. Moves keep the old folders whole as hidden backups beside them. Returns the settings, and after a move `migration: { sourceBackup: string, targetBackup: string|null }`, the backups' paths. The Settings page handles this user decision.

Notes ("entries") belong to a place: `scope` "global" (about the user, `scopeKey` ""), "repo" (a project: `scopeKey` is the project's folder as memory keys it, i.e. the root of its Git repository, the main repository's for a worktree, or the folder itself outside Git; never the home folder) or "session" (`scopeKey` is the session's key). Kinds: "note", "topic" (sums up related notes) and "profile" (a place's summary agents get first). Times are milliseconds since 1970. An entry as the routes return it: `{ id, scope, scopeKey, kind, text, title? (a few words for the index agents start with; the tidy-up writes it, and a new text drops it), basis?: "user"|"checked"|"inferred" (how it is known: the user said so, checked, or not checked yet; none for notes from before this was kept), status: "active"|"deleted", createdAt, updatedAt, expiresAt?, usedAt?, uses (needed: an agent looked it up, cited it as #id, or worked with the file or command it is about after it was given the note; being given it does not count), given (how often agents got it without asking, at the start, with a message or on a file), givenAt?, hitAt?, hits (looked up by an agent: recall found it, or it was corrected; what ranking weighs), anchors? (the files and folders of the project it names, repository-relative, a folder ending in "/"), drift? ({ paths: string[], since: string — the commit it was written at, at: number — when seen }: those files changed since; the next tidy-up checks the note against the code), covers? (topics/profiles: notes summed up), session?, turn? (who wrote or changed it last), source: "user"|"agent"|"auto" (auto = the helper's tidy-up or an expiry), scopeLabel ("About you", the project's folder name, or the session's title), sessionTitle? }`. A change (in a tidy-up report or a memory chip): `{ entry, action: "added"|"updated"|"deleted"|"expiry"|"checked"|"moved" (checked: Learn from conversations found a conversation that shows a note not checked yet true, or the tidy-up found a note still true against the code; moved: the tidy-up moved a note from About you to the project it is about, `before` and `after` naming the places and `text` the note), scope, scopeKey, kind?, before?, after?, text? (for moved), expiresAt?, expiredBefore?, basisBefore? (for checked), driftBefore?, commitBefore? (for checked against the code), why?, session? (the conversation it was learned from), source, undone? }`.

- `GET /api/memory` — memory's state: the switches, counts, the user's profile, how many notes agents start with, search by meaning, the last tidy-up. Returns: `{ enabled: boolean (memory for all sessions), counts: { global, repo, session } (active notes, past-end-date ones and the user's profile left out), profile: entry | null (the user's profile; its text may have several lines), profileWords: number (the most words it may have: 1,000 unless changed), profileFit?: { running: boolean, at, error? } (the helper model shortening a profile longer than that, or why it could not lately), topK: number (how many notes and topics about the user and the project agents start with, besides the profile: 10 unless changed), meaning: { on, status: "off"|"setting-up"|"starting"|"ready"|"failed", detail? (progress, such as the model's download, or why it failed), model, embedded, total }, tidyOn: boolean, learnOn: boolean (the tidy-up learns from the conversations since the last one), tidy?: { at, running?, changes: change[], changesAt? (set when the latest found nothing and these are an earlier run's), checked, sessions? (conversations learned from), learnError? (why learning stopped; the rest still ran), upkeepError? (why a step after the near-copies stopped: checking notes against the code, moving notes to their project, notes into the wiki, AGENTS.md; the rest still ran), error? }, stale: number (notes suggested for removal) }`.
- `PUT /api/memory` — changes the switches; only the fields given change. Body: `{ enabled?: boolean — memory for all sessions, topK?: integer 1–50 — how many notes agents start with (Settings offers 5, 10, 20, 40), profileWords?: integer 50–5000 — the most words the user's profile may have (Settings offers 250, 500, 1000, 2000; a profile longer than a new limit is shortened by the helper model in the background), meaning?: boolean — search by meaning (on unless turned off; it sets itself up a minute after the app first starts, at once when turned on: about a minute, then a 340 MB model download), retry?: true — try again after it failed, tidy?: boolean — tidy up automatically (about once a day, whenever the app is on: 20 hours after the last tidy-up, once there is something new and the user has not written for ten minutes), learn?: boolean — learn from conversations with each tidy-up (on unless turned off) }`. Returns: the state, as `GET /api/memory`.
- `GET /api/memory/entries` — lists notes. Query: `q?: string — search: with status active, every word must match (the last as a word's start) plus, when search by meaning runs, notes alike in meaning, best first, at most 100; with deleted/all, a plain all-words filter`, `scope?: "global"|"repo"|"session"`, `key?: string — one place's scopeKey (with `scope` and `q`, only that place is searched, so a project's matches never lose out to other projects' notes)`, `status?: "active" (default)|"deleted"|"all"`, `kind?: "note"|"topic"|"profile"|"finding" (a finding’s entry has `finding: { verdict: "supported"|"refuted"|"unclear"|"open", statement, learned?, evidence: [{ kind: "run"|"score"|"table"|"commit"|"file"|"paper"|"session", ref, label? }], replaces?, run?: { id, app? } }`, and its text is written from it)`, `ids?: "1,2,3" — these entries in any status (at most 200)`, `stale?: "1" — only the notes no agent needed for 120 days (about the user or projects, plain notes without an end date; at most 100; other parameters ignored)`. Without `q`, newest change first. Returns: `{ entries: entry[] }`.
- `GET /api/memory/profile` — the user's profile: what every agent gets at the start of a session while memory is on, right after the user's guidelines. Returns: `{ profile: entry | null, maxWords: number }`.
- `PUT /api/memory/profile` — writes the user's profile as the user (agents use their own tool, update_user_profile). Body: `{ text: string — the whole profile, lines kept; "" clears it (it stays among the deleted notes for 30 days); refused (400) when it seems to hold a password, key, token or card number, or has more than 40,000 characters }`. Longer than its words, it is saved as written and the helper model shortens it to fit in the background (`GET /api/memory` shows `profileFit`). Returns: `{ profile: entry | null, maxWords: number, fitting: boolean (a shortening started) }`.
- `POST /api/memory/profile/fit` — shortens the user's profile to its words now, with the helper model in the background (after it could not). Returns: `{ started: boolean }` (false when it fits already).
- `GET /api/memory/entries/:id/history` — one note's history, newest first. Returns: `{ history: [{ id, entry, at, action: "added"|"updated"|"deleted"|"restored"|"expiry"|"basis"|"moved", before?, after? (for expiry: the end date as an ISO time, none when taken away; for basis: how it was and is known, "" for none; for moved: the place names, as "About you" and the project's folder name), why?, session?, sessionTitle?, turn?, source }] }`.
- `GET /api/memory/entries/:id/children` — the notes a topic or profile sums up (any status, at most 500). Returns: `{ entries: entry[] }`.
- `POST /api/memory/entries` — adds a note as the user (its basis "user"). Body: `{ text: string — at least 2 characters; kept as one paragraph, cut at 500; refused (400) when it seems to hold a password, key, token or card number, scope?: "global" (default)|"repo"|"session", key?: string — required unless global: the project's folder exactly as memory keys it (see above; another folder makes another place) or the session's key, expiresAt?: number — when it stops being true, from now to five years on }`. Returns: `{ entry }`.
- `PATCH /api/memory/entries/:id` — edits a note: its text, its end date, or both (other fields untouched); or a finding’s verdict and words. Body: `{ text?: string — new text (same rules as above; on a deleted note it restores it with this text), expiresAt?: number|null — new end date, from now to five years on; null takes it away, finding?: { verdict?: "supported"|"refuted"|"unclear"|"open", statement?: string — one sentence, at most 300 characters, learned?: string — at most 450 characters, "" takes it away } — for a finding alone, which takes no text or end date (400) }`. Returns: `{ entry }` (404 when the entry is gone). Findings are recorded by agents with their finding_record tool; there is no route to add one.
- `DELETE /api/memory/entries/:id` — deletes a note: it stays restorable for 30 days. Query: `forever?: "1" — erase it and its history at once (cannot be undone)`. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/memory/entries/:id/restore` — brings back a deleted note (an end date that has passed is dropped). Returns: `{ entry }` (404 when it is not deleted).
- `POST /api/memory/entries/:id/keep` — keeps a note suggested for removal: it counts as needed now and leaves the stale list. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/memory/warm` — starts the model search by meaning uses (when it is on and enough memory is free), as the start page does when someone begins a message, so the session about to start finds its notes and skills by meaning too; one already loaded stays loaded a while longer. A session never waits for it. No body. Returns: `{ ready: boolean — loaded now }`.
- `POST /api/memory/tidy` — starts a tidy-up now with the helper model, in the background, learning from the conversations since the last one first when that is on (works whether or not it runs by itself; 400 when memory is off). Returns: `{ started: true }`; `GET /api/memory` shows `tidy.running`, then its changes or error.
- `POST /api/memory/tidy/undo` — undoes one change of the last tidy-up: what it added goes, what it changed gets its old text back, what it deleted returns, an end date it set is taken away, a note it checked is not checked again (one checked against the code is flagged again), a note it moved goes back to About you. Body: `{ index: number — the change's position in tidy.changes }`. Returns: `{ tidy }` (the report, that change marked undone).
- `GET /api/memory/agents-suggestions` — the sentences the tidy-up suggests for a project's `AGENTS.md` (the dialog **What the last tidy-up changed** → **Suggested for AGENTS.md**), newest first, each from a project note. Returns: `{ suggestions: [{ id, entry (the note it comes from), repo (the project's folder), text, at, status: "pending"|"applied"|"dismissed", project (the folder's name), file: "AGENTS.md"|"CLAUDE.md" (where it would go; CLAUDE.md when the project has no AGENTS.md; none when it has neither), note? (the note's text) }] }`.
- `POST /api/memory/agents-suggestions/:id/apply` — adds a suggested sentence to the project's `AGENTS.md` (or `CLAUDE.md`) as a bullet under "## Learned conventions" (the heading is made at the end of the file when there is none) and deletes the note it came from. Returns: `{ suggestion }` (status "applied"). 400 when the suggestion was dismissed or added already, or the project has no `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md` to add it to. **Careful**: it changes a file in the user's project.
- `POST /api/memory/agents-suggestions/:id/dismiss` — dismisses a suggestion: the file and the note stay, and it is not suggested again. Returns: `{ suggestion }` (status "dismissed"). 404 "No such suggestion".
- `POST /api/memory/notes/:id/undo` — undoes one change from the memory chip under a reply (the session note of kind "memory", from `GET /api/sessions/:key/notes`; its `data.changes` lists the changes). Body: `{ index: number — the change's position in data.changes }`. An added note goes, an updated one gets its old text back, a removed one returns. Returns: `{ note }` (the session note, that change marked undone).
- `DELETE /api/memory` — clears all memory: every note, all history. Cannot be undone. Body: `{ confirm: "all" }` (required). Returns: `{ cleared: number }`.
- `GET /api/memory/bring-in` — Bring in (Settings → Memory → Bring in…): what other agents keep on this computer, the export files the user added, and the reading, if one is under way or waiting to be picked from. Returns: `{ sources: [{ id: "claude-code"|"codex"|"hermes"|"openclaw"|"pi"|"atomic", name, memory: number — memory files, projects: number — projects they are for, own?: string — the instructions the user wrote for it, by file name ("CLAUDE.md", "AGENTS.md", "AGENTS.md and SYSTEM.md"), chars }], files: [{ id, name, size, kind: "chatgpt"|"claude", chats: number, memory: boolean — Claude's memory or ChatGPT's custom instructions, from?, to? }], job: null | { state: "reading"|"ready"|"failed", done, total — parts read and to read, missed — parts the helper did not answer, error?, sources: string[], notes: [{ id: number, text, title?, scope: "global"|"repo", scopeKey, source }] (when ready), profile?: { added: string[] } — lines it would add to the profile }`.
- `POST /api/memory/bring-in/read` — starts reading with the helper model, in the background (one reading at a time: 409 while one runs); `GET /api/memory/bring-in` follows it. Body: `{ sources?: string[] — ids from sources, files?: string[] — ids from files }` (at least one). Returns: the same object as GET.
- `POST /api/memory/bring-in/apply` — adds the picked notes (each with "Brought in from …" in its history) and, with `profile`, the new lines to the user's profile; the reading ends and the export files it used are deleted. Show the user the notes and ask before adding. Body: `{ notes: number[] — ids from job.notes, profile?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ added: number, profile: boolean, …the same object as GET }` (409 when nothing is ready).
- `DELETE /api/memory/bring-in` — stops a reading, or drops what it found. Returns: the same object as GET.
- `DELETE /api/memory/bring-in/file/:id` — removes an export file the user added. Returns: the same object as GET.

## Skill library

Settings → Agents → Skills shows these with the agents' own skills (below). Skill names (`:name`) are lowercase letters, digits and dashes, up to 64 characters.

- `GET /api/skillpack` — the skill library: every skill (built in, the user's, and those of enabled plugins), proposals waiting for the user, removed skills of the user's, the keywords switch, and the projects with skills of their own. Returns: `{ skills: [{ name, description, triggers: string[] (keywords that bring it into Custom's turn), category?: string (its group: work, writing, documents, research, planning, data, creative, coding, ai, app), source: 'bundled'|'learned'|'plugin' (learned: the user's own), plugin?: { name, title } (a plugin's skill), off?: true (turned off: agents do not get it), overrides?: true (the user's in a built-in one's place), path, updatedAt?: ms (learned only), uses: number, lastUsed?: ms }], pending: [proposal] (up to 50, newest first; shape as in GET /api/skillpack/proposals/:id), removed: [{ name, at }] (removed skills of the user's that can be brought back), triggers: boolean, suggest: boolean (a new session's agent hears which skills fit its first message), folders: { bundled, learned, view }, spaces: [{ id, name, skills: [{ name, description }] }] (projects with skills of their own; change those through the project's routes), switched: [{ id, name, switches }] (projects that switch skills on or off for their own sessions; switches as in GET /api/projects/:id/skills) }`.
- `POST /api/skillpack/skills` — adds a skill of the user's own, for every agent (no proposal: as if the user wrote it). Body: `{ name: string (made lowercase with dashes), description: string (when to use it, starting "Use when"), body: string (the instructions, Markdown; at least 40 characters), category?: string (as above), triggers?: string[] (up to 12) }`, or `{ name, text: string (a whole SKILL.md whose name: is the name) }`. Returns: `{ name }`. 400 when it lacks a description or instructions, holds a secret, or is over 20,000 characters; 409 when the library has a skill of that name.
- `PUT /api/skillpack/skills/:name` — saves a new text for one of the user's skills, or the user's own version of a built-in or a plugin's skill (it takes that one's place for every agent). Body: `{ text: string (the whole SKILL.md; its name: must stay) }`. Kept in the skill's history. Returns: `{ ok: true }`. 404 when the library has no skill of that name.
- `PUT /api/skillpack/skills/:name/off` — turns a library skill (built in, the user's, or a plugin's) off or on for every agent, but in projects that switch it the other way for their own sessions (PUT /api/projects/:id/skill-switches). Body: `{ off: boolean }`. Returns: `{ off: boolean }`. 404 when the library has no skill of that name.
- `GET /api/skillpack/skills/:name` — one library skill's whole text and its history of changes. Query: `key?` (a session key: the skill that session's agent gets, so in a project its own skill of that name, or that of a project its group brings together, before the library's; then `source` is 'space' with `space: { id, name }`). Returns: `{ name, text (the whole SKILL.md), source: 'bundled'|'learned'|'plugin'|'space', space?, plugin?: { name, title }, off?: true, file, history: [{ id, name, at, action: 'create'|'update'|'remove'|'restore', key? (the session it came from), diff (unified), added, removed }] (newest first, up to 100) }`. 404 when the library has no skill of that name (the agents' own skills are not in it).
- `GET /api/skillpack/proposals/:id` — a skill an agent proposed, or a change it proposed to one. Returns: `{ proposal: { id, key (the session it came from), kind: 'new'|'patch', name, status: 'pending'|'approved'|'dismissed'|'replaced', reason?, before? (the whole SKILL.md before, for a change), after (as proposed), diff, added, removed, findings: [{ kind: 'secret'|'injection', what, line, excerpt }], bundled?: true (it changes a built-in skill), space?: { id, name } (the project of the session it came from, where it can be kept alone, or the project whose own skill it changes), scope: 'library'|'space' (where it is kept: as proposed while pending, as saved once approved), spaceSkill?: true (it changes a skill of the project's own, which stays the project's), createdAt, decidedAt?, saved? (the text the user edited before approving), note? (the user's note when dismissing) } }`. 404 if there is none.
- `PUT /api/skillpack/settings` — turns "Bring skills in by keywords" (for Custom) and "Suggest skills that fit" (a new session's agent hears which skills fit its first message, found by words and by meaning) on or off. Body: `{ triggers?: boolean, suggest?: boolean }` (nothing else is read; both on by default). Returns: `{ triggers: boolean, suggest: boolean }`.
- `POST /api/skillpack/skills/:name/remove` — removes a learned skill; agents stop seeing it (for the user's version of a built-in skill, the built-in one comes back). Its text stays in the history. No body. Returns: `{ ok: true }`. 404 when it is not a learned skill (built-in ones cannot be removed).
- `POST /api/skillpack/skills/:name/restore` — brings back a removed learned skill with the text it had when removed. No body. Returns: `{ ok: true }`. 404 when there is nothing to bring back; 409 when it is in the library already.

## Claude Code and Codex skills

These are the agents' own skills, which only that agent gets (Settings → Agents → Skills shows them under Claude Code's own and Codex's own), not the app's skill library.

- `GET /api/setup/skills?cwd=` — Claude Code's and Codex's own skills, for one folder (Settings → Skills lists them for the home folder, a project's Skills tab for the project's folder). Query: `cwd?: string` (a folder; default the home folder; 400 "Not a folder"). Returns: `{ skills: [{ agent: 'claude'|'codex', name, description, path (its SKILL.md), scope: 'user'|'project'|'repo'|'system'|'admin'|'plugin' (shown as All folders, This folder, This repository, Built in, Admin, Plugin), enabled: boolean, plugin?: string, deletable: boolean }], errors: string[] }`. Claude Code: ~/.claude/skills (user) and <cwd>/.claude/skills (project; none when cwd is the home folder). Codex: what Codex itself reports for the folder.
- `POST /api/setup/skills` — makes a new skill for one agent: its folder and a SKILL.md (name, the when-to-use line, and the instructions, or a placeholder to fill in). Body: `{ agent?: 'claude'|'codex' (anything else means claude), name: string (made lowercase with dashes, up to 64 characters), description?: string (when to use it; empty gives "Use when …"), body?: string (the instructions, Markdown), scope?: 'user'|'project' (Claude Code only: 'project' puts it in <cwd>/.claude/skills, otherwise ~/.claude/skills; Codex skills always go in ~/.agents/skills), cwd?: string (the folder, for 'project') }`. Returns: `{ path }` (the new SKILL.md). 409 when a skill of that name exists there.
- `POST /api/setup/skills/toggle` — turns one of the agents' own skills on or off, for every session (and the agent used outside the app too). Body: `{ agent: 'claude'|'codex', name: string (Claude Code: the skill's name, which is what it records, in ~/.claude/settings.json), path: string (Codex: its SKILL.md, which must exist), enabled: boolean }`. Returns: `{ ok: true }`. 400 "No such skill".
- `POST /api/setup/skills/delete` — deletes a skill's folder with every file in it; there is no undo. Body: `{ agent: 'claude'|'codex', path: string (its SKILL.md), cwd?: string (the folder, for a Claude Code project skill) }`. Only folders right inside ~/.claude/skills or <cwd>/.claude/skills (Claude Code), ~/.agents/skills or ~/.codex/skills (Codex); anything else gives 400. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Web search

- `GET /api/search/settings` — the web search providers in the order they are tried, Results per search, and Naget Search's allowance. Returns: `{ providers: [{ id: "naget" | "searxng" | "brave" | "duckduckgo", name, description, enabled: boolean, key: "required" | "optional" | "none", keyHint?: string — a hint, never the key, keyUrl?, url?: { value: string — "" when none saved, placeholder, required: boolean, default? }, caps: { maxK, sites, excludeSites, categories, dates, languages, fileTypes, homepagesOnly, filteredSnippets }, missing?: "Needs a key" | "Needs your instance’s address", lastError?: { at, kind, message } }], defaultK: number, credits?: { remaining?, limit?, resetAt?, exhausted?, at } }`. The first provider that is on is the default.
- `PUT /api/search/settings` — changes the order, Results per search, or one provider. It merges: only the fields you send change. Body: `{ order?: string[] — provider ids to try first, in this order (the rest keep their order after them; unknown ids and repeats are dropped), defaultK?: integer 1–100, provider?: { id: provider id, enabled?: boolean, key?: string | null — (a key: not for agents) "" or null removes it, no spaces, refused for providers that take no key, url?: string | null — http(s) address, https:// added when missing; "", null or the default resets it } }`. A new key or address turns the provider on unless `enabled` is sent. Changing a provider clears its last error. Returns: same as GET. Bad values get a 400 with a message.
- `POST /api/search/test` — runs one 5-result search on one provider alone, with no fallback and a 45 s limit. Body: `{ id: provider id, key?: string — (a key: not for agents) a key not saved yet, url?: string — an address not saved yet }`. Returns: `{ ok: boolean, ms: number, count?: number, sample?: [{ title, url }] — up to 3, error?: string }`. With the saved setup, a success clears the provider's last error and a failure is recorded on it.
- `GET /api/site-icon/:host` — a site's icon (`:host` like `ign.com`), as the sources a reply cites show it: the app fetches it from the site the first time and keeps it. Returns: the picture (PNG, ICO, SVG…); 404 when the site has none (asked again days later), 400 for a host that is not one.

## Browser

- `GET /api/browser/settings` — the Browser settings. Returns: `{ settings: { enabled, chrome: string — "" = the best found, else a path, or "desktop" for the desktop app's own browser, headless: boolean — true = no window (it means nothing for the desktop app's browser), blockPrivate, screenshots, confirmSignedIn: boolean — ask before an agent presses a button that sends, posts, deletes or transfers on a site it is signed in to (true unless turned off), allow: string[], block: string[] }, chromes: [{ path ("desktop" for the desktop app's own browser, listed first in the desktop app), source: "system" | "playwright" | "downloaded" | "desktop", label, version?, shell?: true }], chrome: the one in use | null, download: { state: "idle" | "running" | "done" | "failed", received?, total?, version?, error? }, profiles: [{ id, name, createdAt }], noSandbox?: true, open: number — browsers open now }`.
- `PUT /api/browser/settings` — changes the Browser settings. It merges field by field, and a list you send replaces the old one. Body: `{ enabled?: boolean — A browser for agents, chrome?: string — "" or a path from chromes ("desktop" for the desktop app's own browser), headless?: boolean — false shows a window, blockPrivate?: boolean — Keep pages off this machine and its network, screenshots?: boolean — Screenshots for models that see images, confirmSignedIn?: boolean — Ask before sending on signed-in sites, allow?: string[] | string — Only these sites (cut down to bare sites like example.com, a port allowed, at most 500), block?: string[] | string — Never these sites (same rules) }`. A Chromium or window change applies when the browser next starts. Returns: same as GET.
- `POST /api/browser/download` — starts downloading the current stable Chrome for Testing (about 170 MB) in the background; does nothing while a download is already running. No body. Returns: same as GET, with `download.state` "running". Poll GET for progress and for the new entry in `chromes`.
- `POST /api/browser/profiles` — makes a profile. Body: `{ name: string — 1–40 characters, not the same as another's, ignoring case }`. Returns: `{ profile: { id, name, createdAt }, …same as GET }`. Errors: 400 with no name, 409 when the name is taken. Signing in to sites in it is the user's job.
- `PATCH /api/browser/profiles/:id` — renames a profile. Body: `{ name: string — 1–40 characters }`. Returns: same as GET.
- `DELETE /api/browser/profiles/:id` — deletes a profile with its sign-ins, and closes the browsers using it. Returns: same as GET.

Your own browser (the Browser page) is the user's alone, and agents never use it: its tabs and the routes under `/api/browser/browsing/`, its Read later list (`/api/browser/browsing/later`), its bookmarks (`/api/browser/bookmarks`) and its Chrome extensions (`/api/browser/extensions`) are refused. Your own browser is in the desktop app only. When the user asks you to bookmark something, sort their bookmarks into folders, highlight words on a page, add an extension, or change its home page, search engine or ad blocker, tell them where: the Browser page's **Bookmarks** (its **Organize with AI** button sorts them into folders by topic; and, for a page to read later, **Read this page later** in its right-click menu or ⋯ menu), **Highlights** (select words, right-click, **Highlight**), **Extensions** and **Settings** (/browser?panel=bookmarks, /browser?panel=highlights, /browser?panel=extensions, /browser?panel=settings). They can give you one page to work on with **Ask** → **Do** beside the page.


## Computer use (the desktop apps)

Agents see the computer's screen and use its mouse and keyboard with their own tools (computer_screenshot, computer_act), only in the desktop app (Mac, Windows, or Linux on an X11 desktop) with Computer use on, for an agent that may use the computer (its `tools` include `computer`, or are left out: see the assistant routes), and only after the user allowed it on a card in the session. While an agent acts, a glow lines the screen's edges, which its screenshots never show, and Esc stops it. **ComputerState**: `{ available: boolean — the server runs in a desktop app (elsewhere nothing works), system: 'mac'|'windows'|'linux'|null — which one, problem?: string | null — what keeps it from working on this computer now (a Linux desktop on Wayland, a library missing), in words, enabled: boolean — Computer use is on (off at first), permissions?: { accessibility: boolean — macOS lets Hydor use the mouse and keyboard, screen: 'granted'|'denied'|'not-determined'|'restricted'|'unknown' — Screen Recording } | null — only in the Mac app; null when it did not say, active: { key, title, since } | null — the session whose agent is using the computer now, allowed: number — sessions the user allowed (until Stop or the app restarts) }`.

- `GET /api/computer` — Settings → Computer use. Returns: ComputerState.
- `PUT /api/computer/settings` — turns computer use on or off. Body: `{ enabled: boolean }`. Turning it off stops what an agent is doing, and every session asks again. Returns: ComputerState.
- `POST /api/computer/permissions/:which` — `accessibility` or `screen`: macOS's own question the first time, then its System Settings at the right place, where the user turns Hydor on. Only the user does this (agents are refused). Returns: ComputerState with the permissions read again. 400 outside the Mac app.
- `POST /api/computer/stop` — Stop (as Esc does): what agents do on the computer ends, and every session asks again before its agent goes on. Returns: ComputerState.

## Deep research

- `GET /api/research/:key` — where the deep research led by session `:key` stands. Returns: `{ key, settings: { breadth: number — researchers at a time (1–8), depth: number — rounds (1–5), queries: number — searches in all (10–500), format: "page" | "document" }, used: number — web searches so far, the lead's and its researchers' together, rounds: number — rounds begun (depth + 1 means the checking round), researchers: string[] — the researchers' session keys, oldest first }`; for a session that leads none: `{ key, settings: null }`.

## Subagents

- `GET /api/children/settings` — Settings → Capabilities → Subagents. Returns: `{ maxRunning: number — Hydor subagents of one session working at once (1–12, default 4), swarmSize: number — members of one swarm working at once (1–16, default 5) }`.
- `PUT /api/children/settings` — changes either or both; a field left out stays as it is. Body: `{ maxRunning?: integer 1–12, swarmSize?: integer 1–16 }` (400 "Pick a number from 1 to …" otherwise). Returns: the settings, as GET.

## Swarm

- `GET /api/swarm/:key` — the board of the swarm or campaign led by session `:key` (404 "That session does not lead a swarm" otherwise). Returns: `{ swarm: string — the lead's key, mode: "swarm" | "campaign", size: number — members working at once, members: string[] — member session keys, oldest first, stopped: string[] — members that were stopped, posts: [{ id, swarm, from: string — the lead's or a member's key, to?: string — one member's key or the lead's (everyone when absent), kind: "progress" | "finding" | "question" | "note" (a campaign's also "plan" | "done" | "failed"), text, at: ms, part?: string — the part it is about, parts?: string[] — a plan's parts }] — the latest 300, oldest first, parts?: [{ name, status: "waiting" | "working" | "done" | "failed", member?: string — the member that last posted about it, note?: string — the latest post about it, tries: number — times it failed, at?: ms }] — a campaign's parts, in its plan's order }`.
- `POST /api/swarm/:key/stop` — stops one member, or every member still working; stopped members keep what they did and posts no longer wake them; the lead gets a message saying who was stopped. Body: `{ member?: string — one member's session key (404 if it is not one); leave out for all working members }`. Returns: `{ stopped: string[] }`.

## Second opinion

- `GET /api/opinion/:key` — who may check session `:key`'s work, and its turns. Returns: `{ reviewers: [{ id: string — "claude", "codex" or one of Custom's model ids, label, detail? }] — only other companies' models, the suggested one first (for a confidential session, only Custom's models on this computer), turns: [{ id: string — the turn's id, text: string — the start of the message that began it, at?: ms, files: number — files it changed }] — the latest 12 finished turns, newest first }`. 404 for an unknown session.
- `POST /api/opinion/:key` — asks for a second opinion on one turn; its card shows under the turn at once and fills in when the reviewer answers (a minute or two; at most 8 minutes). Body: `{ turn?: string — a turn id from GET (default: the latest finished turn), reviewer?: string — a reviewer id from GET (default: the first) }`. Returns: `{ note: { id, key, kind: "opinion", at, anchor, data: { status: "running", reviewer, reviewerLabel, turn, request, startedAt, files, trimmed? } } }`. Errors: 404 no finished turn yet / not a turn of this session, 409 the turn is still running, 400 reviewer not offered (same company). The answer lands in the same note: read it with `GET /api/sessions/:key/notes` (notes of kind "opinion"): `data.status: "done"` with `verdict: "good" | "issues" | "unclear"`, `summary?`, `findings: [{ severity: "critical" | "important" | "minor", title, detail?, file?, line? }]`; or `"failed"` with `error`.
- `DELETE /api/opinion/note/:id` — dismisses a second opinion's card (`:id` is the note's id; 404 if it is not a second opinion). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Model arena

- `GET /api/arena` — matches and which models win (Usage → Arena). Returns: `{ matches: [{ id, prompt, createdAt, entries: [{ key: string — the model's session, agent: "claude" | "codex" | "acp", acpAgent?: string — for "acp" ("console" is Custom), model?: string }], winner?: string — the kept session's key, decidedAt? }] — the latest 100, newest first, board: [{ id, agent, acpAgent?, model?, rating: number — Elo, from 1000, wins: number, played: number }] — best first }`.
- `GET /api/arena/:id` — one match (404 "No such match"). Returns: the match, as in `matches` above.
- `POST /api/arena/:id/winner` — keeps one answer: that session wins and is unarchived, the match's other sessions are archived; picking again changes the winner. Body: `{ key: string — one of the match's session keys (400 otherwise) }`. Returns: the match.

## Spending caps

- `GET /api/budget/:key` — where session `:key` stands against its cap. Returns: `{ key, capUsd?: number — the cap in US dollars (absent: none), spentUsd: number — what counts against it, at API prices (its own and its Hydor subagents'), ownUsd, childrenUsd, unpriced?: true — some tokens have no known price, so it may have spent more, reached: boolean, parent?: { key, title, capUsd, spentUsd, reached } — a Hydor subagent's parent, whose cap covers it too }`.
- `PUT /api/budget/:key` — sets, changes or removes the session's cap (it covers its Hydor subagents too); messages its queue held for the old cap go out once the new cap is above what was spent. Body: `{ capUsd: number | string | null — dollars from 0.01 to 100,000 (strings like "12.50" or "$12.50" work, rounded to cents); null, "" or leaving it out removes the cap }` (400 "A spending cap is an amount in dollars, from $0.01 to $100,000" otherwise). Returns: the state, as GET.

## Documents

- None for agents: every `/api/documents*` route serves the file viewer's previews, and every `/api/pdf/*` route Files' Fill & sign (see Not for agents). Agents read documents with their own document tool, make them with the app's document skills, and see, fill in, write on and change PDFs with their own PDF tools (pdf_look, pdf_form, pdf_fill, pdf_write, pdf_pages).

## Highlights and notes on PDFs (Files → a PDF → Highlight)

Files' reader keeps a PDF's highlights and notes beside the file, by its path; agents read them with their own pdf_notes tool, and highlight words for the user (when asked) with these routes. A **Highlight** is `{ id, page — from 1, kind: text|area — words, or an area around a figure or an equation, rects: [{ x, y, w, h }] — in points from the page's top-left as it shows: a box for each line of words, one for an area, text? — its words as they read (an area: the words inside it), color: yellow|green|blue|pink, note?, o? — where it starts in the page's reading order, anchor? — an area's longest run of words and its box, which the area moves with when the file changes, at, edited? — ms, lost?: true — the file changed and its words are no longer in it (it stays where it was) }`.

- `GET /api/pdf-notes?path=` — a PDF's highlights and notes, by page in reading order; when the file changed since, each is found again by its words first, and a file moved outside Files finds its notes again by its contents. Returns: `{ path, highlights: [Highlight], kept?: { space — the project, spaceName?, base — its knowledge base, file — the document there, at } }`; 404 when there is no such file.
- `POST /api/pdf-notes` — adds, changes or removes highlights. Body: `{ path: string, put?: [a Highlight, or part of one: a new one needs page and either text — its words, found on that page (yellow unless color says) — or kind and rects; one with an existing id changes only the fields given], remove?: [id] }`. Returns: the same as GET; 400 when the words are not on that page (it says which pages have them), or a field is wrong. A document kept in a project follows a moment later.
- `GET /api/pdf-notes/text?path=&page=` — a page's words with where they are, as the reader lays them over its picture: runs in reading order (a paper's columns one after the other). Returns: `{ page, width, height — points, runs: [{ t — its text, x, y, w, h, e: [number] — where each character starts, and the last ends }] }`.
- `POST /api/pdf-notes/keep` — keeps the notes in a project's knowledge as one document named after the paper ("<title> (notes).md", in the project's first knowledge base, one made for it when it has none), which follows every later change; or stops keeping them (the document is taken out). Body: `{ path: string, space: string|null — the project's id; null: no longer }`. Returns: the same as GET; 404 for a project that is not one.
