- `POST /api/pdf-notes/save` — writes the highlights into the PDF as its own annotations (a Highlight over words, a Square around an area, each note as its comment), in place of those saved before: a copy beside it ("name (highlighted).pdf", which keeps the notes too) or over it, its old version to the Trash. Body: `{ path: string, replace?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ path, replaced: boolean, trashed?: string, count, notes: [string] }`; 400 with no highlights, 422 when the file's owner or a certifying signature forbids comments.

## Images, videos and audio

Agents make them with their own tools: generate_image, generate_video and generate_audio, which show the user a form with the model's settings and price first (see the guide's Documents, images, videos and voice). These routes are Settings → Images, video and audio. A model's **settings** are `params: [{ id — OpenRouter's name: aspect_ratio, resolution, quality, background, n, output_format, seed…, kind: enum|range|boolean, values?: [string] — enum, min?, max? — range }]`.

- `GET /api/images/settings` — whether agents may make images, and with which models. Returns: `{ enabled: boolean, model: string — for most pictures (Best); "" = the app's choice, using: string — the model id in use, usingInfo?: ImageModel — that model, as in /api/images/models, quick: string — for simple pictures (Quick); "" = the app's choice, usingQuick: string, usingQuickInfo?: ImageModel, keySet: boolean — an OpenRouter key is set (Settings → Models) }`.
- `PUT /api/images/settings` — turns making images on or off, or picks the models; merges with what is saved. Body: `{ enabled?: boolean, model?: string — for most pictures: an OpenRouter model id (letters, digits and . _ ~ : / - only, up to 120 characters); "" = the app's choice; any other value is ignored without an error, quick?: string — for simple pictures, the same way }`. Returns: the same as GET.
- `GET /api/images/models?q=&sort=&for=` — OpenRouter's image models, up to 100. `q`: words that must all appear in the id, name or maker; `sort`: `price` (cheapest first), `picks` (the app's suggestions first, then the newest), anything else newest first; `for`: `quick` (the suggestions for simple pictures). Returns: `{ models: [ImageModel] }`, each `{ id, label, org?, description?, created?: ms, perImage?: number — US dollars for one picture of about a megapixel, textOut: boolean, edits: boolean — takes pictures to change, maxRefs?: number — how many pictures it takes to work from, params?: [setting] — the settings it takes }`.
- `GET /api/videos/settings` — whether agents may make videos, and with which model. Returns: `{ enabled: boolean, model: string — "" = the app's choice, using: string, usingInfo?: VideoModel, keySet: boolean }`.
- `PUT /api/videos/settings` — turns making videos on or off, or picks the model, as for images. Body: `{ enabled?: boolean, model?: string }`. Returns: the same as GET.
- `GET /api/videos/models?q=&sort=` — OpenRouter's video models, up to 100; `q` and `sort` (`price`: the cheapest second first; `picks`: the app's suggestions first) as for images. Returns: `{ models: [VideoModel] }`, each `{ id, label, org?, description?, created?: ms, durations: [seconds] — the lengths it makes (none: it keeps the length of the video it is given), resolutions: [string], aspects: [string], audio: boolean — it makes sound, frames: [first_frame|last_frame] — pictures it starts or ends on, videoIn: boolean — it changes a video it is given, perSecond?: number — US dollars a second, the cheapest way (lowest resolution, no sound), perSecondMax?: number — the dearest, skus?: { [sku]: string } — OpenRouter's prices as it lists them }`.

**AudioModel**: `{ id — OpenRouter's id, label, org?, description?, created?: ms, kind: music|speech — music: songs and pieces (Lyria); speech: voices, and sounds for a model that takes a description, via: speech|chat — OpenRouter's speech API, or a streamed chat completion (Lyria, gpt-audio), prompted?: true — it reads its input as a description of what to make (the voice, its delivery, sound effects, ambience: Seed Audio) rather than words to read aloud, voices?: [string] — the voice ids it speaks in, cloning?: boolean — it speaks in a voice copied from short recordings (from its endpoints; filled in by /api/audio/models), maxSamples?: number — how many recordings it takes at once, instructions?: true — it takes directions for the delivery, multiSpeaker?: true — it voices a conversation in turns, formats: [mp3|wav] — the files it makes, maxSeconds?: number — the longest it makes, perSong?: number — US dollars a song or clip, perSecond?: number — a second of sound, perChar?: number — a character read, perToken?: { input, output } — a token in and out, perMinute?: number — about what a minute of speech costs, however it is sold, free?: true }`.

- `GET /api/audio/settings` — whether agents may make audio, and with which models. Returns: `{ enabled: boolean, music: string — the music model picked, "" = the app's choice (Google's Lyria 3 Pro, else Lyria 3 Clip), speech: string — the voice and sound model picked, "" = the app's choice (ByteDance's Seed Audio 1.0, else Gemini 3.8 Flash TTS, else ElevenLabs v3), usingMusic: string, usingSpeech: string — the model ids in use, musicInfo?: AudioModel, speechInfo?: AudioModel — those models, keySet: boolean — an OpenRouter key is set (Settings → Models) }`.
- `PUT /api/audio/settings` — turns making audio on or off, or picks either model; merges with what is saved. Body: `{ enabled?: boolean, music?: string, speech?: string — OpenRouter model ids, as for images; "" = the app's choice; any other value is ignored without an error }`. Returns: the same as GET.
- `GET /api/audio/models?q=&sort=&kind=` — OpenRouter's models that make sound, up to 100: `kind` `music` (the chat models that make music) or `speech` (its speech models and the gpt-audio voices), both when left out; `q` as for images; `sort`: `price` (music: the cheapest song first; voices: about the cheapest minute of speech first), `picks` (the app's suggestions first), anything else newest first. Returns: `{ models: [AudioModel] }`.

**Pictures for documents, slides and pages.** The editors make and change pictures through routes of their own; agents use their tools instead (generate_image, make_svg, remove_background, erase_from_image, upscale_image, make_plot, generate_video, edit_image, edit_video), which save what they make in their folder. These routes say what the editors would use, and set up the picture tools on this computer:

- `GET /api/images/tiers` — the models the editors' AI tools would use now, for their prices. Returns: `{ keySet: boolean — an OpenRouter key is set, quick: TierModel — for simple pictures (icons, backgrounds, textures), best: TierModel — for the rest and every change to a picture, vector: TierModel — drawings (SVG) at Best, transparent: TierModel — see-through backgrounds when a tier's model cannot make them, writer: string — who writes drawings' SVG and plots' code ("Claude", or the helper model's name), video: { id, label, perSecond?: number }, local: LocalToolsState }`, each **TierModel** `{ id, label, perImage?: number — US dollars for one picture }`.
- `GET /api/videos/gpu` — whether edited videos are made on the graphics card, and the card found. Returns: **GpuState** `{ mode: auto|off, forcedOff?: boolean — turned off on this computer (HYDOR_VIDEO_GPU=off), checked: boolean — the card was tried (the first video made tries it), checking?: boolean, found: { api: nvenc|vaapi|qsv|videotoolbox|amf|mf, name: string, label: string — "NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090 · NVENC", encoders: { h264?, h265?, av1? } } | null, broken?: [string] — encoders that failed an edit, not used until the app starts again }`.
- `PUT /api/videos/gpu` — Settings → Images, video and audio → Use the graphics card for videos. Body: `{ mode: auto|off — auto: the graphics card when there is one that works, off: the processor }`. Returns: GpuState.
- `POST /api/videos/gpu/check` — tries the graphics card again now (a tiny test encode with each of its encoders). Returns: GpuState.
- `GET /api/images/local` — the picture tools on this computer (free and private: taking backgrounds out, erasing, making pictures larger, running plots' Python), each model loaded only when it is used and put away after a few idle minutes. Returns: **LocalToolsState** `{ ready: boolean — set up, installing: boolean, step?: string — what the set-up or a download is doing, error?: string — why the last set-up or use failed, canInstall: boolean — this computer has a Python 3.10 or later, or uv, why?: string — why it cannot, sizeMb: number — about what setting up downloads, downloaded: [cutout|erase|upscale] — the models already here, loaded: [cutout|erase|upscale] — the models in memory now, device: cuda|coreml|cpu — where they run: an NVIDIA GPU (through CUDA), a Mac's GPU (through Core ML) or the CPU, gpuBuildFailed?: boolean — with an NVIDIA GPU, the GPU build would not install, so the CPU's was, onCpu?: [cutout|erase] — models the GPU failed, which run on the CPU until the app starts again or they are set up again, gpuWhy?: string — why }`.
- `POST /api/images/local/setup` — sets the picture tools up: a private Python with ONNX Runtime, NumPy, Pillow, Matplotlib, pandas and a portable ffmpeg (about 145 MB; with an NVIDIA GPU, ONNX Runtime's build for it with NVIDIA's CUDA and cuDNN libraries, about 2.1 GB, and the usual one where that will not install); each model downloads the first time its tool is used (the background remover 192 MB, the eraser 208 MB, the upscaler 5 MB). Answers at once; ask GET again while `installing`. Returns: LocalToolsState.
- `POST /api/images/upscale` — a picture made 2× or 4× larger on this computer (Real-ESRGAN, a tile at a time; see-through parts kept), free and private; it needs the picture tools set up, and the result at most 8,192 pixels a side and 40 million in all. Body: `{ src: string — a kept picture (naget-asset:…) or a data: address, scale: 2|4 }`. Returns: the picture itself, a PNG (its size in the `x-width` and `x-height` headers, the model in `x-model`).
- `DELETE /api/images/local` — removes the picture tools and their models. Returns: LocalToolsState.

**The sound editor.** Its own routes, listed for what they do; agents edit sounds with edit_audio instead, and these are refused to them. A sound is named by `src`: a kept picture, video or sound (`media:<id>`), a sound the editor keeps (`naget-sound:<sha>`), a deck's video (`naget-video:<sha>`), or a file's path (a sound, or a video whose sound it is). **SoundRecipe**: `{ tracks: [{ id, name?, clips: [{ id, src — "" for the sound edited, else another sound, at — where it starts on the timeline (s), from, to — the part of its sound it plays (s), speed?: 0.25–4 — its pitch kept, gain?: dB, fadeIn?, fadeOut?: s, fadeSmooth?: true — the fades along a half sine, env?: [{ t: s from the clip's start, v: volume as a multiple, 0–4 }] — its volume line, straight between its points }], volume?: dB, pan?: −1–1, mute?, solo? }], effects?: [{ id, type: gain|fadeIn|fadeOut|normalize|loudness|denoise|eq|highpass|lowpass|compressor|limiter|pitch|reverse|echo|reverb|mono, p?: { its settings }, bypass?, track? — on that track only (else the whole sound), range?: [from, to] — on that part only (s) }], length?: s — a video's sound keeps this length }`.

- `GET /api/mediaedit/audio/info?src=` — what a sound is. Returns: `{ ffmpeg: boolean, why?: string — how to get ffmpeg when there is none, duration?, rate?, channels?, codec?, format?: mp3|wav|m4a|ogg|flac|null — its kind of file, video?: boolean — a video's sound, bytes?, name? }`.
- `GET /api/mediaedit/audio/source?src=&as=` — the sound's bytes for the browser to read, in byte ranges: the file as it is, or (a video's sound, or `as=m4a`) an M4A of it.
- `POST /api/mediaedit/audio/sounds?name=` — keeps a sound the editor brings in (a recording, a file from the device). Body: the sound's bytes (`application/octet-stream`, up to 700 MB). Returns: `{ src: "naget-sound:<sha>", duration, rate?, channels?, name? }`. 415 for what is not a sound.
- `GET /api/mediaedit/audio/sounds/:sha` — a kept sound, in byte ranges (kept 30 days after it was last used).
- `POST /api/mediaedit/audio` — makes an edit with ffmpeg, as a job. Body: `{ src: string — the sound edited, recipe: SoundRecipe, out: { format: mp3|m4a|ogg|wav|flac, kbps?: number, bits?: 16|24, channels?: 1|2, rate?: number }, to: { sound: true } (kept, for a video) | { library: true, session?, title? } | { media: id, as: replace|copy } | { file: path, as: replace|copy }, name?: string }`. Returns: **SoundEditJob** `{ job, status: queued|running|done|failed|stopped, progress?: 0–1, step?: string — what it is doing, format, src?, media?, path? — where it went, seconds?, bytes?, error?, startedAt, endedAt? }`; 400 for what cannot be made (written over a file, it keeps its kind).
- `GET /api/mediaedit/audio/:job` — how an edit is going. Returns: SoundEditJob; 404 an hour after it ended.
- `DELETE /api/mediaedit/audio/:job` — stops an edit; nothing is saved. Returns: SoundEditJob.
- `POST /api/mediaedit/audio/words` — starts reading what is said in a sound (speech recognition on this computer), or gives the words read before. Body: `{ src: string }`. Returns: `{ status: none|running|done|failed, progress?: 0–1, words?: [{ w: string, a: number, b: number — when it is said (s) }], error?: string }`.
- `GET /api/mediaedit/audio/words?src=` — how reading it goes. Returns: the same.
- `POST /api/mediaedit/audio/make/estimate` — what a sound made with AI would cost, and with which model. Body: `{ kind: music|speech|sound, prompt: string, seconds?: number, voice?: string }`. Returns: `{ model, label, voices: [string], voice?, described: boolean, keySet: boolean, cost?: number — US dollars, seconds, maxSeconds?, why?: string — why it cannot be made }`.
- `POST /api/mediaedit/audio/make` — makes music, a voice or a sound effect through OpenRouter and keeps it. Body: `{ kind, prompt: string — what to make, or the words to say, seconds?, voice?, how?: string — how the words sound, instrumental?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ src: "naget-sound:<sha>", seconds, cost?, model, name }`.

## Kept pictures, videos and sounds

What the user keeps of the pictures, videos and sounds agents made (or any on this computer), each copied into the app's data folder with a title and a description a model writes from it (a video: from ten of its frames on one picture; a sound, which no model hears: from its prompt, its lyrics and its model). **MediaItem**: `{ id — 10 hex characters, kind: image|video|audio, title, description, mime, bytes, width?, height?, duration? — seconds (a video or a sound), peaks?: [number] — a sound's shape: how loud each of 64 parts of it is, 0 to 1 (with ffmpeg), lyrics? — the words sung or spoken in a sound, prompt? — what it was made from, model? — what made it, sessionKey? — the session it was saved from, space? — the project it is in (none: in no project), folder? — the folder of kept things it is in (see Pages; not shown in the app), projectFile? — the whole path of its own file in the project's folder, projectFileAdopted?: true — that file was in the project's folder already when it was kept, so it is the user's own and is never trashed or moved, source? — the file it was saved from, caption: pending|done|failed|edited — its title and description: being written, written by the model, written from its prompt because no model could (captionError says why), or the user's own, captionError?, captionBy? — the model that wrote them, as Settings names it, createdAt, updatedAt, file — the kept copy's path, a path an agent can work from (generate_image's images, generate_video's first_frame) }`.

- `GET /api/media` — every image, video and sound kept, newest first. Returns: `{ items: [MediaItem] }`.
- `POST /api/media` — keeps a file as a kept thing: a picture (PNG, JPEG, WebP, GIF, AVIF), a video (MP4, WebM, MOV) or a sound (MP3, WAV, M4A, AAC, OGG, OPUS, FLAC), up to 1 GB; at most 5,000 in all. Its title comes from its prompt (or its file's name) until the model has written one. Body: `{ path: string — the file, prompt?: string — what it was made from, a hint for its description, model?: string, lyrics?: string — a sound's lyrics or script (≤8,000 characters), a hint for its title and description, sessionKey?: string, source?: string — what it came from, shown as the item's `source` (up to 2,000 characters: a page's address, for a picture kept from a page), space?: string|null — the project it goes in (left out: the session's, when it is in one; null: none), dir?: string — the folder its file goes in: the whole path of the project's folder or of a folder inside it (400 otherwise, or in no project) }`. In a project it also gets a file of its own in the project's folder; a path already inside that folder becomes that file, with no copy. Returns: `{ item: MediaItem, existed: boolean — it was kept already (the same bytes, or the same file at the same size): that item }`. 400 for another kind of file, 404 when it is not there.
- `GET /api/media/:id` — one item. Returns: `{ item: MediaItem }`; 404 when there is no such item.
- `PATCH /api/media/:id` — the user's own title or description, which stay until written again (caption: edited), the project it is in, the folder of kept things it is in, and/or the folder of its project for its file. Body: `{ title?: string (≤120, not empty), description?: string (≤2,000), space?: string|null — a project's id, or null for none (it leaves a folder of another project), folder?: string|null — a folder's id, which groups kept things and is not shown in the app (it goes to that folder's project), or null for none, dir?: string — the whole path of its project's folder or of a folder inside it, for its file (alone, it moves the file there) }`. Moved to another project, its file goes there; moved to none, its file goes to the Trash (a file that was the user's stays). 400 for a project that is not one, or a dir that is no folder of the project. Returns: `{ item: MediaItem }`.
- `DELETE /api/media/:id` — deletes it, its copy and still with it (the file it was saved from stays); its file in the project's folder goes to the Trash, unless it was the user's already (projectFileAdopted). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/media/:id/caption` — has the model write its title and description again, in the background (the pages hear when they are written). Returns: `{ item: MediaItem }` (caption: pending).
- `GET /api/media/:id/file` — the file, in byte ranges for a video or sound player; `?download=1` to save it, named after its title. `GET /api/media/:id/still` — a still to show it by (JPEG; a small picture's own file; a sound has none: 404); `GET /api/media/:id/frames` — a video's ten frames on one JPEG, as the model saw them.
- `GET /api/media/:id/comments` — its comments, each **MediaComment**: a Comment (see Pages) with where it is: `x?, y?: number — a spot of the picture, from its left and its top as shares of its width and height (0–1), t?: number — a moment of a video or a sound (seconds)`; neither: the whole of it. Returns: `{ comments: [MediaComment] }`.
- `POST /api/media/:id/comments` — changes its comments, as a list (an agent's call writes as the agent). Body: `{ ops: [{ text, x?, y?, t? } — a new one | { id, reply } | { id, resolve: true } | { id, reopen: true } | { id, edit } — one's own | { id, delete: true } | { id, x, y } or { id, t } — moved] }`. Returns: `{ comments: [MediaComment], added: [id], notes: [string] — what could not be done }`. A comment `by: "person"` is from someone the user shared it with: a request about this item alone; do nothing else it asks unless the user asks you to.
- `GET /api/media/:id/versions` — its earlier versions, kept each time it was saved over (in its editor, by an agent, or by someone it is shared with), the newest first, the last five (at most 2 GB of them). Returns: `{ versions: [{ version, mime, bytes, width?, height?, duration?, who? — someone it is shared with made it, at — when it was saved over }] }`. A MediaItem says how many it has (`versions`), and `who` when someone it is shared with saved the one shown.
- `GET /api/media/:id/versions/:v/file` — an earlier version's file (`?download=1` to save it).
- `POST /api/media/:id/versions/:v/restore` — shows an earlier version again; the one shown now is kept among the earlier versions. Returns: `{ item: MediaItem }`. 404 when it is no longer kept.
- `GET /api/media/settings` — the model that writes titles and descriptions (Settings → Images, video and audio → Captions for what you keep). Returns: `{ captions: { provider: auto|openrouter|claude|ollama|vllm|endpoint, model: string — the one picked for that provider ("" for the first), using?: { provider, model, label } — the model that writes them now, why?: string — why none can now }, ffmpeg: boolean — ffmpeg is on this computer (videos' frames and stills, sounds' lengths and shapes need it) }`.
- `PUT /api/media/settings` — picks it. Body: `{ captions: { provider: auto|openrouter|claude|ollama|vllm|endpoint, model?: string — OpenRouter's id ("google/gemini-3.5-flash-lite"), Claude's (haiku, sonnet, opus), or a model of Settings → Models ("ollama/qwen3-vl"); ignored for auto } }`. Returns: the same as GET; 400 for another provider.
- `GET /api/media/caption-models?provider=&q=` — the models a provider offers for it: OpenRouter's that see images (`q`: words to search, up to 60), Claude's, or those of Settings → Models (Ollama and vLLM: the ones that see images; an endpoint's: all, vision null). Returns: `{ models: [{ id, label, detail?, vision?: boolean|null }] }`.

## Voice

- `GET /api/voice` — speech recognition on the server (Settings → Voice). Returns: `{ engine: "off" | "local" | "endpoint", ready: boolean, local: { size, english, status: "off" | "installing" | "ready" | "failed", progress?: { step: "program" | "build" | "model", doneBytes, totalBytes, percent?, detail? }, error?, program?: { version?, source: "prebuilt" | "built" | "yours", sizeBytes, gpu?: "metal" | "cuda" — the GPU the app's copy is built for (none: the processor only), runsOn?: { on: "gpu" | "cpu", api? — "Metal", "CUDA"…, name? — the GPU's } — what it ran on the last time it was used or tested, gpuError? — the GPU failed lately: recordings go to the processor until it is tried again, a few minutes later }, gpuBuild?: { status: "building" | "failed" | "needs-toolkit", percent?, detail?, error? } — whisper.cpp for this machine's NVIDIA GPU (Linux), built in the background with NVIDIA's CUDA toolkit, models: [{ size, english, sizeBytes }] — downloaded, needBytes }, endpoint?: { url, model, keyHint? }, ffmpeg }`.
- `PUT /api/voice` — changes the engine, the model or the service. Merges, except that `endpoint` replaces the saved address and model. Body: `{ engine?: "off" | "local" | "endpoint" — "local" downloads whatever is missing (any other engine stops a download); "endpoint" needs an address saved first, size?: "tiny" | "base" | "small" | "medium" — another model downloads at once while "local" is on, english?: boolean — the English-only model, endpoint?: { url: string — http(s); a trailing /audio/transcriptions is dropped, model?: string — default "whisper-1", apiKey?: string | null — (a key: not for agents) null removes it, leaving it out keeps it } }`. Errors: 400 for a bad address, size or engine. Returns: the state, as GET.
- `POST /api/voice/cancel` — stops a download under way, and turns "local" off (the page's own button; agents send `PUT /api/voice { engine: "off" }`, which stops it too). Returns: the state, as GET.
- `POST /api/voice/remove` — removes downloaded files. Body: `{ size?: "tiny" | "base" | "small" | "medium", english?: boolean }` removes one model; `{ all: true }` removes the app's whisper.cpp and every model. Removing the model in use, or all of them, while "local" is on turns it off. Errors: 400 without `size` or `all`. Returns: the state, as GET.
- `POST /api/voice/gpu-build` — builds whisper.cpp for this machine's NVIDIA GPU again, in the background: after a build that failed, or once NVIDIA's CUDA toolkit is installed (it is looked for again). Returns: the state, as GET.
- `POST /api/voice/test` — runs one second of silence through the engine that is on. Returns: `{ ok: true, ms, text }`. Errors: 409 when it is off or not ready, 502 when the service can't be reached.

## Word suggestions (Settings → Capabilities → Word suggestions)

- `GET /api/suggest` — word suggestions: the settings and what it is doing. Returns: `{ settings: { enabled: boolean, llm: "auto" | "on" | "off" — Qwen3.5 beside the small model, quant: "q8" | "q4" — its size, precision: number — 0.2 more suggestions … 0.6 only the surest, nextWord: boolean, where: { chat, docs, notes, other: boolean }, typedWords: number, contextWords: number, switchByLanguage: boolean, sessionCache: boolean, threads: number — 0 chooses, personalise: boolean, schedule: "nightly" | "weekly" | "manual", sources: { messages, replies, notes, docs: boolean }, userWeight: number — 1 to 16, trainThreads: number, trainGpu: boolean — learn on the graphics card when there is a free one (an NVIDIA card with 4 GB free and under half busy, or a Mac with Apple silicon) }, status: { state: "off" | "waiting" — on, but never used here, so nothing is downloaded yet | "setting-up" | "starting" | "ready" | "failed", detail?, llm: { state: "off" | "starting" | "ready" | "memory" | "failed", detail?, freeMb?, needMb? }, model: { kind: "base" | "personal", trainedAt?, tokens?, languages? }, training: { state: "idle" | "waiting" | "running" | "paused" — the person paused the run: nothing starts it until it is resumed, progress? — where it is or stopped: { done, total }, startedAt?, lastAt?, next?, detail?, device?: { kind: "cpu" | "cuda" | "mps", name? } — where the run that is going (or paused) learns, noGpu? — why the graphics card is not used when `trainGpu` is on: no card with room, the card is held back for a day after a failure, …, in plain English }, stats?: { requests, p50Ms?, p90Ms? }, usage: { since: number — when counting began (ms), total, today, week: each { shown, taken, typedThrough, wrong, saved, typedChars }: whole numbers, today is the local day and week the last 7 days }, thresholds: { judged: number — guesses judged against what was then typed, needed: number — 400, what one kind of guess needs, fitted: boolean — whether how sure a suggestion must be is fitted to the person's writing } } }`. The numbers are counts of suggestions shown, taken with Tab or →, typed through by the person, wrong, keystrokes saved, and characters typed; they never hold any text. Suggestions right are `taken + typedThrough`.
- `PUT /api/suggest` — changes settings; a part of them is enough (`where` and `sources` merge too). Word suggestions are on at first but set themselves up only when first used (`POST /api/suggest/warm`, or a suggestion asked for); turning `enabled` on starts setting it up at once (about 1.3 GB the first time), off stops both processes. Body: `{ enabled?: boolean, llm?: "auto" | "on" | "off", quant?: "q8" | "q4", precision?: number (0.2 to 0.6), nextWord?: boolean, where?: { chat?, docs?, notes?, other?: boolean }, typedWords?: integer (20 to 200), contextWords?: integer (0 to 200), switchByLanguage?: boolean, sessionCache?: boolean, threads?: integer (0 to 32), personalise?: boolean, schedule?: "nightly" | "weekly" | "manual", sources?: { messages?, replies?, notes?, docs?: boolean }, userWeight?: integer (1 to 16), trainThreads?: integer (0 to 64), trainGpu?: boolean }`. Errors: 400 naming the field that is wrong. Returns: the settings and status, as GET.
- `POST /api/suggest` — the page's own call while someone types: a suggestion for the text before the caret. Body: `{ surface: "chat" | "doc" | "text", text: string, reply?: string — the agent's last reply, prev?: string — the person's previous message, doc?: string — the document before the caret's paragraph, session?: string — the session's key, client?: string — the page's id: a new request cancels its one before }`. Returns: `{ word, next, conf, confNext, show, showNext, plan?, lang?, model?, ms? }` (`plan`: `"mix"` both models, `"llm"` Qwen3.5 alone, `"slm"` the small model alone; `lang`: the language guessed; `model`: which small model answered, `"personal"` (the one that learned from the person: in the languages they write in, and where no language was guessed) or `"base"` (the one that came with the app: in every other language); not there when Qwen3.5 alone answered), or 204 when there is no answer now (off, not ready yet, the place is switched off, or a newer request came). With `show: false` the answer still holds the guessed `word` and its `conf`: the page keeps it unseen and judges it against what is then typed.
- `POST /api/suggest/warm` — a message box has the focus: starts what is set up, so the first suggestion is quick; the first time, while the status is `waiting`, it starts setting it up (about 1.3 GB). A setup that failed is tried again ten minutes later, then twenty, forty and so on, at most a day; turning `enabled` off and on tries at once. Returns: the settings and status, as GET.
- `POST /api/suggest/train` — **Retrain now**: the small model learns from all that was written, starting again from the small model that came with the app (never from the one it learned before, so it never drifts; its newest part when there is a great deal), in the background at the lowest priority, on the graphics card when `trainGpu` is on and there is one free (the first time on an NVIDIA card it first downloads PyTorch's build for it, about 2.5 GB, and the status says so), else on the processor. It is also **Resume**: a paused run goes on from where it stopped, with the same writing. Nothing is learned when nothing was written since the last run (`status.training.detail` says "Nothing new to learn from"). Errors: 409 when word suggestions or learning are off. Returns: the settings and status, as GET.
- `POST /api/suggest/train/pause` — **Pause**: the run that is going stops where it is and keeps what it learned so far (`status.training.state` is `paused`, with its `progress`); the schedule starts nothing until it is resumed, also after a restart of the server. Errors: 409 when no run is going. Returns: the settings and status, as GET.
- `DELETE /api/suggest/train` — **Stop**: ends the run that is going or paused and drops what it learned so far (the model in use stays); the next scheduled run waits its usual time from now. Ask the person first: it cannot be undone. Errors: 409 when no run is going or paused. Returns: the settings and status, as GET.
- `DELETE /api/suggest/personal` — **Forget what it learned**: deletes the small model that learned from the person (and a paused run); the one that came with the app is used again. Returns: the settings and status, as GET.
- `POST /api/suggest/usage` — the page's own call, in batches while someone types: adds to the counts kept for **How it's helping**, and to the judged guesses the thresholds are fitted from (never any text). Body: `{ shown?: integer, taken?: integer, typedThrough?: integer, wrong?: integer, saved?: integer, typedChars?: integer, judged?: { mix?, llm?, slm?: { right: integer[40], total: integer[40] } — guesses by how sure they were, in bins 0.025 wide, and how many were right } }`, each number from 0 to 5,000; a count left out is 0. Errors: 400 naming a count that is not a whole number in range, a field that is not a count, or a `judged` that is not of that shape (or has more right than judged in a bin); nothing is added then. Returns: `{ usage, thresholds }`, as in the status.
- `DELETE /api/suggest/usage` — **Reset the numbers**: starts the counts again from nothing, since now, and forgets the judged guesses, so how sure a suggestion must be is fitted again from the start. Returns: the settings and status, as GET.

## Apps: read

Shapes used below. **Run**: `{ id, app — app id or tpl:<id>, appName, key? — its session (an app with steps has one only once a change was asked of its finished run; each agent step has its own), source: web|test|api|mcp|public|batch|screen — from its screen|step — an agent step of another run|app — started by another app's step|session — an agent ran it for you in a session (use_app), status: queued|starting|running|waiting|done|failed|stopped, inputs, result? — the answer fields, reply?, error?, costUsd?, agent?, model?, batch?, row?, parent? — the run it is a step of, step? — where in that run's flow, flow? — which of its other flows it ran (absent: the main one), misses?: [{ step — the step's title, what? — the host and path it asked or a search's words, why — in plain words: "it limits requests", "it keeps programs out", "no answer in time", "it needs the key NAME", "it would do more with the key NAME (optional)"…, path? — where in its flow, times? — the same miss more than once }] — what its steps could not have: calls that still failed after their tries (also when a program caught the error) and steps that failed (at most 30), lang? — the language it writes in, a tag ("el"; absent: English), person?: { id, name, role: owner|admin|member|guest, email?, teamId? } | null — who it acts for, as the signed token of the person at the web app's screen proved it (null: it acts for nobody; see Apps, from outside), partial?: { [answerFieldId]: value } — while a run of an app's main flow works: the answer fields the steps that have ended can fill, each checked on its own and left out until it fits (pictures on the web at once, files on this computer only in the result); gone once the run ends, when `result` has the whole answer, createdAt, startedAt?, endedAt? }`. **Step** (of a run with steps): `{ path — its id, inside a Repeat "loop#2/read", id, kind — agent|model|tool|decide|repeat|parallel|app|data, or round (a Repeat's round), title, status: running|waiting|done|failed|skipped|stopped, error?, costUsd?, run? — the run behind an agent step or a called app, session? — its session key, startedAt?, endedAt?, values? — what it gave (long ones shortened) }`. **App**: the Recipe below plus `{ id, slug, webapp?: { id, name } — it is the flows half of this web app, mcp, agents: boolean — agents in your sessions may run it (its page's Agents can use it; true unless turned off), fromFile?: true — installed from a file (its page's More says Uninstall), public, template?, createdAt, updatedAt }`.

- `GET /api/apps` — your apps, the ready-made ones, the latest runs, the pinned apps and the apps used lately. Returns: `{ apps: [App & { usage: { runs, spent } — today, lastRunAt?: number — when it last ran, uses?: number — runs all told (not tests, steps or conversations), needsKey?: true — a key its main flow cannot run without that the vault lacks }], templates: [Recipe & { id, installed: boolean — they come installed until you uninstall one, agents: boolean — agents in your sessions may run it, lastRunAt?, needsKey?: true }], recent: [Run] (up to 6, no CSV-row or public-page runs), pins: [string] — the pinned apps' ids in their order (ready-made ones as tpl:<id>), used: [{ app, run: Run }] — up to 8 apps used lately, newest first, each once with its latest run }`.
- `GET /api/apps/pins` — the apps pinned to the top of the Apps page, in their order (one deleted or uninstalled since is left out). Returns: `{ ids: [string] — yours by id, ready-made ones as tpl:<id> }`.
- `GET /api/apps/suggested?n=2` — the apps the start page suggests under its empty message box: those run most (most runs first, then the one run last; not tries in the builder, steps or conversations), each still here; to make `n` (1 to 6, else 2), Deep research report and Trip planner when installed. Returns: `{ apps: [{ id: string — yours by id, ready-made ones as tpl:<id>, name, description?, icon, color, uses?: number — how many times it ran }] }`.
- `PUT /api/apps/pins` — pins apps to the top of the Apps page, in this order, in place of those pinned before; every device shows the same. Body: `{ ids: [string] — at most 12: yours by id or address, ready-made ones as tpl:<id> (or their id alone) }`. 400 for an app that is not here, a ready-made one uninstalled, or more than 12. Returns: `{ ids }` as kept.
- `GET /api/apps/:id` — one app, by id or address (slug); a ready-made one as `tpl:<id>` (e.g. `tpl:trip-planner`). Returns: yours `{ app: App, keys: [{ id, name, hint, createdAt, lastUsed? }], usage: { runs, spent }, publicUsage: { runs, spent }, workDir, env: Env, secrets: [SecretState] — the keys it needs, view?: { … } — what its own page's view keeps, when its view has pages for it, home?: Home — then its settings there, latest?: number — then when its latest run of its main flow ended well, starts?: { [fieldId]: answer } — what its questions start from: About you for one with `from` (as set, or worked out), the last answer for one with `remember`; those with nothing to start from are left out }`; ready-made `{ template, installed: boolean, agents: boolean, secrets: [SecretState], view?, home?, latest?, starts? — as for yours, for one that opens on its own page (Explore, tpl:explore) }`. A **Home** is `{ inputs: { question: answer } — the answers its runs from its page and its automatic runs take: those kept (under a question's id or a former one), else what it starts from (About you for `from`, the last answer for `remember`), else its form's defaults, started?: [fieldId] — the questions whose answer is what they start from, not one kept (they follow About you as it changes; an answer saved that is the same, or empty, is not kept as its own, and Update automatically's task leaves them for each run to take afresh), daily: { on: boolean, time: "HH:MM", days?: [0–6] — 0 is Sunday; absent: every day } — Update automatically (off, the time and days it would start with: the app's own, as the Weekly progress report's Fridays at 16:00, or those last picked) }`. A **SecretState** is one of its `secrets` as the vault has it: `{ …Secret, have: boolean, hint? — the saved value's masked end, blocked?: [host] — hosts the app sends it to that the saved key may not go to, noCommands?: true — the app runs it in commands, which the saved key does not allow }`; never a value.
- `GET /api/apps/tools` — Hydor's own tools an app may allow (for `tools`). Returns: `{ tools: [{ name, description, web: boolean — usable on public pages, step?: true — it can be a Use a tool step on its own, stepOnly?: true — only steps have it (user_profile, rank_by_interest, work_log, experiment_wait, read_paper, reading_list, zotero_sync), not an app's agents, inputs?: [{ name, type?, required?, description? }] — a step tool's inputs (for its `with`) }] }`.
- `GET /api/apps/models` — Custom's models (a public page must use one). Returns: `{ models: [{ id, label, provider }] }`.

Agents in your sessions run your apps with their `use_app` tool: the apps installed whose `agents` is on and that are made for it (`sessions` in the recipe; not blocks). What a request leaves out, the user fills in on the app's card in the conversation (a question that starts from About you or its last answer shows filled in there, and an app that runs without asking takes it as it is); its run (source `session`) shows there too. The tool takes `language`: the language the user writes in (a tag such as `el`, or a language's name), which the run writes in. A change the user then asks of what it gave goes to that run with `use_app`'s `run` and `change` (as POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/ask): the run's card moves under the agent's reply and shows the new answer, and the call waits for the change as for a run and gives back the updated answer; a change that outlasts the call comes to the session as a message when it is done ("[App changed] The change you asked of it…").

## Apps: make, change, delete

**Recipe**: `{ name: string (≤60, default "Untitled app"), description?: string (≤300), icon?: sparkles|telescope|plane|scale|file-search|list-checks|mail|graduation-cap|newspaper|scroll-text|app-window|pen-line|lightbulb|calendar|chart|code|globe|book-open|briefcase|heart|shopping-cart|utensils|dumbbell|music|camera|map|message-square|languages|calculator|home|wrench|bot|cloud-sun|sun|trophy|chef-hat|gift|clapperboard|tv|gamepad|ticket|piggy-bank|trending-up|map-pin|history (default sparkles), color?: blue|indigo|violet|pink|red|orange|amber|green|teal|cyan|slate (default blue), category?: string (≤30), assistant?: string — an assistant's id, agent?: claude|codex|acp (default claude), acpAgent?: string — for acp; "console" = Custom (default), model?: string, tier?: quick|standard|best|max — the tier its agent thinks with in place of a model by name (it follows Settings → Models → Tiers and works whichever program runs the app; a model given wins), effort?: string, mode?: string (runs default to auto), cwd?: string — the folder every run works in (absent: a new folder per run), prompt: string — the task (≤30,000), with {{fieldId}}, {{#fieldId}}…{{/fieldId}} (kept when answered and not "No"), {{^fieldId}}…{{/fieldId}} (kept when empty), {{today}}, {{now}}, {{weekday}}, {{timezone}}, {{language}}, {{lang}} and About you's {{me.home}}… (see Values below; a list as one line), fields?: [{ id?: snake_case (default from label), label: string (≤80), type: text|long|number|date|choice|yesno|file (default text), required?: boolean, help?: string, placeholder?: string, options?: string[] — choice only (≤30; empty becomes Yes, No), multi?: boolean — choice only: more than one may be picked; its value has one option a line, default?: string, from?: me.home|me.country|me.units|me.currency|me.diet|me.household|me.services|me.teams|me.fields|me.topics|me.venues|me.author|me.compute — it starts from that field of About you (a choice as the option that matches it in any case, several as those of its options the list has, a list as one line, the author by name; not for a file): its form shows it filled in (folded into one line when it is not required), and a run that leaves it out (the API, a row, a schedule, use_app) takes it before default, a public page's never, remember?: boolean — it starts from the person's last answer to it in this app (kept from their runs from its form and its card in a session, not from programs; not for a file; not with from), was?: string[] — its former ids, for a question renamed (≤5, ids no other question has): an answer given or kept under one of them (a run's inputs, a schedule's, its own page's settings, a last answer) counts as its own while it has none }] (≤30), answer?: [{ id?, label, type: text|markdown|number|yesno|date|url|list|table|media|ui, description?, required?, columns?: [{ id?, label, type: text|number|yesno|date|url|image, description? }] — table only (≤12), ui?: string — ui only: the card that shows it, an answer UI's name as find_ui gives it ("comparison", "itinerary"; another name for one is taken as its own) }] (≤30; none: the reply is the answer; a ui field's value is the card's data, one object as a ```ui block holds it, checked against its card's type (not a `from` reference: cards that read a spreadsheet or a file are not offered in apps), and comes back as that JSON; a CSV of rows and the tool other agents call give it as text; a media field is a list of `{ src, caption?, kind?: image|video|audio|file, name?, poster? }` — src an https:// address, or the path of a file on this computer, which submit_result keeps a copy of (a picture on the web is kept too, up to 15 MB) and gives as `/app-media/<sha>/<name>`; an image column is one such address), tools?: string[] | null — names from GET /api/apps/tools (null or absent: all), limits?: { runsPerDay?: number (≤100,000), capUsd?: number — US$ per run (≤10,000) }, flow?: Flow — what it does, step by step (absent: one agent does the task in prompt; with a flow, prompt may be empty), data?: Data — what it keeps between runs, memory?: boolean — its agents remember across its runs, in a memory of its own (a project in Settings → Memory), requires?: Requires — what it needs on this computer, secrets?: [Secret] (≤10) — the keys it needs, by name: `{ name: NAME in the vault (capitals, digits, _), label, help?, url? — where to get one, domains?: [host] — where it may go, commands?: boolean — commands on this computer use it, header?, prefix?, query? — how http_request sends it by default, optional?: boolean, flows?: [name] — only these of its flows need it ("run": the main one), so it is asked for only when one of them runs }` (domains or commands required); values are never part of it, assets?: { [name]: { sha, mime, bytes, caption? } } — its own files (≤100, 150 MB in all), which its view and answer show as `asset:<name>`; add them with POST /api/apps/:id/assets (or app_save's assets), not by hand, flows?: { [name]: Flow & { title?: string, inputs?: [field] — the form it takes, as fields } } — flows beside the main one, which its screen's forms and buttons (and programs, with flow) start; their outputs are names of their own (≤20), screens?: { [name]: { page, title? } } — made and changed only with the screens routes below (saving a recipe keeps the app's own; a new app starts without any), examples?: [{ id?, name?, flow?, inputs: { [fieldId]: answer }, checks?: [Rule] — each on the run's values: {{answer.field}}, {{stepId.value}}, {{reply}}, the inputs }] (≤20, ≤10 checks each) — tried with the examples routes below, graph?: { at?: { [stepId]: { x, y } } — where a step sits in the graph (those not placed are laid out by themselves), groups?: [{ id, note, steps: [stepId] }] — steps drawn in a frame with a note, bends?: { ["<giver>><user>"]: { x, y } } — where a value's wire bends } — the graph's layout, which the editor keeps (agents may leave it out: saving a whole recipe without it keeps the one saved), block?: boolean — a block: made to be a step of other apps (Add a step offers it; a Call an app step runs it; ready-made ones are in GET /api/apps templates with block: true), sessions?: boolean — made to be run by agents in sessions, its form asked and its answer shown there (default true, false for a block; set false for an app used on its own page: a screen, or a view of what it keeps), ui?: Ui — its view: how its answer looks and what a click there does (see Apps: views), language?: "person" — its model and agent steps (and a one-task app's agent) are told, in a line after their instructions, to write what people read in the run's language, keeping field names, the choices given, codes and numbers as written (nothing is said in English; absent: nothing is said of language; new apps made in the builder start with it) }`.
**Flow**: `{ steps: [Step], outputs?: { [answerFieldId]: "{{stepId.value}}" } — what the run gives back (an answer field it leaves out comes from the last step that gave a value of its name) }`; a bare list of steps is a flow too. Steps run in order. Every **Step** has `{ id: a letter, then letters, digits or _ (≤40; default from its title; unique in the flow), do: agent|model|tool|decide|repeat|parallel|app|data|ask|code|value, title?, if?: Rule — it runs only when this holds, else it is skipped and its values are empty, failed?: [Step] — run instead when it fails ({{error}} says why; then the flow goes on) | "skip" | "agent" — an agent does its work (a tool, model, code, data or app step): it gets what the step was given and why it failed, and hands in the values the steps after it use (the run's steps then show it with rescued: true and why it failed) (absent: the run stops) }`, and by kind: **agent** `{ instructions: string (≤30,000), outputs?: Outputs — handed in with submit_result (absent: it gives {{id.reply}}), agent?, acpAgent?, model?, tier?: quick|standard|best|max — what it thinks with instead of a model by name (a model given wins; a step that names a tier or a model replaces the app's), effort?, mode?, assistant?, tools?, capUsd? — the app's own unless set }` (also gives {{id.reply}}); **model** — one answer from a model, no tools: `{ instructions, outputs?: Outputs (absent: {{id.text}}), model?: one of Custom's (default the helper model) }`; **tool** `{ tool: web_search|read_page|http_request|read_document|generate_image|knowledge_search|knowledge_read|recall|findings|finding_record|user_profile|rank_by_interest|prior_work|notify_user, with?: { [input]: a value, or text with {{values}} } (a web_search's words are searched as written, as_written: true unless it says false: a date or a site in them is part of what is looked for, and from, to, sites, categories or languages filter), cache?: false | seconds (≤604,800) — how long the answer of a look-up (read_page, web_search, http_request GET) is kept for the same call by any step or run (unset: 5 minutes, a day for places and encyclopedias; false: never) }` — gives {{id.text}}, and {{id.data}} when that is JSON. A failure that may pass (408, 425, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504, no answer in time, a dropped or refused connection, a search service limiting requests) is tried again, three tries in all, after the wait the service asks for (Retry-After) or about 1 s then 3 s, at most 20 s of waiting a call (a request that changes something only when the service turned it away: 408, 425, 429, 503); calls to one host are spaced as its service asks (Semantic Scholar and Nominatim one a second, arXiv one every 3 s), for every run; a read_page that meets 401, 403, 429 or a bot check is read once in this computer's own headless browser, when there is one (not in a public page's runs) (findings `{ query?, verdict?, limit?, folder? }` gives `{ findings: [{ number, statement, verdict, learned?, evidence, when, code_changed, changed_files?, replaces? }], note }`, a project’s findings; finding_record `{ statement, verdict: supported|refuted|unclear|open, learned?, evidence?: [{ kind, ref, label? }] (at most 12; a number in learned needs a run, score or table among them), replaces?, folder? }` records one and gives it back with its number; folder, a step’s alone, is the project’s folder (the run’s own otherwise); notify_user `{ message, title? }` sends the user a notification that opens the run; prior_work `{ idea: two to six sentences, with what makes it different, field?, since?: a year, limit?: 1–8, queries?: searches to run too, separated by |, language?: the language of its sentences }`, which agents have too, checks whether an idea or a result has been done before (searches on arXiv, OpenAlex and Semantic Scholar, the nearest papers compared by the helper model; up to two minutes) and gives `{ verdict: known|partly|new|unjudged, summary, closest: [{ title, authors, year, venue, url, relation: same|overlaps|related|unjudged, same, differs }], searched: { queries, sources, candidates }, note }` — finding nothing is not proof that it is new; user_profile `{ sessions?: number — titles of the latest sessions, 200 unless set, at most 1000, notes?: number }`, a step's alone, gives `{ profile, notes: [text], sessions: [{ title, days, times }], count, projects: [{ name, about, recent: [title] }] }`: the user's profile, memory's notes about them and their latest sessions' titles, newest first, those with memory off, Hydor subagents and unwatched app runs left out, and their projects, the one at work most lately first, each with its description and its latest sessions' titles; rank_by_interest `{ items: [{ id, text }] (≤2,000), interests?: [text] (≤60; absent: the user's profile, their projects and their latest sessions' titles, with memory off their projects alone), liked?: [text], disliked?: [text] (≤200 each: what the user kept and put aside, which move the ranking toward and away from them), limit? }`, a step's alone, gives `[{ id, score — 0 to 1, why — what it fits, in a few words, interest? — the place of the interest it fits best among those given }]`, best first: by meaning with the embedding model on this computer when search by meaning is on, else by words (BM25)); **decide** `{ rule?: Rule, ask?: string — a question in words the helper model answers yes or no, then: [Step], else?: [Step] }` — gives {{id.yes}}, {{id.why}}; **repeat** `{ over?: "{{a list}}" — its steps once per item ({{item}}, {{index}}; text goes by lines; ≤200), until?: Rule, untilAsk?: string — or again until this holds (each round sees the last one's values), max?: 1–50 (default 5), atOnce?: 1–6, as?: the item's name, collect?: the step whose values make each round's record (default its last with values), steps: [Step] }` — gives {{id.items}} (one record per round), {{id.count}}, {{id.last}}; **parallel** `{ branches: [[Step]] (≤6) }`; **app** `{ app: another app's address, with?: { [fieldId]: value } }` — gives its answer fields and {{id.reply}}; **ask** — the run waits for the person (status waiting, a notice that opens it, an inbox item): `{ question: string with {{values}}, fields?: [field] (≤12, no files; absent: yes or no — a field's label, help, placeholder, default and options are filled with values when it asks, and a choice's option that is one {{step.value}} alone gives that value's items: a list's items, or a text's lines, so a run can offer what it found, with multi: true to tick several; a choice left with no options becomes a text box), waitHours?: 1–720 (default 168, then it fails) }` — gives {{id.yes}}, or its fields' values (a multi choice's one option a line); answered with POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/answer; **code** — a program run in a sandbox (bubblewrap: no network, none of this computer's files but the app's packages): `{ language: javascript|typescript|python, code: string — the body of a function of input and ctx (async in JavaScript and TypeScript; await import() for packages; TypeScript runs as it is, its types stripped) that returns the step's values (≤50,000 chars), with?: { [name]: value or text with {{values}} } — input, outputs?: Outputs — the fields of the object it returns, seconds?: 1–120 (default 20; the time its ctx calls take is not counted) }` — gives the returned object's fields (another value: {{id.result}}) and {{id.printed}}. It reaches out only through ctx, each call a step of the run under it (path "<id>/c1", "<id>/c2"…), kept: after a restart the program runs again, and a call it made before gives what it gave then (a different call there: what was kept from there on is dropped). JavaScript and TypeScript: `await ctx.model(instructions, { with?, outputs?, model?, title? })`, `ctx.agent(instructions, { with?, outputs?, assistant?, agent?, model?, tier?, tools?, capUsd?, title? })`, `ctx.tool(name, inputs, { title?, cache? })` (tried again and kept as a tool step is), `ctx.ask(question, { with?, fields?, waitHours? })`, `ctx.app(address, inputs)`, `ctx.data.add(collection, record)`, `.get(collection, id)`, `.find(collection, { where?, search?, sort?, limit? })`, `.search(collection, query)`, `.update(collection, { id?, where?, set })`, `.remove(collection, { id?, where? })` — each gives the step's values, and throws when it fails; Python the same, without await, with_= for with; with's values go in the words by {{name}} (those not named come after them); at most 500 calls a program; `ctx.locale` is no call: `{ lang — the run's language ("el"), language — its English name, country — two letters, timeZone — IANA, currency — three letters, units — metric|imperial }` (a public page's run: the visitor's language, UTC and the rest empty), frozen; in Python `ctx.locale.lang` or `ctx.locale['lang']`; JavaScript's Intl has every language's data there (`new Intl.NumberFormat(ctx.locale.lang, { style: 'currency', currency: ctx.locale.currency })`); **value** — a value worked out from another, at once and at no cost: `{ of: "{{a value}}", op: first|last|count|sum|average|min|max|pick|join|unique|sort|number|date, arg?: string — pick: the field; sum, average, min, max: of this field of each; sort: by this field ("-price": highest first); join: between the items (default ", "; \n a new line); date: days to add }` — text goes by lines; gives {{id.value}}; **data** `{ op: add|get|find|search|update|remove (or the short way: add: "collection"), collection, with?: the record (add) or what to change (update), record?: one record's id, where?: { [field]: value or { "<": 10 } } (=, !=, <, >, <=, >=, contains, in, empty; {} for all), search?: words (find), query?: a question (search: the records that answer it best, by words and by meaning, as knowledge bases are searched), mode?: auto|words|meaning (search), sort?: field or -field, limit?: ≤1,000 (default 100; search ≤100, default 10) }` — add gives {{id.record}}, {{id.id}}; get {{id.record}}, {{id.found}}; find {{id.records}}, {{id.count}}, {{id.first}}; search {{id.records}} (best first, each with its score), {{id.count}}, {{id.first}}, {{id.meaning}} (whether meaning took part: search by meaning is on); update {{id.records}}, {{id.count}}; remove {{id.count}}. The knowledge bases and memory notes are searched with tool steps (knowledge_search, recall). **Values**: `{{inputs.fieldId}}` (or `{{fieldId}}`), `{{stepId.value}}`, `{{stepId.list[0].field}}`, `[-1]` for the last; the built-in values `{{today}}` (2026-11-02), `{{now}}` (2026-11-02 14:30), `{{weekday}}`, `{{timezone}}` (all in the user's time zone), `{{run.source}}` (web, test, api, mcp, public, batch, task, screen, chat, app, session) and `{{run.id}}`; the run's language, `{{language}}` (its English name, "Greek") and `{{lang}}` (its tag, "el"): the one its start gave (the page's language, a public page's visitor's, the one use_app was given), else About you's, else English; and About you, `{{me.home}}`, `{{me.country}}` (two letters), `{{me.units}}` (metric or imperial), `{{me.currency}}` (three letters), `{{me.diet}}`, `{{me.household}}` (a number), `{{me.services}}`, `{{me.teams}}`, `{{me.fields}}`, `{{me.topics}}`, `{{me.venues}}` (lists), `{{me.author}}` (a name), `{{me.author_id}}` (an ORCID or OpenAlex id), `{{me.compute}}`: as set or worked out, empty when there is nothing, and empty in a public page's run (an app's own step or question named me, language or lang keeps its name: `{{me.text}}` is then that step's); none of the built-in values is translated (`{{weekday}}` is Monday in every language); sections `{{#x}}…{{/x}}` (kept when x has a value), `{{^x}}…{{/x}}`. Lists go into text as lines, rows as a table. A **Rule** compares values (=, !=, <, >, <=, >=, contains, does not contain, is empty, is not empty, or a value alone: whether it is there), joined by and / or: `{{price}} < 100 and {{kind}} = bug`. **Outputs**: `{ [id]: kind }` with kinds text|long|number|yesno|date|url|list|table(column: kind, …)|ui(card name) — a card's data, as a ui answer field takes it (a model is told its type and an example), or a list like answer fields (≤30). At most 60 steps, 5 deep.
**Data**: `{ [collection]: { [field]: kind } | { fields?: Outputs, description?: string } | {} — any records }` (≤20 collections; each record has its own id, createdAt and updatedAt; 20,000 records in all, 64 KB each).
**Requires**: `{ node?: { [package]: version or range } (≤40), python?: ["pandas>=2", …] (≤40), pythonVersion?: "3.12", commands?: ["ffmpeg", …] — looked for, not installed, models?: [{ as: the name its steps use, agent: claude|codex|custom, provider?: openrouter|ollama|vllm|endpoint, model?: the provider's name for it, label? }] — said for you in an app's file (Custom's models with where they run and their provider's name), so another computer finds them }`. Packages install in the app's own folder, never for the whole computer: npm without the packages' install scripts, Python in a uv environment (python3 -m venv without uv); names and versions only, no links, git or options. Its agents get them first on PATH (VIRTUAL_ENV, NODE_PATH).
**Env**: `{ state: none|installing|ready|failed|outdated, missing: [command], log?: the end of the last install's output, error?, at? }`.
**Public**: `{ on?: boolean, model?: string — one of GET /api/apps/models (needed to turn on), web?: boolean — web search and page reading (default true), perIpPerHour?: number (default 5, ≤1000), runsPerDay?: number (default 50), spendPerDay?: number — US$ (default 2) }`.

- `POST /api/apps/draft` — drafts an app from a turn of a session with the helper model, without saving it: it reads the person's messages up to the reply (the one the work began with becomes the form and the task; those after it, corrections, go into the task), the reply with its ```ui cards (each becomes a ui answer field, ids from the cards' names: compare, compare_2…; its words beside them, when more than a line or two, a markdown field "summary") and the tools the agent called. Its first example, "From the session", holds this request's answers, with checks from the reply. If the helper model fails, the draft is the turn's request in one "Request" field. Body: `{ key: string — the session's key, claude:…|codex:…|acp:…, turn?: number — the reply to draft from, by its place in the session (its timeline item's seq); absent: the latest }`. Returns: `{ recipe: Recipe, note?: string — why it fell back, or what to check, parts?: [{ text: string, field?: string — the question whose answer takes the place of these words }] — the request in pieces, which joined give it exactly }`. 404 when that reply is not there; 409 for a confidential session.
- `POST /api/apps` — makes an app. Body: `{ recipe: Recipe (or its fields at the top level), mcp?: boolean — offered to other agents as a tool (default true), public?: Public, slug?: string — its address (default from the name, made unique), template?: string — the ready-made id it started from }`. Gives 400 saying what to fix: an empty prompt without steps, a flow whose values come later or name nothing, a rule that cannot be read, a tool a step cannot call on its own, a collection it does not keep, a package that cannot be installed safely; or public.on without a Custom model. The packages it requires start installing. Returns: `{ app: App, warnings: string[] — what may surprise, such as a value that is empty when a Decide goes the other way }`.
- `POST /api/apps/check` — checks a recipe without saving it, as POST does. Body: `{ recipe: Recipe }`. Returns: `{ errors: string[], warnings: string[] }`.
- `PUT /api/apps/:id` — changes an app. Body: as POST. The recipe is replaced whole, so GET it first and send every part (its flow, data, memory and requires too); a body with neither a prompt nor steps is refused. `public` merges into the current public settings. `mcp`, `agents`, `slug` and `template` keep their values when left out; a new `slug` changes the address. Returns: `{ app: App, warnings: string[] }`.
- `DELETE /api/apps/:id` — deletes an app with its runs, batches, keys, public page and the data it keeps (an app installed from a file, its page's Uninstall, too). Its active runs are stopped first; their sessions stay; it leaves the pinned apps. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/uninstall` — uninstalls a ready-made app (`tpl:<id>`): it leaves your apps and agents' tools, runs no more (409) and waits under Ready-made apps to install on the Apps page; its runs and records stay, its Update automatically task goes, and it leaves the pinned apps. Returns: `{ template, installed: false, agents }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/install` — installs a ready-made app (`tpl:<id>`) again. Body (the user's own pages only): `{ secrets?: { NAME: value }, allow?: [NAME] }` — keys it needs, saved to the vault. Returns: `{ template, installed: true, agents }`.
- `PUT /api/apps/:id/agents` — whether agents in your sessions may run an app (yours by id or slug, or `tpl:<id>`): its page's Agents can use it. Body: `{ on: boolean }`. Returns: `{ agents: boolean, made: boolean — it is made for that (when false, agents never get it) }`.
- `GET /api/apps/allowed` — the apps agents run for you without asking first (Settings → Apps → Run without asking), and the other apps agents may run, which could be added. Returns: `{ apps: [AppRef] — those that run without asking, others: [AppRef] — the rest }`, each `AppRef` `{ id — yours by id, ready-made ones as tpl:<id>, name, icon, color, own: boolean — one of yours }`.
- `PUT /api/apps/allowed/:id` — adds an app to those agents run without asking first, or takes it off so agents ask again on its card (yours by id, or ready-made as `tpl:<id>`). Adding needs an app agents can run (404 otherwise). Body: `{ allowed: boolean }`. Returns: the same as `GET /api/apps/allowed`.
- `GET /api/apps/me` — About you (Settings → Apps → About you): what apps start their questions from and their steps read as `{{me.…}}`. Returns: `{ me: Me — as the person set it, values: Me — with what is not set worked out, derived: { [field]: "timezone"|"country"|"app" } — the fields worked out and from what: the country from the time zone, units and currency from the country, the language from the one the app was last used in }`. A **Me** is `{ home?: string — a place as typed, country?: two letters, language?: a tag, units?: metric|imperial, currency?: three letters, diet?: string, household?: number (1–50), services?: [string], teams?: [string], fields?: [string], topics?: [string], venues?: [string], author?: { name, id? — an ORCID or OpenAlex id }, compute?: string }`.
- `PUT /api/apps/me` — changes About you: only the fields given; null or an empty value clears one (and a field that can be worked out is worked out again); a list may be given as text with commas, a language by its name. Agents are asked the person's OK each time. Body: part of a Me. Returns: as GET.

## App runs

- `POST /api/apps/:id/runs` — runs an app now, in the background (yours by id or slug, or ready-made as `tpl:<id>`). For the flows half of a web app, the run is recorded as acting for the person signed in (see Apps, from outside). Body: `{ flow?: string — another of its flows (absent or "run": the main one; its inputs are that flow's), inputs?: { [fieldId]: string|number|boolean } — checked against the form (text ≤2,000, long/file ≤60,000 characters; choice must match an option (a multi choice: each line one); dates YYYY-MM-DD; yes/no), files?: { [fileFieldId]: { name: string, data: base64 } } (≤25 MB each), agent?: claude|codex|acp, acpAgent?, model?, tier?: quick|standard|best|max — a tier in place of a model by name (a model given wins), effort?, mode? — ready-made apps only (default Claude Code, mode auto), recipe?: Recipe — test this recipe instead (:id "test" for one not saved), source?: "test"|"screen", pause?: string[] — a test run (with steps) pauses before these steps until told to go on (POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/go), freeze?: { from: runId, steps: string[] } — a test run's steps that give what they gave in that earlier run of the same app, without running again, secrets?: { NAME: value }, allow?: [NAME] — the user's own pages only: keys it needs, saved to the vault first (allow: saved keys that may now go to its hosts too), lang?: string — the language it writes in, a tag (the app's pages send theirs; absent: About you's, else English) }`. A question it leaves out (not sent; an empty answer stays empty) takes what it starts from (About you for `from`, the last answer for `remember`) before its default; the answers of a run from the app's form to questions with `remember` are kept for its next runs. Gives 400 saying what to fix in the form, 409 `{ error, secrets: [{ name, label, help?, url?, optional? }] }` when the flow it runs needs keys the vault does not have (the keys that flow names, and an optional key the flow signs its requests with, as the Annotation workbench's publish flow does with HF_TOKEN; the optional ones are listed too, to ask for together; a run started by an agent: tell the user to add them on its page, or run it with use_app, whose card asks), and 429 past the app's runs a day. Returns: `{ run: Run }`.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/runs` — an app's runs, newest first, 50 at a time (ready-made: `tpl:<id>`). Query: `before?: number — createdAt of the oldest run seen, to get older ones`. Returns: `{ runs: [Run] }`.
- `GET /api/apps/runs/:runId` — one run, with how it is going and its follow-ups. Returns: `{ run: Run, progress: { steps: [{ title, status: running|ok|error }] (last 8), said?, waiting?, own?: true — a run with steps: the titles are the app's own words, paused?: string[] — a test run paused before these steps (see …/go) }, recipe: { name, icon, color, fields, answer, agent, acpAgent, model, ui?: Ui — the view its answer shows in (its app's as it is now, a test's as it was tried; none for a run of another of its flows), assets?: { [name]: { sha, mime, bytes } } — its app's own files, steps?: true — its main flow works in steps }, slug: string|null, asks: [{ id, text, at, reply?, updated: boolean — handed in a new result }], first?: string — the reply to the task itself (a run with steps: the reply its steps gave; every change asked of it is in asks, its words alone), queued: [{ id, text, at }], noAsks?: string — why it takes no follow-ups now (a public page's run, a run of another of its flows, a run with steps still at work or that did not finish, its session gone or open in another program), steps?: [Step] — a run with steps: every step so far, with what it gave, question?: { step, question, fields? — the form as it was asked, with its options filled in, since } — an Ask a person step waiting for the answer }`. With a view, also `view: { … }` — what its view keeps for it (see Apps: views), `openApp?: { id, name, path, row: boolean }` — for a run of an agent app that has an app of its own (it is the flows half of a web app): the web app's id and name, and the address inside it that shows this run: the row the run was kept as (`row: true`, found by the run's `ref` or by the `run` column of the app's kept list) or the app's home (`/`). A session's card uses it for **Open the app**.
- `POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/ask` — a follow-up that refines the result. It goes to the run's session and waits its turn if the agent is busy; the agent hands in the fields that change, and those it leaves out stay. A finished run with steps gets its session with the first (again if that session was deleted): the app's agent with its tier (Standard when it has none), its tools, the run's folder and what is left of its spending cap, told what the app does and its steps (and to write in the run's language when the app writes in the person's), with `answers.json`, `result.json` (or `reply.md`) and `steps/<id>.json` (what each step gave) written in that folder (beside it, under the app's runs, when the app works in a folder of its own); its first message ends with the person's words, which `asks` lists alone. What the session spends adds to the run's `costUsd` (and what an earlier one spent stays when it was deleted). Body: `{ text: string — ≤20,000 characters }`. Gives 409 for public-page runs, runs not started yet, runs whose session is gone or open in another program, and runs with steps that are still at work, did not finish or ran another of the app's flows. Returns: `{ run: Run }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/examples/run` — tries its examples (or those named): a test run each, whose checks are read when it ends. Body: `{ recipe?: Recipe — try this instead of the app as saved (the builder's, unsaved; :id may be "test"), only?: string[] — example ids, trial?: boolean — a change tried before it is used (Firm it up): its runs' example is "trial:<id>", and they are not the examples' last try }`. Returns: `{ runs: [{ example, run?: Run, error?: string — why it could not start }] }`. A run that tried an example has `example`, and once it has ended `checks: [{ rule, ok, why? — what it saw }]`.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/examples` — a saved app's examples, each with its last run. Returns: `{ examples: [{ id, name?, flow?, inputs, checks?, last?: Run }] }`.
- `GET /api/apps/schema.json` — the JSON Schema of an app's app.json (its folder's), for an editor's completion and checks.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/folder` — its folder of files, if it has one. Returns: `{ folder: { path, error? — the mistake in it, which changed nothing } | null, suggested: string — where one would go }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/folder` — makes its folder and keeps it in step with the app: each change saved there is a version by "folder", a save here writes it again. Body: `{ path?: string — an empty folder, or one of this app's (default: Hydor/Apps/<address> in the home folder) }`. Returns: `{ app, folder }`. The folder: app.json (the recipe, with { file } for texts kept in files), instructions/<step>.md, steps/<step>.js|py (export default async function step(input) { … } / def step(input):), screens/<name>.html, ui/<element>.html and .css and ui/style.css (its view's elements and style), graph.json, .naget/app.schema.json and step.d.ts.
- `DELETE /api/apps/:id/folder` — stops keeping it in step; the files stay. Returns: `{ app }`.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/versions` — its versions, newest first: each save that changed what it does (not where its steps sit in the graph, nor its screens). Returns: `{ versions: [{ n, at, by: "you"|"agent"|"folder"|"install", line — what changed (or the why of the save), examples?: { tried, passed, seconds, costUsd } — its examples tried with it as saved: the latest run of each, and a run's average time and cost }] }`. A run's `version` is the one it ran (none: a try of changes not saved). A save takes `why` (≤300) for its version's line.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/versions/:n` — one version, whole. Returns: `{ version: { n, at, by, line }, recipe: Recipe }`; save the recipe (PUT /api/apps/:id) to bring it back as the newest version.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/advice` — what its latest runs (the last 12) say of its steps. Returns: `{ rescued: [{ step, title, runs, of — in how many of the runs read, error — why it failed the last time, run — that run }] — steps that failed and had an agent do their work, firm: [{ step, title, runs, calls: string[] — the tools its agent called each time, in order, steps: [Step] — fixed steps to put in its place (a tool step for each call, then a model step that keeps its id), agent: { seconds, costUsd } — what its agent took a run, on average, inputs: [object] — the answers of the runs it was seen in }] — agent steps that did the same in each of their last runs (at least 3) }`. Put the steps in with the app's flow and try them (examples/run with trial, or a test run) before saving them.
- `POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/answer` — answers the run's waiting Ask a person step, and the run goes on. Body: `{ step?: string — its path, as GET gives it in question.step, yes?: boolean — for a yes or no, values?: { [fieldId]: answer } — for its form; a multi choice takes a list of options or text with one a line }`. Gives 409 when nothing waits for an answer there, 400 when the answer does not fit. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/again` — runs a run with steps again from one of its steps: the same answers, the app as it is now, and the steps before that one giving what they gave in this run (marked `reused` in its steps, at no cost). Or, with `inputs` (what a view's again does), runs any app's run again from the start with some answers changed: the others as they were, its files along. Body: `{ from?: string — a step's id in the main list of steps (default: the first: all run again), recipe?: Recipe — run this instead of the app as it is (a test from the builder; the run is a test), inputs?: { [fieldId]: answer } — the answers that change }`. Gives 409 while it still runs (from a step) or when a file it had is gone, 400 when the app has no such step or question now, or (from a step) the run had files. Returns: `{ run }`.
- `POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/stop` — stops a run: one waiting in line ends at once, a working one is interrupted.
- `POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/go` — a test run paused before a step (its progress has `paused: [stepPath]`, its status is waiting) goes on. Body: `{ step?: boolean — run one step, then pause before the next }`. Returns: `{ ok: true }`; 409 when it is not paused. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `DELETE /api/apps/runs/:runId` — removes an ended run from the lists, with its steps (409 while it is going); sessions stay. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Apps: keys and files

An app names the keys it needs (`secrets` in its recipe), never their values: the user gives each one on its page, on the card that installs it, or on its card in a session (use_app asks for those the vault lacks), and it goes to the vault under that name with the hosts the app gives (a key the vault has keeps the user's own hosts). Its runs are told each key's name and how to use it (http_request `auth: NAME`, the browser, run_with_secrets). An app's own files (`assets`) are kept by the app and show by name as `asset:<name>`; the files an answer points at are kept too. Both are served at `/app-media/<sha>/<name>`, an address nobody can guess, which views (in their sandboxed frame) and public pages can reach without the sign-in; anything but a picture, video, sound or PDF downloads instead of opening.

- `GET /api/apps/:id/secrets` — the keys it needs and how the vault has them (yours, or `tpl:<id>`). Returns: `{ secrets: [SecretState] }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/secrets` — saves values for the keys it needs (the user's pages only: agents are refused, as for any key's value). Body: `{ values?: { NAME: value } — only its own keys are taken, allow?: [NAME] — saved keys that may now go to its hosts too (and to commands, when it needs that) }`. Returns: `{ secrets: [SecretState] }`.
- `POST /api/apps/assets` — keeps a file for an app being edited, without changing any app (its Edit saves the entry with the app). Body: `{ name: string — a file name, no folders (≤100), data?: base64 (≤100 MB), url?: https:// or data: address, caption? }`. Returns: `{ name, asset: { sha, mime, bytes, caption? } }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/assets` — adds one of its files, or replaces the one with that name, and saves the app as a new version. Body: as above. Gives 400 for a name that cannot be one or past 100 files, 413 past 100 MB, or 150 MB for all its files. Returns: `{ app: App }`.
- `DELETE /api/apps/:id/assets/:name` — removes one of its files from it (a new version; earlier versions keep theirs). Returns: `{ app: App }`.

## Apps: what they keep

An app with `data` keeps collections of records between its runs: its steps (Keep data), its agents (with their app_data tool), its view and these routes share them. A ready-made app keeps them too, under `tpl:<id>` (Paper radar's saved papers, `/api/apps/tpl:paper-radar/data/saved`; the Annotation workbench's `items`; the Lab notebook's `entries`), while it is installed: its own page reads and changes them. Fields with kinds are checked and tidied (numbers, dates, yes or no); unknown fields are refused. The Reading list's papers are `GET /api/apps/tpl:reading-list/data/papers` (fields title, authors, year, venue, doi, arxiv, url, pdf, abstract, bibtex, key, tags, status — To read, Reading or Read —, notes, project — one of your projects' id, or the words given when they name none —, project_name — its name, to show —, added, read_on, zotero_key, zotero_version), and its syncs with Zotero `…/data/syncs`.

- `GET /api/apps/:id/data` — the collections it keeps. Returns: `{ collections: [{ name, count, fields: [field] | null, description: string | null }], limits: { collections, records, record, find } }`.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/data/:collection` — records, oldest first; or with `ask`, those that answer a question best; or, with `format`, a file to download.
