Query: `q?: words anywhere in a record, where?: JSON — { "field": value, "other": { "<": 10 } } (=, !=, <, >, <=, >=, contains, in, empty), sort?: field or -field, limit?: ≤1,000 (default 100; 1,000 for a download), format?: csv|jsonl — the records as a CSV file (a row each) or JSON lines (one object a line), as an attachment, fields?: a,b,c — the fields to put in it (default: the collection's own), name?: the file's name`; or `ask: a question — by words (BM25) and by meaning (each record's vector, when search by meaning is on), joined by rank, mode?: auto|words|meaning, limit?: ≤100 (default 10)`. Returns: `{ records: [{ id, createdAt, updatedAt, …fields }], count — how many match in all }`; with ask, each record has its `score`, best first, and `meaning: boolean` says whether meaning took part.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/data/:collection` — adds a record. Body: `{ record: { …fields, id?: string — keeps this id (letters, digits, - and _) } }`. Gives 400 naming a field it does not have or a value that does not fit, 409 for an id it has, 413 past 64 KB or 20,000 records. Returns: `{ record }`.
- `PATCH /api/apps/:id/data/:collection/:recordId` — changes a record: the fields in `set` replace its own; null clears one. Body: `{ set: { …fields } }`. Returns: `{ record }`.
- `DELETE /api/apps/:id/data/:collection/:recordId` — removes a record. Returns: `{ ok: true, removed: 0|1 }`.
- `DELETE /api/apps/:id/data/:collection` — removes every record of a collection. Returns: `{ ok: true, removed: number }`.

## Apps: screens

An app's screen is a page of its own (Pages keeps it with its versions, and leaves it out of the library) that its page shows in place of its form. It is HTML as any page (`<body class="n-page">`, the kit's classes and colours) and runs with `window.hydor.app` for its app (`window.naget.app`, its name before the rename, works too):

- `hydor.app.flows` — `[{ id, title, inputs: [field], outputs: [{ id, label }] }]` ("run" is the main one); `hydor.app.collections` — `[{ name, fields }]`.
- `hydor.app.run(flow = "run", inputs)` — the run as it starts `{ id, status, … }`; `hydor.app.wait(id)` — `{ run, steps }` once it has ended; `hydor.app.get(id)`; `hydor.app.runs({ flow?, limit? })` — its latest runs.
- `hydor.app.data(name)` — `.find({ where?, search?, sort?, limit? })` → `{ records, count }`; `.search(question, { mode?, limit? })` → the records that answer it best, by words and by meaning; `.add(record)`; `.update(id, set)`; `.remove(id)`; `.watch(fn)` — called when the list changes.
- `hydor.app.on("run", fn)` — each change of its runs; `hydor.app.on("data", fn)` — a list's name when it changes.
- `hydor.app.chat(text, id?)` — a message to its agent: a new conversation, or more of the one named; `hydor.app.conversation(id)`; both give `{ id, status, working, messages: [{ from: you|agent, text, queued? }], error? }`. `hydor.app.chats()` — its latest conversations `[{ id, at, first, status }]`.
- App parts, elements that use it: `<n-form flow="run" submit="Words" keep>` (the flow's inputs as a form that runs it; the page hears an `n-run` event with `{ run, steps }`), `<n-button flow="name" inputs='{"field":"value"}' plain>Words</n-button>`, `<n-result flow="run">` (its latest answer, live), `<n-runs flow="run" limit="5">`, `<n-table data="list" columns="a,b" edit="aYesNoField" search remove where='{"read":false}' sort="-createdAt" limit="100">` (records, live), `<n-count data="list" where='{…}'>`, `<n-chat placeholder="Words" hint="Words" fresh>` (a conversation with its agent: the latest one unless `fresh`, with a box to write in, New conversation and Earlier conversations).

- `GET /api/apps/:id/screens` — its screens. Returns: `{ screens: [{ name, page, title, version }] }`.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/screens/:name` — one screen with its HTML. Returns: `{ screen: { name, page, title, version, html } }`.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/chats` — its conversations with its agent (a screen's `<n-chat>`), newest first, 20 at most. Each is a run of its own (kept apart from its runs): read it with `GET /api/apps/runs/:runId` (its first message is `run.inputs.message`, the replies `first` and `asks`), go on with it with `POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/ask`. Returns: `{ chats: [{ id, at, first — its first message, status }] }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/chats` — starts a conversation with its agent: its agent, model, tools, records and memory, and an `app_run` tool to run its flows and wait for what they give. Counts against its runs a day. Body: `{ text: string — the first message, ≤20,000 characters }`. Returns: `{ run: Run }` (source `chat`).
- `POST /api/apps/:id/screens` — gives it a screen: the HTML given, or one made from its parts (its form, its answer, a table for each list it keeps and a conversation with its agent when it keeps one, its runs). Body: `{ name?: string — "main" (the default) opens on its page, title?: string, html?: string — ≤2 MB }`. Gives 409 when it has a screen of that name. Returns: `{ screen, app: App }`.
- `PUT /api/apps/:id/screens/:name` — a new version of a screen (the earlier ones stay, in Pages). Body: `{ html: string, title?: string }`. Returns: `{ screen }`.
- `DELETE /api/apps/:id/screens/:name` — removes a screen and its page. Returns: `{ app: App }`.

## Apps: views

An app's view (`ui` in its recipe) is how its answer looks: pages of blocks, elements of its own that its values fill each time they show, and events, which say what a click does. A run's page shows the run's answer in it (**Plain answer** shows the card and tables instead); pages with `on: "app"` show on the app's own page in place of its form. A view draws in the sandbox pages use, with the kit's classes and colours (`var(--n-card)`, `var(--n-accent)`…), light or dark as the app is. A save checks it and says what to fix; app_try says which names it uses that a try's answer did not give, and with `look: true` gives pictures of the first finished try's answer in it (on a computer in the dark and in the light, and on a phone in the dark) where this computer has a Chromium to draw them with.

**Ui**: `{ pages: [Page] (≤12), elements?: { [name]: { html: string — a template (≤40,000), css?: string — its style: rules for what is inside it, & for the element itself (≤20,000), description?: string } } (≤40; a name in small letters, digits and -, not a built-in's), events?: { [name]: [Action] (≤8) } (≤60), style?: string — CSS for the whole view (@keyframes, fonts, classes several share), widgets?: { [name]: Widget } (≤12; names apart from its elements and the built-ins), libs?: { [specifier]: exact version } (≤16) — npm packages its widgets import, loaded from jsDelivr: "three": "0.170.0", "chart.js/auto": "4.4.6", a stylesheet as its path ("leaflet/dist/leaflet.css": "1.9.4"); a package's other modules by path ("three/examples/jsm/controls/OrbitControls.js") come from the same version }`.
**Widget**: `{ code: string — an ES module (≤60,000) whose default export draws it: export default function (el, value, view) { … }; el: the element to draw in, value: what fills it (the block's data, as for an element), view: { with — the block's with, theme: { accent, fg, muted, line, card, soft, bg, dark } (rgb() colours), lang, asset(name) → its file's address, media(src) → an address as it shows, event(name, item?) — fires one of the view's events ({{event.*}} is the item's fields), set(name, value) — a value of the view's state, state, resize() }; it may return a function that cleans up, css?: string — its style (& for itself), description?, height?: CSS length — its height until it draws (default 320px) }`. A block places one as it places an element (`element: "<widget>"`), a template with `<n-widget name="<widget>" value="{{result.points|json}}"></n-widget>`. Its code runs in the view's sandbox: it reaches the app only through view.event and view.set.
**Page**: `{ id, title — its tab, on?: run|app (default run), tab?: false — no tab of its own, opened by an event, if?: Value — shown only when that is there, blocks: [Block] (≤40) }`.
**Block**: `{ title?, element?: one of its elements or widgets, or a built-in: text|markdown|list|checklist (ticks kept with the run)|table|chart (bars)|image|video|audio|map|plot|answer (the plain card)|form|runs|chat (the app's own page) (no element draws a card field yet: the plain card does), html?: a template for this place, text?: Markdown with {{values}} — one of element, html and text, data?: Value — what fills it ("result.days", "inputs", "data.items", "state.picked", "page.item", "last.result"), each?: boolean — drawn once for each item, where?: { field: value | { "<"|">"|"<="|">="|"!="|"contains": value } } — an empty value matches all, sort?: field|-field, limit?: number, layout?: list|grid|row, width?: full|half|third|two-thirds, empty?: text — when there is nothing, if?: Value, with?: { columns?, label?, value?, flow?, limit?, placeholder?, submit? } — a built-in's settings }`.
**Media elements** take what fills them as addresses (`https://…`, `/app-media/…`, `asset:<name>`), media items `{ src, caption? }`, or rows with the field named in `with.src` (and `with.caption`): `image` (`with: { columns?: 1–6, height?: "180px", fit?: cover|contain }`; a click opens it large), `video` (a file, or a YouTube or Vimeo link, shown as its still and played over the page on a click: their players cannot run in the sandbox; `with: { poster? }`), `audio`. `map` draws rows with a latitude and longitude (`with: { lat?, lng? — the fields (lat/latitude, lng/lon/longitude unless named), label?, popup?, line?: true|"dashed" — joined in order, numbered?: true, zoom?, height?, tiles? — https://…{z}/{x}/{y} for other tiles, attribution?, event? — fired with the place clicked, empty? }`; OpenFreeMap's map by default, drawn by MapLibre, light or dark with the app). `plot` draws rows with Plotly (`with: { kind: line|bar|hbar|scatter|area|pie|donut|histogram|box|heatmap|scatter3d|line3d|surface, x?, y?: field or [fields], z?, series? — one trace per value of a field, stacked?: true (bar), title?, xLabel?, yLabel?, zLabel?, height? — hbar's grows with its rows when unset, event? — fired with the row clicked }`; hbar draws the first row on top and puts long names on two or three lines), or a Plotly figure `{ data, layout }` (or a grid of numbers for a surface or heatmap) as its value. Templates may place them too: `<n-map value="{{result.stops|json}}" lat="lat" lng="lng" line>`, `<n-plot value="{{result.costs|json}}" kind="bar" x="day" y="cost">`. They load Leaflet, MapLibre and Plotly from jsDelivr, so they need the device online.
**Templates**: HTML with `{{name}}` (escaped), `{{#name}}…{{/name}}` (for each item of a list; once for a record, or a value that is there), `{{^name}}…{{/name}}` (when it is empty) and `{{name|format|format:arg}}`. Names: `result.<answer field>`, `inputs.<question>`, `run.status`, `run.createdAt`, `run.costUsd`, `run.misses` (what its steps could not have: `[{ step, what?, why, times? }]`), `run.missed` (how many), `app.name`, `state.<name>` (the view's own values), `page.item` (what its page was opened with), `data.<list>` (the records it keeps), `last.result` (on the app's own page: its latest run), `today`, `now`; in an element drawn for an item, the item's fields by name, `{{.}}` or `{{item}}` for the item, and `@index`, `@number`, `@first`, `@last`, `@key` (its id, else its place in the list, as in `state.done.@key`). Formats: date, shortdate, longdate, day, month, weekday (weekday:short: Fri), year, time, ago, number, percent, plus:n, times:n, upper, lower, slug, initials, short:n, host, url, count, first, last, take:n, join:sep, split:sep, unique:field, sort:field, where:field=value (or !=), sum:field, is:x, not:x, has:x, over:n, under:n, empty, or:text, yesno, markdown, lines, json; an argument that starts with $ is a value (`where:topic=$state.topic`). A **Value** is a name with formats, with ! in front for "not there" (`result.flags|count|over:2`). `data-event="name"` on anything makes a click on it the event (a form's submit, a field's change, a search box as it is typed in), and `data-key` on it names the item `@key` stands for. `asset:<name>` in a `src`, `href` or `poster` shows one of the app's own files. Scripts, frames and `on…` attributes are left out (a widget is the way to run code).
**Action**: `{ do, confirm?: text — asked first; no stops the event }` and one of: `page` (`page`, `item?`), `back`, `set` (`name`, `value`, `keep?`), `toggle` (`name`, `keep?`), `say` (`text`), `copy` (`text`), `open` (`url`: an address of this app, or https://…), `ask` (`text`: a follow-up to the run's agent, as POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/ask; a run with steps once it is done), `run` (`flow`, `inputs?`, `as?`: its run as `state.<as>`, with `working`, `status`, `result`, `error`), `again` (`inputs`: the answers that change; a new run, its files along, which opens), `start` (`app`: an app's id or address, or `tpl:<id>` for a ready-made one; `inputs`; it opens), `experiments` (`inputs`: `items`, a list of rows with `what` and any of `why`, `kind`, `effort`, `point`, as `"{{result.asks}}"`; `source`: `reviewers`, `rebuttal` or `paper`; `paper?`; `venue?`: the start page opens with a request to run them, not sent, in the repository or folder the Research sprint or Sweep planner was last given; with `source: paper` the request is to try one paper's idea on the person's code and measure it: one item, its `what` the paper's title and `why` a note on what it means for them, and `paper` the paper's link), `data` (`op`: add|update|remove, `collection`, `record?`, `id?`). Their texts and values take names: `{{item}}` (what the element was filled with), `{{event.x}}` (the clicked element's `data-x`, a form's field `x`, a field's `{{event.value}}`, and `{{event.text}}`, what it says on screen, in the app's language) and every name a template takes; one `{{name}}` alone keeps its value as it is (a list stays a list). `keep` keeps a value with the run (or the app's own page). On a public page a view keeps only page, back, set, toggle, say, copy and open, and keeps nothing.

- `PUT /api/apps/runs/:runId/view` — keeps one value of a run's view (what `keep` keeps: a tick, a value an event set). Body: `{ name: string — up to four parts of letters, digits, - and _, joined by dots ("done.3"), value: any JSON | null — null removes it }`. Gives 400 for a name that cannot be one, 413 past 64 KB or 500 values. Returns: `{ view: { …what it keeps } }`.
- `PUT /api/apps/:id/view` — keeps one value of its own page's view (its pages with on: "app"), yours or a ready-made one (`tpl:<id>`, Explore's stories read). Body: as above. Gives 400 when its view has no page of its own. Returns: `{ view }`.
- `PUT /api/apps/:id/home` — the settings of an app that opens on its own page (yours, or `tpl:<id>` for a ready-made one such as Explore, the Weekly progress report or the Lab notebook): the answers its runs from there take, and Update automatically, a scheduled task (origin `daily:<id>`, listed with the others) that runs it with them at a time, every day or on some days of the week. Body: `{ inputs?: { question: answer } — only its form's questions are kept, daily?: { on?: boolean, time?: "HH:MM", days?: [0–6] | null — 0 is Sunday: [1,2,3,4,5] on weekdays, [5] once a week on Fridays; null every day } }`. Gives 400 when it has no page of its own, for a time or days that are not ones, or for turning it on while a question it needs is empty (or it needs a file each time); 503 without scheduled tasks. Returns: `{ home: Home }`.

## Apps: as a file

An app travels as one file: a .json, or a .hydor-app (a zip of its app.json, data/ with its records, assets/ with its own files, and apps/ with the apps its steps call, their files in apps/<address>/). In a .json its files are base64 (`files: { name: base64 }`). Its screens go in as their HTML (`screens: { name: { title, html } }`) and come back as pages of the app installed. A ready-made app's file says which it is (`builtin: id`). Its folder, API keys and public page stay behind; the keys it needs travel by name only.

- `GET /api/apps/:id/export` — an app as a file to keep or share (yours or `tpl:<id>`), with the apps its steps call. Query: `as?: json (default: a .hydor-app zip), data?: 1 — its records too (zip)`. Returns: the file; as=json its package `{ naget: "app", format: 1, slug, app: Recipe, records?, apps?: [package], files?: { name: base64 }, builtin?: id }`.
- `POST /api/apps/install/check` — what an app from a file would do here, before anything is made. Body: `{ file: { name, data: base64 } } — a .json, .hydor-app or .zip (≤160 MB)` or `{ package: a package or a bare Recipe }`. Returns: `{ preview: { name, description, icon, color, category?, summary: { asks: [{ label, optional }], gets: [string] — its view's pages or its answer's fields, inView, how: [{ kind: one|tool|model|agent|code|parallel|repeat|decide|ask|app|data, tool? }], steps, sessions: boolean — made to be run by agents in sessions, ownPage: boolean }, steps, agents: [string], tools: string[] | null, apps: [called app's address], data: [{ name, records }], memory, limits, needs: { node: [string], python: [string], commands: [string], missing: [command not on this computer] }, models: [{ as, agent: claude|codex|custom, label, status: here — set up here as it is | matched — the same model here under another name | missing, use: what it becomes unless you pick ("" : the agent's default, or the helper model for a model step), options: [{ value, label }] — models here that could stand in }], bundled: [{ name, from — its address where it was made, installs: new|reuse }], secrets: [SecretState] — the keys it (and what comes with it) needs, files: [{ name, mime, bytes }] — its own files, view: { widgets: [name], libs: ["three 0.170.0"] } — what its view runs of its own, builtin?: { id, installed } — a ready-made app's file (installed: it installs as a copy), notes: [what changes on the way in: its folder left out, a mode that skips asking set to Auto, an assistant that is not here, its view's scripts, a ready-made app coming back], warnings: [string], problems: [why it cannot be installed] } }`.
- `POST /api/apps/install` — installs it as the check showed: the apps that come with it first (or the same one you have), then it, with its records; its steps call the others at the addresses they got here, and the models picked; its public page is closed and it has no keys. The packages each requires start installing. Body: as the check, plus `models?: { "agent:as": a model here, or "" for the default }, agents?: boolean — agents in your sessions may run it (default true), secrets?: { NAME: value }, allow?: [NAME] — the user's own pages only: keys it needs, saved to the vault`. Its own files are kept, under their names. Gives 400 with the problems. Returns: `{ app: App, notes: [string], records: number, skipped: number — records that did not fit, bundled: [{ name, slug, reused }] }`; a ready-made app's file where that app was uninstalled installs it again instead: `{ builtin: id, notes, records: 0, skipped: 0, bundled: [] }`.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/env` — how what it needs stands on this computer. Returns: `{ env: Env, requires: Requires | null }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/env` — installs its packages again (after a failure, or afresh). Returns: `{ env: Env }`; runs started meanwhile wait for it.

## Apps: for each row of a CSV

- `GET /api/apps/:id/estimate` — a rough cost for a number of runs, from the median of the last 30 finished runs. Query: `rows: number (1–500)`. Returns: `{ rows, perRun: number|null, basis: runs|none, total: number|null, cap: number|null, most: number|null — cap × rows, from: number — how many runs it is based on }`.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/batches` — runs your app once per row (your apps only, not ready-made). Body: `{ rows: [{ [fieldId]: value }] (1–500; a file field takes a path or web address), concurrency?: number (1–6, default 2), name?: string (≤120, e.g. the CSV's name) }`. Rows that don't fit the form fail at once with the reason. Gives 429 if the rows would pass the app's runs a day. Returns: `{ batch: { id, app, status: running|done|stopped, total, done, failed, concurrency, name?, createdAt, endedAt? } }`.
- `GET /api/apps/:id/batches` — your app's last 30 batches. Returns: `{ batches: [Batch] }`.
- `GET /api/apps/batches/:batchId` — a batch and its runs, in row order. Returns: `{ batch: Batch, runs: [Run] }`.
- `POST /api/apps/batches/:batchId/stop` — stops a batch: waiting rows end and running ones are interrupted. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Apps: API keys

- `GET /api/apps/:id/keys` — your app's API keys, never their values. Returns: `{ keys: [{ id, name, hint — first and last characters, createdAt, lastUsed? }] }`.
- `DELETE /api/apps/:id/keys/:keyId` — revokes a key; programs using it stop at once. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Apps, from outside (an app's key)

For a program that is not signed in to Hydor: another server, a script, or a web app that Hydor keeps running. It holds the key of one app (`Authorization: Bearer napp_…`, made on the app's **API & sharing** tab, or by Hydor for a web app's flows half) and may start that app's runs, follow them and answer what they ask; the key opens that app and nothing else. Every call counts toward 30 a minute for the key. Starting a run also counts toward the app's runs a day (429 past it) and the run is held to the app's own cap on what it may spend; a key that belongs to a web app's flows half is held to what that web app may spend in a day as well (its **dailyCapUsd** setting: what its runs have spent today, plus what those still going may spend at the most, 429 past it). A signed-in person may use the same routes without a key. Errors say what is wrong in `{ error }`: 401 for a key that is not valid or opens another app, 404 for an app that is not there (“There is no app with that address”) and a different 404 for a run that is not there (“The app … has no run …”), 400 for what the body or query gets wrong, 429 for the limits.

**Run**, as every route here gives it: `{ id, run — the same id, flow — the flow it runs, "run" for the app's main one, status: queued|starting|running|waiting|done|failed|stopped — Hydor's own word, as it has always been (waiting: it is stopped on a question, an approval or an answer for a person), state: waiting|working|needs-you|done|failed|stopped — the same moment in plainer words, the one a new program should read: waiting has not started (queued, starting), working is going (running), needs-you is stopped on a question for a person (waiting; see question), done, failed and stopped are the same, person?: { id, name, role: owner|admin|member|guest, email?, teamId? } | null — who it acts for: present only for a run the web app started with its own key, the person its `personToken` proved, or null when it started with no token (a job of the web app, nobody at the screen), absent for any other run; for a run started on Hydor's own pages for the flows half of a web app it is the person signed in to Hydor (`{ id: "owner", name, role: "owner" }`, the same shape), ref?: string — the short reference of the caller's own the run was started with, inputs — what it was started with, steps?: [{ step — the step's path, its id, inside a Repeat "loop#2/read", title, status: waiting|working|done|failed|skipped|stopped — working also while a step waits for a person, error?, costUsd?, startedAt?, endedAt? }] — for an app with steps, as far as they have gone (a step shows once it starts); each call an agent made to one of the web app's own functions is a step too (`fn#1`, `fn#2`…, titled "Called find_trips for Ada", failed with the app's sentence when it refused), whether or not the app has steps (see Web apps: An app's functions as its agents' tools), result: { [answerFieldId]: value } | null — the answer fields once the run has them (a card field as the card's data), reply: string | null — the agent's last words, error?: string — why a run failed, in a sentence (also for a run that failed without a reason), costUsd?: number — what it cost, in US dollars, question?: { step, title — what it asks, kind: form|yesno, fields?: [{ id, label, type: text|long|number|date|choice|yesno|file, required?, help?, placeholder?, options?, multi?, default? }] — the form to fill, kind form only, since }, only while state is needs-you and the question is one a program may answer (an Ask a person step; a run that waits on an approval or a question in its session has none, and is answered on its page), session: { key, url } | null — its session, misses? — as in a Run above, partial? — as in a Run above, createdAt, startedAt?, endedAt? — in ms since 1970 }`. An app without steps has no `steps`.

- `POST /api/apps/:slug/run` — starts a run, in the background, and gives it back as it is at that moment (or once it has ended or `wait` seconds have passed). Body: `{ inputs?: { [fieldId]: value } — the form's answers (or at the top level, beside the options), files?: { [fileFieldId]: { name, data: base64 } }, flow?: string — another of the app's flows, by name ("run" or none: the main one; its inputs are that flow's), personToken?: string — who the run acts for, proven and not claimed: the value of the signed `x-hydor-person` header exactly as the web app received it from Hydor with the request of the person at its screen (Hydor made it with that web app's secret, and it runs out after a minute). Hydor checks its signature with that web app's secret and that it has not run out (it may be up to two minutes late, as the web app forwards it a moment after it got it), and records the person it names on the run (shown on its page, found by `GET …/run?person=`). Only for an app that is the flows half of a web app (400 otherwise) and only with that web app's own key (403 with another key); a token that is not that web app's, was changed or has run out is 403 saying which, and starts nothing. A run started with that web app's key and no token (a job of the web app, with nobody at the screen) acts for nobody, and its `person` is null. A bare `person` object is refused (400): a person cannot be claimed, only proven. A run started with no key (the person signed in to Hydor, by the same route) for such an app acts for that person. ref?: string — a short reference of your own, one line of at most 120 characters (400 otherwise), such as the id of the row of your app the run belongs to: kept with the run, given back as `ref` in every shape of it and usable to list the runs by it. wait?: number — seconds to wait for the run to end, 0 by default (answer at once), at most 900; a run still going after them comes back as it is }`. Returns: Run; 400 saying what to fix in the form or that the app has no such flow, 409 `{ error }` when the flow needs a key the vault does not have, 429 past the limits above.
- `GET /api/apps/:slug/flows` — describes the app's flows, for the pages of a web app that start them, so those pages need not repeat the form and the answer by hand. Returns: `{ app: { slug, name, description }, flows: [{ id — what `flow` is when a run is started: "run" for the main flow, else its name, name — its title, form: [{ id, label, type: text|long|number|date|choice|yesno|file, required?, help?, placeholder?, options?, multi?, default? }] — its questions in order, answer: [{ id, label, type: text|markdown|number|yesno|date|url|list|table|media|ui, required?, description?, columns?: [{ id, label, type }] }] — the fields of its `result` }] }`. The main flow's form is the app's form and its answer the app's answer fields; another flow has its own form, and gives the answer fields its `outputs` name (all of the app's when it names none). Counts toward the key's 30 a minute, like every call here. 404 when the app is not there.
- `GET /api/apps/:slug/run` — the runs started with a key (not those started on Hydor's own pages), newest first. Query: `limit?: number — 1 to 100, default 20, person?: string — only runs that act for this person id, ref?: string — only runs started with this `ref`, before?: number — createdAt of the oldest run you have, to get older ones`. Returns: `{ runs: [Run] }`, each in brief: with no `inputs`, `steps`, `question` or `partial`, and `result` null (read one run for them).
- `GET /api/apps/:slug/run/:runId` — one run of the app (also one started on Hydor's own pages). Query: `wait?: number — seconds to wait for it to end (0 by default, at most 900; a run still going comes back as it is)`. Returns: Run; 404 for a run that is not one of the app's.
- `GET /api/apps/:slug/run/:runId/events` — follows one run as it goes, without asking again and again: server-sent events (`text/event-stream`) that close when the run ends. `event: run` first, with the whole Run as it is; `event: step` with `{ step }` (as in a Run's steps) each time a step starts or changes how it stands, not for those already as the first event showed them; `event: status` with `{ status, state, question? }` (both words, as in a Run) each time the status changes or a new question is asked; `event: end` with the whole Run when it has ended, after which Hydor closes the stream (a run that had ended already gets `run` and `end` at once). A line `: keep-alive` goes by every 15 seconds. A stream is closed after 30 minutes with `event: timeout` and `{ "reconnect": true }`: open it again, it starts with the whole run again. A key may follow 8 runs at once (429 past that). Behind a proxy that cuts quiet connections, the keep-alives hold it. Returns: the stream; 404 and 401 as above.
- `POST /api/apps/:slug/run/:runId/answer` — answers the question a run whose state is needs-you waits on, checked as it is on the run's own page. Body: `{ values?: { [fieldId]: value } — for a form, yes?: boolean — for a yes or no, step?: string — the question's step, to be sure it is the one asked }`. Query: `wait?: number — seconds to wait for the run to end afterwards`. Returns: Run, as it is a moment after the answer (no longer on the same question); 400 `Check the answer: …` for values that do not fit the form, 409 when nothing waits for an answer now or it is not ready for it yet (try again), 403 for a run that was not started with a key.
- `POST /api/apps/:slug/run/:runId/stop` — stops a run that has not ended; a waiting one ends at once, a working one is interrupted. Returns: Run (up to 5 seconds after, so usually stopped); 409 when it has ended already, 403 for a run that was not started with a key.
- `POST /api/apps/:slug/embed` — vectors for a web app's search by meaning (a web app that lists `embed` in its hydor.json), made with the model that memory and the search box use, on this computer's processor. Only for such an app, with the key Hydor made for it (the flows half's own key; a web app with no flows half is started with its own slug as `HYDOR_APP_SLUG` and a key that opens this route, the model route and the mail route below and nothing else: `HYDOR_URL`, `HYDOR_APP_SLUG` and `HYDOR_APP_KEY`); any other key is refused (403), a key that is not valid 401. Body: `{ texts: string[] — 1 to 64, each at most 8,000 characters (413 past that), kind: "query" | "document" — the model's own prefixes are put on for a question and for a passage }`. Returns: `{ model — the model's name and version: vectors made with another are made again, dim — numbers in a vector, vectors: [string] — one for each text, in order: base64 of `dim` little-endian float32 numbers, L2-normalised }`. 429 past 30 calls a minute for the app, or past 20,000 texts a day (`Retry-After` says when; the day's count is on the app's page); 501 when search by meaning is turned off in this Hydor or its model is not set up (nothing is ever downloaded because an app asked: the app carries on with words); 503 with `Retry-After` while the model is starting, memory is short or Hydor is busy with its own search: Hydor's own work goes first, since an app's texts go to the model four at a time, one such piece at a time in all, each only while no request of Hydor's own is being answered.
- `POST /api/apps/:slug/ai` — the owner's models for a web app's AI (a web app that lists `ai` and its tiers in its hydor.json): one turn with a model, answered in one shape whichever model it is. Only for such an app, with the key Hydor made for it (as for `embed`), and only for a tier its hydor.json lists (`services.ai.tiers`) and its owner allowed (`settings.tiers`, on the app's page: nothing is used until they do); a key that is not valid is 401, one that is Hydor's but not for this 403. The person is proven, never claimed: the app forwards the `x-hydor-person` header Hydor signed on the person's own request, exactly as it got it (a call with none is a job and counts for the app and for nobody); a header Hydor did not sign is refused (403), one that ran out is 401 with `kind: "key"`. The app gets a model's answer and nothing else of Hydor: no tools of Hydor's, no memory, no files, no session; the tools in the request are the app's own, which the model may ask for and the app runs itself (Hydor never calls the app). Body: `{ tier: "quick" | "standard" | "best" — the model Settings → Models → Tiers names for Custom (else the helper model if that is one of Custom's, else Automatic: plain text and an answer to fit a schema, in one piece, no tools), messages: [{ role: "system" | "user" | "assistant" | "tool", content: string, toolCalls?: [{ id, name, input }] — on an assistant message, toolCallId?: string — on a tool message, the call its content answers }] — 1 to 200, 200,000 characters each and 400,000 in all (413 with `kind: "too-long"`), the last not the model's own, schema?: object — JSON Schema the answer must fit (not with tools), tools?: [{ name, description, input: JSON Schema }] — at most 40 (a model whose tools are not native is refused with 403), maxOutputTokens?: number — at most 8,192, stream?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ text, data? — when a schema was sent, toolCalls: [{ id, name, input }] — only tools the request named, stop: "end" | "tools" | "length" | "refused", model, usage: { inputTokens, outputTokens, usd? — what it cost the owner, left out when Hydor cannot know it }, cap: { usedUsd, limitUsd } }`; with `stream: true`, server-sent events `text` (`{ text }`), then `done` (the same object) or `error` (`{ error, kind?, until? }`), with `:` lines as keep-alives. Errors are `{ error — a sentence safe to show a person, kind?: "none" | "cap" | "key" | "too-long" | "busy" | "failed", until? }`: 402 when the owner has no credit with the provider (`cap`); 403 when the app may not use models or this tier, the owner has not allowed it yet, or the model cannot call tools (`none`); 413 for a request too long for the model; 429 with `kind: "cap"`, `until` (midnight on this computer, as a date and time) and `cap` when the app's cap for the day is reached, else 60 calls a minute for the app, 20 for a person and 4 at once (`busy`, with `Retry-After`); 400 for a request that is not as asked (`failed`); 502 when the model could not answer (`busy`). The cap is the app's `settings.dailyCapUsd`, the one its flows half's runs are held to as well: what both spent today adds up, and a call in flight may pass it by what it costs. A model with no price here (your plan's, one on this computer) counts as $0, so a day's ceiling of 5,000,000 tokens stops it too. Hydor keeps, for 14 days, the app, the person's id, the tier, the model, the tokens, the cost and how it ended, never a word of what was asked or answered.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/ai` — what the owner sees of the app's model use: today's count and, for each person, their answers and cost, and the last 30 calls (no words of any). Returns: `{ ai: Web app's ai, people: [{ id, name, calls, usd, tokens }], recent: [{ at, person, tier, model, inputTokens, outputTokens, usd, status: ok|refused-off|refused-tier|refused-cap|refused-person|refused-limit|failed|stopped }] }`; 404 for a web app whose hydor.json does not list `ai`.
- `POST /api/apps/:slug/mail` — sends a web app's mail (a web app that lists `mail` in its hydor.json; for what an app sends beyond accounts, since inside Hydor it has no sign-up, password or invitation mail of its own) from the mail account the owner chose for it (Apps → Connected apps → Email). For a web app's own server only: agents are refused it (use the email tools). Only for such an app (it is started with `HYDOR_URL`, `HYDOR_APP_SLUG` and `HYDOR_APP_KEY`), with the key Hydor made for it; a key that is not valid is 401, one that is Hydor's but not for this 403. Nothing is sent until the owner allows it for that app (`settings.mail`, on the card **Mail it may send** on the app's page). Only the launched version sends: Hydor marks the key it gives the process it starts as the launched version, and a call without that mark (Preview, the development server, an app not launched yet) is answered 501, so the app keeps the mail, unless the owner also switched on sending while it is being made (`settings.mail.whileMaking`). The two sides have their own idempotency keys, day's count and minute. The request header `Idempotency-Key` (at most 256 characters): the same key from the same app within 24 hours answers 200 again with the same id and sends nothing. Body: `{ to: [string] — 1 to 50 plain addresses; each is sent a mail of their own, and the same one twice is one, subject: string — one line, at most 900 characters, text: string — at most 200,000 characters, html?: string — at most 200,000, passed on as given, replyTo?: string — one address, fromName?: string — the name beside the owner's address, at most 80 characters, without <, > or @ (left out: the web app's name), unsubscribe?: string — an https:// or mailto: address, put in List-Unsubscribe (and, for https, List-Unsubscribe-Post) }`; the sender's address is never the request's. Returns: `{ id, status: "sent" | "queued" }` — queued: the mail server was slow or could take only some for now, and Hydor carries on (it tries again after half a minute, 2, 10 and 30 minutes, from memory: a restart loses what waits). Errors are `{ error }`: 400 for what the request gets wrong (a line break or control character in an address, the subject, a name or the unsubscribe address among them), an address or a mail the server refuses, or more people than the app may ever send in a day; 403 when the owner turned mail off, or the app does not use mail; 413 past the sizes; 429 `{ error, limit, resetsAt }` with `Retry-After` past the app's mails a day (UTC; 100 unless the owner raised it; each person counts as one, and a request that would pass it is refused whole), past 60 a minute, or past 30 requests a minute; 501 while this Hydor will not send it (from the version being made without `whileMaking`, not allowed yet, no mail account that can send, the chosen one removed or refusing its sign-in); 503 with `Retry-After` when the mail server cannot take it now. What is kept is when, which app, how many people, the subject's length and the outcome, never an address or a word of the mail; no copy is added to the account's Sent folder (Gmail keeps one itself).
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/mail` — what the owner sees of an app's mail. Returns: `{ mail: Web app's mail, accounts: [{ id, address, keepsCopy: boolean — its provider keeps a copy of what is sent }] — the connected accounts that can send, recent: [{ at, recipients: number, subjectChars: number, sent: number, status: sending|queued|sent|partial|failed|lost|refused, reason?: ask|off|making|no-account|limit|sign-in|server|recipient|content|restart, making?: true — from the version being made }] — the last 20 requests, no address and no word of any }`; 404 for a web app that sends no mail through Hydor.

## App-wide switches (Settings → General → Resume after usage limits, Settings → Accounts → Switch accounts at a limit and Use API credit at a limit)

- `GET /api/app-settings` — whether sessions stopped by a plan's usage limit continue by themselves a minute after it resets, whether a session whose account runs out of usage moves to another of its agent's accounts with usage left and carries on (Claude Code, Codex and Grok Build), and whether Claude Code sessions carry on billed to an Anthropic API key (API credit) when every Claude account is at its limit (autoResume and apiCredit are off by default, autoSwitch is on). Returns: `{ autoResume: boolean, autoSwitch: boolean, apiCredit: boolean }`.
- `PUT /api/app-settings` — turns any of them on or off. Body: `{ autoResume?: boolean, autoSwitch?: boolean, apiCredit?: boolean }` (each left as is when absent). Returns: `{ autoResume: boolean, autoSwitch: boolean, apiCredit: boolean }`.

## Answer cards

Agents find answer cards with their own find_ui tool. A card that reads its numbers from a spreadsheet or a file (its data has `from`; find_ui says how to write one) is drawn from this:

- `POST /api/answers/data` — a card's data worked out from a kept spreadsheet or the data file its `from` names, in its card's own shape, as the card draws it (for the table, chart, stats, ranking, distribution and map-values cards). It reads a kept spreadsheet by its name or id (not one made in a confidential session, unless asked for that session or another confidential one), and a CSV, TSV, JSON, JSONL or GeoJSON file only in the session's folder or its project's folders (a board's: its project's, and its card's session's), of up to 50 MB (JSON 20 MB) and its first 1,000,000 rows; what it read is kept for a few minutes under the spreadsheet's version or the file's size and time of change. Body: `{ ui: string — the card's name, data: object — the card's data as written, with from (and where, groupBy, sort, limit, and its own fields: x and y, columns, stats, items, points, values), session?: string — the session it is drawn in, board?: string — or the board (a kept page) it is pinned to }`. Returns: `{ data: object — the card's data with its values, source: { kind: "sheet", id, name, tab, range, version } | { kind: "file", path, name, mtime }, readAt: number — when the source was read, rows: number — the rows it holds, matched: number — the rows the filter kept, shown?: number, total?: number — when the card shows only part, notes: string[] — what else to know (groups left out, a sample drawn) }`. Errors: 400 a reference that cannot be read (a column that is not there, a filter that cannot be read: the message says which), 403 a file outside those folders or a confidential session's spreadsheet, 404 no such spreadsheet, tab or file, 413 a file too big. A board shared by link asks its link's own `POST /p-api/<token>/answers/data` (the same body, with its board), which answers only for a card that board holds, with that card's own data, and leaves out the file's path.

## Pages (kept things)

Agents also have their own tools for pages: show_html shows a page in the conversation, save_page saves a new page or (with an id) a new version of one, save_slides, save_doc, save_sheet, save_latex, save_notebook, save_diagram and save_board do the same for slide decks, documents, spreadsheets, LaTeX documents, Jupyter notebooks, diagrams and boards of answer cards (each with libraryFolder: an older way to group kept things, still accepted and not shown in the app, and description: what it is, under 100 words, given with each new one; with an id and nothing else to save, the title and description change alone; each but save_board also takes projectFolder: the folder of the project its file goes in, a path from the project's folder such as "reports/2026", made when missing; left out, a new one's file goes at the top of the project's folder, or in the agent's working folder when that is a folder inside it, and an existing one's stays where it is), get_page lists them all (with each one's project and folder, and with few, their descriptions), finds the ones that fit a query (by their titles and descriptions, by words and by meaning) or reads one (a page with its stored data, another kind as it is kept), export_page saves one as a file (PDF, Word, OpenDocument, PowerPoint, Excel, CSV, Markdown, EPUB, plain text, LaTeX, a notebook, a Python script, SVG, PNG, a draw.io file or a web page). When the user changes one in its editor, the agent of that session (and of the session that made it) hears of it with the user's next message. In a project, each thing but a board, an app's screen or what a confidential session kept also has a file of its own in the project's folder (a document .docx, a spreadsheet .xlsx, a notebook .ipynb, a diagram .drawio, a page .html, slides .pptx, or .html in the web format, a LaTeX document .tex or a folder), written again after each save and read back in as a new version (by: file) when it is changed outside the app; change a thing with its save tool rather than by editing its file. The file list the app's Files page reads marks such a file (a LaTeX document's folder too, and the main.tex inside it) with `kept: { type: page|media, id, kind, title }` (kind: the page's, "web" for a saved web page, or image|video|audio). **Page**: `{ id — 10 hex characters, title, version — the current one, versions — how many are kept, bytes — the current version's HTML (or a deck's text) size, storageBytes — the size of its stored data, createdAt, updatedAt, sessionKey? — the session it came from, space? — the project it is in (none: in no project; what an agent saves goes in its session's), projectFile? — the whole path of its own file in the project's folder (none: in no project, not written yet, or one that has no file), folder? — the folder of kept things it is in (a Folder of its project, not shown in the app; none: at the project's top), by?: agent|user|restore|edit|made|file — who made the current version (edit: changed by the user in the editor; made: a page the user made; file: changed in its file in the project's folder), who? — the name of someone the project is shared with who made it (through their link), thumb? — set once its picture is ready, published?: true — it is published at a link, kind?: slides|doc|sheet|tex|notebook|diagram|board — another kind than a page of HTML, description? — what it is, ≤100 words: written by the agent that saved it, by the helper model for a file brought in or a page kept from a conversation, or by the user; the search box finds it by it }`. Another kind keeps a **model** instead of HTML, the shape its save tool describes: slides `{ v: 1, ratio: "16:9"|"4:3", theme: { id?, bg, fg, head, muted, accent, accentFg, soft, colors: [6], font, headFont }, slides: [{ id, els: [part — text, shape, image, line, table, chart, icon, math, video or group (its own els)], anims?, transition?, notes?, hidden?, bg?, nofooter? }], footer?: { num?, text?, date?, skipTitle? } — slide numbers and a footer, guides?: { x: [px], y: [px] } }`; a document `{ v: 1, page: { size: a4|letter, margin — mm }, font, size — pt, blocks: [{ id, type: p|li|table|image|hr|pagebreak|toc|math, … }], header?: { text, align }, footer?: { text, align } — {page} and {pages} in their text are the page's number and the count, titlePage?: boolean — none on the first page, comments?: [Comment] }`; a workbook `{ v: 1, sheets: [{ id, name, cells: { "B2": { v?: number|string|boolean, f?: string — a formula without its "=", s?: style, note?: string } }, cols?: { [index]: { w?, hidden? } }, rows?: { [index]: { h?, hidden? } }, freeze?: { rows?, cols? }, merges?: [range], filter?: { range, cols? }, conditional?: [rule], validation?: [rule], charts?: [chart — its range (several split by commas), from and to: { cell, dx?, dy? } the cells its corners are on], board?: { at, by, fields?, lanes?, open? } — its Kanban board, comments?: [Comment and its cell] }] }`; a LaTeX document `{ v: 1, engine: pdflatex|xelatex|lualatex, main — main.tex's text, files: [{ name, text }], pictures: [{ name — as \includegraphics names it, src }], comments?: [Comment and its file, from, to — where its words are], track?: true — Track changes is on, changes?: [{ id, kind: ins|del — words put in (in the text at [from, to)) or taken out (no longer in it: text, at from), file, from, to, text, before? — the characters before words taken out, by: user|agent|ai — ai: AI in the editor (a rewrite, a fix or proofreading accepted), at — ms }] — tracked changes waiting; the text reads as if all were accepted }`; a notebook the .ipynb's own object, nbformat 4 `{ cells: [{ id, cell_type: code|markdown|raw, source, metadata, outputs? — a code cell's, execution_count? }], metadata, nbformat: 4, nbformat_minor }` (kept as nbformat writes it: each source and text output as a list of lines); a diagram `{ v: 1, grid?: number — the grid it snaps to (0: none), sketch?: true — drawn by hand, font?: serif|mono|hand, pages: [{ id, name, background?, items: [shape or connector, in the order drawn] }] }`, a shape `{ id, type: "shape", shape — its kind (rect, rounded, ellipse, diamond, terminator, cylinder, document, cloud, icon, image, text, sticky, container, swimlane, lane, class, entity, table, actor, lifeline…), x, y, w, h, rot?, flipH?, flipV?, text?, rows?: [string] — a class's, entity's or table's, icon?: { name, paths }, src?, parent? — the container it is in, group?, link?, note?, locked?, style?: { fill?, gradient?, stroke?, width?, dash?: dashed|dotted|dash-dot, opacity?, shadow?, radius?, sketch?, font?, size?, color?, bold?, italic?, underline?, align?, valign?, label?: inside|below|above|left|right } }` and a connector `{ id, type: "edge", from and to: { id?, port?: [x, y] — a fixed point as fractions of the shape's box, x?, y? — a free end }, route?: orthogonal|straight|curved, points?: [[x, y]], text?, at? — 0..1 along it, fromText?, toText?, style?: { stroke?, width?, dash?, start?, end? — arrow, open, block, triangle, diamond, diamond-open, circle, circle-open, bar, cross, er-one, er-only-one, er-zero-one, er-many, er-one-many, er-zero-many or none, radius?, flow? } }`. Pictures in a model are `naget-asset:<sha>` sources. A **Comment** is `{ id, by: user|agent|person, name? — a person's name (by: person: someone the project is shared with), who? — that person's share id, author? — someone else's name (a Word file's comments), text, at — ms, resolved?, quote? — the words it was about, replies?: [{ id, by, name?, who?, author?, text, at }] }`; agents answer, resolve and add comments with the save tools. A **Folder** (it only groups kept things, and the app does not show it) is `{ id — 10 hex characters, name, parent? — the folder it is in (none: at its place's top), space? — the project it is in (none: in no project), createdAt }`: it holds kept pages, pictures, videos and sounds of its place, and other folders (8 deep at most); deleting one deletes nothing it holds.

- `GET /api/slides/themes` — the user's own slide themes (**Theme → My themes**). Returns: `{ themes: [{ id, name, theme — a deck's theme, logo?: { src, at: tl|tr|bl|br, w, h, skipTitle }, createdAt, updatedAt }] }`.
- `POST /api/slides/themes` — saves one, over one of the same name (**Save as my theme**). Body: `{ name: string, theme: object — a deck's theme, logo?: { src, at: tl|tr|bl|br, w, h, skipTitle } }`. Returns: `{ theme }`.
- `PATCH /api/slides/themes/:id` — renames one, or changes its look or logo. Body: `{ name?, theme?, logo?: object|null }`. Returns: `{ theme }`.
- `DELETE /api/slides/themes/:id` — deletes one. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/write/design-ideas` — design ideas for a slide: its own words, pictures and data laid out anew a few ways, nothing made up or left out (what **Design ideas** shows). Body: `{ deck: object|string — the deck's model, or a saved deck's id, slide: number — its place from 0, count?: number — 1 to 4 (3), avoid?: [string] — ways already shown }`. Returns: `{ ideas: [slide], names: [string], ways: [string], model }`.
- `POST /api/write/rewrite` — words rewritten by the helper model (what **Rewrite** does). Body: `{ text: string — a paragraph a line, up to 6000 characters, how: shorter|longer|simpler|formal|friendly|fix|translate|ask, lang?: string — a language's English name, ask?: string — how to rewrite them, context?: string — the words around them }`. Returns: `{ text, model }`.
- `POST /api/write/page-ai` — the AI on a page (**Ask AI** in the page editor: Space on an empty line, Ctrl+J, a block's menu): the helper model writes on the words or blocks picked, or the page, and answers in Markdown, which the editor shows as blocks to keep. Body: `{ action: continue|write|summarize|shorter|longer|improve|fix|simplify|tone|translate|explain|table|list|brainstorm|actions|ask, text?: string — what is picked, as Markdown (up to 24000 characters), before?: string — the page before that place, page?: string — the whole page, title?: string, topic?: string — write, list, table, brainstorm: what about, tone?: professional|casual|straightforward|confident|friendly, lang?: string — translate: a language's English name, ask?: string — the person's own request, or what to change in answer, answer?: string — the answer before, to change, pageId?: string — a kept page (a confidential session's goes only to a helper model on this computer) }`. Returns: `{ markdown, model, cost? — US dollars, when known }`.
- `POST /api/write/db-ai` — one cell of a database's AI column, from the row's other cells (what **Fill every row** does a row at a time). Body: `{ how: summarize|extract|classify|translate|custom, prompt?: string — extract: what to take out; custom: what the column holds, lang?: string — translate, options?: string[] — classify: at least two, column: string — the column's name, row: { [column]: string } — the row's other cells as words, pageId?: string }`. Returns: `{ value, model, cost? }`.
- `POST /api/write/design-words` — new words for a design's text element, written by the helper model (**Write for me**). Body: `{ about: string — what they are about, role?: heading|subheading|body|button, now?: string — its words now, design?: string — the design's name, lang?: string }`. Returns: `{ text, model }`.
- `POST /api/write/palette` — five colours for a design from a mood, by the helper model (the brand kit's **Suggest**). Body: `{ mood: string }`. Returns: `{ colours: string[] — #rrggbb: a background, a colour for words, two accents and a tint, model }`.
- `POST /api/write/describe` — a picture the app keeps described for people who cannot see it, by the model that describes kept pictures (**Describe it with AI**). Body: `{ src: "naget-asset:<sha>", context?: string — the slide's words, for its sense and language }`. Returns: `{ alt, by }`.
- `POST /api/write/latex/rewrite` — the LaTeX words picked rewritten by the helper model (what the LaTeX editor's **Rewrite with AI** does), commands, maths, the keys of \cite, \ref and \label, addresses and comments kept; checked, and asked again once when it dropped, changed or made up any of them. Body: `{ text: string — the source picked, ≤8000 characters, how: shorter|clearer|academic|fix|translate|ask, lang?: string — a language's English name (translate), ask?: string — how to rewrite it (ask), context?: string — the section and paragraph it is in }`. Returns: `{ text, problems: [string] — what it still dropped, changed or made up, in plain words (then the editor puts nothing in), model, cost? — US dollars, when known and not nothing }`.
- `POST /api/write/latex/write` — LaTeX for the cursor's place, written by the helper model as described (what **Write with AI** and Ctrl+K do). Body: `{ ask: string — what to write ("a results table: 3 models, accuracy and F1"), before?: string, after?: string — the source around the cursor, preamble?: string — the document's preamble, math?: boolean — the cursor is in maths }`. Returns: `{ latex, packages: [string] — the \usepackage lines it needs that the preamble lacks, note?, model, cost? }`.
- `GET /api/write/latex/picture` — who reads a picture into LaTeX here. Returns: `{ label?, where?: helper|openrouter|claude — the helper model when it sees pictures, else a vision model through OpenRouter, else Claude through Claude Code, cost?: number — about what a picture costs in US dollars (OpenRouter), why?: string — when none can }`.
- `POST /api/write/latex/picture` — a picture of an equation, a table or text (a screenshot, a photo of handwriting) read into LaTeX (**LaTeX from a picture**). Body: `{ data: string — a PNG, JPEG, WebP or GIF, base64 or a data: address (≤8 MB), kind?: equation|table|text, preamble?: string }`. Returns: `{ latex, packages, kind, note?, model, cost? }`; 415 when it is no picture, 422 when nothing could be read in it.
- `POST /api/write/latex/error` — a typesetting's error explained in plain words, or the lines it comes from changed to fix it (**Explain** and **Fix** beside it). Body: `{ how: explain|fix, message: string, hint?: string, warning?: boolean, file?: string, line?: number, lines: [{ n: number — its number in the file, text }] — the lines around it, log?: string — what TeX's log says of it }`. Returns: `{ text — the explanation, or why the fix, fix?: { from, to — line numbers, text — those lines as they should be }, model, cost? }`.
- `POST /api/write/latex/cite` — papers that back a claim (**Find a citation for this**): searches written by the helper model, run in OpenAlex and arXiv, and the papers found judged against their abstracts; only the claim and its paragraph are sent. Body: `{ claim: string — the words picked, ≤1500 characters, context?: string — the paragraph around them }`. Returns: `{ queries: [string], papers: [{ title, authors: [string], year?, venue?, doi?, arxiv?, abstract, cited? — how often it is cited, backs: strong|some, why, bibtex — made from its record }], notes?: [string] — what could not be searched, model, cost? }`.
- `POST /api/write/latex/proofread` — paragraphs of a LaTeX file proofread by the helper model (**Proofread with AI**): spelling, grammar, punctuation and wrong words, each correction found once in its paragraph and touching no command, maths, key or comment. Body: `{ paragraphs: [{ id: number, text: string — as it is in the file }] — ≤8000 characters in all }`. Returns: `{ corrections: [{ id — its paragraph, from, to — where in it, was, now, why }], model, cost? }`.
- `GET /api/write/sheet-ai-cost` — what the spreadsheet's AI helpers cost with the helper model picked now (the line the editor shows under them). Returns: `{ model: string, note: string, free: boolean }`.
- `POST /api/write/sheet-ai` — the answers of a spreadsheet's AI cells (`=AI(prompt, cells…)`), many asked of the helper model in one call (those with the same prompt together). Body: `{ asks: [{ key: string — what the answer is kept by, prompt: string, context: string[] — the cells' values as words }] — up to 25 }`. Returns: `{ answers: { [key]: string|number|boolean }, failed?: { [key]: string }, model: string }`.
- `POST /api/write/sheet-formula` — a formula written by the helper model from words (**Write a formula**), checked to read and to use only functions the sheet has. Body: `{ ask: string — "total of column C where B is Paid", sheet: string, at: string — the cell it goes in, table: string — the cells around it as rows of words, sheets?: string[], names?: string[] }`. Returns: `{ formula: string — with its "=", explain: string, model: string }`.
- `POST /api/write/sheet-explain` — a formula in plain words (**Explain this formula**). Body: `{ formula: string, sheet: string, at: string, table: string }`. Returns: `{ text: string, model: string }`.
- `POST /api/write/sheet-fill` — a column filled by the helper model from a few examples (**Fill with AI**, when no pattern of the editor's own fits). Body: `{ headers: string[], target?: string, examples: [{ inputs: string[], output: string }], rows: string[][] — the values beside each cell to fill }`. Returns: `{ outputs: (string|null)[] — one a row, model: string }`.
- `POST /api/write/sheet-analyze` — a table's question answered, with charts, pivot tables and formulas to put in (**Analyze the data**). Body: `{ question?: string, sheet: string, range: string, table: string, columns: [{ letter: string, name: string, about: string — what it holds over all its rows }] }`. Returns: `{ answer: string, ideas: [{ kind: chart|pivot|formula, title: string, why?: string, … }], model: string }`.
- `POST /api/write/sheet-table` — a table made from words (**Insert → Table made with AI**). Body: `{ ask: string }`. Returns: `{ title?: string, columns: [{ name: string, type: text|number|currency|percent|date|checkbox|list|rating, options?: string[] }], rows: (string|number|boolean|null)[][], model: string }`.
- `POST /api/write/compose` — words for a document written by the helper model, with the document around the caret as context (what **Write with AI** does). Body: `{ kind: write|continue|summary|conclusion|list|table|explain|summarize|tablify — what to write (the last three for words picked), ask?: string — what to write about (needed for write, list and table), before?: string — the document before the caret, after?: string — after it, title?: string, outline?: string, words?: string — the words picked }`. Returns: `{ markdown, model, cost?: number — US dollars, when the helper model is paid by the word }`.
- `POST /api/write/proofread` — paragraphs proofread by the helper model: spelling, grammar, punctuation and clarity, as little changed as it can (what **Tools → Proofread** does). Body: `{ items: [{ id: string, text: string — a paragraph; things of their own in it as ⟦1⟧ marks, kept }] — up to 60, about 12,000 characters }`. Returns: `{ items: [{ id, text — the paragraph corrected, why — what was fixed }] — only those that change, model, cost? }`.
- `POST /api/write/paragraphs` — every paragraph rewritten in a tone, or translated (**Tools → Change the tone**, **Translate the document**). Body: `{ how: tone|translate, tone?: string — "more formal", "friendlier", lang?: string — a language's English name, items: [{ id, text }] — as proofread's }`. Returns: `{ items: [{ id, text }], model, cost? }`.
- `POST /api/write/complete` — the next few words of a sentence being typed (**View → Suggest as I type**). Body: `{ before: string — the words up to the caret, after?: string }`. Returns: `{ text — "" when there is no good guess, model }`.
- `POST /api/write/ask-doc` — a question answered from a document alone, each statement citing the passages it rests on as [P3] (**Ask about this document**). Body: `{ question: string, paragraphs: [{ id, text }] — the document's passages, in order, title?: string, history?: [{ q, a }] — earlier questions and answers }`. Returns: `{ answer, cites: [string] — the ids of the passages cited, short?: true — the document was too long and only its start was read, model, cost? }`.
- `GET /api/pages` — the kept pages, the latest changed first, and the folders that group them. Returns: `{ pages: [Page], folders: [Folder] }` (folders oldest first).
- `GET /api/pages/:id` — one page with its current HTML (another kind: its model), its versions and its own address. Returns: `{ page: Page, html?: string — a page's, model?: object — another kind's, versions: [{ version, title, bytes, createdAt, by: agent|user|restore|edit|made|file, who? — someone the project is shared with made it, name? — the name the user gave it ("Sent to ICML"): a named version is kept past the newest 40 }] (newest first), link: "/pg/<token>" — the page's own address: it opens the page with its data without signing in, and anyone with it can change the data }`. 404 when there is no such page.
- `GET /api/pages/:id/versions/:v` — one version of a page (another kind: its own page, with its pictures in it). Query: `model?: 1 — another kind's model instead`. Returns: `{ title, html, version }` (with model=1: `{ title, version, model }`); 404 "That version is no longer kept" (a page keeps its newest 40 versions, ≤24 MB of them; the current one always stays).
- `GET /api/pages/kept?key=` — which of a session's show_html pages were kept, and as which saved page. Query: `key: string — the session's key`. Returns: `{ items: { [timelineItemId]: pageId } }`.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/storage` — one call on the data a saved page keeps (what the page itself reads and writes with hydor.storage). Body: `{ op: get|set|remove|keys, key?: string — 1–200 characters, needed except for keys, value?: any JSON — for set; with its key ≤512 KB }`. A page keeps ≤1,000 keys and ≤2 MB in all (413 past either). Returns: `{ value }` (get: the value or null; keys: the key names; set and remove: null).
- `POST /api/pages` — keeps a page in Pages (what **Keep** does for a page shown in the conversation), or makes a new page (without item: what **New page** does), or a new deck, document, spreadsheet, LaTeX document, notebook, diagram or board (with kind; a board starts empty). Body: `{ title?: string — ≤120 characters (default "Page"), html: string — the whole page, non-empty, ≤2 MB, kind?: slides|doc|sheet|tex|notebook|diagram|board — instead of html, another kind, model?: object — its model (checked; the shorthand its save tool takes works too; left out: a new empty one), sessionKey?: string, item?: string — the show_html call's timeline item (if it was kept already, that page comes back instead), data?: { [key]: any JSON } — values to store with it, edited?: string — the user's changed HTML, kept on top as version 2 (**Keep with changes**; the session's agent hears of it), space?: string|null — the project it goes in (left out: the session's, when it is in one; null: none), folder?: string — a folder of kept things to make it in (its project wins over space), dir?: string — the folder its file goes in: the whole path of the project's folder or of a folder inside it (400 otherwise, or in no project), description?: string — what it is (cut to 100 words; a page kept with item and none gets one from the helper model) }`. 409 past 1,000 pages. Returns: `{ page: Page }`.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/versions` — saves the user's changed page as a new version (what **Save** does in each editor). Body: `{ html: string — the whole page, ≤2 MB (model?: object — another kind's model instead), title?: string, base?: number — the version that was changed, sessionKey?: string — the session it was changed beside, force?: boolean — save even when a newer version was saved since base }`. The agents of sessionKey and of the session that made the page hear of the change with the user's next message. 409 "Version … was saved after the one you changed" when base is not the current version and force is not set, unless only comments of people the project is shared with came since base: those are kept in what is saved (with force too); 400 when a page of HTML is sent a model or another kind HTML. Returns: `{ page: Page }`.
- `POST /api/pages/import` — makes a page of a file someone opens (what **Open a PowerPoint file**, **Open a Word, PDF or text file**, **Open an Excel or CSV file**, **Open a .tex or .zip file**, **Open a notebook (.ipynb)** and **Open a draw.io or Mermaid file** do): a .pptx (or .ppsx, .potx, .pptm) file becomes slides (its videos kept by the app), a .docx, .pdf (its text, headings, paragraphs and lists; a PDF without text, a scan, is refused with 422), .md, .txt or .html file a document, an .xlsx, .ods, .csv or .tsv file a spreadsheet, a .tex file or a LaTeX project's .zip a LaTeX document, an .ipynb a notebook, a .drawio (or .dio, .drawio.svg, .drawio.png) file or Mermaid (.mmd, .mermaid) a diagram; a .ppt (PowerPoint 97–2003) is refused with 415. Body: `{ path?: string — a file of this computer (absolute or from ~) to keep a copy of (what keeping a file does; up to 30 MB, a PowerPoint file 200 MB), or name: string — the file's name (its extension says what it is) and data: string — its bytes as base64 (≤30 MB), kind?: "diagram" — read a .svg, .png or .xml file as a draw.io diagram, title?: string — default: the name without its extension, sessionKey?: string, space?: string|null — the project it goes in (left out: the session's; null: none), folder?: string — a folder of kept things to put it in (its project wins over space), dir?: string — the folder of the project its new file goes in: the whole path of the project's folder or of a folder inside it (400 otherwise, or in no project; the file it was made from is left as it is), description?: string — what it is (cut to 100 words; left out, the helper model writes one from the start of the file, in the background) }`. 422 when the file cannot be read. Returns: `{ page: Page, notes: [string] — what could not come along (a picture of a kind LaTeX cannot use, say) }`.
- `POST /api/pages/sheet-file` — reads the sheets of a spreadsheet file without making a page of it (what dropping an .xlsx or .ods file on an open spreadsheet does, to add its sheets to it). Body: `{ name: string — the file's name (.xlsx, .xlsm, .ods, .csv or .tsv), data: string — its bytes as base64 (≤30 MB) }`. Returns: `{ sheets: [object] — the sheets as the editor keeps them, notes: [string] — what could not come along }`. 400 for another kind of file, 422 when it cannot be read.
- `POST /api/library/uploads` — starts sending a file of this device to a project or to the kept things (what **Upload** and dropping files on a project's **Files** do), before any of its bytes, and says what it will become by its name: a document, a spreadsheet, slides, a LaTeX document, a notebook or a diagram (as POST /api/pages/import makes them, up to 30 MB; a PowerPoint file up to 200 MB), a picture, a video or a sound (as POST /api/media keeps them, up to 1 GB), or, in a project, any other file (and one too large for those) put in the project's folder, where Files shows it. Body: `{ name: string — the file's name (its extension says what it is), size: number — its bytes, space?: string|null — the project it goes in (left out or null: none), rel?: string — its place in a folder that was dropped ("Receipts/june.pdf"), kept in the project's folder (the file of a page or a picture made of it goes in that folder too) }`. Returns: `{ upload: string — its id, becomes: page|image|video|audio|file, kind?: doc|sheet|slides|tex|notebook|diagram, piece: number — the most bytes a piece may carry (32 MB) }`. Refused before anything is sent: 415 with `why: "kind"` for a kind of file that is not kept, out of a project; 413 with `why: "size"` past its limits, out of a project; 507 with `why: "room"` when this computer's disk has no room for it; 400 for a project that is not one; 429 with 200 uploads on their way.
- `PUT /api/library/uploads/:id?offset=` — one piece of the file: its bytes as they are (`application/octet-stream`), from where the last piece ended (`offset`), in order. Returns: `{ received: number — the bytes so far }`, and for the last piece `{ done: FileAdded }` — **FileAdded**: `{ as: page|media|file, page?: Page, item?: MediaItem, existed?: true — these bytes were kept already: that item, and no copy was made, path?: string — where a file put in a project's folder is, space?: string — the project it went in, notes: [string] — what did not come along (the import's notes), or why it went in the project's folder rather than becoming a page }`. 409 with `received` for a piece out of step (send again from there); 413 for a piece over 32 MB or past the file's size (nothing of it is kept); 404 when the upload is gone (cancelled, finished, or the app restarted). An upload no piece came to for an hour goes.
- `DELETE /api/library/uploads/:id` — gives up on an upload: what came of it goes. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/library/move` — moves a file of this computer into the app by its path, the way a move between folders does (what dropping a file on the app does in the desktop app): a picture, a video or a sound moves into the folder of kept pictures, videos and sounds; any other file, dropped on a project, into the project's folder; a file that becomes a page becomes one, and the original goes to the app's Trash (where **Put back** brings it back), unless the page could not keep all of it: then it stays where it was. On its own disk a file is renamed; from another disk it is copied, the copy checked whole, then the original removed. Body: `{ path: string — the file's absolute path, space?: string|null, rel?: string — as for an upload, root?: string — the folder that was dropped, which goes once the drop emptied it (what systems leave in folders aside) }`. Returns: FileAdded, with `moved?: boolean — the original left its place, trashed?: string — where the original is in the Trash, stayed?: true — the original stayed where it was, lost?: [string] — what the page could not keep of it (as Files' editors name it: headers, footnotes, charts…; cut: not all of it fitted; files: a LaTeX file that reads others of its folder; pictures: a Markdown or HTML file that shows pictures of its folder)`. 403 with `copy: project|data|system|link` for a file in a project's folder, in the app's own, in the system's, or a link: none is moved, so send a copy with POST /api/library/uploads instead (the original stays); 400 for a path that is not absolute or not a file; 404 when it is not there; 415 and 413 as for an upload.
- `GET /api/pages/file?path=` — a file of this computer as one of the editors' models, to change in place (what Files does when it opens a Word, Excel, CSV, TSV or LaTeX file). Query: `path: string — absolute or from ~; a .docx, .xlsx, .csv, .tsv, .tex or .ltx file`. Returns: `{ kind: doc|sheet|tex, path, mtime, model, loses: [string] — what of the file the editor does not keep, which saving over it loses (a Word file's headers, footnotes, comments, changes, equations, charts, diagrams, objects, boxes, columns, toc, layout; an Excel file's pictures, shapes, charts it cannot draw, formulas it cannot work out, pivots, tables, links to other files, hyperlinks, names, macros, groups, sparklines, icons, protection, print, rich — words formatted in parts of a cell), cut?: true — it did not come in whole: to read only, notes: [string], tex?: { folder, main — its main file there, open — the model's name of the file asked for ("main.tex" for the main one), files: { [its path in the folder]: mtime }, inFolder: boolean — typeset among its folder's files, uses?: [{ name — its path in the folder, kind: tex|bib|style|picture|data|other, size — bytes }] — the other files of the folder the document uses, which TeX takes from it as they are (pictures, styles and classes, data, sources past a document's limits) } }`. A LaTeX chapter comes as the document it names (`% !TEX root = …`, a subfile's class option) or the one that reads it, the files it uses read as TeX finds them; a LaTeX project's model has the comments the app keeps on its files (`model.comments`, each with its `file`). 415 for another kind of file, or a .tex that is no whole document and that no document beside it reads; 422 when it cannot be read; 413 over 60 MB.
- `PUT /api/pages/file` — writes an editor's model back to its file (**Save** in Files), in the file's own format: a CSV's rows and cells nobody changed keep their text, a LaTeX project writes only the files that changed and moves the ones the editor removed to the trash. Body: `{ path: string, model: object — as GET gave it, changed, mtime?: number — the file's time when it was read (409 when it changed since), force?: boolean — write over it anyway, backup?: boolean — put the file as it is in the trash first, copy?: boolean — write "name (edited).ext" beside it instead, tex?: object — a LaTeX file's tex as GET gave it, its files' times from the last save, and comments: the model's comments as GET (or the last save) gave them, to merge what an agent did since }`. A LaTeX project's comments are kept by the app by each file's path, not in the files. Returns: `{ path — where it was written, mtime, size, backup? — where the old file is in the trash, files? — a LaTeX project's times now, comments? — a LaTeX project's comments as kept now, dropped: [string] — what a CSV could not keep: sheets, formulas (saved as their values), formatting }`.
- `GET /api/pages/file/comments?path=` — the comments the app keeps on a LaTeX file of this computer and the files its document reads, as the editor's model has them (Files' editor reads them again when an agent answered one). Query: `path: string — a .tex file`. Returns: `{ comments: [{ id, by: user|agent, text, at, quote?, resolved?, replies?: [{ id, by, text, at }], file — its name in the project ("main.tex" for the document's own file), from, to }] }`.
- `GET /api/pages/tex/setup` — what this computer has for LaTeX. Returns: `{ engines: { pdflatex, xelatex, lualatex: boolean }, latexmk, bibtex, pictures — its pages can be drawn, sync — from a page to its source and back, sandbox — TeX runs shut in by bubblewrap: booleans, missing?: string — no TeX here: what to install, notes: { [engine]: why it cannot be used here }, installed?: [string] — which of the common classes and packages its TeX has, by file name ("amsmath.sty", "moderncv.cls": those the templates need and \usepackage offers), add?: { cmd?: string — what adds the classes and packages the templates need and its TeX lacks, on this computer ("sudo apt install texlive-publishers"), auto?: true — MiKTeX, which installs them the first time a document asks for them }, timeLimit: number — the most one typesetting may take, in seconds, words: boolean — the typeset pages' words can be read (pdftotext) }`.
- `POST /api/pages/tex/compile` — typesets a LaTeX document, saved or not (what the LaTeX editor's preview does). Body: `{ model: object — the LaTeX document, base?: string — the key of the typesetting before, to build on, folder?: string — a file's document (opened from Files): typeset among the other files of its folder (pictures, styles, data), which TeX reads but cannot change; not the home folder or one holding it, draft?: boolean — quicker, its pictures drawn as boxes (**Draft: pictures as boxes**), halt?: boolean — stop at the first error (**Stop on the first error**) }`. Returns: `{ key — its pages and PDF are at /api/pages/tex/<key>/…, ok — typeset with no errors, pdf: boolean — a PDF was made, pages, sizes?: [{ w, h }] — each page in points, errors: [{ file?, line?, message, hint? }], warnings: [the same], log — TeX's log, trimmed, engine, missing?: string — what to install, ms?, sandboxed? }`.
- `POST /api/pages/tex/clear` — throws away kept typesettings, so the next starts afresh (**Clear the cached files**). Body: `{ keys: [string] — typesettings' keys (≤20) }`. Returns: `{ cleared: number }`.
- `GET /api/pages/tex/:key/words` — the words of a typesetting's pages and where each is (**Find in the pages**, words picked on a page). Returns: `{ pages: [{ w, h — the page in points, words: [[text, x, y, w, h, line]] — each word's box in points from the page's top left, and its line on the page }] }`; 404 when there is no such PDF or no pdftotext.
- `POST /api/pages/tex/bib-fill` — doi.org's BibTeX for DOIs, three asked at a time (**Tidy the bibliography**'s fields filled from them). Body: `{ dois: [string] — ≤60 }`. Returns: `{ entries: { [doi]: string — its BibTeX }, missing: [string] — the DOIs doi.org had no record for }`.
- `GET /api/pages/tex/:key/sync` — where a place on a typeset page comes from in the source. Query: `page: number, x: number, y: number — in points from the page's top left`. Returns: `{ file, line }`, or `{}` when it is not known.
- `GET /api/pages/tex/:key/place` — where a line of the source shows on the typeset pages. Query: `file: string, line: number`. Returns: `{ page, x, y, w, h }` (points), or `{}` when it is not known.
