- `POST /api/pages/tex/picture` — keeps a picture for a LaTeX document (what adding one in its editor does); a PDF picture becomes a PNG of it. Body: `{ name: string — its file name, data: string — PNG, JPEG or PDF, base64 or a data: address (≤40 MB) }`. Returns: `{ name — the name to use in \includegraphics, src: "naget-asset:<sha>" }`; 415 when it is none of those, 422 when a PDF cannot be drawn.
- `POST /api/pages/tex/reference` — finds a reference on the web for a LaTeX document's bibliography (what **Insert → Reference** does): a DOI's BibTeX from doi.org, an arXiv paper's entry made from arXiv's record, or a search by title or authors in Crossref and arXiv. Only public addresses are reached, and only the query is sent. Body: `{ query: string — a DOI (bare, doi:… or a doi.org address), an arXiv id or address, or words of a title or its authors (≤500 characters) }`. Returns: `{ kind: doi|arxiv|search, entries: [{ key — a key as people make them ("knuth1984literate"), bibtex — the entry, one field a line, title, authors, year, venue }] }` (one for a DOI or an arXiv id, up to ten for words); 404 when a DOI or an arXiv id is no paper, 502 when the sites could not be reached.
- `POST /api/pages/assets` — keeps a picture for slides or a document (what **Picture** does in the slides editor, and **Insert** in a document's), or a 3D part's model. Body: `{ data?: string — a picture as a data: address (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP or SVG, ≤12 MB), url?: string — or a picture's public web address, fetched and kept, model?: true — data is a 3D model instead (a .glb, or a .gltf with its files inside it), for a web deck's 3D part }`. Returns: `{ src: "naget-asset:<sha>" — the source to put in a deck's image part (or a 3D part's model) }`; 415 when it is not a picture (or not a 3D model in glTF).
- `POST /api/pages/video-still` — a YouTube or Vimeo video's still (Vimeo's from its oEmbed), fetched by this server from public addresses and kept as a picture (what putting a video on a slide does). Body: `{ url: string — the video's address (a watch page, a short link or a player's) }`. Returns: `{ src: "naget-asset:<sha>" — to put in a video part's poster }`; 400 for an address that is no YouTube or Vimeo video, 422 when its site gave no still (a private video, or the site could not be reached).
- `POST /api/pages/files` — keeps a file for pages (a page's File, Sound or PDF block, a database's Files & pictures cell), up to 50 MB, once by its bytes. Body: the file's bytes (`application/octet-stream`). Query: `name: string — its name, type?: string — its kind as the browser says it`. Returns: `{ src: "/pg/file/<sha>/<signature>/<name>" — its address, its own permission (no sign-in), name, type, size }`; pictures, sounds, videos and PDFs show where it is opened, anything else downloads.
- `POST /api/pages/bookmark` — what a link shows as a **Bookmark** block, read from its page by this server (public addresses only). Body: `{ url: string }`. Returns: `{ url — where it landed, title, description, image?: an https address — its og:image, icon?: an https address, site, unread?: why the page could not be read (the card then shows its address) }`.
- `POST /api/pages/import-blocks` — a file as blocks to put in a page (**Add → Import into the page**). Body: `{ name: string — its name, which says its kind: .md, .markdown, .txt, .html, .htm, .docx, .csv or .zip (a Notion export of Markdown & CSV), data: string — its bytes, base64, up to 30 MB }`. Returns: `{ title, html — the editor's blocks (a CSV, and a Notion export's databases, as database blocks with their rows; their rows' own pages too), notes: string[] — what came in as what }`; 415 for another kind of file.
- `POST /api/pages/import-page` — the same, as a new page of its own (**New → Page → Import a page**). Body: `{ name, data, space?: string — a project's id, folder?: string — a folder's id (older, not shown in the app) }`. Returns: `{ page: Page, notes: string[] }`.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/lock` — locks or unlocks a page (**More → Lock the page**): locked, it takes no new version (from the editor or an agent) until it is unlocked; its databases still take rows. Body: `{ locked: boolean }`. Returns: `{ page: Page — with locked: true while it is }`.
- `GET /api/pages/:id/backlinks` — the pages whose current version links to it (**More → Linked from…**). Returns: `{ pages: [{ id, title, icon? }] }`.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/copy` — a copy of the page as it is now, as a new page "… (copy)", without its data. Returns: `{ page: Page }`.
- `GET /api/pages/:id/data` — everything the page keeps (its **What it keeps** view). Returns: `{ entries: [{ key, value — any JSON, bytes, updatedAt }] }` (by key).
- `DELETE /api/pages/:id/data` — forgets everything the page keeps; the page stays. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `GET /api/pages/:id/comments` — the comments on a page's blocks (its editor's **Comments** tab; a page of HTML's, kept outside its HTML). Returns: `{ comments: [{ id, by: user|agent, text, at, resolved?, quote? — the words it is about, on?: { path?: number[] — its block's place in the page, tag?, words? — the start of the block's words }, replies?: [{ id, by, text, at }] }] }`.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/comments` — changes them, as the user. Body: `{ ops: [ { text: string, on?: { path?, tag?, words? } | string — the words of its block, quote?: string } — a new comment | { id, reply: string } | { id, resolve: true } | { id, reopen: true } | { id, edit: string } | { id, delete: true } | { id, on } — its block's new place ] }`. Returns: `{ comments, added: string[] — the new comments' ids }`.
- `GET /api/pages/:id/thumb` — the page's picture in Pages (WebP), taken in the background when the page or its data changes; 404 until it is ready (Page.thumb is then set).
- `GET /api/pages/drafts?key=` — the pages an agent of this session is writing right now (show_html, save_page, or an HTML file written whole), and the documents, slides, spreadsheets and LaTeX documents it is saving (save_doc, save_slides, save_sheet, save_latex), as far as they came; with page instead of key, the saves of that page being written now, from any session (its editor, opened mid-way). Query: `key?: string — the session's key, page?: string — a saved page's id`. Returns: `{ drafts: [{ id — the tool call's id, tool: show_html|save_page|file|save_doc|save_slides|save_sheet|save_latex, title?, path? — an HTML file's, pageId? — the saved page it changes, text — the HTML so far, or a save's own input so far (JSON still being written), done: boolean, startedAt, key — the session, seq — counts what was sent of it, saved?: { pageId, version } — the version a save kept, failed?: true — it saved nothing }] }` (a finished one stays two minutes).
- `POST /api/pages/file-edit` — tells a session's agent that the user changed an HTML file in the page editor (the editor saves the file itself first). Body: `{ sessionKey: string, path: string — absolute, before: string, after: string — the file's text before and after }`. The agent hears of it, with a diff, with the user's next message. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `PATCH /api/pages/:id` — renames a page, gives it a description, moves it to a project or into a folder of kept things, and/or moves its file into a folder of its project (none of these is a new version). Moved to another project, its file goes into that project's folder; moved to none, its file goes to the Trash. After a move the answer says where the file is (projectFile). Body: `{ title?: string — ≤120 characters; empty becomes "Page", description?: string — what it is, cut to 100 words; empty takes it away, space?: string|null — a project's id, or null for none (it leaves a folder of another project), folder?: string|null — a folder's id (the page goes to that folder's project), or null for its project's top, dir?: string — the whole path of its project's folder or of a folder inside it, for its file (alone, it moves the file there; checked against the project it will be in: 400 otherwise, or in no project) }` (one of them at least). 400 for a project that is not one, or for an app's screen; 404 for a folder that is not one. Returns: `{ page: Page }`.
- `GET /api/library/search?q=<words>` — the kept things that fit the words, best first (what the search box's **Kept files** shows): pages of every kind and the pictures, videos and sounds kept, found by their titles and descriptions, by their words (BM25) and, with search by meaning on, by what they mean, the two lists joined by rank. Query: `q: string — up to 300 characters, limit?: number — 50 unless set, at most 200`. Returns: `{ hits: [{ id: "page:<id>"|"media:<id>", score, by: ("words"|"meaning")[], meaning? — how alike in meaning (cosine) }], meaning: boolean — whether meaning took part, starting?: true — meaning's model is starting: the same search in a few seconds finds by meaning too }`.
- `POST /api/pages/folders` — makes a folder that groups kept things (the app does not show these folders; its **New folder** makes a real folder in a project's **Files**). Body: `{ name: string — ≤80 characters (a / becomes -), parent?: string — the folder to make it in (it takes that folder's project), space?: string|null — the project at whose top it goes (left out or null: none) }`. 409 when a folder there has the name (capitals aside); 400 past 8 deep. Returns: `{ folder: Folder }`.
- `PATCH /api/pages/folders/:id` — renames a folder and/or moves it (a folder's **Rename**, **Move to a folder** and **Move to a project**). Body: `{ name?: string, parent?: string|null — a folder to move it into (it takes that folder's project), or null for its place's top, space?: string|null — the top of another project, or of none (with parent left out) }`. Moved to another project, the folders and things in it go along. A name taken where it goes gets a number ("Drafts 2"); a new name taken there is 409; 400 for a folder moved into itself or past 8 deep. Returns: `{ folder: Folder }`.
- `DELETE /api/pages/folders/:id` — deletes a folder of kept things: the things and folders in it go up a level (to its folder, or its place's top); nothing in it is deleted. Returns: `{ ok: true, moved: number — the things and folders that went up }`.
- `POST /api/pages/import-folder` — brings a folder of this computer in as kept things: a LaTeX project (a .tex file at its top that is a whole document) as one LaTeX document with its files and pictures in their folders, the files it uses first past a document's limits; any other folder as a folder of kept things with its documents, spreadsheets, notebooks and whole LaTeX documents as things in it and its folders as folders in it (one with nothing to bring in makes no folder), 300 things at most. Body: `{ path: string — absolute or from ~ (not the home folder itself, nor the root), space?: string|null — the project it goes in (left out: the session's; null: none), folder?: string — a folder of kept things to bring it into (its project wins over space), sessionKey?: string }`. 404 when there is no such folder. Returns: `{ folder?: Folder — the one made for it (none for a LaTeX project), pages: [Page] — what came in, folders: number — the folders made, skipped: [{ path — in the folder, why: picture|video|audio|pdf|slides|part (a part of a LaTeX document)|large (over 30 MB)|unreadable|kind (another kind of file)|limit }], notes: [string] — what else was said (a LaTeX project's files that did not fit) }`.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/versions/:v/restore` — brings an older version back as a new current version (its title and HTML); the stored data is not touched. Returns: `{ page: Page }`; 404 when that version is no longer kept.
- `PUT /api/pages/:id/versions/:v/name` — names a version ("Sent to ICML"; what the LaTeX editor's **Name this version** and **Compare versions** do), or takes its name away; a named version is kept past the newest 40 and never folded into an agent's next save. Body: `{ name: string — ≤80 characters; "" takes the name away }`. Returns: `{ versions: [as GET /api/pages/:id gives them] }`; 404 when that version is no longer kept.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/link` — a new own address for the page; the old one stops working at once, also on home screens it was added to. Returns: `{ link: "/pg/<token>" }`.
- `DELETE /api/pages/:id` — deletes the page with all its versions, its stored data and its published copies (a notebook: the folder it ran in too); its file in the project's folder goes to the Trash. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/boards/pin` — pins an answer card to a board (what an answer card's **More** (…) → **Pin to a board** does): the card goes at the board's end, its data copied as it is now, as a new version; with no board, a new board is made for it (in the project asked for, else the session's). Body: `{ board?: string — a board's id, title?: string — instead, a new board's name, space?: string|null — a new board's project, folder?: string — a new board's folder, sessionKey?: string — the session it was pinned from, card: { ui: string — an answer UI's name (find_ui's; another name for one works too), data: object — the JSON of the answer card's block (≤256 KB), title?: string — its title on the board, size?: s|m|l|wide — a quarter, a third, a half or the whole width (left out: its UI's own), from?: { session?: string, at?: number — the reply's place in its session, title?: string, cwd?: string, run?: string — an app run's id, task?: string — a scheduled task's id } } }`. Returns: `{ page: Page, card: { id, ui, data, title?, size, pinnedAt, by: user, from? } }`; 400 with a plain reason for a UI there is not, data that is no object or too large, a page that is no board, or a board that holds 100 cards already. A board's model is `{ format: 1, cap?: number|null — the monthly spending cap for its cards' refreshes in dollars (left out: $5; null: none), cards: [card…] }` in their order, each card `{ id, ui, data, title?, size, pinnedAt, by?, from?, refresh?: { ask: string — the question asked again, schedule?: { kind: "every", minutes: 60 } | { kind: "daily", time: "HH:MM", days?: [0–6] } — none: only when asked, tier?: quick|standard|best|max — Quick when left out, costUsd?: number — its last refresh's cost, paused?: true — it does not refresh by itself until turned on }, refreshedAt?: number }`; a board made from a model (`POST /api/pages`, `POST /api/pages/:id/copy`, an agent's copy) gets its cards' scheduled refreshes paused, so a copy never starts spending by itself; change it as any kind's, with `POST /api/pages/:id/versions` (its cards' sizes, order, titles, how each refreshes, the cap; a card taken off), and agents with save_board. A card with a schedule (not paused) is a scheduled task of the board's (id `board-<page>-<card>`, kept in step with each save: taking the card off, stopping its refresh or deleting the board removes it); `GET /api/tasks` and Schedules list it with `board: { page, card, title, cardTitle }`, and it is changed on the board, not through the task routes.
- `GET /api/boards/:id/refresh` — what a board's cards' refreshes are doing, as its page shows them. Returns: `{ cap: number|null, spentUsd: number — this month's refreshes, reached: boolean — the cap is reached (refreshes wait for the next month), monthEnds: number, tiers: { quick|standard|best|max: number|null — what a refresh typically costs on it }, cards: { [cardId]: { task? — its scheduled task, enabled?, pausedReason? — three failures in a row, nextAt?, running?: { since, key? }, last?: { status, at, error? — why it could not update, costUsd?, key? }, fails?, typicalUsd? — what its own refreshes cost } } }`; 400 for a page that is no board.
- `GET /api/boards/:id/cards/:card/question` — the question a card asks again: its own, else the request of the turn it was pinned from (or the scheduled task's prompt). Returns: `{ question: string }` (empty when there is none).
- `POST /api/boards/:id/cards/:card/refresh` — refreshes a card now (**Refresh now**, **Try again**): an agent on its tier, in an archived session that sends nothing, answers its question with a new card of the same UI, which replaces its data (a failed refresh keeps it). Returns: `{ runId, key }`; 400 for a card that reads a spreadsheet or a file, or has no question; 409 when the board's monthly cap is reached, the board is locked or the card is being refreshed already.
- `POST /api/boards/:id/refresh` — refreshes every card of the board that keeps itself up to date (**Refresh all**). Returns: `{ started: number, skipped: string[] — each card that did not start, and why }`.
- `GET /api/boards/refreshes` — every board's cards that refresh (what `hydor library refreshes` lists). Returns: `{ boards: [{ id, title, cap, spentUsd, reached }], cards: [{ board, boardTitle, card, title, ui, when — in words, tier, perRefreshUsd?, refreshedAt?, nextAt?, running?, paused?, lastError? }] }`.
- `GET /api/pages/:id/notebook` — where a kept notebook runs: the file its kernel is known by, in a folder of its own in the app's data folder (made when a kernel first starts). Returns: `{ path, savedAt — when its current version was saved }`; 400 for another kind.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/notebook` — writes the notebook, as it is kept now, to that file, for a kernel to start from it (the notebook page does this before starting one; then `POST /api/kernels` with that path). Returns: `{ path }`; 400 for another kind.

## Published sites

Agents publish with their own publish_site tool (a built folder with an index.html, or a saved page by id, and a name): the user decides on its card and picks a private or a public link, and the agent then gets a message with the outcome and the link. Don't publish with POST /api/sites. **Site**: `{ id, name, visibility: private|public, kind: folder|page, folder?, pageId?, pageTitle?, path — "/site/<token or name>/", from the app's address, createdAt, sessionKey?, description?, image?: thumb|none|naget-asset:<sha> — a published page's social picture }`.

- `GET /api/sites` — everything published, newest first. Returns: `{ sites: [Site] }`.
- `GET /api/sites/requests/:id` — a publish_site request and how the user decided. Returns: `{ request: { id, sessionKey, name, kind: folder|page, folder?, pageId?, pageTitle?, files, bytes, status: open|published|declined|failed, site?: Site, error?, createdAt, decidedAt? } }`; 404 when unknown.
- `DELETE /api/sites/:id` — unpublishes a site: its link stops working at once (publishing again makes a new private link). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `PATCH /api/sites/:id` — a published page's description and social picture, where its link is shared (its og:description and og:image). Body: `{ description?: string — up to 300 characters, image?: thumb — the page as it looks | none | "naget-asset:<sha>" — a picture the app keeps (POST /api/pages/assets) }`. Returns: `{ site: Site }`.

## Web apps (small full-stack apps)

A web app is a small app of its own that you or an agent built, in any stack, that lives in a folder on this computer and meets Hydor's contract (a `hydor.json` at its root). Hydor builds it, keeps it running while someone uses it (and stops it after 15 minutes with nobody; the person who runs Hydor can change that time with `APPS_IDLE_MINUTES`) and, when its file says so, signs the person in to it. It is told the address people reach it at, which an app needs for the links it writes to itself. It is shown at an address of its own, apart from Hydor's, so its pages can't reach anything of Hydor's. Every route here says plainly when web apps are turned off on this computer (`APPS_PORT=0`). **Web app**: `{ id, slug — the name in its address, <slug>.localhost, name, folder — an absolute path, shape: { ok — it can be read and run, problem?, problems?: [string] — everything wrong with its file, in sentences, signIn: hydor|own — Hydor says who is there, or the app has accounts of its own, build: boolean, dev: boolean — it has a development command, services: { ai, embed, mail: boolean }, database: { file, sqlite } | null, keys: number — listed in its hydor.json } — what hydor.json says, by which Hydor runs it, stage: brief|design|build|check|launch|run | null — where it is, decisions: [{ at, stage, what, choice?, shape?: designs|one }] — what was picked along the way, oldest first, project?: string — the project for its folder, where its sessions, files and memory stay together, settings: { tiers: [quick|standard|best|max], dailyCapUsd: number — a day's spending cap, default 1, apps: [string], connections: [string], notify: boolean, mail?: { on?: boolean — allowed (false: turned off; absent: not decided), account: string — the connected mail account it sends from, perDay: number, whileMaking?: true — it sends while being made too } }, mail?: { state: allowed|off|ask, canSend: boolean — this Hydor has an account that can send, account?: { id, address } — the chosen one while it is connected, accountGone?: boolean, perDay: number — for each side, launched: boolean — it has a launched version, whileMaking: boolean — the version being made sends too, today: { counted — mails accepted today, against the limit, sent, waiting, failed, refused — requests refused } — the launched version's, atLimit: boolean, making: { today: as today, atLimit } — the version being made's, resetsAt — midnight UTC, links: { host — what a link in the app's mail points at, local: boolean — a name only this computer knows (no `APPS_DOMAIN`); either way the app opens only for someone who opened it from Hydor } } — Web app's mail, for an app whose hydor.json lists `mail`, flows?: { app: { id, slug, name }, folder, key: { id, hint, ok } | null, since } — its flows half, an agent app linked to it (never the key's value), run: { state: stopped|starting|running|crashed, pid, since, lastUse, restarts, sandboxed: boolean, sandboxNote: string | null — why it isn't sandboxed, lastExit }, build: { state: none|building|built|failed, exists: boolean — it has been built, startedAt, endedAt, exitCode, problem?, log: [string] — the end of the build's output, box: { access: registry|open|own, registries: [string] } — where a build of it runs: in the sandbox with only these registries to reach, in the sandbox with the network open, or with the owner's own access (no sandbox) }, refused?: [string] — hosts the last build tried to reach and may not (it reaches only the package registry), mode: { launched: boolean — a version is launched inside this Hydor, runsAs: dev|built, servers?: [string] — the names of its places on servers of yours }, ai?: { asks: [quick|standard|best] — the tiers its hydor.json asks for, allowed: [quick|standard|best] — those its owner allowed, asksUsdPerDay?: number, capUsd: number, spentUsd: number — its model use and its flows half's runs today, today: { usd, flowsUsd, calls, inputTokens, outputTokens, people, refused, unpriced }, atCap: boolean, until: string — when the day's count starts again } — when its hydor.json lists `ai`, meaning?: { texts, limit } — texts its search had made into vectors today, and the most a day, createdAt, updatedAt }`. One asked for by id also has `log: [string]`, the last 200 lines its server printed.

**hydor.json** says what Hydor needs: `{ name?, build?: a shell line, start: one command, dev?: one command, env: { port, data, url?, secret?, host? — the variable for the address it listens on, default HOST } — the names of the variables it reads, ready: an address that answers 2xx or 3xx once it is ready, signIn: "hydor" | "own", private?: an address only a signed-in person gets (sign-in by Hydor), keys?: [{ name, from: a provider | "you" | "made", why, how?, url?, label?, optional?, group?, looks? }], services?: { ai?: { tiers, usdPerDay?, try? }, embed?, mail? }, database?: { file: a name in the data folder, sqlite }, accounts?: { signUp, signIn } — with signIn "own", requests that make a test person for the page checks, smoke?: a command or a request a launch tries it with }`. Its schema is `shared/hydor.schema.json`, and the builders' page is `references/hydor-contract.md` of the web-app-builder skill. The app is run with `HOST=127.0.0.1` (on a server `0.0.0.0`), the variables it names, its keys by name and, when it lists a service, `HYDOR_URL`, `HYDOR_APP_SLUG` and `HYDOR_APP_KEY`; its data is in a folder of Hydor's for each side (`<data>/webapps/<id>/data` and `<id>_live/data`), never in its own folder, which is read-only in the sandbox (a development command may write in it).

**Which side runs.** An app that is being made is not a production app: **Preview** runs its development command (`dev`; without one, `start` after `build`). **A launched version** (see Launch and Run below) is another server on another folder: the launched commit, built, in `<data>/webapps/<id>/live/releases/<launch>/`, with a data folder of its own kept between versions. The two run at the same time, at two hostnames: `<slug>.localhost` is the launched version (while there is one; before, the version being made), `<slug>--preview.localhost` the one being changed.

**An app's functions as its agents' tools.** A web app with an agent app of its own writes `functions.json` at its root: `{ format: 1, app, functions: [{ name, description, input, writes, path? }] }`. `name` is lower case letters, digits and `_` (at most 40 letters: agents' tool names are short), `description` is what the agent reads to know when to call it (at most 600 characters), `input` is a JSON Schema object (`{ type: "object", properties: { … }, required? }`: the shape of what the function takes) and `writes` says it changes what the app keeps. `path` is the address on the app that serves the function, `/api/agent-functions/<name>` when it is left out. The app has to serve that address: Hydor sends `POST <path>` with the input as JSON and the header `x-hydor-person`, signed by Hydor with the app's secret for the person the run acts for, so the app's own access rules decide what that person may touch, exactly as for a click; the app answers with JSON (any 2xx) or, when it refuses, a status with `{ error: "a sentence" }`.

Hydor reads the file when a run of the flows half begins, and only when the agent app's tools allow a function (see `PUT /api/webapps/:id/flows/functions`: entries `fn:<name>`, or `fn:*` for the functions that only read; a new link allows none, and a list of tools that is "all of Hydor's own" does not reach into the app's functions). A missing or broken file, a function that cannot be offered and a run that acts for nobody are each said on the run, in `misses`, and the run goes on without them. For each function allowed, the run's agents (the run's own session, and each agent step's) have a tool named `appfn_<name>`, and a tool step can call one as `fn:<name>`. The tool list is what it was when the run began, whatever the file says afterwards. A run acts for the person Hydor proved when it began (a token, or the person signed in to Hydor on its own pages; a child run of an agent step carries its parent's): a run for nobody, such as one a job of the web app started, gets none. The app is started when the run begins, so a slow start is over before the first call, and held awake until the run ends. A call that takes longer than 30 seconds fails; an answer is cut to 20,000 characters, with a line saying so; one run may make 60 calls, 20 a minute, and all the runs of one web app 120 a minute (a call costs no model use, so the web app's daily cap is not touched). Each call is a step of the run (`fn#1`, `fn#2`…: which function, for whom, how it ended, never what the app returned).

**The same functions for agents in sessions.** The owner can also offer each function to the agents in their sessions (`PUT /api/webapps/:id/functions/sessions`; a second switch on the Flows tab). An agent then finds them with the tool `webapp_list_functions` and calls one with `webapp_call_function`, acting as the person the session belongs to (this Hydor's owner), with the header signed as for a run. A function that changes things asks the person on a card the first time a session calls it, unless the owner chose to offer it without asking; Plan mode refuses it. A session may make 20 calls a minute and 60 an hour, and the web app's 120 a minute are shared with its runs. The app is called at the version people use: its launched version when it is launched inside Hydor (reading that version's own `functions.json`), else the version being made. An app's own steps never get these two tools: their agents have the `appfn_` tools of their own app.

- `GET /api/webapps` — the web apps, each with its state. Returns: `{ reach: { enabled: boolean, port: number | null — where the apps' own server listens, origin: string | null, domain: string | null, sandbox: boolean, error: string | null — why that server isn't listening }, apps: [Web app] }`.
- `POST /api/webapps` — registers an app that is already in a folder, with a hydor.json at its root. Body: `{ folder: string — a full path to it, name?: string — default: the name in that file, else the folder's }`. Returns: `{ app: Web app }`; 400 for a folder with no hydor.json, a file that cannot be used yet (every problem in its own line) or a folder inside (or holding) Hydor's own data folder, 409 for one that is already registered. The app is not built or started yet.
- `POST /api/webapps/scratch` — makes a new app before a line of it is written: an empty folder of its own, a git repository, a project and the app's place in the list, at the stage `brief` (it shows as being made until its hydor.json is there). **What do you want to make?** does this, and agents with `webapp_new`. Body: `{ name: string, folder?: string — a full path that does not exist or is empty, default: a folder in Hydor's web apps folder, placeholder?: boolean — the name is not yours: the agent may suggest one }`. Returns: `{ app: Web app, folder }`; 409 when the folder is not empty. A conversation that starts from the sheet is told what was picked through `extra.webKind` of `POST /api/sessions`: `{ kind?: an idea's id, name?, line?, app?: the new web app's id, agentApp?: an agent app to give pages of its own }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/check` — runs the check of the app as a job (returns at once): on a copy of its folder it reads hydor.json, builds (the build line runs as any build does: in the sandbox, with only the package registry to reach), starts the app in the sandbox on a port and data folder of its own with made-up keys and a stand-in for Hydor's services, and tries each thing the file says (ready, listening where it is told and nowhere else, sign-in by Hydor's header, no key in anything a browser gets, each service asked as Hydor takes it, its smoke test, stopping when asked, keeping data across a restart, writing only in the data folder), then, while that copy runs, looks at its pages in a browser (forms posted too; signed in by Hydor's header, or as a test person its `accounts` make). `GET /api/webapps/:id/checks` has it, with `running` while it goes. Returns: `{ running: { startedAt, step } }`; 409 while it is being built or while a check runs.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id` — one web app with the last lines of its log. Returns: `{ app: Web app }`; 404 when unknown.
- `PUT /api/webapps/:id` — changes its name and what it may use of Hydor (`dailyCapUsd` is the most the app's model use and its flows half's runs may spend in a day, together; `tiers` are the model tiers its owner allows it, none until they do; see `POST /api/apps/:slug/ai` and Apps, from outside; `mail` is its owner's say on the app's mail, see `POST /api/apps/:slug/mail`: a change to it asks the user on a card every time; the rest is stored). Its address keeps its first name, except that an app made without a name takes its address from the first name it gets, while nobody uses it. Body: `{ name?: string, settings?: { tiers?, dailyCapUsd?: number, apps?: [string], connections?: [string], notify?: boolean, mail?: { on?: boolean — allow it (needs an account) or turn it off, account?: string — the id of a connected mail account that can send (`GET /api/webapps/:id/mail` lists them), perDay?: number — 1 to 10,000, whileMaking?: boolean — send from the version being made too (only once allowed; off by default, and off again when mail is turned off) } } — fields you leave out stay as they are }`. Returns: `{ app: Web app }`.
- `DELETE /api/webapps/:id` — stops the app and forgets it. Its folder and the data it kept stay (you can register the folder again), unless you add `?data=1`, which also removes what Hydor kept for it (its data, its secret and its build log). Its flows half, when it has one, stays as an agent app (its key is revoked). Returns: `{ ok: true, data: kept|removed, flows?: { kept: true, app, folder, note } }`; 409 while it is being built. The app's own keys go with it, each place's; `keysRemoved?: [string]` names them. What runs on a server of yours keeps running: `servers?: [{ env, host, address }]` says where.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/build` — builds it: runs the app's own build (the `build` line of its hydor.json; one with none is only stopped, so it starts afresh) in its folder, with an environment of its own (nothing of Hydor's reaches it), in the sandbox where there is one (`build.box`: only the app's folder, a home of the build's own and the package registry), stopping the app first if it runs. It returns at once with `build.state` `building`; ask for the app again to see `built` or `failed`, with the end of the output (up to 15 minutes). One build at a time per app. Returns: `{ app: Web app }`; 409 while it is already being built or when its hydor.json cannot be read.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/start` — starts it and waits until it answers (about 20 seconds at most; a development command has four times as long, as its first start may compile the app). An app that is being made runs its development command, so nothing has to be built first; one with none runs its build. Returns: `{ app: Web app }`; 409 when it isn't built yet (and has to be) or is being built, 502 with the reason when it doesn't start.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/stop` — stops it. Returns: `{ app: Web app }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/data` — the tables of what the app keeps in its own database (the SQLite file its hydor.json names, 404 when it names none; in the data folder Hydor gives that side), to look through, read only. Hydor opens the file itself, read only, from that folder and from nowhere else. Query: `side?: launched — the launched version's data instead (409 when there is none), file?: string — another database file of that folder, by its name (it ends in .db, .sqlite or .sqlite3); a name with a path in it, or a link or a file that is not the app's own, is refused with 400`. Returns: `{ file: string, exists: boolean — false until the app has made its database, tables: [{ name, rows: number, columns: [{ name, type, pk: boolean }] }] }`; 400 for a file that is not a SQLite database or not a plain file of the folder, 503 while the app is using its data.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/data/:table` — one page of a table's rows, in the order the file keeps them. The table has to be one that `GET /api/webapps/:id/data` lists; nothing is ever run as a query. Values of columns that hold a password, a hash, a salt, a token or a key are not sent (`••••••` in their place), cells longer than 2000 characters are cut, and binary data is told as `[4 bytes of binary data]`. Query: `{ offset?: number — the first row, default 0, limit?: number — rows in the page, 1 to 200, default 50, file?: string — as above }`. Returns: `{ table, columns: [{ name, type, pk, hidden?: boolean — its values are not shown }], rows: [[cell]] — each row a list of cells in the order of the columns (text, number or null), total: number — rows in the table, offset, limit, truncated: boolean — some cell was cut }`; 404 for a table the file does not have or an app with no data yet.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/open` — starts the app if it isn't running and gives an address that signs this person in to it, the best way for the address the request came from: an address of its own for the app (`<slug>.localhost` when Hydor is opened on localhost or 127.0.0.1, `<slug>.<APPS_DOMAIN>` when that is set), else a path with a token for a tunnel with a single hostname (the page then runs in a sandbox). The address works once, within two minutes. Body: `{ path?: string — where in the app to open, starts with a slash, default /, frame?: boolean — the address is for showing the app inside Hydor's own page (an app's Preview): the app then lets that page, and no other, frame it, and keeps its session as a cookie of that page; it has no effect on the path way, whose page is not framed, point?: boolean — the app's pages in that frame get Hydor's pointing helper (a tap on a part of the app is told to Hydor's page: its words, what kind of thing it is, and the page it is on); only for a frame of Hydor's own page, resume?: boolean — open at the page the frame was last at (as far as Hydor saw it) instead of `path`, so turning pointing on does not lose the place }`. Returns: `{ url: string, way: hostname|path }`; 409 when it isn't built yet or is being built.

- `GET /api/webapps/kinds` — the ideas of **What do you want to make?**, as that page and the agent's kind card show them. They are ideas to start from, not templates: every one can be started. Returns: `{ kinds: [{ id, name, who — one sentence on who it is for, needs: [string] — what such an app usually needs, agent?: true — an agent app with pages of its own }] }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/flows` — gives the app a flows half: an agent app, run by Hydor's agents, that its own pages start and follow with a key. The agent app's definition is kept as files in a folder `flows` in the app's own folder (the folder an agent app can always be kept in: **Its folder** on its page), and the two are linked on both sides: `GET` of the web app shows `flows`, and the agent app has `webapp`. Either an agent app is made from the recipe the folder `flows` already holds (`flows/app.json`, with the files it points at, checked as any recipe is; the folder's other files stay), or from a small starting recipe when there is no folder (one flow, a form with one question, one agent step, at most $0.50 a run), or an agent app that is there is linked: its definition moves into that folder, and its address, keys, schedules and runs stay. A key for the web app is made on the agent app (named after the web app: an existing app's own keys cannot be used, as Hydor keeps only a hash of a key), kept by Hydor in a file of its own beside the app's secret, and given to the web app only in its environment when it starts: `HYDOR_URL` (where it reaches Hydor from this computer), `HYDOR_APP_SLUG` (the agent app's address) and `HYDOR_APP_KEY`. No route shows the key, and it is never written in the app's folder. A key that was revoked is made again at the next start. A web app that is running is stopped (it starts again at its next use, with these). Only that key may start a run for a person, and only with the signed token of that person (`personToken`). Body: `{ app?: string — an agent app's id or address (not a ready-made one); without it a new one is made }`. Returns: `{ app: Web app, flows: { app: { id, slug, name }, folder: string | null, key: { id, hint, ok: boolean — it still opens the app } | null, since }, created: boolean — a new agent app was made, from: "folder" | "starting" | "app" — where its definition came from: the recipe the folder held, the starting recipe, or the agent app named }`; 400 `{ error, problems: [string] }` when the recipe in the folder cannot be used (it names what is wrong, in plain words: a file it points at that is not there, a step that cannot run, no task, screens, which a linked app does not have), 409 when the app has a flows half already, when the agent app belongs to another web app or its folder holds this web app, or when the folder `flows` in the web app's folder is not free (it has other files but no `app.json` to say what the agent app is, or files while an agent app is named, or is a file or a link), 404 for an agent app that is not there; nothing is left half-made.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/flows/functions` — the functions the web app offers its agents (what its `functions.json` says: see below) and which the flows half allows. Returns: `{ file: "ok" | "missing" | "broken" — missing: the app has no `functions.json` (or has not written it yet), problems: [string] — what is wrong, in plain words, one for each function left out, functions: [{ name, description, writes: boolean — it changes what the app keeps, allowed: boolean — the agent app's tools allow it, withoutAsking: boolean — it changes things and the owner chose that it need not ask the person first, path — the address on the app that serves it, inSessions: boolean — the owner offers it to agents in sessions too, inSessionsWithoutAsking: boolean — it changes things and may be called from a session without asking the person }], allowedIn: string — where the maker allows them }`; 404 when the app has no flows half.
- `PUT /api/webapps/:id/flows/functions` — allows some of the web app's functions to the agents of its flows half, by editing the `fn:` entries of the agent app's tools (the app gets a new version, and its folder is written). Body: `{ allow: "*" | string[], free?: string[] }` — `"*"` is every function that only reads; a function that changes what the app keeps (`writes`) is allowed only when its name is in the list (`["*", "add_trip"]`); `[]` allows none. A function that changes things asks the person on a card the first time an agent calls it in a run (once for each function in a run), unless its name is also in `free` (the entry `fn:<name>!` in the agent app's tools): the owner's choice to allow it without asking. A function that only reads never asks, and `free` holds only functions that are allowed and change things. An agent app whose tools are "all of Hydor's own" (no list) becomes the list of those tools as they are now, with the functions added, since only a list can name a function. Returns: as `GET`, with `tools` — the agent app's tools now; 400 for a name the app does not offer, saying what it does offer, 404 when the app has no flows half.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/functions` — the same view as `GET …/flows/functions`, for any web app (no flows half needed): the functions it declares with both switches, the one for the agents of its own flows (`allowed`, `withoutAsking`) and the one for agents in sessions (`inSessions`, `inSessionsWithoutAsking`).
- `PUT /api/webapps/:id/functions/sessions` — offers some of the web app's functions to the agents in sessions. Body: `{ allow: "*" | string[], free?: string[] }`, as for `PUT …/flows/functions`: `"*"` is every function that only reads, a function that changes what the app keeps is offered only when named, `[]` offers none, and `free` names those of them that change things and need not ask the person first. Nothing is offered until then. Returns: as `GET …/functions`; 400 for a name the app does not declare (saying what it does) or a `free` that is not offered or only reads.
- `GET /api/webapps/functions/offered` — what agents in sessions are offered, by app. Returns: `{ apps: [{ app: { id, slug, name }, version: "launched" | "in progress" — the version a call goes to: the launched one when the app is launched inside Hydor (its own `functions.json`), else the one being made, functions: [{ name, description, input, writes, withoutAsking }], problems: [string] }] }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/functions/:name/call` — calls a function that is offered to sessions, as the person signed in (this Hydor's owner): Hydor signs the header for them with the app's secret, exactly as for a run, so the app's own access rules decide what they may touch. Body: `{ input?: object, session?: string — the session's key, which the limits of a session count by }`. Returns: `{ text — the app's answer, cut to 20,000 characters, chars, ms, as: { id, name }, version, writes }`; 403 for a function that is not offered, 404 when the app does not declare it, 413 for an input over 20,000 characters, 429 past a session's 20 calls a minute and 60 an hour or the app's 120 a minute, 502 with the app's own sentence when it refuses or fails (`error`, `ended`, `failure`). Agents use the tool `webapp_call_function`, which asks the person on a card first for a function that changes things (the first time in a session); this route is refused to `app_api`.
- `DELETE /api/webapps/:id/flows` — unlinks the flows half: the agent app stays, with its folder moved back to where agent apps keep theirs (Hydor/Apps/<address>, a new name if that is taken), its own keys and runs as they were; the key made for the web app is revoked and forgotten, and a running web app is stopped. The agent app cannot be deleted while it is linked (409). Returns: `{ app: Web app, flows: null, agentApp: { id, slug, name, folder } | null }`; 404 when the app has none. Forgetting the web app (`DELETE /api/webapps/:id`) does the same without moving the folder: it says `flows: { kept: true, app: { id, slug, name }, folder, note }` and leaves the agent app.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/flows/overview` — what the app's **Flows** tab shows. Returns: `{ app: { id, slug, name, description }, flows: [as above], runs: [{ id, flow, status, person: { id, name } | null — who it acted for, inputs?: string — what was asked in a line, createdAt, endedAt?, costUsd?, ref?, path — Hydor's own page for the run }] — the last 15, functions: { file, problems, list: [{ name, description, writes, allowed, withoutAsking, inSessions, inSessionsWithoutAsking }] }, spend: { todayUsd, capUsd, meaning?: { texts, limit } } — what this app's runs started with its key spent today and its cap for a day (its Settings), and, for an app whose hydor.json lists `embed`, the texts its search had Hydor make into vectors today and the most a day }`; 404 when the app has no flows half.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/flows/offer` — what **Add agents' work** can link to this app: `{ apps: [{ id, slug, name, description, icon, color, flows }] — the agent apps that are free, starting: { name, description, flow } — the small starting recipe, problem?: string — it has agents' work already }`. Linking is `POST /api/webapps/:id/flows` with `{ app }`, or without it for the starting recipe; the app's own pages are then written by an agent, with the requests of the builders' page.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/shot` — a picture of a page of the running app, taken here the way Preview's pictures of dev servers are (starts the app when it isn't running; one with no development command has to be built). Body: `{ path?: string — a path in the app, starts with a slash, default /, as?: { name, role? } — who looks, for an app that Hydor signs people in to; default the signed-in person, device?: phone|desktop — 390 or 1280 wide, default desktop, scheme?: dark|light — the colour scheme the page is told the device prefers, default dark }`. Returns: a PNG (`x-shot-size` says its size in pixels; `x-picture` is the id it is kept under, for `GET /api/webapps/:id/pictures/:pid` and for the picture of an option on a card); 400 for a body that does not fit, 409 while it builds, 502 with the reason when the app does not start or no browser can take the picture.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/pictures/:pid` — a picture kept from `POST /api/webapps/:id/shot` or taken of a design's page (the last 160 are kept for each app). Returns: the PNG; 404 for one that is not there.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/brief` — the app's own brief, the file `docs/brief.md` in its folder, which the agent that made it keeps true. Returns: `{ exists: boolean, text: string — Markdown, at most 40,000 characters }`. A link out of the folder is not followed.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/steps` — the app's steps. Each web app's folder is a git repository of its own (Hydor makes one when it makes or registers an app that has none, leaving out packages, builds, data and keys), and each accepted change is a **step** with words that say what changed for the maker. Query: `limit?: number — at most 200, default 50`. Returns: `{ repo: boolean — the folder is a repository, steps: [{ sha, message, at — ms since 1970, files: number — how many it changed, back?: { to: sha } — it put the files back as they were at that step }] — newest first, unsaved: number — files changed and not in a step yet }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/steps` — keeps what changed as a step. Body: `{ message: string — what changed for the maker, in a few plain words, at most 200 characters, stage?: brief|design|build|check|launch|run — the stage the app is at now }`. Returns: `{ step: { sha, message, at, files } | null — null when nothing had changed }`; 400 for no words or a stage that is not one, 409 while it is being built.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/steps/:sha/back` — **Back to before this**: puts the app's files back as they were before the step `sha` (that step and the ones after it are undone in the files), saves what was not saved yet as a step first, records the going back as a step of its own on top, and builds the app again. Nothing is destroyed: the steps after it stay in the list, and going back can itself be gone back from. Returns: `{ went: { from, to, step, saved } — the step clicked, the one before it, the step this made, the one made first from unsaved work (null when none), app: Web app }`; 404 for a step the app does not have, 409 for the first step (nothing is before it), while it is being built.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/choices` — the choices that were put to the maker of the app and answered, oldest first (`webapp_choose`: designs to compare, or one of a few). Returns: `{ choices: [{ id, shape: designs|one, stage, title, at, offered: Card, answer, summary — the decision in a sentence }] }`. **Card**: `{ id, app, shape, stage, title, message?, designs?: [{ id, name, line, page — the file it was taken from, pictures: { desktop?, phone? }, light? }], options?: [{ id, title, line?, picture?, url? }], other?, submit? }`. **Answer**: `{ shape: designs, pick, take?: string — in words, what to take from another }` or `{ shape: one, pick?, other? }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/choices/:cid` — one card, answered or waiting. Returns: `{ choice: Choice | null, offered: Card, waiting: boolean }`; 404 for one that was never put.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/choices` — puts a card to the maker (what the `webapp_choose` tool does first) and keeps it; the app moves to that stage. For designs, Hydor takes a picture of each direction's page first (computer and phone, dark and light). Body: `{ shape: designs|one, stage?, title, message?, submit?, designs?: [{ id?, name, line, page — an HTML file in the app's folder }] — two or three, options?: [{ id?, title, line?, picture?, url? }] — two to six, other?: boolean — the maker may write an answer of their own }`. Returns: `{ choose: Card }`; 400 with every problem in words (a page that is not there among them), 502 when no browser can take the pictures.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/choices/:cid/answer` — records an answer to a card that was put: it is read against what was offered and kept as a decision of the app (`decisions`). An answer that was recorded already is not recorded again. A card answered after the agent's wait ended is recorded by this same route as it is given, and the agent's next message says so. Body: `{ answer }` as above. Returns: `{ choice, already? }`; 400 for an answer that is not to this card.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/checks` — the last check of the app, as lines a person can read: first what its hydor.json says (`group` `contract`: file, build, ready, loopback, signin, secrets, ai, embed, mail, smoke, stop, data, writes), then, from the same throwaway copy while it runs, what a browser meets on its pages (`group` `safety`, `pages` or `accessibility`: phone layout, reading, weight, what a page shows that it should not, broken things). Returns: `{ ran: boolean, at: number | null, summary: string | null, ok: boolean | null — nothing failed (a line that could not be tried is not a failure), lines: [{ id, state: passed|failed|note|notRun, text, hint? — what to do about it, title?, group?: contract|safety|pages|accessibility, details?: [string] — where, fixed?: boolean — it failed in the run before and passes now, until the next run }], counts?: { passed, failed, couldNotRun }, pagesSummary?: string — the page checks in at most a dozen lines, signedIn?: string — why signed-in pages were not looked at, when they were not, notCovered?: [string] — what no script can judge, problem?: string — the check did not finish, running?: { startedAt, step } — while one goes }`. Run it with `POST /api/webapps/:id/check`.
- `GET /api/webapps/hosts` — the servers you added for your web apps. Returns: `{ hosts: [Host] }`. **Host**: `{ name, ssh — user@address[:port], user, address, port, state: made|recorded — made: its key waits to be put on the server and its fingerprint accepted, publicKey, authorizedLine — the one line to put on the server, fingerprint?, keyType?, checked?: { at, ok, looked: [{ what, ok: boolean | null, say }] }, apps?: [string] — what Hydor deployed there }`. Their keys are Hydor's own, in its data folder, never your `~/.ssh`.
- `POST /api/webapps/hosts` — adds a server: Hydor makes an ed25519 key pair for it (or takes the one made ahead for an app) and contacts nobody. Body: `{ name: string — lower-case letters, digits and hyphens, ssh: string — user@address or user@address:port, key?: string — an app's id: use the key GET /api/webapps/:id/server-key gave }`. Returns: `{ host }`; 400 for a name or address that is not one, 409 for a name already used.
- `POST /api/webapps/hosts/:name/connect` — reads the fingerprints the machine offers; nothing is recorded. Returns: `{ offered: [{ type, fingerprint }], where — where to compare one, problem? }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/hosts/:name/accept` — records the server with the fingerprint **you** compared with your provider's or the machine's own. Agents cannot: they use `webapp_host`, which asks you on a card. Body: `{ fingerprint: string — SHA256:… }`. Returns: `{ host }`; 409 for a fingerprint the machine does not show, or another than the one recorded.
- `POST /api/webapps/hosts/:name/check` — looks at what is on the server (Docker, Compose, memory, disk, ports 80 and 443, cron); with `prepare`, installs what is missing on Debian or Ubuntu (Docker from its own repository, cron, a firewall that lets in SSH, 80 and 443), which can take minutes. Body: `{ prepare?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ ok, summary, looked: [{ what, ok: boolean | null, say }], steps: [string] — what is left to do by hand, prepared: boolean }`; 409 before it is recorded, 502 when it cannot be reached or shows another key.
- `DELETE /api/webapps/hosts/:name` — forgets the server, the key made for it and what Hydor deployed there; the server itself is not touched. Returns: `{ ok: true }`; 404 for none.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/guide` — what the simple view shows beside an app: the things to try (the agent writes them as a list in the app's `docs/try.md`), what you found of each, and what the agent says it is doing. Returns: `{ try: [{ id, text, result?: works|wrong, note?, at?, again?: boolean — a step was saved since, try it again }], doing: { text, at } | null, placeholderName?: boolean — the app has no name of yours yet }`.
- `PUT /api/webapps/:id/guide` — says what is being made now, or suggests a name for an app that has none of yours (once). Body: `{ doing?: string — a few plain words, "" clears it, name?: string }`. Returns: the same as GET.
- `PUT /api/webapps/:id/guide/try` — keeps what you found of one thing to try. Body: `{ id: string, result: works|wrong|null — null forgets it, note?: string — what happened }`. Returns: the same as `GET /api/webapps/:id/guide`; 404 for no such thing.
- `GET /api/webapps/online-help` — where a server can be rented and a web address bought, from the services catalog, with the clicks for each. Returns: `{ providers: [{ id, name, why, page, price?, read?, catch?, server?: { read, login, open, clicks: [string], box, boxThen, address, compare, compareLine, sources } }], registrars: [{ id, name, why, page, price?, read?, catch?, record?: { read, open, where, type: [field, value], name, value, then, apex, sources } }] }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/server-key` — the key to put on a server before you know its address (a provider asks for it as the server is made): Hydor makes it once for the app and keeps the private half. `POST /api/webapps/hosts` then takes it with `key: <the app's id>`. Returns: `{ line: string — the one line to paste }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/server-text` — the text you paste into your provider's box as you create a new server (cloud-init user data): it gives the server its identity, lets Hydor in and installs what a launch needs. It holds the private half of that identity, which is only for the first connection, and only you can ask for it: agents are refused. The same text until a server has connected with it. Returns: `{ text: string }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/server-connect` — connects to the server you created with that text: only if it shows exactly the identity the text gave it. Over that connection the server makes itself a new identity, which Hydor records and connects under before it takes the server; the text’s identity is then dropped, on the server and here. Agents are refused. Body: `{ address: string — the server's address, with :port if not 22 }`. Returns: `{ state: "ready" | "starting" | "unfinished" | "other" | "unsafe", why?: "make" | "swap" | "again", host?: string, looked?: [{ what, ok, say }] }`; "starting" means ask again in a while, "unfinished" that something is missing (`POST /api/webapps/hosts/:name/check` with `prepare`), "other" that what answers is not that server, "unsafe" that its identity could not be replaced, so nothing was recorded (try again).
- `GET /api/webapps/dns` — whether a web address leads to a server yet. Query: `name` (the web address), `host` (a server's name). Returns: `{ points: boolean, found: [string], want: string }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/steps/:sha/diff` — what one step changed, as git shows it. Returns: `{ sha, diff: string, cut: boolean }`; 404 for no such step.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/code` — the app's code as a `.tar.gz`: what a launch would send, without its data, keys, installed packages, builds or git history.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/contract` — the text of the app's hydor.json (`""` when it has none). Returns: `{ text }`.
- `PUT /api/webapps/:id/contract` — writes the app's hydor.json, only when Hydor can read it. Body: `{ text: string }`. Returns: `{ ok: true }`; 400 with what is wrong.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/values` — the names of the variables each place is started with (never a key's value), and the command that runs the contract's checker on its folder. Returns: `{ values: [{ name, what: port|host|data|secret|url|key }], check?: string }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/launch` — what the **Launch** tab shows. Returns: `{ places: [{ id: hydor|server, title, line — what it means in a sentence, effort — what it takes from the person, cost — each month, state: ready|unavailable, unavailable?: string — why this app cannot go there and what to do (an app with sign-in by Hydor cannot go to a server), launched?: { env, at, version?, address?, host? }, offers?, notOffered? }], hydor: { launched: Launch | null, previous: Launch | null, openable: boolean, ahead: number | null — steps the version being changed is ahead of the launched one, unsaved: boolean, run: Run state | null }, envs: [{ app, env — the place's name, production or another, host, name — the deployment's name on the server, address, commit, at, keys: [Key], last: Launch | null }] — what Hydor remembers being deployed on servers, hosts: [Host], serverBuild?: { ok, say } — what a launch to a server would build it with, or why it cannot, job: Job | null — the one going, or the last that ended, history: [Launch], blocked: string | null }`. **Launch**: `{ id, app, place: hydor|server, env, host?, address?, commit, version?, by, at, ms, result: live|failed|rolled-back|back, overrides: [string], backup?, previous?, downtimeSeconds?, summary?, remote? }`, kept by Hydor in its own database. **Key** (on a server): `{ name, state: vault|server|made|missing — saved for that place, held by the server from a launch before, made by the server, or still to be asked for, why? }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/launch/hydor` — launches the app **inside this Hydor** as a job (returns at once). It saves what is not saved yet as a step, then takes these steps, each shown as it ends: `install` (the commit, in a folder of its own), `build`, `check` (the check of the contract: a failed line puts a card, below; a file that cannot be read, a failed build or an app that never answers stop it), `data` (a data folder of its own for the launched version, and a backup of the one before first), `start`, `ready`, `smoke` (a look) and `record`. A failure after the start puts the version before back by itself. The version being changed keeps running beside it. Body: `{ override?: [string] — check line ids to let through before it asks }`. Returns: `{ job }`; 409 while a launch is going.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/launch/server` — launches the app to a server of yours as a job (returns at once), from a checkout of the saved commit (what is not saved yet is saved as a step first), never the folder being edited. Its steps: `check` (Hydor's check of the app: every failed line of its hydor.json puts a card, and of its pages only a key in a page, a form another site can post to, an unprotected sign-in cookie and a server error), `host`, `keys`, `address`, `files`, `send`, `build` (on the server), `configure` (https by Caddy, the keys in files only their owner reads), `snapshot`, `switch`, `ready`, `smoke` (the ready address, the first page and the app's own `smoke`), `backup` (made and read back before the launch counts) and `record`. A failure from the switch on puts the version before back by itself. The app's keys for that place go to the server and nowhere else. Body: `{ host: string, env?: string — the place's name, default production, address: string — https://… a name that points at the server, override?: [string] — check line ids to let through }`. Returns: `{ job }`; 409 for an app that cannot go to a server, a host not recorded, keys missing (`missing: [string]`: give them first with the keys card for that place), the place deployed on another server, or while a launch is going.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/launch/job` — the launch that is going, or the last that ended. Returns: `{ job: { id, app, place, action: launch|back, env, host?, address?, state: running|asking|done|failed, steps: [{ id, title, state: waiting|running|passed|failed|skipped, say?, details?: [string], ms? }], summary?, problems?: [string], notes?: [string], needsData?: boolean — a way back that only goes with the data put back too, downtimeSeconds?, previousBack?: boolean — the version before came back by itself, ask?: { kind: "override", stops: [{ id, title, group?, text, hint? }] }, overrides?: [string], startedAt, endedAt, record? } | null }`. A step's words never hold a key's value.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/launch/answer` — answers the card a launch put when lines of the check failed. Body: `{ override?: [string] — ids of failed lines to let through (each is written in the launch record with the person's name), cancel?: boolean }`. A line that did not fail is refused (400): a typo lets nothing through. Returns: `{ job }`; 409 when nothing is waiting.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/launch/keys` — where each key a launch to a server needs stands. Query: `env?: string — default production, host?: string`. Returns: `{ env, keys: [Key] }`. Give the missing ones with `POST /api/webapps/:id/keys/card` (`{ env }`).
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/keys` — the keys the app's hydor.json lists, by name: who gives each and how to get it, and whether the app has a value (its keys are its own: inside Hydor the launched version and Preview share them, each place on a server has its own, and no other app gets them). Query: `env?: string — default hydor; a place on a server (production); all for every key with what is set in each place`. Returns: `{ env, envs: [string], servers: [string], inside: boolean — payments take a test key only, keys: [{ name, givenBy: provider|you|host|made — host and made are never asked for, needed, set, savedAt?, refused?: live, places?: [{ env, set, savedAt?, needed, refused? }] — with env=all, from?, label?, why?, how?: [string], url?, looks?, group?: { id, title? }, secret?, or?: [string] — keys any one of which does instead }] }`. `looks`: `{ prefix?, pattern?, say?, not?: [{ starts?, pattern?, say }], whole? }`. Never a value. An agent asks the person for the missing ones with its `webapp_keys` tool.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/launch/history` — each launch inside this Hydor, newest first, with who and the commit; with `env`, the launches and ways back of that place on a server, as the server keeps them (Hydor's own when it cannot be reached). Query: `env?: string — default hydor`. Returns: `{ history: [Launch] }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/launch/back` — what **Back to the launch before** would do now. Returns: `{ ok: boolean, refuse?: string — in a sentence, target?: Launch }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/launch/back` — goes back to the launch before, as a job; going back again goes forward. Inside Hydor, or on a server with `place: "server"`. It starts the one before on the data as it is now; `restoreDatabase` also puts the data back from the backup taken just before the launch being undone (a backup of the data as it is now is made first; what was written since is lost). On a server, a database that changed shape since refuses without it (the job says `needsData`). Body: `{ place?: server, env?: string, restoreDatabase?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ job }`; 409 when there is no way back.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/launch/takedown` — takes the app off a server: it stops and its containers go; its data, keys and backups stay there, and launching it again finds them. Body: `{ env: string }`. Returns: `{ summary }`; 404 for a place it is not deployed to.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/run` — what the **Run** tab shows: the status of each place the app is launched to (inside this Hydor, and each place on a server), and the weekly check. Returns: `{ places: [Status], weekly: WeeklyCheck | null }`. **Status**: `{ place: hydor, or the place's name, kind: hydor|server, host?, up: boolean | null, say, version?, since?, lastLaunch?: { at, result, version? }, lastBackup?: { id, at, ageHours }, disk?: { freeGb, say }, errors?, requests? — counted when the app's log is JSON, address?, services?: [{ name, up }], schedule?: { at, keepDays }, problem? }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/run/:place/status` — is it up (`:place` is `hydor` or a place on a server): inside Hydor from the host and the app's log (an app nobody used for a while is asleep, not down, and `?wake=1` starts it to look); on a server from the server and its real address. Returns: `{ status }`; 409 before a launch inside this Hydor, 404 for a place it is not deployed to.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/run/:place/logs` — the app's own lines, newest last, with anything that looks like a secret hidden. Query: `tail?: number — at most 1000, default 200, errors?: 1 — errors and warnings first, since?: string — on a server, 30m, 6h or 2d, default 1h, service?: app|caddy|backup — on a server, default app`. Returns: `{ lines: [string] }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/run/:place/restart` — a polite restart (the app finishes what it is answering, then starts, and is waited for). Returns: `{ ok: boolean, summary, details?: [string] }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/run/:place/backups` — the backups, newest first, and when they are made. Each is SQLite's online backup of the database (the SQLite file hydor.json names; an app that names none is stopped while its folder is copied, and its backup says `stopped: true`), the rest of the data folder and a manifest of the rows of each table, made daily at the time set (default 03:30 UTC, kept 14 days, the newest three always) and before a launch replaces a version; each counts only after it was restored into a scratch folder and read back. On a server they are made and kept there. Returns: `{ backups: [{ id, label: manual|launch|scheduled|pre-launch|before-restore|…, at, bytes, tables: { name: rows }, files?, stopped?, elsewhere? }], schedule: { at, keepDays } | null }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/run/:place/backups` — makes one now and reads it back. Returns: `{ backup, verified, summary }`.
- `PUT /api/webapps/:id/run/:place/backups/schedule` — the daily time (UTC) and how many days to keep. Body: `{ at?: "HH:MM", keepDays?: number — 1 to 365 }`. Returns: `{ schedule }`; 400 for a time or number that is not one.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/run/:place/backups/:bid/restore` — puts a backup back: the app **stops** for the seconds this takes, what is there now is backed up first (`before-restore`), the backup is checked and put back, and the app starts again. Whatever was written since the backup is lost. Returns: `{ safety: string | null — the id of the backup of what was there, … }`; 404 for a backup that is not there.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/run/:place/backups/:bid/download` — the archive (`application/gzip`); from a server, a copy is brought here, sent and not kept.
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/run/weekly` — the last weekly check. Returns: `{ weekly: { at, needs: [{ id, say, tab, days?: number — how old a too-old backup is }], looked: [string], next } | null }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/run/weekly` — runs the check now. Once a week Hydor looks at each launched app (is it up, are errors rising, is the last backup older than three days) and sends a notice **only** when something needs the person: with **Technical details** off, one plain sentence that opens the app's page, else its findings and the Run tab; the time of the last check is kept in Hydor's database, so a restart neither repeats nor loses it. Body: `{ notify?: boolean — default true }`. Returns: `{ weekly }`.

## Scheduled tasks: read

Agents in a conversation also have their own tools for this: schedule_task and update_task propose a task or a change on a card the user confirms, and list_tasks reads the tasks.

- `GET /api/tasks` — every scheduled task, oldest first. Returns: `{ tasks: [{ id, name, prompt, schedule, agent, acpAgent?, cwd, model? ("auto" = Automatic), effort?, mode?, extra?, target?, enabled, nextAt?, lastAt?, lastStatus? ("ok" or why the last run could not start), lastKey? (the last run's session), hookPath? ("/hooks/<secret>", webhook tasks), failLimit, fails?, pausedReason?, recent?: [{ status, at }] (latest 6, newest first), check?: { at, ok, note } (a watcher's last look), origin?, app?: { id, inputs } (it runs this app), appName? (that app's name; unset when it is gone), createdAt }] }`.
- `GET /api/automations/tasks/:id/runs` — a task's runs, newest first (the latest 50; 100 are kept). Returns: `{ runs: [{ id, taskId, trigger: schedule|webhook|watch|manual|rerun|test, startedAt, endedAt?, status: running|done|partial|blocked|failed|silent|error, summary?, reply? (the agent's last reply, ≤20,000 chars), costUsd?, key? (its session), error?, rerunOf?, hasInput, appRun? (the app run it started: /apps/runs/<id>), dry? (a test that sent nothing: GET /api/outbox?run=<id>&all=1 lists what it would have sent) }] }`.
- `GET /api/automations/runs/:id` — one run, to follow a test run or a re-run until it ends. Returns: `{ run }` (fields as above); 404 when there is none.
- `GET /api/automations/tasks/:id/notes` — the notepad the task's runs keep. Returns: `{ text }`.
- `POST /api/automations/next` — a schedule in words and its next runs in the user's time zone (saves nothing). Body: `{ schedule: object — as in POST /api/tasks }`. Returns: `{ tz, text, next: number[] (up to 3, ms) }`; 400 with the reason for a schedule that does not work.
- `POST /api/automations/watch/test` — what a watcher sees now, as its Test button shows (saves nothing). Body: `{ watch: object — as schedule.watch in POST /api/tasks, cwd?: string — the folder a command runs in }`. Returns: `{ ok, note, sample?, find? }`; for a connected account's watcher (Try it: it calls the tool once), `find: { lists: [{ path, count }] — each list of items in its answer, list: string | null — the one used, fields: [{ path, sample, unique, time }] — its items' fields, id: string | null — the field that tells them apart, start?: string | null — the start time's field (before each event), items: string[] — the first items as lines, raw: string — the answer, shortened }`, also when it fails. A command watcher runs its command on this machine: only with the user's clear OK.
- `GET /api/automations/accounts` — the connected accounts a watcher can call (all but Email, which has its own watcher). Returns: `{ accounts: [{ id, name, category, calendar: boolean — "Before each event" fits it, problem?: why it cannot be watched yet (signed out, never tested), tools: [{ name, description?, schema? }] — its tools that only look and are On, skipped: { outside, notOn } — how many it leaves out, presets: [{ id, label, tool, args, before? }] — starting points (new files, new deals, before each event…) from its own tools }] }`.
- `GET /api/automations/week?start=YYYY-MM-DD&days=n` — everything that runs by itself over a week or a month, as Agents → Schedules shows it (no model; it answers at once). `start`: the first day in the user's time zone (unset: this week's Monday, or today for fewer than 7 days); `days`: 1–42 (default 7). Returns: `{ tz, now, today, start, days: [{ date, from, to (ms; 23 or 25 hours when the clocks change), wd (0 Sunday), runs, by: { [status]: n }, costUsd, planned }], items: [{ id (the task), name, source: task|agent|app|board, kind: daily|every|once|cron|webhook|watch, when (in words), enabled, pausedReason?, nextAt?, last?: { status, at, summary?, key?, appRun? }, recent?, lastStatus?, agent?: { id, name, icon? }, app?: { id, name?, daily? (its Update automatically) }, board?: { id, name, card — a board card's refreshes: the board and the card's title }, edit (the page where it is changed), band (drawn as one band a day: more than 8 runs a day, a watcher or a webhook) }], runs: [{ id, task, at, endedAt?, day, min (minute of the day in the user's time zone), mins (how long), status, trigger, summary?, costUsd?, key?, appRun? }] (items drawn as blocks), next: [{ task, at, day, min }] (runs to come of items drawn as blocks, at most 2,500), bands: [{ task, day, runs, by, costUsd, planned }], capped?: true (some runs to come left out) }`; 400 for a start that is not a day.

## Scheduled tasks: changes

- `POST /api/tasks` — create a task, or replace one when id is given. Body: `{ id?: string — the task to replace; name: string ≤120; prompt: string ≤50,000 (a webhook task's may hold {{body.path}}, {{headers.name}}, {{query.name}}, {{body}}, {{request}}); agent: "claude"|"codex"|"acp"; acpAgent?: string — for acp, which agent ("console" is Custom); cwd: string — an existing folder, ~ allowed; model?: string — a model's id, or "auto" for Automatic (the Best tier's model for the agent, which may move itself to Max when a problem proves too hard; offered when Lead with subagents is on and the program has tiers, never for a confidential task); effort?: string; mode?: string — Claude Code default|acceptEdits|auto|plan|bypassPermissions, Codex plan|read-only|auto|auto-review, Custom ask|edits|auto|full|plan; schedule: { kind: "daily", time: "HH:MM", days?: number[] 0 Sun–6 Sat, omit for every day } | { kind: "every", minutes: ≥5 } | { kind: "once", at: ms } | { kind: "cron", expr: five fields or @hourly/@daily/@weekly/@monthly/@yearly, ≤120 chars } | { kind: "webhook", secret?: signing secret ≤300 chars, header?: the signature header, filter?: { path: string — "body.action", "headers.x-github-event", "query.kind", value: string, skip?: true — skip the run instead of running only then } } | { kind: "watch", minutes: 1–10080, watch: { kind: "feed", url } | { kind: "page", url, match?: text the watched lines contain, selector?: simple CSS selector } | { kind: "json", url, path: "data.price", op?: changed|eq|ne|gt|lt|contains, value?: needed unless changed } | { kind: "folder", path: absolute or ~/…, pattern?: "*.pdf" } | { kind: "command", command, on?: output|exit } | { kind: "mail", account: a connected mail account's address, folder?: default the Inbox, from?: text the sender contains, subject?: text the subject contains, unread?: true — only unread mail } (one run per new email, at most 10 a check) | { kind: "account", app: a connected account's id, tool: one of its tools that only looks (lists or searches) and is On — one that sends, changes, deletes or pays is refused, args?: object — its arguments, where text may hold {{now}}, {{now+2d}}, {{now-1h}}, {{today}}, {{tomorrow}} (filled in at each check), list?: where the items are in its answer ("results", "data.items", "" for the answer itself; found at the first look when left out), id?: the field that tells them apart ("id", "url", "*" for the whole item; found when left out), before?: { minutes?: number | evening?: "HH:MM" — the evening before, start?: the field with each event's start ("start.dateTime"; found when left out), title?: only events whose title contains it, others?: true — only events with other people } — a run that long before each event instead of one per new item } (one run per new item or event, at most 10 a check; name is filled in) (feed, page and json take local?: true to reach this computer's network: only when the user asks) }; target?: string — a session key: each run posts into it instead of starting a new session; extra?: { assistant?: id, memory?: false, capUsd?: number — spending cap per run, space?: id, swarm?, research?, connections? } — what each run's new session starts with (≤8,000 chars as JSON); failLimit?: 0–50 — failures in a row before it pauses (default 3; 0 never); enabled?: boolean — default true; origin?: string — kept only on create; app?: { id: string — an app's id or address, inputs: { [fieldId]: string } — its form's answers, which may hold a webhook's {{body.path}}, {{headers.name}}, {{query.name}} (as they are, no tags) and a watcher's {{change}} } — each run runs that app instead of starting a session (then prompt may be empty, and agent, cwd, model, target and extra go unused); 400 when there is no such app }`. Replaces the whole task: a field left out is cleared and enabled becomes true, so send the task as GET /api/tasks gives it, with your changes. To pause or turn one on, send it with enabled false or true (turning on clears its failure count and pause reason). A webhook task keeps its address. Returns: `{ task }`. A board card’s refreshes (ids `board-<page>-<card>`, listed by `GET /api/tasks` with their `board`) are refused here with a 400, as `DELETE /api/tasks/:id` refuses them: they are set on the board (its card’s refresh, `POST /api/pages/:id/versions`); `POST /api/automations/tasks/:id/enabled` pauses them and `POST /api/tasks/:id/run` runs one now.
- `POST /api/automations/tasks/:id/enabled` — pause a task or turn it on (its switch on Agents → Schedules), keeping everything else as it is. Body: `{ on: boolean }`. Returns: `{ task }`; turning one on clears its failure count and pause reason; 404 when there is no such task.
