- `POST /api/tasks/:id/run` — Run now: a run at once, even while paused. Returns: `{ key }` (the session it started or posted into; none for a task that runs an app); 409 while it is starting already.
- `POST /api/automations/test-run` — run a task once as given, without saving, in a session titled "Test: …"; never counts toward failures. Body: `{ task: object — the fields of POST /api/tasks; with id, it tests changes to that saved task (its notes, its history), dry?: boolean — a test that sends nothing: every call to a connected account's tool that acts outside (or is on Ask first) is kept as "would have sent" and never made }`. Returns: `{ runId, key }`. A run's `dry: true` (GET /api/automations/runs/:id) marks such a test; GET /api/outbox?run=…&all=1 lists what it would have sent.
- `POST /api/automations/runs/:id/rerun` — run a task again with an earlier run's input (the same webhook call or watcher change). Returns: `{ runId, key }`; 400 when that run's input was not kept.
- `PUT /api/automations/tasks/:id/notes` — replace the task's notepad. Body: `{ text: string — ≤8,000 chars; past that the oldest lines go }`. Returns: `{ text }` (as saved).
- `POST /api/tasks/:id/rotate` — a new webhook address; the old one stops working. Returns: `{ task }` (new hookPath); 400 for a task that is not a webhook.
- `DELETE /api/tasks/:id` — delete a task, its run history and notepad (the sessions it started stay). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/automations/draft` — "Make this an automation": the helper model drafts a task from a session; nothing is saved (save it with POST /api/tasks). Body: `{ key: string — the session }`. Returns: `{ draft: { name, prompt, schedule, agent, acpAgent?, cwd, model? ("auto" when the session is on Automatic), effort?, mode?, extra?, origin: "session" }, why?: string }`; can take up to two minutes; 400 for a session with no messages from the user.

## Connections (connected accounts)

- `GET /api/connections/catalog` — the marketplace: every service that can be connected. Returns: `{ apps: [{ id, name, description, category, icon, color?, homepage, docs?, server: { transport: 'http'|'sse', url } | { transport: 'stdio', command, args?, env? } | { transport: 'builtin' } (answered by the app itself: email), auth: { type: 'none'|'oauth'|'api_key', … }, fields?: [{ name, label, secret?, optional?, placeholder?, default?, help?, url? }], keywords?, featured?, verified: { by, date } | null, notes? }], categories: { [id]: label } }`.
- `GET /api/connections` — the connected accounts and the vault's keys (names and masked hints, never values). Returns: `{ connections: [{ id, name, app — its catalog entry, enabled — on for every session, status: 'ok'|'untested'|'signin'|'error', error?, hint?, fields — non-secret values as entered, secretFields — names of secret fields that are set, tools?: [{ name, description?, on — agents get it, state: 'on'|'ask'|'off' — Ask first: each call waits in the Outbox for the user's OK, outside — it sends, posts, deletes or pays }] — from the last test, askOffer?: string[] — tools that act outside and are On only because nobody chose (an account connected before Ask first), testedAt?, expiresAt?, catalogChanged?, createdAt, updatedAt }], secrets: [{ name, hint, domains, commands? — true when commands on this computer may use it, note, header?, prefix?, query?, createdAt, updatedAt, usedAt? }] }`.
- `GET /api/connections/session/:key` — the connected accounts as one session sees them. Returns: `{ apps: [{ id, name, on — on in this session, global — its every-session setting, remote — false when it runs on this computer }] }`.
- `POST /api/connections/:id` — connects a marketplace service that needs no browser sign-in, or changes its settings, then tests it in the background. Agents may use it only for services whose `auth.type` is `none` and that have no `secret` field. Body: `{ fields?: { NAME: string } — the service's fields by name (≤ 4000 chars each; needed unless optional or already saved; a remote service's address with fields filled in must be https), test?: boolean — also test a local service in the background (remote ones always are) }`. The body can also carry `key`, but that is for the user only. Returns: `{ connection }`. Returns 400 for a service with a browser sign-in or a missing field, and 404 if the id is not in the catalog.
- `POST /api/connections/:id/test` — connects the way agents will and lists the tools; the outcome becomes the account's status. Returns: `{ ok: true, tools: number, connection }` or `{ ok: false, error, connection }`. Returns 404 if the service is not connected.
- `PATCH /api/connections/:id` — turns the account on or off for every session, and sets single tools On, Ask first or Off. It merges: fields you leave out stay as they are, and `tools` is merged into the earlier per-tool choices. Body: `{ enabled?: boolean — on for every session, tools?: { [toolName]: true|'ask'|false } — On, Ask first or Off; names as in connection.tools }`. Returns: `{ connection }`. Changing `tools` asks the user on a card every time, in every mode.
- `PUT /api/connections/session/:key` — turns one connected account on or off for one session. Body: `{ id: string — a connected account's id, on?: boolean — on or off here; the same value as its every-session setting, or null/omitted, clears the session's own choice }`. Returns: `{ apps }` (as the GET). Returns 404 if the service is not connected.
- `DELETE /api/connections/:id` — disconnects: deletes the account's sign-in or key here and revokes OAuth tokens where the service allows it (for `email`, every mail account with it). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Email (mail accounts)

The connection `email` (server `{ transport: 'builtin' }`) holds the user's mail accounts, each connected with its address and an app password; agents read and send mail with its tools (list_folders, search_email, read_email, save_attachments, mark_email, move_email, save_draft, send_email, delete_email), not through these routes. Adding an account or changing one takes its app password, so only the user does that, in Apps → Connected apps → Email.

- `GET /api/mail/lookup` — the mail servers for an address and how to make an app password there; only the domain is looked up (the app's own list, the provider's published settings, Mozilla's directory, its mail server), never the whole address. Query: `{ address: string }`. Returns: `{ domain, provider?: { id, name, hint — how to make an app password, help — the provider's help page }, imap?: { host, port, security: 'tls'|'starttls'|'none' }, smtp?: { host, port, security }, username?, smtpUsername?, source: 'known'|'autoconfig'|'guess'|'none', blocked? — why it cannot connect this way (Outlook.com, Microsoft 365) }`.
- `GET /api/mail/accounts` — the connected mail accounts (passwords masked). Returns: `{ accounts: [{ id, address, name?, username, smtpUsername?, imap, smtp, provider?, status: 'ok'|'untested'|'error', error?, hint — the app password's last characters, testedAt?, createdAt }] }`.
- `POST /api/mail/accounts` — connects a mail account after signing in to read (IMAP) and to send (SMTP); nothing is saved if either fails. The user's only (it carries the app password). Body: `{ address: string, password: string — the app password, name?: string — shown on sent mail, imap?: { host, port, security }, smtp?: { host, port, security } — both, to give the servers by hand (else they are looked up), username?: string }`. Returns: `{ account, accounts }`; 400 with what went wrong (a wrong password, an app password needed, a server that did not answer, an Outlook.com or Microsoft 365 address), 409 when the address is connected already.
- `PATCH /api/mail/accounts/:id` — changes an account (its name, app password, servers or user name); a new password or other servers are signed in to first. The user's only. Body: as POST, every field optional (`password` left out keeps the saved one). Returns: `{ account, accounts }`.
- `POST /api/mail/accounts/:id/test` — signs in to the account again, to read and to send; the outcome becomes its status. Returns: `{ ok: true, account, accounts }` or `{ ok: false, error, account, accounts }`.
- `DELETE /api/mail/accounts/:id` — removes an account: its app password is deleted here, nothing in the mailbox changes; removing the last one disconnects `email`. Returns: `{ ok: true, accounts }`.
- `GET /api/mail/accounts/:id/folders` — the account's folders, as the New email watcher's form lists them. Returns: `{ folders: [{ path, name, specialUse? — \Inbox, \Sent, \Drafts, \Trash, \Junk, \Archive, \All or \Flagged }] }`.

## Keys (the vault)

- `GET /api/connections/requests` — the latest key or sign-in request in a session, and the saved key with that name. Query: `{ key: string — session key, name: string — the key's NAME, or what a sign-in's keys start with }`. Returns: `{ request: { card? — a card with tabs or steps (`GET /api/connections/requests/:id` has it), id, key, name, why, domains, fields?: [{ key — the key it is saved as, label, secret — false for a detail the agent gets as typed, type: 'password'|'text'|'email'|'url'|'number', help?, optional? }] — a sign-in's fields, have? — a sign-in's keys already saved: { KEY: hint }, commands? — the agent asked to use it in commands, commandsOn? — a key asked for may already go to commands, state: 'pending'|'saved'|'dismissed', createdAt, doneAt?, exists, hint? } | null, secret: { name, hint, domains, commands?, note, header?, prefix?, query?, createdAt, updatedAt, usedAt? } | null }`.
- `GET /api/connections/requests/:id` — a card that asks for several keys at once, or with how to get each (`request_secret` with `items` or `how`, and a web app's keys). Returns: `{ card: { id, key — the session, or webapp:<id> for a card on a web app's page, name, title?, why?, state: pending|saved|dismissed, items: [{ id, title, from?, why, how?: [string], url?, hint?, fields: [{ key, label, secret, type, help?, looks?: { prefix?, pattern?, say? }, optional?, savedAt? — a value is saved under that name, since when; refused?: live — a saved live payments key an app inside Hydor may not have, commandsOn? }], optional?, skippable?, domains, commands?, replace?, test?, state: todo|saved|used|skipped, reuse?: [{ id, kind: place|app|vault, env?, app?, only? }] — on a web app's card, values of these names elsewhere that the person may take (never a value) }], inside?, app?: { id, name, env, only? }, byName?, createdAt, doneAt? } }`. Never a value, nor any part of one. 404 for the plain one-key card.
- `GET /api/connections/secrets/:name/uses` — what names a key of the vault, told before it is removed. Returns: `{ uses: [{ kind: webapp|app, id, name, where?: [string] — a web app's places, own? — a web app keeps a value of its own under that name, which removing this one does not touch }] }`.
- `DELETE /api/connections/secrets/:name` — deletes a key for good (exact NAME). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- Agents ask for a key with their own `request_secret` tool: the user pastes it into a card. They use a key with `http_request` (`auth: NAME`). They never set or see a value.

## Signatures (Settings → App → Signatures)

- `GET /api/signatures` — the user's saved signatures (a signature, initials), by name; never their pictures. Returns: `{ signatures: [{ id, name, kind: 'signature'|'initials', width, height — its picture's size in pixels, createdAt, updatedAt, usedAt? — when it was last placed, until? — kept only until then (one the user gave for a day) }] }`.
- Agents ask the user to draw one with their own `ask_signature` tool (a card with a pad; the agent hears only its name) and place one by name with `place_signature` (a Word file, a PDF, a PNG picture or a kept document; the user allows each placement on a card). A signature's picture, and every change to one, are the user's alone (see Not for agents). A placed signature is a picture of one, not a cryptographic digital signature.

## MCP servers (Claude Code and Codex)

- `GET /api/setup/mcp` — Claude Code's servers (all folders, this folder only, and the folder's `.mcp.json`) and Codex's servers. Query: `{ cwd?: string — an existing folder, default home }`. Env and header values are never returned, only their names. Returns: `{ servers: [{ agent: 'claude'|'codex', name, scope: 'user'|'local'|'project', transport: 'stdio'|'http'|'sse', command?, args?, url?, envKeys?, headerKeys?, bearerEnv?, enabled? — Codex only }] }`.
- `POST /api/setup/mcp/status` — starts each of one agent's servers the way a new session would. No model is used; it takes up to about 45 s for Claude Code and about 2 min for Codex. Body: `{ agent: 'claude'|'codex' — anything else counts as claude, cwd?: string — the folder (Claude Code) }`. Returns: `{ servers: [{ name, state: 'connected'|'failed'|'auth'|'pending'|'off', error?, tools?: number }] }`.
- `POST /api/setup/mcp` — adds a server to one or both agents through their own CLIs. Body: `{ agents: ('claude'|'codex')[], name: string — letters, digits, . _ -, starting with a letter or digit, ≤ 64, transport: 'stdio'|'http'|'sse' — sse is Claude Code only, command?: string — stdio: the program alone, args?: string[] — stdio, env?: { KEY: value } — stdio, url?: string — http(s), headers?: { Name: value } — Claude Code only, bearerEnv?: string — Codex only: the env var holding the bearer token, scope?: 'user'|'local'|'project' — Claude Code only, default user, cwd?: string — the folder for local/project, default home }`. Leave keys and tokens in env or headers to the user. Returns: `{ results: [{ agent, ok, output }] }`; retry only the agents that failed.
- `POST /api/setup/mcp/remove` — removes a server from one agent. Body: `{ agent: 'claude'|'codex', name: string, scope?: 'user'|'local'|'project' — Claude Code, as listed, default user, cwd?: string }`. Returns: `{ ok, output }`.

## Plugins (for every agent)

Settings → Agents → Plugins. A plugin is a folder in the Agent Plugins 1.0 format (plugin.json, skills/, mcp.json; Claude Code's layout is read too) with the app's own extension `com.naget.agent` for what it needs. Every agent the app starts gets the enabled ones whose setup is done. `:name` is a plugin's name (lowercase letters, digits, dashes and dots). A plugin view (`plugin` below) is `{ name, title, version?, description?, author?, homepage?, repository?, license?, keywords: string[], category?, icon?, format: 'agent-plugins'|'claude', source: { kind: 'builtin' } | { kind: 'git', url, ref?, subdir?, commit? } | { kind: 'file', file } | { kind: 'folder', path, mine?: true (made in the app's editor) }, installed: boolean, enabled: boolean, setup: { state: 'none'|'installing'|'ready'|'failed'|'outdated', error?, at? }, skills: [{ name, description, category?, shadowedBy? (the skill agents get in its place) }], servers: [{ name, type: 'stdio'|'streamable-http'|'sse', command?, args?, url?, problem? }], instructions: boolean, files: [{ name, bytes }] (its own files), editable: boolean, claudeOnly: string[] (Claude Code parts left out), requires: { plugins: [{ name, range }], npm: { name: version }, pip: string[], python?, commands: [{ name, hint?, optional? }], files: [{ url, path, sha256?, size? }], keys: [{ name, description?, optional?, url? }] }, missing: { commands, keys, plugins } (what is not here), dependents: string[] (installed plugins that need it), agents: ('claude'|'codex'|'custom'|'acp')[], problems: string[], root (its folder), data (its data folder), installedAt?, readme? (details only) }`. A job (adding, setting up, updating) is `{ id, plugin, action: 'install'|'setup'|'update', state: 'running'|'done'|'failed', steps: [{ label, state: 'waiting'|'running'|'done'|'failed' }], log (the output's end), error?, startedAt, endedAt? }`; the pages also get it as it runs.

- `GET /api/plugins` — every plugin: installed and built in. Returns: `{ installed: [plugin], available: [plugin] (built-in ones not added yet), jobs: [job] (running, or ended in the last minute), folders: { installed, data, mine } }`.
- `GET /api/plugins/:name` — one plugin, installed or built in, with its README. Returns: `plugin`. 404 when there is none.
- `GET /api/plugins/jobs/:id` — a job, while it runs and for 15 minutes after. Returns: `{ job }`. 404 after that.
- `POST /api/plugins/inspect` — looks at a plugin before adding it, from a Git repository, a .zip or a folder on this computer; nothing is installed. Body: `{ url: string (owner/repo, https://…, a GitHub link to a folder …/tree/<branch>/<folder>, or git@host:path), ref?: string (a branch or tag), subdir?: string (a folder inside the repository) }`, or `{ file: { name, data: base64 } }` (a .zip, up to 160 MB, its plugin.json at the top or in its one folder), or `{ path: string (a folder; ~ works) }`. Returns: `{ token (for install, valid 30 minutes), view: plugin, brings: string[] (built-in plugins it needs that are added first), unavailable: string[] (plugins it needs that nobody has: it cannot be added), replaces?: string (a plugin of its name is installed) }`. 400 with the reason when it is not a plugin, its manifest is invalid, or Git fails.
- `POST /api/plugins/install` — adds a plugin, with the built-in plugins it needs first, sets each up (packages, downloads) and turns them on. Body: `{ name: string }` (a built-in one) or `{ token: string }` (one looked at). A plugin from a folder is used in place; from Git or a .zip it is copied into the app's data folder. Returns: `{ job }`. 400 when it needs plugins nobody has; 409 when a git or file plugin takes a built-in one's name.
- `POST /api/plugins/:name/enable` — turns it on, with the plugins it needs. No body. Returns: `{ on: string[] (every plugin turned on) }`.
- `POST /api/plugins/:name/disable` — turns it off, with the plugins that need it. No body. Returns: `{ off: string[] }`.
- `POST /api/plugins/:name/setup` — sets it up again (its packages and downloads, from scratch). No body. Returns: `{ job }`. 409 while it is being set up.
- `POST /api/plugins/:name/update` — a Git plugin: gets the latest from its repository; any plugin: sets it up when its needs changed. No body. Returns: `{ job }`. 400 for one from a .zip (add the newer file instead).
- `POST /api/plugins/:name/remove` — removes it and the plugins that need it, their packages and downloads (unless `keepData`); one made in the editor is deleted with its folder, one used from a folder of the user's stays there. Body: `{ keepData?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ removed: string[] }`.
- `POST /api/plugins/new` — makes a plugin of the user's own, in the app's data folder (plugins/mine/<name>), added and on. Body: `{ name: string (lowercase letters, digits, dashes and dots), title?: string, description?: string }`. Returns: `{ plugin }`. 409 when a plugin of that name is here.
- `GET /api/plugins/:name/draft` — a plugin as its editor shows it (only ones made here or used from a folder, in the Agent Plugins layout). Returns: `{ name, title, version, description, category?, icon?, keywords, instructions (its com.naget.agent/instructions.md), skills: [{ name, text (the whole SKILL.md) }], servers: [{ name, type: 'stdio', command, args, env } | { name, type: 'streamable-http', url, headers }], requires (as in the plugin view), files: [{ name, bytes }] }`. 400 for a plugin that cannot be changed here.
- `PUT /api/plugins/:name/draft` — saves it from the editor: plugin.json, mcp.json, its skills (ones left out are deleted) and its instructions. Body: the draft's fields (`title`, `version` (three numbers), `description`, `category?`, `icon?`, `keywords`, `instructions`, `skills`, `servers`, `requires: { plugins: { name: range } | ['name@range'], npm: { name: version }, pip: string[], python?, commands: (string | { name, hint?, optional? })[], files: [{ url (https), path, sha256? }], keys: (string | { name, description?, optional?, url? })[] }`). Everything is checked first; 400 lists what to fix. Returns: `{ plugin, draft, job? (when its packages changed and it is on: set up again) }`.
- `POST /api/plugins/:name/files` — adds (or replaces) one of its own files, in its files/ folder. Body: `{ name: string (letters, digits, spaces, . _ - ( ) + , and / for folders), data: base64 }` (up to 50 MB; 200 MB together). Returns: `{ files: [{ name, bytes }] }`.
- `DELETE /api/plugins/:name/files?name=` — removes one of its own files. Returns: `{ files }`.
- `GET /api/plugins/:name/export` — the plugin as a .zip (its folder under <name>/, without setup or Git leftovers), to share; any plugin, installed or built in. Returns the file (application/zip).

## Plugins (Claude Code's own)

- `GET /api/setup/plugins` — installed plugins, the ones available to install, and the marketplaces. The list is cached for 10 minutes; a fresh fetch can take up to 3 minutes. Query: `{ q?: string — all words must match name, description or marketplace, limit?: number — available ones to return, 1–200, default 60 }`. Returns: `{ installed: [{ id — name@marketplace, name, description?, marketplace?, version?, scope?, enabled?, installed: true }], available: [{ id, name, description?, marketplace?, installCount?, version?, installed: false }] — most installed first, marketplaces: [{ name, source?, repo?, url? }], total — number of matching available plugins }`.
- `POST /api/setup/plugins/:action` — `:action` is `install`, `uninstall`, `enable` or `disable`. Body: `{ id: string — as listed, scope?: 'user'|'project'|'local' — install defaults to user }`. The last three return 400 if the plugin is not installed. Returns: `{ ok, output }`.
- `POST /api/setup/marketplaces/:action` — `:action` is `add` or `remove`. Body: `{ target: string — add: a GitHub owner/repo, a git URL or a folder path; remove: the marketplace's name }`. Removing a marketplace also uninstalls the plugins installed from it. Returns: `{ ok, output }`.

## Plugins (Codex's own)

- `GET /api/setup/codex-plugins` — Codex's added plugins, those its marketplaces offer (only ones with a readable name), and its marketplaces. Cached for 10 minutes; a fresh fetch can take up to 3 minutes. Query: `{ q?: string — all words must match, limit?: number — 1–200, default 60 }`. Returns: `{ installed: [{ id — name@marketplace, name, marketplace?, version?, enabled?, installed: true }], available: [{ id, name, marketplace?, version?, installed: false }], marketplaces: [{ name, source? (its folder) }], total }`.
- `POST /api/setup/codex-plugins/:action` — `:action` is `add` or `remove`. Body: `{ id: string — as listed }`. Returns: `{ ok, output }`.
- `POST /api/setup/codex-marketplaces/:action` — `:action` is `add`, `remove` or `upgrade` (refresh a Git marketplace). Body: `{ target: string — add: a GitHub owner/repo, a git URL or a folder; remove and upgrade: the marketplace's name }`. Returns: `{ ok, output }`.

## Notifications

- `POST /api/push/test` — sends a test notification ("Notifications work on this device.") to every device that turned on Notify this device, or to one; it shows on the devices where the app is not in view. Body: `{ endpoint?: string — one device's push subscription (the page sends its own, as Use on your phone's Send a test does); left out: every device }`. Returns: `{ sent: number — how many devices it reached }` (0: no device has notifications on).

## Use on your phone (Settings → App → Use on your phone)

The app published with Tailscale Serve over HTTPS to the devices signed in to the user's Tailscale account only (never Tailscale Funnel, which is public), at `https://<machine>.<tailnet>.ts.net`, and that address accepted by the server. The password is still asked there.

- `GET /api/phone` — where things stand. Returns: `{ tailscale: { state: 'missing'|'not-running'|'signed-out'|'stopped'|'needs-approval'|'too-old'|'ready', path?, version?, syntax?: 'new'|'old'|'none' — Serve's command line (new from Tailscale 1.52), dnsName? — this machine's name on the tailnet (laptop.tail1234.ts.net), machine?, tailnet?, https?: boolean — HTTPS certificates can be had for it, loginUrl? — a sign-in under way, detail? }, serving: { url, port: 443|8443|10000, servedHere: boolean — false: published by hand and taken on, funnel: boolean — Funnel is on for it (public), at? } | null, taken: { port: 443, target, free: number|null } | null — something else is published at the usual address, accepted: boolean — the server accepts the address, port — this server's, platform: 'mac'|'windows'|'linux', operatorCommand — the command a Linux user runs once, download — Tailscale's download page }`.
- `POST /api/phone/serve` — publishes the app on the tailnet (`tailscale serve --bg --https=<port> http://127.0.0.1:<this server's port>`, or the older form before Tailscale 1.52), adds its address to the ones the server accepts, and records both for Stop serving. Already published at this app (by hand too): taken on as it is. Never replaces something else published at that port. Body: `{ port?: 443|8443|10000 — default 443 }`. Returns: the same as `GET /api/phone`. 409 with `{ error — what happened and the one thing to do, problem: { kind: 'not-ready'|'enable'|'operator'|'port-taken'|'failed', link? — the page that turns Serve and HTTPS certificates on in the tailnet, or Tailscale's download page, command? — the operator command, port? — a free port to serve on instead } }`. **Careful**: makes the app reachable from every device on the user's Tailscale account: agents get a card each time, Full access too.
- `POST /api/phone/stop` — undoes exactly what serve did: turns off its one handler (`tailscale serve --https=<port> --set-path=/ off`), and takes the address out of the ones the server accepts if serve added it. Anything else served stays. Returns: the same as `GET /api/phone`. 409 when Tailscale is not running or signed in (it must run to turn Serve off), with `problem` as above (`kind: 'not-ready'|'operator'|'stop-failed'`).

## Chat apps (Telegram, Discord and Slack)

Settings → Chat apps: each of the three has the same four routes under its own name. Telegram's are below; Discord's (`/api/discord`) and Slack's (`/api/slack`) differ only in what is said after them.

- `GET /api/telegram` — Settings → Chat apps → Telegram: whether a bot is connected and how it is doing, its name, the linked chats, the options, and a code to link a chat while one is out. Returns: `{ connected: boolean, state: "off"|"connecting"|"running"|"error", error?: string — what needs the user (a token Telegram no longer takes, another program getting the bot's messages), note?: string — while connecting, why the last try did not get through (Hydor keeps trying), bot?: { username, name }, chats: [{ id: number, name, at }], notify: boolean — every session's notices to the chats, agent: "claude"|"codex" — what a message from a chat in no session starts, as: string|null — one of your agents (its id) a new session starts as, pairing?: { code, link?: string — t.me link that sends the code, until } }`.
- `PUT /api/telegram` — changes the options; with `token`, connects a bot (checked with Telegram first: 400 only when Telegram turns it down; when Telegram is slow or not reached it is kept, and `state` is "connecting" until Telegram answers; the user pastes it in Settings, agents leave it out). Body: `{ token?: string|null — the bot's token from @BotFather (null removes the bot and unlinks every chat), notify?: boolean, agent?: "claude"|"codex", as?: string|null — one of your agents' ids (GET /api/assistants) new sessions start as; null for none (404 for no such agent) }`. Returns: the same object as GET.
- `POST /api/telegram/pair` — a code that links the chat that sends it to the bot, once, within ten minutes (400 without a bot). No body. Returns: the same object as GET, with `pairing`.
- `DELETE /api/telegram/chats/:id` — unlinks a chat: it can no longer use the agents. Returns: the same object as GET.
- `GET /api/discord` — Settings → Chat apps → Discord: whether a bot is connected and how it is doing, the linked chats, the options, a code to link a chat while one is out, and the link that adds the bot to a server. Returns: `{ connected: boolean, state: "off"|"connecting"|"running"|"error", error?: string — why the Gateway is not connected, note?: string — while connecting, why the last try did not get through, warning?: string — e.g. Message Content Intent is off, bot?: { username }, chats: [{ id: string — the channel, user: string — who may use it, name, at, kind: "dm"|"channel" }], notify: boolean, agent: "claude"|"codex", as: string|null, pairing?: { code, until }, invite?: string — adds the bot to a server }`.
- `PUT /api/discord` — changes the options; with `token`, connects a bot (checked with Discord first: 400 only when Discord turns it down; when Discord is slow or not reached it is kept and connects by itself; the user pastes it in Settings, agents leave it out). Body: `{ token?: string|null — the bot's token from the Developer Portal (null removes the bot and unlinks every chat), notify?: boolean, agent?: "claude"|"codex", as?: string|null }`. Returns: the same object as GET.
- `POST /api/discord/pair` — a code that links the chat that sends it (in a direct message to the bot, or mentioning it in a channel), once, within ten minutes (400 without a bot). No body. Returns: the same object as GET, with `pairing`.
- `DELETE /api/discord/chats/:id` — unlinks a chat (`:id` is the channel's id): it can no longer use the agents. Returns: the same object as GET.
- `GET /api/slack` — Settings → Chat apps → Slack: whether the app is connected and how it is doing, the linked chats, the options, and a code to link a chat while one is out. Returns: `{ connected: boolean — both tokens are saved, state: "off"|"connecting"|"running"|"error", error?: string — why Socket Mode is not connected, note?: string — while connecting, why the last try did not get through, warning?: string — scopes the app lacks, bot?: { username, name — the workspace }, chats: [{ id: string — the channel, user: string, name, at, kind: "dm"|"channel" }], notify: boolean, agent: "claude"|"codex", as: string|null, pairing?: { code, until } }`.
- `PUT /api/slack` — changes the options; with the tokens, connects the app (both checked with Slack first: 400 only when Slack turns one down; when Slack is slow or not reached they are kept and the app connects by itself; the user pastes them in Settings, agents leave them out). Body: `{ botToken?: string — the Bot User OAuth Token (xoxb-…), appToken?: string — the App-Level Token with connections:write (xapp-…), token?: null — removes the app and unlinks every chat, notify?: boolean, agent?: "claude"|"codex", as?: string|null }`. Both tokens are needed the first time. Returns: the same object as GET.
- `POST /api/slack/pair` — a code that links the chat that sends it (in a direct message to the app, or mentioning it in a channel), once, within ten minutes (400 without an app). No body. Returns: the same object as GET, with `pairing`.
- `DELETE /api/slack/chats/:id` — unlinks a chat (`:id` is the channel's id): it can no longer use the agents. Returns: the same object as GET.

## Agents and their programs

- `GET /api/clients` — the agents' programs on this computer, and keeping them up to date. Returns: `{ clients: [{ id: "claude"|"codex"|"gemini"|"opencode"|"qwen"|"auggie"|"pi-acp", name, source: "yours"|"app"|"bundled"|"missing", version? — the one the sessions run, path?, installing?, latest? — a newer version out, failed? — a newer version that did not work here (the sessions keep version), yours?: { version?, path } — your own copy when the sessions run another (Codex: the one your terminal runs) }], updates: { auto: boolean — Update automatically, checkedAt? — when it last looked, error? — why that lookup failed, running?: "check"|"update", current? — the program it is updating, last?: { at, auto: boolean — it ran by itself, items: [{ id, name, yours?: boolean, from?, to?, ok: boolean, error?, kept? — the version kept in use because to did not work }] } } }`.
- `POST /api/clients/check` — Check now: looks up the newest version of each program, in the background (seconds); `updates.running` is `"check"` until it is done. Body: none. Returns: `{ clients, updates }` as GET; 409 while a check or an update runs.
- `POST /api/clients/update` — Update now: looks up the newest versions and updates every program that is behind: the app's copies with npm, your own with their own updater (`claude update`, `codex update`, `opencode upgrade`; npm for those installed with `npm install -g`). Each new version is checked to work the way the sessions use it, and undone when it does not. Runs in the background (it can take minutes): GET until `updates.running` is gone, then `updates.last` says what changed. Body: none. Returns: `{ clients, updates }` as GET; 409 while a check or an update runs.
- `PUT /api/clients/auto` — Update automatically (on by default: once a day; off: the daily run only looks). Body: `{ on: boolean }`. Returns: `{ clients, updates }` as GET.
- `POST /api/clients/:id/install` — installs (or updates) a program with npm into the app's own folder; waits until done (up to 15 min). `:id` as above; 400 for others. Body: none. Returns: `{ clients, updates }` as GET.

## Accounts (Claude Code and Codex)

- `GET /api/accounts` — sign-in state of each CLI's own account and of every added account. Returns: `{ claude: AccountStatus, codex: AccountStatus, grok?: AccountStatus — Grok Build's own sign-in, only once Grok Build is set up (Settings → More agents), antigravity?: AccountStatus — Antigravity CLI's own sign-in (its email and, once a session has opened, its plan), only once Antigravity CLI is added (Settings → More agents); it has no added accounts, extra: [{ id, agent: "claude"|"codex"|"grok", name, status: AccountStatus }], apiKeys: { main: boolean, [accountId]: boolean } — whether the CLI's own Claude sign-in (main) and each added Claude account has an Anthropic API key saved for API credit }`, where AccountStatus is `{ agent, loggedIn: boolean, email?, plan?, method?, detail?, error? }`.
- `POST /api/agent-accounts` — adds another account for an agent (its own folder; settings, instructions, skills and plugins shared). It then needs signing in, which only the user can do (Settings → Accounts). Body: `{ agent: "claude"|"codex"|"grok" — grok once Grok Build is set up (`antigravity` is refused: Antigravity CLI has one sign-in only), its own folder with its sign-in and Grok's session history (config, rules, skills, plugins and MCP sign-ins shared), name: string — 1–40 chars, unique per agent (409 if taken) }`. Returns: `{ id, agent, name }`.
- `PATCH /api/agent-accounts/:id` — renames an added account. Body: `{ name: string — 1–40 chars }`. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `DELETE /api/agent-accounts/:id` — removes an added account; its sessions leave the list. Query: `wipe?: "1" — also delete its sign-in and its sessions' history (cannot be undone)`. 409 while sessions run as it. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `PUT /api/accounts/claude/api-keys/:account` — saves the Anthropic API key (from console.anthropic.com) of a Claude Code account, its API credit for when every account is at its usage limit and **Use API credit at a limit** is on; `:account` is `main` (the CLI's own sign-in) or an added account's id. The key is checked with Anthropic and kept encrypted in the vault. The user's own call, from Settings → Accounts. Body: `{ key: string — starts with sk-ant- }`. Returns: `{ ok: true, checked: boolean — false when Anthropic could not be reached to check it }`.
- `DELETE /api/accounts/claude/api-keys/:account` — removes that account's API key. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## More agents (ACP)

- `GET /api/acp-agents` — the agents in Settings → More agents. Returns: `{ agents: [{ id, name, command, args: string[], env?, hint?, builtin?, installed: boolean }], presets: [same, for known agents not added yet: gemini, opencode, qwen, goose, kimi, auggie, hermes, pi, openclaw] }`. Custom comes first (`id: "console"`, `builtin: true`).
- `POST /api/acp-agents` — adds an agent, or replaces one with the same id. To add a preset, send its id, name, command and args from `presets`. The program installs in the background when the app knows its npm package (Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Qwen Code, Auggie and others, with an ACP adapter when the agent needs one). Body: `{ id?: string — [a-z0-9-]{1,40}, an existing id replaces that agent (new id made from the name when absent); name: string — required, cut to 40 chars; command: string — required, a command or full path; args?: string[]; env?: { NAME: value } — names must match [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]* }`. 400 for id "console". Returns: `{ id, name, command, args, env }`.
- `DELETE /api/acp-agents/:id` — removes an added agent (its sessions stay listed). 400 for "console". Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Models for Custom (Settings → Models)

- `GET /api/models` — everything on the Models page. Returns: `{ subscriptions: [{ provider, program: "Claude Code"|"Codex", installed: boolean, signedIn: boolean (at least one account signed in), models: [{ id: "claude/sonnet", label: "Sonnet" }] — what it lends Custom (your Claude and ChatGPT plans' models) }], openrouter: { keyHint?, pinned: [OpenRouter model] }, ollama: { url, version? (absent: not running) }, vllm: { command?, path?, version? }, endpoints: [{ id, name, baseUrl, keyHint?, models: string[], modelCaps: { [model]: { vision: boolean, reasoning: boolean — what Custom does with the model: the setting, else what the endpoint says, else no, endpoint: { vision?: boolean, reasoning?: boolean } — what the endpoint says (left out: it does not say), set: { vision?: boolean, reasoning?: boolean } — the setting made by hand (left out: Automatic) } } }], hfTokenHint?, local: [{ id: "ollama/<name>"|"vllm/<org/name>", repo, runtime: "ollama"|"vllm", sizeBytes, detail?, vision?, supported?: boolean|null, reason?, server?: { state: "starting"|"ready"|"failed", gpu (-1 on a Mac), gpus? (every GPU it is split across, when more than one), port, error? } }], downloads: [{ key, name, target: "ollama"|"vllm", doneBytes, totalBytes, status: "running"|"done"|"failed"|"cancelled", error? }], machine: { gpus: [{ index, name, totalBytes, freeBytes }] (NVIDIA), ramBytes, ramFreeBytes, diskFreeBytes, cpus, apple?: { chip } (a Mac with Apple silicon, whose graphics share ramBytes) }, storage: { usedBytes, freeBytes } }`.
- `GET /api/models/openrouter/picks` — the OpenRouter models suggested for Custom before any search, in order, each with why: GPT-6 Luna (fast and cheap), Grok 4.7 (good, with fewer refusals), GPT-6.1 Sol (better), Claude Opus 5.5 (best overall); those OpenRouter no longer lists are left out. Returns: `{ models: [{ …the fields of GET /api/models/openrouter, note: string — why this one }] }`. Pin one with PUT /api/models `{ pin }`.
- `GET /api/models/openrouter` — searches OpenRouter's catalog (kept an hour; works without a key, but pinned models only reach Custom once a key is set). Query: `q?: string — words, all must match name, id or maker; sort?: "match"|"new"|"price"|"context" (default match with words, else new); f?: comma list of "tools","vision","reasoning","free"`. Returns: `{ models: [{ id: "openrouter/<slug>", label, org?, detail (slug), contextLength?, maxOutput?, price?: { input, output } ($/M tokens), vision?, reasoning?, tools: "native"|"text", created?, description? }] }` (up to 50).
- `GET /api/models/hf` — searches Hugging Face for text models, each with a first guess of how it runs here (kept 10 min). Query: `q?: string — name words or org/name; sort?: "downloads"|"trending"|"likes"|"new" (default downloads); f?: comma list of "fits" (runs well here), "gguf" (Ollama), "safetensors" (vLLM), "vision"`. Returns: `{ models: [{ id: "org/name", kind: "gguf"|"safetensors"|"other", params?, downloads? (30 days), likes?, updated?, created?, vision?, gated?, fit?: ModelFit, fitGguf?: ModelFit (safetensors at 4-bit with Ollama) }] }` (up to 40). ModelFit: `{ verdict: "good"|"slow"|"tight"|"no", where: "gpu"|"cpu"|"none" ("gpu" is an NVIDIA card or a Mac's Apple silicon), gpu?, gpus?: number[] (split across these GPUs, for vLLM, when no one has room), fp8?: true (a 16-bit model vLLM stores at 8-bit as it loads, when it does not fit as it is), needBytes, tokensPerSec?, downloadBytes, notes: string[] }`.
- `GET /api/models/picks` — the models the app suggests to run on this computer (Settings → Models, the welcome tour), smallest first, each as it would be here: LFM3-VL 2B, Gemma 4 12B, Flint (Macs with Apple silicon) and GLM-5.3 Flash (servers with several large GPUs). They run with vLLM by default, split across GPUs when needed; a computer with neither NVIDIA graphics nor Apple silicon gets a model's build for Ollama. Returns: `{ picks: [{ repo, name, maker, blurb, vision?, params (billions), active? (billions read per word), mac?, install?: { repo } | { ollama } (the body for POST /api/models/install; absent: it cannot run here), fit: ModelFit, runs: boolean (it runs here: suggested), best?: true (the strongest that runs well), sizeBytes (the download), have?: string (its local[].id, when installed), coming?: string (its download's key, while downloading), needs?: string (what this computer lacks, when it does not run here), ready: boolean (what runs it is set up: vLLM installed and able to run it, or Ollama running) }] }`.
- `POST /api/models/inspect` — "Check": exactly how a Hugging Face model would install and run here, before downloading. Body: `{ input: string — org/name or a huggingface.co / hf.co address }`. Returns: `{ repo, kind: "gguf"|"safetensors"|"other", params?, contextLength?, vision?, arch?, gated?, quants: [{ tag, sizeBytes, ollamaName: "hf.co/<org/name>:<tag>", fit: ModelFit }] (GGUF, smallest first), downloadBytes, vllm?: { supported: boolean|null (null: vLLM not installed), reason?, fit: ModelFit }, ggufRepo?: string (a GGUF version to check instead) }`.
- `PUT /api/models` — changes Models settings; merges (only the fields sent change). Body: `{ pin?: string — an OpenRouter model id (with or without "openrouter/") to add to the pickers; unpin?: string — take one out; ollamaUrl?: string — Ollama's http(s) address (default http://127.0.0.1:11434); vllmCommand?: string|null — the vllm command's path (null or "": find it by itself); endpoint?: { id?: string, name: string, baseUrl: string — http(s)…/v1, apiKey?: string, remove?: boolean } — adds or replaces an OpenAI-compatible endpoint (id made from the name; same name replaces), or with { id, remove: true } removes it; apiKey left out keeps the old key; endpointModel?: { endpoint: string — the endpoint's id, model: string — a model id from its models, vision?: boolean|null — whether Custom sends the model images as images (true), as descriptions (false), or goes by what the endpoint says (null: Automatic), reasoning?: boolean|null — whether Custom offers the model a reasoning effort and sends it as reasoning_effort (true or false), or goes by what the endpoint says (null) } — what one endpoint's model can do, kept with the endpoint (404 for an endpoint that is not there); a field left out stays; openrouterKey?: string|null; hfToken?: string|null }`. openrouterKey, hfToken and endpoint.apiKey are key values: only the user types them, in Settings; agents leave them out (null removes a key, which the user should decide). Returns: the same object as `GET /api/models`.
- `POST /api/models/install` — starts a download in the background; progress shows in `downloads`. Send one of the two. Body: `{ ollama?: string — for GGUF: a quant's ollamaName from inspect (or any Ollama model name); Ollama must be running (400); repo?: string — for vLLM: org/name of a safetensors model (400 if it has none) }`. 409 if it is downloading already. Returns: the models state.
- `POST /api/models/cancel` — cancels a running download, or clears a finished, failed or cancelled one from the list. Body: `{ key: string — the download's key, "ollama:<name>" or "vllm:<org/name>" }`. Returns: the models state.
- `POST /api/models/remove` — deletes a model from this computer (a running vLLM server stops first). Body: `{ id: string — "ollama/<name>" or "vllm/<org/name>" from local[].id }`. Returns: the models state.
- `POST /api/models/start` — starts a downloaded vLLM model's server (sessions also start it by themselves). Body: `{ repo: string — org/name; gpu?: number — GPU index (default: the one with the most free memory); maxLen?: number — context length (default 32768, at most the model's own) }`. 400 when vLLM, an NVIDIA GPU or enough GPU memory is missing. A 16-bit model too big for one GPU starts there at 8-bit (FP8, on cards from the RTX 30 series and A100 on), else split across 2, 4 or 8 (unless gpu is given); on a Mac, vLLM runs on its Apple silicon. Returns: the models state (server.state "starting" until ready).
- `POST /api/models/stop` — stops a vLLM model's server, freeing its GPU. Body: `{ repo: string — org/name }`. Returns: the models state.

## Helper model

- `GET /api/helper` — the model for small jobs beside sessions (Auto mode checks, goal checks, memory tidy-up, reading a page for one question, describing images for Custom). Returns: `{ model: "auto"|<Custom model id>, options: [{ value, label, detail?, vision?: boolean }] }` ("auto" = Claude Haiku through Claude Code, else Codex; it cannot see images).
- `PUT /api/helper` — picks the helper model. Body: `{ model: "auto" | one of options[].value }` (400 for an unknown model). Returns: as GET.

## Custom (Settings → Capabilities → Custom)

- `GET /api/custom/settings` — Custom's settings. Returns: `{ sandbox: boolean — commands in Auto mode run in the sandbox; Full access bypasses it (default true); bwrap: boolean — a sandbox can run on this computer (bubblewrap, Seatbelt or low integrity; the name is from when only bubblewrap existed); sandboxKind: "bwrap" | "seatbelt" | "lowbox" | null — which one; platform: string — the system's name ("linux", "darwin", "win32") }`.
- `PUT /api/custom/settings` — changes them; merges. Body: `{ sandbox?: boolean }`. Returns: as GET. (A new goal's rounds are in `/api/goal-settings`: `goalRounds` is no longer returned or taken here.)

## Usage and limits

- `GET /api/usage` — each plan's limits: the CLIs' own sign-ins and every added account (Claude Code, Codex, Grok Build and Antigravity CLI). Read fresh when older than 2 minutes. Query: `refresh?: "1" — read every plan's usage afresh`. Returns: `{ claude: AgentUsage, codex: AgentUsage, grok?: AgentUsage — Grok Build's own sign-in, only once Grok Build is set up (Settings → More agents), antigravity?: AgentUsage — Antigravity CLI's own sign-in, only once it is added: its email and plan, a `note` that says to read what is left with /usage in its own terminal, and no windows (the app does not read its quota: Google's guidance rules out background calls), and not counted in the rail's Usage bar, accounts: { <accountId>: AgentUsage } — every added account, Grok's included }`. AgentUsage: `{ agent: "claude"|"codex"|"grok"|"antigravity", ok, error?, note?: string — a plain line for when there are no limits to draw (Grok Build on a team plan: "Usage limits are managed by your team."), fetchedAt, account?: { email?, plan? }, windows: [{ id, label, usedPercent: number|null, resetsAt?: number|null }], limited?: { label, resetsAt? } | null (set while used up), resetCredits?: { available, items: [{ id, title, left?, expiresAt?, usableNow: boolean, note? }], nextAvailableAt?, cooldownUntil? }, extra?: { enabled, monthlyLimit?, used?, currency? } (Grok Build: pay as you go, its spending cap and what is spent this month), credits?: { balance?, unlimited? }, breakdown?: [{ label, percent }], daily?: [{ date, tokens }], lifetimeTokens? }`.
- `POST /api/usage/:agent/reset` — spends one limit reset to clear the plan's limits now (cannot be undone; confirm with the user first). `:agent` = `claude` | `codex` (Grok Build has no resets). Body: `{ id?: string — a reset's id from resetCredits.items (required for claude; for codex, absent lets the provider pick); account?: string — an added account's id (absent: the CLI's own sign-in) }`. Returns: `{ ok: boolean, outcome: "reset"|"nothing_to_reset"|"no_credit"|"already_used"|"cooldown"|"unavailable"|"ineligible"|"error", message: string, usage: AgentUsage }`.

## Activity, prices and keeping sessions

- `GET /api/stats` — activity over time (the Activity tab). Query: `granularity?: "day"|"week"|"month"|"year" (default day); agent?: "all"|"claude"|"codex"|"acp"|"acp:<agent id>" — one ACP agent, "acp:console" is Custom (default all); model?: string — one model alone, as byModel names it; tz?: string — IANA time zone for the buckets (default UTC); count?: number — buckets, 1–400 (default 30, 26, 12, 10); period?: "current"|string — one period instead of the whole window: "current" (the one that holds now, counted so far, as the Activity tab shows it) or a period's key (a day "2026-11-18", any day of a week, a month "2026-11", a year "2026"; another answers 400)`. Returns: `{ granularity, tz, agent, model?, buckets: [{ key, label, start, sessions, prompts, turns, toolCalls, commands, fileEdits, linesAdded, linesRemoved, tokensIn, tokensCached, tokensOut, genMs, cost? }] (the window, always), totals (same fields: the window's, or the period's), previous (the same span just before the window; with a period, the period before it, up to the same point while the period runs), period?: { key, label, start, current: boolean — it holds now } (with a period), window? (the whole window's totals, with a period), byAgent: { claude, codex, acp }, agents: [{ id: "claude"|"codex"|"acp:<id>", name: "Claude Code"|"Codex"|"Custom"|…, …totals }] (each agent with activity in the window, or the period), windowAgents: [{ id, name }] (each agent with activity in the window), projects: [{ cwd, …totals }] (top 12 by lines changed), byModel: [{ model, sessions, prompts, tokensIn, tokensCached, tokensOut, cost: number|null (null: no known price), price: { input, output, cacheRead?, source: "custom"|"table" } | null }], firstAt?, firstByAgent? }`. byAgent, projects and byModel cover what totals do. cost is the estimate at API prices. Sessions deleted as inactive keep their numbers here.
- `GET /api/prices` — your own model prices (not the public table's). Returns: `{ prices: { <model id>: { input, output, cacheRead? } } }`.
- `PUT /api/prices` — saves your model prices. REPLACES the whole set: a model left out goes back to the public table, so GET first and send the merged map. Body: `{ prices: { <model id as Activity names it>: { input: number ≥ 0; output: number ≥ 0; cacheRead?: number ≥ 0 — cached input, defaults to input } } }` (entries missing input or output are dropped). Returns: `{ prices }`.
- `GET /api/retention` — Settings → General → Delete inactive sessions. Returns: `{ on: boolean, days: number }` (on, 365 at first).
- `PUT /api/retention` — changes it. Body: `{ on?: boolean, days?: number — how long a session may stay inactive, 30 to 3650 }`. Returns: `{ on, days }`. Once a day the app deletes up to 200 sessions inactive for longer (never pinned ones, nor ones at work, waiting, queued, snoozed, due to resume, running elsewhere or with an open pull request); their numbers stay in Activity.
- `GET /api/claude-retention` — whether Claude Code keeps its conversations (it deletes them after 30 days by default; the app tells every Claude Code config that says nothing to keep them, and keeps its own link to every conversation anyway). Returns: `{ days: number, keep: boolean }`.
- `PUT /api/claude-retention` — keep: writes cleanupPeriodDays 36500 to Claude Code's settings.json; not keep: removes it (back to 30 days). Body: `{ keep: boolean }`. Returns: `{ days, keep }`.

## Machines (Settings → App → Machines)

- `GET /api/machines` — this machine and the other machines this app uses. Returns: `{ self: { host, version, home, platform, user }, machines: [{ id, name, via: 'ssh'|'url', url?, ssh?, port?, status: { state: 'connecting'|'online'|'offline', host?, version?, error?, waiting: number — sessions there that need the user, working: number, since?, finished?: number — sessions there that finished since the user last looked at that machine, news?: [{ key — the session's key, title, at — when it finished (ms), failed?: true }] — the newest of them, at most 5, update?: { phase — the step of an update running there, share?: number — how far it is, 0 to 1 } (a machine updating), restarting?: true — it went away while restarting into an update and is expected back within a couple of minutes } }] }`. `self` also has `status: { waiting, working, finished, news }`, this machine's own counts, which the pages of another machine show in the machine row. A machine on an older Hydor leaves out the fields it does not know. A machine's pages are `/m/<id>/…`.
- `GET /api/machine` — this machine as another Hydor sees it. Returns: `{ host, version, home, platform, user }`.
- `POST /api/machines` — adds a machine; it must answer before it is kept (400 "Could not use …: …" otherwise). Body: `{ name: string — required, up to 40 chars; via?: 'ssh'|'url' — anything but 'ssh' means 'url'; ssh?: string — with via 'ssh': a host from ~/.ssh/config or user@host (letters, digits, _ . @ % : [ ] -, not starting with -); port?: number — with via 'ssh': 1–65535, the port Hydor listens on there, default 8790; url?: string — with via 'url': http:// or https:// address, no user or password in it; token: string — required, the access token made on that machine (starts with acm_) }`. Returns: `{ machine }` (as in the list). The token is a secret the user makes on the other machine: let the user add machines in Settings → Machines rather than asking for the token in chat.
- `PATCH /api/machines/:id` — changes a machine; merges (fields left out keep their value, an empty or missing token keeps the current one). Body: the same fields as POST, all optional. A new name alone is saved at once; a change in how it is reached (via, url, ssh, port, token) is checked first and undone if the machine doesn't answer. Returns: `{ machine }`. 404 for an unknown id.
- `POST /api/machines/:id/reconnect` — tries a machine that isn't reachable again now. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `DELETE /api/machines/:id` — stops using the machine (its sessions stay on it). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Access tokens: other machines using this one (Settings → App → Machines → Access from other machines)

- `GET /api/access-tokens` — the tokens other machines use to reach this one (full access). Returns: `{ tokens: [{ id, name, createdAt, lastUsed? }] }` (times in ms; no token values).
- `DELETE /api/access-tokens/:id` — revokes one: the machine using it loses access at once. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Tokens for other agents (Settings → App → Connect other agents)

- `GET /api/mcp/tokens` — tokens agents elsewhere use with Hydor's MCP server (`POST /mcp`, tools list_sessions, read_session, start_session, send_message, list_models, plus apps offered to other agents). Returns: `{ tokens: [{ id, name, createdAt, lastUsed? }] }`.
- `DELETE /api/mcp/tokens/:id` — revokes one: agents using it lose access at once. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Source control accounts (Settings → App → Source control)

- `GET /api/scm/accounts` — the GitHub and Gitea accounts pull requests open with. Returns: `{ accounts: [{ id, provider: 'github'|'gitea', web — server address, login?, useGh? — signs in through the GitHub CLI }], gh: boolean — the GitHub CLI on this machine is signed in }` (no tokens).
- `POST /api/scm/accounts` — adds an account, after asking the server who the credentials belong to (400 with the server's message when they don't work); an account for the same server is replaced. Body: `{ provider: 'github'|'gitea'; web?: string — server address: for GitHub only for GitHub Enterprise (default https://github.com), required for Gitea; useGh?: boolean — GitHub only: use the GitHub CLI's sign-in, no token; token?: string — access token, required unless useGh }`. Returns: `{ account: { id, provider, web, login, useGh } }`. Agents: use `useGh: true` when `gh` is true; a token is a secret the user pastes in Settings → Source control.
- `DELETE /api/scm/accounts/:id` — removes it; pull requests already linked stay linked but stop updating. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Pull requests and branch reviews (a session's)

`:key` is the session's key. They work in the session's folder (its worktree's, when it has one); 404 "That session has no folder" otherwise.

## Themes (Settings → App → General → Appearance)

- `GET /api/themes` — the themes the user made (the built-in ones, ids naget-dark, naget-light, midnight and sage, are not listed). Returns: `{ themes: [{ id, name, base: 'dark'|'light', colors: { <colour>: '#rrggbb' } }] }`.
- `PUT /api/themes` — REPLACES the whole list of the user's themes (read it first, then send every theme to keep, changed or added). Body: `{ themes: [{ id: string — 1–48 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, unique, not a built-in id; name: string — up to 40 chars; base: 'dark'|'light' — sets code and terminal colours and fills colours left out; colors: { <colour>: hex — '#abc', 'aabbcc' or '#aabbcc' } }] — up to 40 }`. Colours: bg (page), sidebar, panel (cards), elevated (menus and dialogs), bg-1 (fields), bg-2 (hover), bg-3 (selected), fg, fg-2, fg-3 (text, secondary, muted), line, line-2 (borders, strong borders), accent, accent-fg (text on accent), busy, warn, ok, err, info, claude, codex, code-bg, term-bg, term-fg; unknown keys and bad values are dropped. Returns: `{ themes }`; open pages update at once. Which theme shows (dark/light/system and the pick for each) is chosen in each browser, not through the API: tell the user to pick it in Settings → General.

## The rail's tools and the sidebar's groups

- `GET /api/sidebar` — the rail's and the sidebar's orders, as the user dragged them: the same on every device, whichever machine the page shows. Returns: `{ tools: string[] (every tool of the rail, first to last; the usual order is browser (the Browser page), spaces (the Projects page), files (the Files page), agents, apps, git (the Git page), experiments (the Experiments page), search, usage, system, settings, help. The rail shows the first seven, the sections, then the Inbox and Search, and the others below, each group in this order; a phone's drawer shows all of them in one grid), places: string[] (the groups of projects and folders among the sessions that were dragged into an order, first to last: "space:<project id>" or "dir:<folder>"; a group it does not name comes before them, the one used last first), names: { [folder]: name } (names the user gave folders, which the app shows for the folders' own) }`.
- `PUT /api/sidebar` — sets either order or both; open pages change at once. Body: `{ tools?: string[] — tools first to last, by the names above; a tool left out takes its usual place among its own group (the sections, the first seven, or the rest): right before the next tool of its group that follows it in the usual order (files, the Files page, goes before agents), else right after the one before it (git after apps), else last, so to move some and keep the rest where the user put them, send the whole list as GET gave it, changed; [] puts every tool back in its usual place (terminal and preview, which moved into System, are passed over), places?: string[] — the groups first to last, as "space:<project id>" (ids from GET /api/projects) or "dir:<folder>" (its full path); send the whole list as GET gave it, changed; [] puts them back in their usual order, the one used last first }`. Returns: `{ tools, places }`. A tool or a group it does not know how to read is refused (400).
- `PUT /api/sidebar/folder-name` — gives a folder a name the app shows for it (in the sidebar, under sessions, in the folder menus), on every device; the folder on disk keeps its own. Body: `{ path: string — the folder's full path, name: string — up to 80 characters; "" gives it its own name back }`. Returns: `{ names: { [folder]: name } }`. To rename a project, `PATCH /api/projects/:id` with `{ name }`.
- `GET /api/session-folders` — the folders the user made for sessions in the sidebar. A folder holds sessions: at the top level for sessions that are in no project, or inside one project for that project's sessions; a session is in one folder at most (`extra.folder` in its entry of `GET /api/sessions`). Returns: `{ folders: [{ id, name, space?: string — the project's id, none for a top-level folder, createdAt }] }`, in the order they were made.
- `POST /api/session-folders` — makes a folder. Body: `{ name: string — up to 60 characters, not empty, and not the name of another folder in the same place (any case), space?: string — a project's id from GET /api/projects, to make the folder inside that project; left out, it is at the top level }`. Returns: `{ folder, folders }`; open pages update at once. 400 for an empty or repeated name, or at 200 folders; 404 for a project that is not there.
- `PATCH /api/session-folders/:id` — renames a folder. Body: `{ name: string }`, by the same rules. Returns: `{ folder, folders }`. 404 for a folder that is not there.
- `DELETE /api/session-folders/:id` — deletes a folder; its sessions stay, out of any folder. Deleting a project deletes its folders the same way. Returns: `{ folders }`. **Careful**: confirm with the user, naming the folder. To put a session in a folder or take it out: `POST /api/sessions/:key/folder`.

## Language (Settings → App → General → Language)

- `GET /api/me/language` — the language agents are asked to write in. Returns: `{ lang: 'en'|'de'|'es'|'fr'|'pt'|'el'|'ja'|'zh' }`.
- `PUT /api/me/language` — sets it. Body: `{ lang: 'en'|'de'|'es'|'fr'|'pt'|'el'|'ja'|'zh' }` (other values are ignored). Returns: `{ lang }`. It reaches agents as they start or resume. The language the pages show is chosen in each browser, and every page sends its own when it opens, which replaces this: to change the app's language, use `PUT /device { language }` (agents are refused this route).

## Updates, startup and the app's switches (Settings → App → General)

- `GET /api/health` — no sign-in needed. Returns: `{ ok: true, version }`.
- `POST /api/update-check` — asks the upstream for a newer version now (copies installed from git; the Mac app asks GitHub's newest release, and with Update automatically on starts downloading a newer one at once). Returns: `{ update: UpdateState | null }` (null: not installed from git with an upstream).
- `POST /api/update` — Update now: gets the new version, installs packages when they changed, builds, and restarts when the app can restart itself; running agents carry on. When upstream's history was rewritten and this copy has no commits of its own, it moves to the new history first (files changed in it stay; `update.last.error` says why when it cannot). The Mac app downloads the new version, checks it and restarts into it (a version already downloaded and waiting installs at once). Returns: `{ update }` at once (progress in `update.running.phase`: 'pull'|'download'|'install'|'build'|'restart'; read it again later). 409 while an update runs, 400 when not installed from git.
- `PUT /api/update/auto` — Update automatically (on by default, only when the app can restart itself: from git after 5 hours without a message from the user, at most once a day; the Mac app downloads a new version as soon as it is out and restarts into it a minute after saying so). Body: `{ on: boolean }`. Returns: `{ update }`. Turning it off while a version waits stops its countdown (it waits for Restart now); turning it on starts one.
- `POST /api/update/install` — the Mac app's Restart now: the version downloaded and waiting (`update.ready`) installs at once; the app restarts into it and running agents carry on. Returns: `{ update }` (`running.phase` 'restart'). 409 when no version is waiting or an update runs.
- `POST /api/update/later` — the Mac app's Later: the waiting version's countdown stops, and it asks again in 4 hours (`update.ready.later`); Restart now and the menu bar's Restart to Update still install it meanwhile. Returns: `{ update }`. 409 when no version is waiting.
- `GET /api/startup` — Start when this computer starts. Returns: `{ on: boolean, how: 'login-item'|'user-service'|'system-service'|'docker'|'none' — 'login-item': a desktop app, which opens when you log in, unit?, linger? — a user service starts before anyone logs in, platform? — the desktop app's system, can: boolean — whether it can be changed from here }`.
- `PUT /api/startup` — turns it on or off. Body: `{ on: boolean }`. Returns: the same as GET. 400 in Docker (its restart policy decides) or without systemd for the user; 403 for a system service without passwordless sudo.
- `GET /api/keep-awake` — Keep the computer awake: in the desktop apps, whether the computer is kept from going to sleep by itself while agents work (sessions at work, app runs, experiments on this computer) and, with `tasks`, from 5 minutes before a scheduled run is due until it starts (watchers and webhooks left out). Returns: `{ available: boolean — false outside a desktop app, where it does nothing, platform?: 'mac'|'windows'|'linux', mode: 'tasks'|'working'|'never', awake: boolean — kept awake now, working: number — what works here now, next?: { name, at } — the next scheduled run, with tasks }`.
- `PUT /api/keep-awake` — changes it. Body: `{ mode: 'tasks'|'working'|'never' — while agents work and before scheduled tasks (the default), only while agents work, or never }`. Returns: the same as GET. 400 for another mode.
- `GET /api/releases` — the release notes of this copy. Returns: `{ current: string — the version running, releases: [{ version, date?, summary — the first paragraph, notes — all of it, Markdown }] }` (newest first).
- `POST /api/tour` — marks the welcome tour as finished up to a version, so it doesn't open by itself on any device (the current tour is version 2). Body: `{ version: integer 0–1000 }`. Returns: `{ ok: true }`. Starting the tour happens in the browser (Settings → General → Take the tour).
- `GET /api/tips` — the tips after the tour, which point out a part of the app the first time it is on screen (Settings → General → Tips). Returns: `{ on: boolean, seen: string[] — the tips shown already, in order, tour: number — the tour version taken (tips wait for the tour), explained: string[] — the costly modes explained already, in the order they were: swarm, campaign, research, arena (the first time someone picks one of these, a dialog says what it does and costs; it shows once, on every device, whether tips are on or not) }`.
- `PUT /api/tips` — turns tips on or off, marks some as shown, marks costly modes as explained, or shows everything again, on every device. Body: `{ on?: boolean, seen?: string[] — tips to mark as shown: search, inbox, session-menu, details, queue, limit, memory-note, sources, files-made, page, browser, skill, goal, children, research, budget, context, pane, groups, dictate, handsfree, machines, files, git, terminal, preview, review, library, apps, spaces, knowledge, usage, ask, tasks, memory, guidelines, assistants, connections, skills, explained?: string[] — costly modes to mark as explained: swarm, campaign, research, arena, goal, reset?: true — show every tip and every costly mode's explanation again (before seen and explained) }`. Returns: the same as GET. 400 for a tip or a mode it doesn't know.
UpdateState: `{ kind: 'git'|'app' — from git commit by commit, or the Mac app release by release, behind: number — commits behind the upstream (the Mac app: 1 when a newer release is out), latest? — the newest one's subject (the Mac app: its version), checkedAt, error? — why the check failed, auto: boolean, canRestart: boolean, running?: { phase, since, auto, to? — the desktop apps: the version it downloads or installs, done?, total? — the desktop apps' download: bytes so far and in all (done reaches total, then it is checked), share?: number — a copy from git: how far the whole update has got, 0 to 1, never going back, step?: number — the step it is on, from 1, steps?: number — how many there are (installing packages is one only when they changed), left?: number — about how long is left, in ms; missing while slow, slow?: true — a step with nothing to measure it by has run over 1.5 times as long as last time; a copy from before these leaves them out }, ready?: { version, at, installIn? — ms until it installs by itself (the pages count down), later? — put off with Later until then (ms since 1970); neither: it waits for Restart now } — the Mac app: a new version downloaded and checked, waiting, last?: { ok, at, auto, from?, to?, needsRestart?, error? } }`.

## This computer and previews (System and Preview pages)

- `GET /api/system` — the computer now and the last 10 minutes. Returns: `{ info: { hostname, os, kernel, uptime — seconds, cpuModel, cores, mounts: [{ mount, device, fstype, size, used, avail }] — bytes, processes: { byCpu, byMem, gpu }: [{ pid, name, cmd, user, cpu — %, rss — bytes, gpuMem? — bytes }] — the 12 busiest each }, history: [{ t, cpu — %, cores — % each, load: [1, 5, 15 min], mem: { total, used, available, cached, buffers }, swap: { total, used }, gpus: [{ index, name, util — %, memUtil, memUsed, memTotal, temp — °C, power, powerLimit — W, fan, clock — MHz, pstate }], net: { rx, tx, ifaces } — bytes/s, disk: { read, write } — bytes/s, temps: [{ label, value — °C }] }] }` (samples 2 s apart, newest last, up to 300; a call starts sampling for 30 s when nobody watches, so the first may take a second).
- `GET /api/preview` — servers listening on this machine that Preview can open (ports from 1024, not the app's own). Returns: `{ servers: [{ port, program, command, cwd?, pid?, web?: boolean — it answers with a web page (Preview's Web apps), title? — that page's title, project? — its package's name, else its folder's, framework? — Vite, Next.js… when it can be told }], port: number | null — the preview port (null: previews are off), origin: string | null — the preview's own address when set }`.
- `GET /api/preview/shot?port=&path=&device=&live=` — a picture (PNG) of a dev server's page, taken on this computer (Preview's **Show it as a picture**, and **Save a picture of it**). Query: `port: number, path?: string — starts with / (default /), device?: desktop|tablet|phone, live?: 1 — the page as its live stream in Preview shows it now (when one is open), instead of opened afresh`. 502 when nothing answers.
- `POST /api/preview/shot` — the same picture kept as a file for a week, to start a session with (**Ask an agent about this screen**). Body: `{ port: number, path?: string, device?: desktop|tablet|phone, live?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ path — the PNG's path on this computer, size }`.

## Before and after screenshots

Agents take them with their own ui_screenshot tool when they change how a web app looks (phase before, then after, with the same label), and show screenshots they already have as files with show_screenshots; the user reviews each screen in the conversation, one card for a reply's. **Shot**: `{ id — 12 hex characters, key — the session's, label — which screen (the same for its sizes and themes), phase: before|after|check, viewport: desktop|phone, url — the page's address, or file:// and the path for a picture shown from a file, title — the page's own (empty for a file), width, height — the picture's, in pixels, createdAt, theme?: dark|light — the colour scheme it shows, when known, section?: string — the heading its screen shows under, note?: string — the agent's line about the screen, source: capture|file — taken by the app's browser, or copied from a file the agent had }`.

- `GET /api/shots?key=` — a session's screenshots, oldest first. Query: `key: string — the session's key`. Returns: `{ shots: [Shot] }`.
- `GET /api/shots/pairs?ids=a,b,c` — several screenshots at once, each with its before one as `GET /api/shots/:id/pair` has it. Query: `ids: string — up to 300 ids, comma-separated`. Returns: `{ pairs: [{ shot: Shot, before: Shot | null }] }`, in the order asked, with the ones that are no longer kept left out.
- `GET /api/shots/:id/pair` — a screenshot and, for an after one, the before one it answers (the session's latest before with the same label, viewport, heading and theme, taken earlier). Returns: `{ shot: Shot, before: Shot | null }`; 404 when it is no longer kept.
- `GET /api/shots/:id` — the picture itself, as the type it was given (WebP, PNG, JPEG or GIF). 404 when it is no longer kept (the pictures go with their session, and the oldest go past 500 MB in all).

## Experiments

Jobs that wait for free GPUs (or a cluster's queue), run on their own and are kept as records. Agents in sessions start and read runs with their own tools (experiment_run, experiment_sweep, experiment_list, experiment_get, experiment_stop, experiment_table, and for evaluations evaluation_get, evaluation_score and evaluation_propose); these routes serve the page and app_api. A run (`run` below) is `{ id, name, group — its experiment, command, cwd, where: "local" | "m:<machine>" | "c:<cluster>", gpus, gpuMemGb?, cpus?, memGb?, hours?, partition?, env?, params?: { name: value }, seed?, tags?, priority?, metricsFile?, scoresFile?, better?: { metric: "max"|"min" }, onCall?: { on, act: "tell"|"agent", watch?: ("nan"|"oom"|"stall"|"crash"|"diverge")[], stallMinutes?, rules?, agent?, model? }, notify?: { done?, failed?, when?: { metric, op: ">"|"<"|">="|"<=", value } }, report?, sweep?, project?, status: "queued"|"waiting"|"starting"|"running"|"done"|"failed"|"stopped"|"lost", why? — what it waits for or why it ended, in words, createdAt, startedAt?, endedAt? — ms, exitCode?, host?, gpuIds?, commit?, branch?, dirty?, untracked?, patchPath?, environment?: { host, Python, PyTorch, CUDA, driver, GPU… }, folder? — its own folder (log.txt, changes.patch, exit, metrics.json, scores.jsonl), logPath?, metrics?: { last: { metric: number }, best: { metric: number }, step? }, startedBy?: { session? | app? | page? }, sealed?: [{ evaluation, name, ok, changed?: string[] — the first 10 }] — for a run in an evaluation’s folder: whether the files it protects were as sealed when it started, from? — the run it is a new run of, remote?: { machine, id } — kept on another machine, machine?: { id, name } — listed from another machine, clusterJob?, stopping? — being stopped (why), outputAt?, events?: [{ at, kind: "queued"|"waiting"|"started"|"nan"|"oom"|"stall"|"crash"|"diverge"|"agent"|"stopped"|"done"|"failed"|"note", text, session? — the agent on call's }] }`.

- `GET /api/experiments/runs` — runs, newest first. Query: `group?`, `tag?`, `status?` (comma-separated, or `active` for those not ended), `cwd?` (runs in that folder or under it), `project?`, `sweep?`, `q?` (words in the name, experiment, command or id), `limit?` (default 200, at most 1000), `everywhere?` (`1`: every connected machine's runs too, each with `machine`), `evaluation?` (`1`: the runs that score evaluations too; they also come with their `group`, with `tag=evaluation` and with a `status` of runs not ended). Through app_api a final test's runs are never given, and its run cannot be read, stopped or deleted (403). Returns: `{ runs: run[] }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/runs/:id` — one run with its events, its metrics' series and the end of its log (a run kept on another machine is read there). Query: `lines?` (default 300, at most 5000), `log?` (`0`: no log). Returns: `{ run, series: { metric: [step, value][] } — a few hundred points each, log: string, logSize: number — bytes }`. A run on this computer in a git repository has `kept?: string[]`, the files its command names that git does not track, copied into its folder under `untracked/`, and `notKept?: string[]`, those too large to keep; `scores?: true` when its per-example scores were kept.
- `GET /api/experiments/runs/:id/log` — the log after a point, to follow it. Query: `from` (bytes, as logSize or next gave). Returns: `{ text, next — where the next call starts, size }` (at most 256 KB at a time).
- `GET /api/experiments/runs/:id/patch` — the changes not committed it ran with. Returns: `{ patch: string | null }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/jobs` — a new job: in line here, sent to a cluster, or sent to another machine (kept there, followed here). Body: `{ command: string, cwd?: string — a folder that is there (default the app's), where?, gpus?: number — default 1 on a computer with GPUs, else 0, gpuMemGb?: number — free memory each (default a GPU almost all free), name?, group?, params?, seed?, tags?, priority?: -100–100, hours?, cpus?, memGb?, partition?, env?, metricsFile?, scoresFile?, better?, onCall?, notify?, report? }` (`{name}` placeholders in the command are filled from params and seed; in metricsFile and scoresFile `{run}` is the run's id too, and either may start with `$HYDOR_RUN_DIR/`, the run's own folder, whose `scores.jsonl` is kept without being named). Returns: `{ run }`; 400 says what is wrong.
- `POST /api/experiments/runs/:id/stop` — stops a run: one in line at once, one running with Ctrl-C, then harder after 10 s (it ends as stopped); on a cluster with scancel, on another machine there. Body: `{ why?: string }`. Returns: `{ run }` (`stopping` set while it ends).
- `POST /api/experiments/runs/:id/again` — runs it again, as it was or with changes. Body: any field of a new job, replacing the run's. Returns: `{ run }` (its `from` is the first run); 400 for a run that scored an evaluation (score it again from the evaluation).
- `DELETE /api/experiments/runs/:id` — deletes an ended run's record, events, series and its own folder (never the job's own files). Returns: `{ ok: true }`; 409 while it has not ended.
- `GET /api/experiments/queue` — the queue now. Returns: `{ running, waiting, gpus: [{ index, name, memUsed, memTotal — MiB, util — %, run? — the run of yours holding it }], gpuNote?, cpuRunning, cpuSlots }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/settings` — Returns: `{ cpuSlots — jobs without a GPU running at once here }`.
- `PUT /api/experiments/settings` — Body: `{ cpuSlots: 1–64 }`. Returns: the settings.
- `GET /api/experiments/series` — the metrics' curves of many runs in one call (for charts). Query: `runs` (run ids, comma-separated, at most 100; ids that are not valid or not known are left out). Returns: `{ series: { runId: { metric: [step, value][] } } }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/groups` — the experiments runs belong to, the latest first, each with what it is. The runs that score evaluations are left out. Query: `evaluation?` (`1`: their groups too, named `eval-<id>`). Returns: `{ groups: [{ name, runs, done, lastAt, title?, description?, category?, by?: "helper"|"agent"|"user" — who wrote them last, at? }] }`.
- `PUT /api/experiments/groups` — changes an experiment's title, description or category, kept as the user's (the fields given replace the old ones, an empty string clears one, the ones left out stay). Body: `{ name: string — the experiment (group), title?: string — one line, at most 80 characters, description?: string — at most 500, category?: string — at most 40 }`. Returns: `{ group: { name, title?, description?, category?, by, at } }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/groups/describe` — has the helper model write the experiment's title, description and category now, over what is there. Body: `{ name: string }`. Returns: `{ group: { name, title?, description?, category?, by: "helper", at } }`; 404 when no run has that experiment, 409 for runs a confidential session started when no helper model runs on this computer, 502 when the helper gave nothing to use.
