- `POST /api/experiments/sweeps` — starts a sweep: one run per combination of the settings' values, in one experiment. Body: `{ command — with {name} for each setting, params: { name: values[] }, group: string, mode?: "grid"|"random", maxRuns? (at most 200), earlyStop?: { metric, better?: "max"|"min", afterSteps? } — stop a run worse than the median of the others at the same step }`, and any field of a new job for all its runs (`name` is a template, its `{name}` placeholders filled as the command's are). Returns: `{ sweep: { id, group, command, params, mode, maxRuns?, earlyStop?, createdAt }, runs: run[] }`; 400 when a setting has no placeholder (nothing starts).
- `GET /api/experiments/sweeps/:id` — a sweep and its runs, oldest first. Returns: `{ sweep, runs }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/clusters` — the clusters runs can go to. Returns: `{ clusters: [{ id, name, host, kind: "slurm"|"ssh", workdir, setup?, partition?, account?, checked?: { at, ok, text } }] }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/clusters` — adds a cluster, or changes one (with its id). Body: `{ id?, name, host — an entry of ~/.ssh/config or user@host, kind?: "slurm"|"ssh" (default slurm), workdir? (default ~/hydor-runs), setup? — lines run before each job, partition?, account? }`. Returns: `{ cluster }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/clusters/check` — Check: `ssh host true`, then sinfo (SLURM) or nvidia-smi and tmux (plain SSH). Body: `{ id }` (a cluster kept: the result is kept with it) or `{ host, kind }`. Returns: `{ ok: boolean, text — what it found or what went wrong }`.
- `DELETE /api/experiments/clusters/:id` — removes a cluster. Returns: `{ ok: true }`; 409 while runs go on it.
- `GET /api/experiments/tables` — the results tables kept, newest first. Returns: `{ tables: table[] }`, a table being `{ id, spec, rows: [{ key, label, runs: string[], cells: [{ mean, std, n, best?, p?, pAdj? — after Holm's correction, significant? — better than the baseline, verdict?: "better"|"worse"|"same" — absent when not tested }] }], latex, markdown, csv, madeAt, footnote?, notes?: string[] — runs left out, comparisons not tested because the seeds barely change the number, tests?: string[] — each column's test }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/tables` — makes a results table and keeps it. Body: `{ runs?: string[] — run ids, group?: string — or every finished run of an experiment, rows?: "group" | "param:<name>", columns: [{ metric, better?: "max"|"min" (default by the name), label?, digits?, value?: "last"|"best", scale? }], baseline? — a row's key (default the first), test?: "welch"|"paired"|"bootstrap"|"none" (left out: paired over examples when the runs kept scores, else over seeds when they pair, else Welch), alpha? (0.05), correction?: "holm"|"none", caption?, labels?: { rowKey: name } }`. Returns: `{ table }`; its LaTeX carries `% hydor:table <id>` above its tabular.
- `POST /api/experiments/tables/preview` — the same table, worked out and not kept (id "preview"). Body: as above. Returns: `{ table }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/tables/:id` — a table as it was made. Returns: `{ table }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/tables/:id/recompute` — works the table out again from its runs as they are now, and keeps it. Returns: `{ table, changed: boolean — whether any number moved }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/tables/:id/insert` — puts the table into a file of a kept LaTeX document, as a new version of it: in place of the table with its marker there, else before \end{document} (or at the end of the file). Body: `{ page: string — the document's id, file?: string — default main.tex }`. Returns: `{ replaced: boolean, version: number, title }`.
- `DELETE /api/experiments/tables/:id` — deletes a kept table. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

### Evaluations

An evaluation is how a project keeps score: one command and one number, sealed, so every run and every agent is scored the same way. The files it protects are sealed by their content, and no score is given while they differ; a score is always worked out by the app from its own runs of the sealed command (one for each seed, tagged `evaluation`, in the group `eval-<id>`); the baseline and the final test are yours alone. A protected path that is a link out of the folder is sealed by what is behind it, and the link too; putting files back never writes through it. Agents read and score with evaluation_get and evaluation_score and may propose one with evaluation_propose (you decide on its card); through app_api they may read and may ask for scores and put files back, and nothing else.

An `evaluation` is `{ id, name, cwd — its home folder, command, metric, better: "higher"|"lower", seeds: number[], protect: string[] — from cwd, folders ending in "/", "*" and "**" allowed, final?: { command, heldOut? — a folder outside cwd, handed to the command as $HYDOR_HELD_OUT; not given through app_api }, gpus?, gpuMemGb?, hours?, env?, createdAt, updatedAt, seal: { n — goes up with each seal, at, files, bytes }, state: { ok, checkedAt, changed: [{ path — "held-out:…" in the held-out folder, what: "changed"|"missing"|"added" }] — at most 50, more? } — its home folder as last checked, baseline? — a score's id, finals — final tests run under this seal }`. A `score` is `{ id, evaluation, seal, kind: "score"|"baseline"|"final", status: "waiting"|"running"|"done"|"failed"|"stopped", why?, failedRun? — the run to look at, printed?: string[] — when the number was not printed, the ones that were, folder, commit?, branch?, dirty?, lent?: string[] — read from the home folder because the folder scored lacks them, runs: string[], seeds, values: number[], mean?, std?, against?: { baseline, delta, p: number | null, verdict: "better"|"worse"|"same" } — a score of kind "score" against the baseline of the same seal, by: { who: "you"|"agent"|"goal"|"app", session? }, createdAt, endedAt? }`. With 3 or more values on both sides the verdict is Welch's t-test at 0.05; with fewer, p is null and the sign of the difference says it.

- `GET /api/experiments/evaluations` — the evaluations, newest first. Query: `cwd?` (those of a folder: whose home is it, above it, under it, or in the same git repository). Returns: `{ evaluations: [evaluation & { last?: score — the latest finished one of this seal, baselineScore?: score, testReadable?: true — its final test’s data stays readable to agents working in the project: no held-out folder, or one inside the project }] }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/evaluations` — creates one and seals it (yours alone). Body: `{ name, cwd, command — {seed} is replaced by each seed, metric — the name the number is printed under, better: "higher"|"lower", seeds?: number[] (1 to 10, default 1, 2, 3), protect: string[], final?: { command, heldOut? }, gpus?, gpuMemGb?, hours?, env? }`. Returns: `{ evaluation }`; 400 says what is wrong (a protected path that matches nothing; more than 20,000 files or 20 GB). Its runs are on this computer.
- `GET /api/experiments/evaluations/suggest` — what a new evaluation might protect, as its command suggests: files of the folder the command names, folders it points at and the folder of a data file it names (never what it writes). Query: `cwd` (the folder), `command?`, `heldOut?` (a final test’s held-out folder). Returns: `{ suggestions: string[] — from the folder, folders ending in "/", at most 20, testReadable?: boolean — with heldOut: whether the test data would stay readable to agents working in the project }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/evaluations/:id` — one, its home folder checked again when the last check is older than 30 seconds. Returns: `{ evaluation — with testReadable?, as in the list, scores: score[] — never final tests, newest first, folders: [{ path, branch?, home }] — the home folder and the git worktrees of its repository }`.
- `PUT /api/experiments/evaluations/:id` — changes it (yours alone). It is sealed again only when cwd, command, metric, better, seeds, protect, final or env changed (then the baseline goes). Body: any field of a new one; `final: null` removes the final test. Returns: `{ evaluation, sealed: boolean — whether it was sealed again }`.
- `DELETE /api/experiments/evaluations/:id` — removes it (yours alone): its runs still going are stopped, then its scores, their runs' records and the kept copies go. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/evaluations/:id/seal` — seals the files as they are now (yours alone): the seal's number goes up, the baseline goes, scores of older seals are not compared with newer ones. Returns: `{ evaluation }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/evaluations/:id/changes` — what differs from the seal in a folder, with a diff where a copy was kept (files up to 1 MB) and it is text. Query: `folder?` (default the home folder). Returns: `{ folder, state, changes: [{ path, what, diff? — unified, at most 200 lines, cut? }] }`. Through app_api, no diffs of the held-out folder.
- `POST /api/experiments/evaluations/:id/restore` — writes the kept copies over the changed and missing files in a folder; files added since stay. Body: `{ folder?: string }`. Returns: `{ folder, restored: string[], left: [{ path, why }] — what could not be put back, state }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/evaluations/:id/scores` — scores a folder: checks its files, then puts one run for each seed in line. Body: `{ folder?: string — the home folder (default), one inside it, or a git worktree of the same repository, seeds?: number[] — not through app_api }`. Returns: `{ score }`; 409 `{ error, changed: [{ path, what }], more? }` when files differ (nothing runs).
- `POST /api/experiments/evaluations/:id/baseline` — measures the baseline, or uses a finished score of this seal as it (yours alone). Body: `{ folder?: string }` to measure, or `{ score: string }`. Returns: `{ evaluation, score }`; a measured one becomes the baseline when it succeeds.
- `POST /api/experiments/evaluations/:id/final` — runs the final test (yours alone), with $HYDOR_HELD_OUT set to the held-out folder. Body: `{ folder?: string }`. Returns: `{ score }`; 409 as a score's.
- `GET /api/experiments/evaluations/:id/final` — the final tests with their numbers (yours alone). Returns: `{ scores: score[] }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/evaluations/scores/:id` — one score. Returns: `{ score }`; 404 for a final test.
- `POST /api/experiments/evaluations/scores/:id/stop` — stops its runs (a final test's only by you). Returns: `{ score }`.
- `DELETE /api/experiments/evaluations/scores/:id` — deletes an ended score and its runs' records (yours alone). Returns: `{ ok: true }`; 409 while it has not ended.

The scores an evolution asks for (below) are left out of an evaluation's own `scores` and of its `last`; `GET /api/experiments/evaluations/:id?evolve=1` gives them too, each with `by: { who: "evolve", evolution, candidate — a candidate's id, or "start" }`.

### Evolve

Evolve tries many small changes to the files you name, scores each with an evaluation, and keeps the best. The helper model proposes each change to a parent chosen from the best versions so far (on 3 islands, 2 for fewer than 30 candidates); each is scored by the evaluation's sealed command in a git worktree of the app's own at the start commit, first with the evaluation's first seed and with all its seeds only when that beats its island's best there; it becomes the best only by its full mean. Your checkout is never changed: **Take the best** makes a branch. Agents start one with evolve_start (you allow it on a card) and read it with evolve_get; through app_api they may read, and starting, pausing, resuming, stopping and taking the best ask you on a card each time; deleting is yours alone.

An `evolution` is `{ id, evaluation, from — the folder whose commit is the start, files: string[] — relative to from, aim?, candidates — at most, hours?, atOnce, stallAfter, seal — the evaluation's seal it started under (a new seal ends it), status: "starting"|"running"|"paused"|"done"|"stopped"|"failed", why? — why it ended, failed or was paused by the app, in plain words, startCommit, branch?, start?: { mean, std?, values, score — its score's id } — the start scored with all seeds first, best? — a candidate's id, tried — candidates that ended, failed — those of them that failed, calls — model calls, createdAt, startedAt?, endedAt?, activeMs — time running (paused time does not count), taken?: { branch, commit, at, candidate }, by: { who: "you"|"agent", session? } }`. A `candidate` is `{ id, evolution, n — 1, 2, 3… as proposed, parent? — a candidate's id (none: the start), island, status: "proposing"|"scoring"|"checking"|"scored"|"failed", why?, summary — the model's line on what it changed, model: "quick"|"strong", quick?: { value, score } — the first seed, full?: { mean, std?, values, score } — all seeds, gain? — against the start, positive is better (by the full mean, else the first seed against the start's), bestWhenScored?, islandBest?, changed: [{ path, added, removed }], createdAt, endedAt? }`.

- `GET /api/experiments/evolutions` — the evolutions, newest first. Query: `evaluation?` (those of one evaluation). Returns: `{ evolutions: (evolution & { bestGain? — its best against the start, positive is better })[] }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/evolutions` — starts one: checked at once, then its worktrees are made and the start is scored before the first change is tried. Body: `{ evaluation: string — it must have a baseline, from?: string — a folder in the evaluation's git repository (default its home), files: string[] — 1 to 8 committed text files, unchanged since, none protected by the evaluation, 200 KB in all at most, aim?: string — up to 2,000 characters, candidates?: number — 10 to 2000 (default 100), hours?: number, atOnce?: number — 1 to 4 (default 2), stallAfter?: number — stop after this many in a row with no new best (default 40; 0 never) }`. Returns: `{ evolution }`; 400 says what is wrong.
- `GET /api/experiments/evolutions/suggest` — files a new evolution might change: those the evaluation's command names, what its scripts import or run, and the repository's entry points, without what the evaluation protects or what git does not have. Query: `evaluation`, `from?`. Returns: `{ files: string[] — relative to from, at most 20 }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/evolutions/:id` — one, with its candidates. Returns: `{ evolution, candidates: candidate[] — newest first, evaluation: { id, name, metric, better } | null, finalTest: boolean — whether that evaluation has a final test }`.
- `GET /api/experiments/evolutions/:id/candidates/:cid` — one candidate with what it changed. Returns: `{ candidate: candidate & { diff: [{ path, diff — unified, against the start, at most 400 lines, cut? }], scores: string[] — its scores' ids } }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/evolutions/:id/pause` — what is being proposed or scored finishes; nothing new starts. Returns: `{ evolution }`; 409 when it is not running.
- `POST /api/experiments/evolutions/:id/resume` — goes on from where it was. Returns: `{ evolution }`; 409 when it is not paused.
- `POST /api/experiments/evolutions/:id/stop` — ends it: its scores still running are stopped. Returns: `{ evolution }`.
- `POST /api/experiments/evolutions/:id/take` — makes a branch `evolve/<a short name>-<the id's last 4>` at the start commit with one commit holding the candidate's files (the best unless one is named), without touching any folder that is checked out; taken again, a new commit on the same branch (a branch checked out somewhere gets a new name with -2, -3…). Body: `{ candidate?: string }`. Returns: `{ branch, commit }`; 400 while there is no best.
- `DELETE /api/experiments/evolutions/:id` — stops it and removes its worktrees, its candidates' files, its scores and their runs' records (yours alone). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.

## Lean, the proof checker (Settings → General → Lean (proof checker))

Agents check proofs with their own tools (lean_check, lean_verify, lean_search; lean_setup asks the person). These routes are the Settings row.

- `GET /api/lean` — how Lean stands on this computer. Returns: **LeanState** `{ ready: boolean — lake and lean are there to use, own: boolean — the person's own Lean, from their PATH, version?: string, installing: boolean, step?: string — what the set-up is doing, error?: string — why the last set-up failed, with its last lines, canInstall: boolean, why?: string — why it cannot, sizeMb: number — about what setting Lean up downloads, mathlib: none|installing|ready, mathlibDownloadMb: number — about what adding Mathlib downloads, mathlibSizeMb: number — about what Mathlib takes on disk }`.
- `POST /api/lean/setup` — sets Lean up in the app's own folder (about 620 MB to download, about 3 GB unpacked) when there is none, and adds Mathlib (about 600 MB to download, about 8 GB unpacked; needs git) when asked. Answers at once; ask GET again while `installing`. Asks the person every time. Body: `{ mathlib?: boolean — add Mathlib too }`. Returns: LeanState.
- `DELETE /api/lean` — removes the Lean and Mathlib the app set up, stopping a set-up first; the person's own Lean is never touched. The person's alone: agents are refused. Query: `mathlib?` (`1`: Mathlib alone). Returns: LeanState.

## Research tools (the LaTeX editor's checks and packages)

A LaTeX paper checked before it is submitted (**Tools → Check before submitting**), made into an arXiv package (**Export → arXiv package (.zip)**), and written to disk for the apps that take a paper. Agents check a paper with their latex_check tool instead of these.

- `POST /api/pages/tex/check` — starts the pre-submission check of a LaTeX document, saved or not; it runs on as the job follows (typesetting, the PDF, the references online). Body: `{ model: object — the LaTeX document, venue: string — arr-long, arr-short, emnlp-long, emnlp-short, naacl-long, naacl-short, sigir-full, sigir-short, cikm, wsdm, www, kdd, neurips, iclr, icml, aaai, ieee, acm or other, other?: { pages?: number, anonymous?: boolean } — Other's rules, page?: string — the kept document's id (its project's runs are where the numbers are traced), folder?: string — a file's document: its folder, base?: string — a typesetting to build on }`. Returns: the check as it starts: `{ id, venue, done: false, stage, findings: [], notes: [], startedAt }`.
- `GET /api/pages/tex/check/:id` — a check as it goes, and once it is done (kept half an hour). Returns: `{ id, venue, done, stage?, findings: [{ id, group: refs|anon|format|parts|numbers, level: must|check|info, title, detail?, file?, line?, from?, to?, quote?, fix?: { label, edits: [{ file, from, to, was, text }], shows? }, ask? }], notes: [string] — what could not be checked, pages?: { total, counted?, limit? }, online?: { done, of }, error? }`; 404 when it is no longer kept.
- `POST /api/pages/tex/check/:id/stop` — stops a check. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `POST /api/pages/tex/arxiv` — makes a LaTeX document's arXiv package from its saved version and typesets it to see it builds. Body: `{ page: string — the document's id, version?: number, options?: { strip, iffalse, unused, bbl, flatten, pdfoutput: boolean } }` (or `{ model, title }` for a document not saved). Returns: `{ token, name, bytes, files: [{ name, bytes }], removed: [string], renamed: [[from, to]], compiled: { ok, pages, errors, missing? }, warnings: [string] }`.
- `GET /api/pages/tex/arxiv/:token` — the arXiv package's .zip, for an hour after it was made. 404 when it is no longer kept.
- `POST /api/pages/tex/paper` — writes a LaTeX document to disk for an app that takes a paper (its files and pictures, and its typeset PDF, in a folder of the app's data; a file's document keeps its own folder). Body: `{ model: object, page?: string, folder?: string, base?: string }`. Returns: `{ folder: string, pdf?: string }` (no pdf when it does not typeset).

## Confidential sessions

A confidential session (`extra.confidential: true`, set with `POST /api/sessions`) runs on Custom with a model on this computer or your private network, and nothing of it leaves the computer. The server keeps it so: its messages are refused while its model is elsewhere (409), and so are a switch to such a model, a hand-off, a read-only link, turning it off and memory on. Agents in other sessions don't reach it: app_api answers "That session is confidential" for its addresses and leaves it out of lists.

- `GET /api/confidential` — what a confidential session can use here. Query: `prefer?: string — a model id that leads the list when it is one of them`. Returns: `{ models: string[] — Custom's models on this computer or your private network (Ollama at a local address but not its cloud models, vLLM, endpoints at localhost, 127.x, ::1, 10.x, 172.16–31.x, 192.168.x, fc00::/7 or a .local name), the best first: native tool calls, then the largest; sandbox: boolean — commands run without network here (bubblewrap; false: each command asks the user); helper: boolean — the helper model runs here (it names confidential sessions; else their first words do) }`.

## Sharing a project (a project's ⋯ → Share with people)

The owner invites people into one project, each with a link of their own (`/p/<token>`): they see its documents, sessions, runs and reading list, and comment on its documents under their name, or with `edit` change them too. Never the agents, files, settings or other projects. A person: `{ id ("ps…"), space — the project's id, name, role: "comment" (View and comment)|"edit" (Edit documents), createdAt, expiresAt? — when the link stops by itself (none: when it is stopped), seenAt? — when they last had it open, views — visits (half an hour apart), activity?: { at, what: "comment"|"reply"|"edit"|"read", page? — a kept thing's id, title? } }`. Only a hash of the token is kept: it is returned once, when the link is made.

- `GET /api/projects/:id/shares` — who the project is shared with, the newest first (one whose link ran out stays listed a month). Returns: `{ shares: [Person], owner: string — the name they see the owner's comments under ("Owner" until set) }`. 404 "No such project".
- `POST /api/projects/:id/shares` — shares the project with someone. **Careful**: whoever has the link sees the project's documents, sessions, runs and reading list, and comments on (with edit, changes) its documents. Body: `{ name: string — required, cut at 60; role?: "comment"|"edit" — default "comment"; days?: number|null — how many days the link works, at most 365, default 30; null: until it is stopped }`. Returns: `{ share: Person, token }`; their address is `/p/<token>` (another machine's project: `/m/<machine>/p/<token>`). 400 without a name or with another role; 409 past 50 people.
- `PATCH /api/projects/:id/shares/:share` — changes what someone may do or until when; their link stays the same. Body: `{ role?: "comment"|"edit"; days?: number|null — from now, null: until stopped; name?: string }`. Returns: `{ share: Person }`. 404 once sharing with them stopped.
- `POST /api/projects/:id/shares/:share/link` — a new link for someone: the old one stops at once (and says so when opened); they stay the same person, their comments theirs. Returns: `{ share: Person, token }`.
- `DELETE /api/projects/:id/shares/:share` — stops sharing with someone: their link stops at once; what they wrote stays, under their name. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `PUT /api/projects/sharing` — the name people see the owner's comments under, for every project. Body: `{ owner: string — cut at 60; "" for "Owner" }`. Returns: `{ owner }`.

A person's comments show in documents, LaTeX documents, slides, spreadsheets and pages (and kept pictures, videos and sounds) as `by: "person"` with their `name` (see **Comment** under Pages), in `GET /api/inbox` (`kind: "comment"`) until someone replies or resolves them, and to agents through get_page under their name. A version a person saved has `who`.

## Sharing one thing (a page's Share → Share with people; a picture's, video's or sound's Share)

The same, for one kept thing alone: a page of any kind (a document, slides, a spreadsheet, a LaTeX document, a notebook, a page of HTML or a design, but not an app's screen) or a picture, a video or a sound. Its link (`/p/<token>`) opens that thing alone: they read it and comment under their name (on words, a slide, a sheet's cell, a spot of a picture or a moment of a video or a sound), or with `edit` change it (a page's words, pictures and layout, never its code; a picture, a video or a sound in its editor, the one before kept among its versions). A Person here has `space: ""` and `item: { kind: "page"|"media", id }`; `role` "edit" reads **Edit**. The routes take a page's id under `/api/pages/:id/shares…` and a picture's, video's or sound's under `/api/media/:id/shares…`; 404 for an app's screen or something there is not; 50 people at most each.

- `GET /api/pages/:id/shares` — who the page is shared with, the newest first. Returns: `{ shares: [Person], owner: string }`.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/shares` — shares the page with someone. **Careful**: whoever has the link reads it, comments on it and (with edit) changes it. Body: `{ name: string — required, cut at 60; role?: "comment"|"edit" — default "comment"; days?: number|null — default 30; null: until it is stopped }`. Returns: `{ share: Person, token }`; their address is `/p/<token>`.
- `PATCH /api/pages/:id/shares/:share` — what someone may do, until when, or their name. Body: `{ role?, days?, name? }`. Returns: `{ share: Person }`.
- `POST /api/pages/:id/shares/:share/link` — a new link for someone (the old one stops at once). Returns: `{ share: Person, token }`.
- `DELETE /api/pages/:id/shares/:share` — stops sharing with someone. Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `GET /api/media/:id/shares`, `POST /api/media/:id/shares`, `PATCH /api/media/:id/shares/:share`, `POST /api/media/:id/shares/:share/link`, `DELETE /api/media/:id/shares/:share` — the same for a picture, a video or a sound.
- `GET /api/shares` — every thing shared alone, with how many people have a link to it that works now. Returns: `{ things: [{ kind: "page"|"media", id, people: number }] }`.

## Engineering (Git: find what broke it; campaigns)

These use the routes above; Git's own routes are the user's alone.

- The engineering examples (Settings → Scheduled tasks → Start from an example → Show all examples) are scheduled tasks: make one with `POST /api/tasks`, a repository folder as cwd and the prompt and schedule you would write, with `origin` set to say which example it is: "template:ci-doctor:github" (or :gitlab, :buildkite, :circleci), "template:issue-triage", "template:dependencies", "template:incident:sentry" (or :grafana, :datadog, :betterstack, :other). The task's row then says where its webhook address goes. CI doctor's filter: `{ path: "body.workflow_run.conclusion", value: "failure" }` on GitHub, `body.object_attributes.status` on GitLab, `body.build.state` on Buildkite and `body.workflow.status` on CircleCI each `"failed"`; Issue triage's: `{ path: "body.action", value: "opened" }`; the incident responder skips resolved notices (`{ path: "body.action", value: "resolved", skip: true }` for Sentry, `body.status` "resolved" for Grafana, `body.transition` "Recovered" for Datadog).
- Find what broke it starts a session: `POST /api/sessions` with cwd the repository's folder, `worktree: true`, and a prompt that says where it worked (a tag or commit), where it is broken, what is broken and, if there is one, a command that fails while it is broken; its agent follows the git-bisect skill.
- A campaign is a swarm: `POST /api/sessions` with `extra: { swarm: "campaign" }` and cwd in a git repository; `GET /api/swarm/:key` gives its parts and how each stands, `POST /api/swarm/:key/stop` stops its members.

## Not for agents

These routes serve the app’s own pages, need the user’s browser, or are the user’s alone; app_api refuses the ones an agent must never use.

- `POST /api/sessions/:key/approvals/:id` — answers an approval, question or form (`{ decision, message?, answers?, form? }`): the user's own decision; agents never answer another session's approvals. A form an agent asked that outlived its step (still shown among the session's cards) sends its answers to the session as the user's message, and stays when they can't be sent. The card that asks to build the project's memory first (id `memory-ask`) takes the decisions on it: `build`, `start`, `never`, then `now`, `stop` while it builds, and `retry`, `now` when the build failed, or `new`, `now` when the agent could not be started again for its message (`new` starts it in a new session with the same settings and answers `{ ok, key }` with the new session's key).
- `POST /api/uploads` — saves files for an answer to a question; it only serves the approval cards.
- `POST /share` (not under /api: the installed app's share target) — what another app shares with Hydor, as a form (`multipart/form-data`: `title`, `text`, `url`, and `files`, up to 25 MB in all), from the device's share sheet or the app's service worker; signed in only (signed out: refused, and nothing is kept). Its files are saved in the uploads folder. Returns: a redirect to the start page, `/?share=<id>` (or `/?share=signed-out`, `too-big`, `failed`); asked with `Accept: application/json`, `{ id }` (401 signed out, 413 too big).
- `GET /api/share/:id` — what a share brought, for the start page to put in its message box (kept for an hour). Returns: `{ id, title?, text?, url?, files: [{ path, name, size }], at }`; 404 when it is gone.
- `GET /api/memory/bring-in/found` — which other agents have a folder on this computer (the setup tour asks), by the folder alone, nothing read. Returns: `{ found: [{ id: "claude-code"|"codex"|"hermes"|"openclaw"|"pi"|"atomic", name }] }`.
- `GET /api/memory/bring-in/skills` — the skills the user made in other agents on this computer (every profile and workspace), as the preview of what would be copied into the user's own skills; nothing is written. Returns: `{ skills: [{ id, source: string — the agent's name, where?: string — "profile coder", "workspace"…, name — the name it would have, description, files: number, bytes: number, left: string[] — files that would not be copied (keys, sign-ins, over the size caps), skip?: string — why it is not offered }] }`.
- `POST /api/memory/bring-in/skills` — copies the picked skills (from the list above, by `id`) into the user's own skills, each through the same checks as a skill written in Settings → Skills (a name already in the library, a missing description, over 20,000 characters or a key in the SKILL.md are skipped), with its files up to 200 files, 512 KB each and 2 MB in all and never a secret. Body: `{ ids: string[] }` (required). Returns: `{ results: [{ id, name, status: "copied"|"skipped", reason?, files?, left? }], skills: as the list above, now }`.
- `PUT /api/memory/bring-in/file` — receives an export from ChatGPT or Claude from the user's browser, in parts (`?id=&name=&offset=&final=1` on the last; the body is the part's bytes); the user adds it in Settings → Memory → Bring in…. Returns: `{ file }` after the last part, as `files` in `GET /api/memory/bring-in`.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/image` — serves a message's picture as raw bytes.
- `GET /api/sessions/:key/queue/:id/attachment` — serves a queued message's attachment as a raw file.
- `GET /api/find/usage`, `POST /api/find/usage` — the search box's own record of what the user picks and visits; writing to it would skew what it suggests.
- `/ws` — the live socket the pages listen on (it streams).
- `GET /guest-api/:token` and the `/g/<token>` page — the public view of a read-only link, for guests.
- `/p/<token>` and `/p-api/<token>/…` — a shared project as the person at its link sees it (their own calls: the project, its documents and comments, its sessions, Edit documents' saves, the editors' pictures, files and LaTeX); the token is the permission, and an agent has the owner's routes above.
- `GET /agent/run/:token/assistant` — Custom's own runtime asks it before each turn which tools its session's assistant allows; it needs the running agent's token.
- `GET /api/memory/export` — downloads every active note to the browser as a file (`?format=json` with histories, or `?format=md`); `GET /api/memory/entries` gives the same notes as JSON.
- `POST /api/skillpack/proposals/:id/approve` — the user's own answer to a skill an agent proposed in a session (like an approval), given on the card, in Settings → Skill library or from the inbox; it saves a file every agent then reads. (Body: `{ text?: string, scope?: 'library'|'space' }`, the whole SKILL.md as edited, and for a session in a project where to keep it.)
- `POST /api/skillpack/proposals/:id/dismiss` — the same answer as a no. (Body: `{ note?: string }`, told to the proposing agent.)
- `POST /api/browser/profiles/:id/open` — opens a profile's browser so the user can sign in to sites through the live view: it needs the user at the screen.
- `GET /api/browser/browsing/downloads?q&before&limit` — the files the user's own browser downloaded, for the Browser's **Downloads** panel (desktop app). Agents are refused: the user's own browsing is theirs alone. Query: `q` — words in the file's name or the address it came from, `before` — only those older than this time in milliseconds (the next page), `limit` — up to 200. Returns: `{ downloads: [{ id, name, path, url, at, failed }] }`, newest first.
- `GET /api/browser/browsing/overview?q` — a few of each list the user's own browser keeps, for the Browser's start (desktop app): the bookmarks they use most, the pages waiting in Read later, the latest pages of the history, the latest downloads and the newest highlights, each with how many it holds. Agents are refused: the user's own browsing is theirs alone. Query: `q` — words: only what has all of them, and the counts of what matches. Returns: `{ bookmarks: { count, items: [{ id, title, url, parent, pos, addedAt }] }, later: { count, items: [{ id, url, title, addedAt }] }, history: { count, items: [{ id, url, title, at, visits, typed }] }, downloads: { count, items: [{ id, name, path, url, at, failed }] }, highlights: { count, items: [{ id, key, url, title, text, prefix, suffix, note, at }] } }`, with at most 8 bookmarks, 4 pages to read, 6 pages of history, 4 downloads and 3 highlights.
- `DELETE /api/browser/browsing/downloads/:id` — takes a download out of that list (the file stays where it was saved). Returns: `{ removed: boolean }`.
- `GET /api/browser/browsing/history/sources` — the other browsers on this computer (and their profiles) whose history can be brought into the user's own browser, for the **Import** part of the Browser's **History** panel and the welcome tour (desktop app). Agents are refused: the user's own browsing is theirs alone. Returns: `{ sources: [{ id, browser, profile?, count (pages that would come in, at most 20,000), error? (why it can't be read now: Safari without Full Disk Access, a history the browser locks while it is open), needs? ("full-disk-access": macOS keeps Hydor from reading it until the user turns Hydor on in System Settings → Privacy & Security → Full Disk Access; the app then shows **Open System Settings** in place of **Import**) }] }`, with ids as in `GET /api/browser/bookmarks/sources`, whose sources have `needs` too.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/history/import` — brings a browser's history (its latest 20,000 pages, each with its title, last visit, visits and typed visits) into the user's own browser's history; importing again adds nothing twice, and the history still keeps its last 20,000 pages. Agents are refused. Body: `{ source: string — an id from …/history/sources }`. Returns: `{ added (pages new to the history), updated (pages it had, given that browser's visits) }`. It is refused with the reason when that browser can't be read now (Safari without Full Disk Access, a history the browser locks while it is open).
- `GET /api/browser/browsing/highlights?url&q&before&limit` — the highlights the user made on web pages in their own browser, for the Browser's **Highlights** column and its **Highlights** panel (desktop app). Agents are refused: the user's own browsing is theirs alone. Query: `url` — only the highlights of that page (any address of it: its `#fragment` and tracking parameters do not matter), oldest first; without it, all of them, newest first, `q` — words in the highlight's words, note, page title or address, `before` — only those made before this time in milliseconds, `limit` — up to 1000. Returns: `{ highlights: [{ id, key, url, title, text, prefix, suffix, note, at }] }`, and with `url` also `count`.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/highlights` — highlights what is selected in one of the user's own tabs: marks the words on the page and keeps them. Agents are refused. Body: `{ tab: string — the tab's id }`. Returns: `{ highlight, count }`; 409 when nothing is selected, the selection is longer than 5,000 characters or the page has 500 highlights already.
- `PATCH /api/browser/browsing/highlights/:id` — a highlight's note. Agents are refused. Body: `{ note: string — empty takes it away }`. Returns: `{ highlight }`.
- `DELETE /api/browser/browsing/highlights/:id` — takes a highlight away, and its marks from the pages open in the user's tabs. Agents are refused. Returns: `{ removed: boolean }`.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/highlights/:id/open` — shows a highlight in the user's own browser: its page in the tab it is open in, or in a new tab, scrolled to the words. Agents are refused. Returns: `{ view }`.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/:id/save-page` — the Browser's **Save**: keeps a reader copy of one of the user's own tabs (the article as a page of HTML with its pictures kept, and what is selected quoted above it; a page with no article keeps its readable text as a document) as a kept thing, with the address it came from (shown as a **Web page** in the project's **Files**). It can take some seconds while the pictures are downloaded. Body: `{ space?: string | null — a project's id, none = no project, folder?: string | null — a folder's id (older, not shown in the app) }`. Returns: `{ id: string }`. Errors: 404 for no such tab, 409 for a tab with no page to save.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/groups` — makes a tab group in the Browser's tab column (desktop app). Agents are refused: the user's own tabs are theirs alone. Body: `{ tabs: string[] — ids of open tabs (taken out of the group they were in), name?: string — up to 40 characters, color?: "grey" | "blue" | "green" | "amber" | "orange" | "red" | "purple" — one not in use yet when left out }`. Returns: `{ group: { id, name, color }, view }`. Errors: 400 for no tabs, a private tab (private tabs can't be grouped) or a colour that is not one of those, 404 for a tab that is not open, 409 at 20 groups.
- `PATCH /api/browser/browsing/groups/:id` — renames a group, changes its colour, folds it or opens it. Body: `{ name?: string, color?: string, folded?: boolean }`. Returns: `{ view }` (its `groups` and each tab's `group`). Errors: 404 for no such group.
- `DELETE /api/browser/browsing/groups/:id?close=1` — takes a group apart (its tabs stay in no group); with `close=1` its tabs are closed too. Returns: `{ view }`.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/group` — puts tabs in a group, or in none. Body: `{ tabs: string[] — tab ids, group: string | null — a group's id, null for none }`. Returns: `{ view }`. Errors: 404 for a tab or group that is not there.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/close` — closes several tabs at once (Close other tabs, Close tabs below, Close group); the browser keeps at least one. Body: `{ tabs: string[] }`. Returns: `{ view }`.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/:id/duplicate` — opens the tab's address in a new tab beside it, in the same group, and shows it. Returns: `{ view }`. Errors: 404 for no such tab.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/:id/reload` — reloads that tab, not only the one in front. Returns: `{ view }`. Errors: 404 for no such tab.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/group-with-ai` — the Browser's **Group my tabs with AI**: the helper model (Settings → Models) is sent the title and address (without what follows a ? or #; a file as its name only) of each open tab that is in no group (a private tab is never counted or sent), up to 60, and nothing of the pages, and answers with groups of tab ids, each with a short name and a colour; they are made at once. It runs only when the user presses the button, and agents are refused it. Body: none. Returns: `{ applied: number — groups made (0: nothing went together), before: { groups, of } — what Undo sends to /groups/restore, view }`. Errors: 409 for fewer than three tabs in no group, 502 when the helper model cannot answer.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/groups/restore` — puts a whole grouping back (Undo of Group my tabs with AI), for the tabs still open. Body: `{ groups, of }` as `before` was returned above. Returns: `{ view }`.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs` — opens a new tab in the user's own browser (desktop app) and shows it. Agents are refused: the user's own browsing is theirs alone. Body: `{ url?: string — an address or words to search for (the home page when left out), private?: boolean — true opens a private tab: its sign-ins, cookies, site data and cache are kept apart in memory and forgotten when the last private tab closes, and its pages are not kept in the history, the closed tabs or the tabs reopened at the next start }`. Returns: `{ view }` (each private tab has `private: true`). Errors: 400 when `private` is not true or false.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/go` — the address bar of the user's own browser: opens an address, or searches for words with the user's search engine, in a tab. Agents are refused. Body: `{ text: string — an address or words to search for, tab?: string — a tab's id (the one in front when left out), newTab?: boolean — open it in a new tab, private?: boolean — with newTab, in a new private tab, plain?: boolean — search the words with the user's search engine, never as an address or as one of the search shortcuts ("yt lofi" opens YouTube's results unless this is true) }`. Returns: `{ view }`. Errors: 400 for no text, or a `private` or `plain` that is not true or false.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/private/close` — closes every private tab of the user's own browser, which forgets their sign-ins, cookies, site data and cache. Agents are refused. Body: none. Returns: `{ view }`.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/:id/translate` — translates a tab's page into a language where it stands (what is on screen first, then the rest and what comes later), and again into another one when it is translated already. The words of the page go to the helper model (Settings → Models → Helper model), Claude Haiku online on Automatic. It answers at once; the tab's `translation` in the view shows the progress. Agents are refused. Body: `{ to?: string — a language code such as "de" or "zh-TW" (the **Translate into** of the browser's settings when left out) }`. Returns: `{ view }`, whose tab has `translation: { state: "translating" | "translated" | "failed", to, from?, done, total, error?, model? }`. Errors: 400 for a language that is not one of those, 404 for no such tab, 409 for a tab with no web page.
- `DELETE /api/browser/browsing/tabs/:id/translate` — shows a tab's page in its own words again. Agents are refused. Returns: `{ view }`. Errors: 404 for no such tab.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/translate` — translates words picked on a page (the right-click menu's **Translate “…”**), in one call to the helper model. Agents are refused. Body: `{ text: string — the words (only the first 5,000 characters are translated), to?: string — a language code (the browser's **Translate into** when left out) }`. Returns: `{ text: string — the translation, from?: string — the language the words were found to be in, model: string — the model's name }`. Errors: 400 for no text or a language that is not one of those, 502 when the helper model cannot answer.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/:id/reader` — shows one of the user's own tabs in reader view (its article alone, over the page, in a clean column in the look the user chose) or takes it away. Agents are refused. Body: `{ on: boolean — true shows it, false takes it away, theme?: "dark" | "light" — the look the app has now, which "Auto" colours follow }`. Returns: `{ view }` (a tab has `readerable: true` when its page has an article, and `reader: true` while it shows). Errors: 400 when `on` is not true or false or `theme` is something else, 404 for no such tab, 409 when the page has no article to show.
- `GET /api/browser/browsing/prefs` and `PATCH /api/browser/browsing/prefs` — the user's own browser's settings (the Browser page's **Settings** panel). Agents are refused. `PATCH` merges field by field; a list you send replaces the old one. Body: `{ home?: string — an http or https address, engine?: "google" | "duckduckgo" | "bing" | "brave" | "ecosia" | "startpage" | "custom", customSearch?: string | null — a search address with %s where the words go (empty or null takes it away), suggest?: boolean — Search suggestions, shortcuts?: { key: string — one word, up to 24 characters, lowercase; name: string — up to 60 characters; url: string — a web address with %s where the words go }[] — Search shortcuts, up to 50 with different keys ("yt lofi" in the address bar searches that site; the list you send replaces the old one; the usual six are yt, wiki, gh, scholar, arxiv and hf), zoom?: number — Page zoom, one of 0.5, 0.67, 0.75, 0.8, 0.9, 1, 1.1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75 or 2 (1 at first; a site the user zoomed has its own while the app runs), spellcheck?: boolean — Check spelling in pages (on at first), adblock?: boolean — Block ads and trackers, allowAds?: string[] — sites ads are allowed on, cookieBanners?: boolean — Hide cookie banners (with the blocker on), adLists?: string[] — the ad blocker's lists turned on beyond the usual ones: "adguard-ads" (Stricter blocking), "ublock-annoyances" (Hide annoyances) and lists for sites in other languages ("lang-de", "lang-fr", "lang-es", "lang-pt", "lang-el", "lang-ja", "lang-zh" and others; at first the one for the app's language); the list you send replaces the old one, translateTo?: string — the language pages are translated into (a language code; the app's own language until one is set), translateOffer?: boolean — Offer to translate pages in other languages, translateAlways?: string[] and translateNever?: string[] — language codes (up to 100) whose pages are translated as they load, or never offered; a language in one leaves the other, translateNeverSites?: string[] — sites never offered or translated, darkPages?: boolean — Dark pages, darkExcept?: string[] — sites shown as they are while it is on, autoplay?: boolean — Let sites play sound by themselves (off: it waits until the user clicks or types in the page; it applies to tabs opened after), reader?: { size?: number — 14 to 28, theme?: "auto" | "dark" | "light" | "sepia", font?: "sans" | "serif" } — how reader view shows articles, importOffered?: boolean — the Browser page has offered once to bring in the other browsers' bookmarks and history (the page sets it after its bar shows, and the welcome tour's page counts too; it is not a setting the user changes) }`. Returns: the settings as they are now, each field above. Errors: 400 for a value that is not valid.
- `POST /api/browser/browsing/tabs/:id/snapshot` — a copy of one of the user's own tabs' page (its title, address, what is selected and its readable text, up to 200,000 characters) as a file, for **Ask about this page**. Agents are refused. Returns: `{ path, name, title, url }`. Errors: 404 for no such tab, 409 for a private tab (agents never get a private tab's pages: ask from a normal tab) or a tab with no page to copy, 502 when the page cannot be read.
- `GET /api/browser/sessions/:key` and `PATCH /api/browser/sessions/:key/options` — a session's browser view, profile and site lists, for the page. Agents have their own browser tools.
- `POST /api/browser/sessions/:key/open`, `/close`, `/watch` — the page opening or closing a session's browser, and asking for live-view frames.
- `POST /api/browser/sessions/:key/takeover`, `/input`, `/navigate`, `/nav`, `/tab`, `/dialog` — the user driving the browser by hand.
- `POST /api/browser/sessions/:key/asks/:id` — the user's own answer to what the browser waits for them on (Done, Can't, allowing a local address).
- `GET /api/browser/sessions/:key/steps/:n` — a step's picture as a raw JPEG; `<n>.json` says where the step acted on it, for its marks: `{ marks: [{ x, y — fractions of the picture, act: click|double|move|type|select|scroll|…, box? — the field typed into, dir? — a scroll's way }] }`.
- `GET /api/computer/sessions/:key/steps/:n` — a computer step's picture of the screen as a raw JPEG; `<n>.json` where its actions were on it, the same way (a drag with `from`).
- `GET /api/documents/preview?path=` — reads a file's contents for the file viewer.
- `GET /api/documents/deck?path=` — reads a PowerPoint file (.pptx, .pptm, .ppsx, .potx) as a deck of slides for the file viewer to draw, as the app's own viewer reads one: its pictures are kept apart (each from `GET /api/pages/assets/:sha`). Returns: `{ deck, notes: string[] — what the reading says of it, pages: boolean — LibreOffice is installed }`. 404 when there is no such file, 413 over 200 MB, 415 for any other kind of file, 422 when it can't be read.
- `POST /api/documents/pages` — has LibreOffice lay an Office file out as pages for the viewer.
- `GET /api/documents/pages.pdf` — sends that PDF file itself (raw file).
- `GET /api/pdf/info?path=` — a PDF's pages, form fields (each with its kind, value, choices, the words printed beside it and its boxes) and protections, for Files' **Fill & sign**. Agents use pdf_form and pdf_look.
- `GET /api/pdf/page?path=&page=&width=&fields=0` — a page of a PDF as a picture (raw PNG), without its form's fields with `fields=0` (Fill & sign draws its own over it).
- `GET /api/pdf-notes/page?path=&page=&width=` — a page of a PDF as a picture (raw PNG) for Files' reader, without the highlights saved into the file while the app keeps them as notes (the reader draws its own).
- `GET /api/pdf-notes/area?path=&page=&x=&y=&w=&h=&width=` — a part of a page as a picture (raw PNG): an area picked in the reader. Agents see the areas' pictures with pdf_notes.
- `POST /api/pdf/save` — Fill & sign's **Save**: the fields filled and what was placed on the pages (text, ticks, crosses, signatures), written as a copy beside the file or over it (its old version to the Trash). Agents use pdf_fill and pdf_write, and their signature tool.
- `GET /api/pages/:id/export` — sends a page, deck, document, spreadsheet, LaTeX document or notebook as a file itself (PDF, Word, OpenDocument (`format=odt`, a document), PowerPoint, Excel, CSV, Markdown, EPUB (`format=epub`, a document), plain text (`format=txt`, a document), LaTeX, a notebook (.ipynb), a Python script (.py) or a web page; the **Export** menu), or a deck's slides as pictures (`format=png`: a zip of the slides the slideshow shows, or with `slide=<number>` that slide alone as a PNG); a design as pictures exactly its size (`format=png` or `jpg`, `scale=1|2|3`, two when left out, `transparent=1` for a PNG without its background; a zip for several pages, or `slide=<number>` one page) or a PDF whose pages are each its own size; a diagram as SVG (`format=svg`), pictures (`format=png` or `jpg`, `scale`, `transparent=1`), a draw.io file (`format=drawio`) or a PDF of pages each its own size, one page with `slide=<number>`, else each page (a zip for several). A PowerPoint file's answer has an `X-Export-Note` header that says which fonts went inside it and which of them are compressed. Agents make the file with their export_page tool, which writes it where they can hand it over with present_files (and says the same of its fonts).
- `POST /api/pages/docs/print` — a document as it prints, changes not yet saved included (the editor's **Tools → Print**). Body: `{ model: object — the document, as save_doc takes it, title: string — its name }`. Returns: `{ html, paginate?: object }`, its pages with their header, footer and page numbers (`paginate`: a document with footnotes or columns is laid out page by page in the page itself before it prints; these are the sizes it is laid out with).
- `GET /api/pages/assets/:sha`, `GET /api/pages/picture`, `GET /api/pages/fonts.css`, `GET /api/pages/fonts/*` — the pictures and Google fonts the slides and document editors show, and the pictures from the web in agents' replies (`/api/pages/picture?url=`: fetched by this server, public addresses only, and remembered for a while), served as they are (raw files).
- `POST /api/pages/videos` — the slides editor sending a video from a device (**Insert → Video → From this device**, or a video dropped on a slide), as raw bytes in pieces of ≤32 MB, in order: `?upload=<its own id: 8–40 letters a–z and digits>&offset=<where the piece starts>&total=<the video's bytes, ≤200 MB>`. Returns `{ received }` after each piece but the last, then `{ src: "naget-video:<sha>" — a video part's src, mime, bytes }`; 409 for a piece out of order, 413 over 200 MB, 415 when it is no MP4, MOV or WebM video. Agents put a video file on a slide with save_slides instead (its path as the video's src).
- `GET /api/pages/videos/:sha`, `GET /pg/video/:sha/:sig` — a deck's videos from a device, served as they are (raw files, with range requests so a player seeks): the first to the app's own pages, the second, signed by this server, where the app's sign-in is not (a deck's own page, its published copies, the conversation's frame).
- `GET /pg/pic/:sha/:sig` — a picture kept with a web page saved from the user's browser (the page shows it at this address), served as it is. The address is signed by this server and is its own permission, so the picture shows where the app's sign-in is not (the page's own address, its published copy, the conversation's frame). 404 for a wrong signature or a picture not kept here.
- `POST /api/pages/video-address` — where a video the app keeps plays in a page or a design (a page's video from this device, one you kept or one made with AI). Body: `{ src: string — "naget-video:<sha>" }`. Returns: `{ url — "/pg/video/<sha>/<sig>", signed by this server, which plays without the app's sign-in }`; 404 when the video is not kept here.
- `POST /api/pages/videos/from-media` — a kept video made available to pages and designs (**From your saved videos**). Body: `{ media: string — the kept video's id }`. Returns: `{ src — "naget-video:<sha>", url — its address in a page, poster? — its first frame as a data: address, w?, h? }`.
- `POST /api/pages/design-export` — a design (a page of artboards) as a file, from its HTML as the editor holds it (**Download**). Body: `{ html: string — the whole page, title?: string, format: png|jpg|pdf, scale?: 1|2|3 — pictures at that many times its size, transparent?: boolean — a PNG without its background, board?: number — one page alone, from 0 }`. Returns: the file (a PNG or JPG of one page, a .zip of a picture a page, or a PDF whose pages are each its own size); 503 without the browser the app takes pictures with.
- `GET /api/brand`, `PUT /api/brand` — the user's brand kit, for designs (and the agents that make them). Body (PUT): `{ name?: string, colours: string[] — #rrggbb, up to 10, the main one first, fonts: [heading, body] — fonts designs offer, logos: { src: "naget-asset:<sha>", name }[] — up to 8 }`. Returns: `{ brand: { name?, colours, fonts, logos } | null }`.
- `GET /api/pages/tex/:key/:page`, `GET /api/pages/tex/:key/pdf` — a typeset LaTeX document's pages as pictures and its PDF, served as they are (raw files).
- `POST /api/voice/transcribe` — the page's microphone: the body is a raw recording.
- `POST /api/voice/command` — the hands-free page: it acts at once (starts sessions, answers approvals) with no card for the user to OK first.
- Deep research, subagents, swarm, second opinion, model arena and spending caps: none left out; all their routes are listed above.
- `GET /api/search` (no path after it) is the search through sessions, not web search: it belongs to Sessions.
- `POST /api/apps/:id/keys` — makes an API key whose value is shown only once; the person makes keys in the app's API & sharing tab.
- Programs outside Hydor, with an app's key: see Apps, from outside. Inside the app, use `POST /api/apps/:id/runs` and `GET /api/apps/runs/:runId`.
- `GET /api/apps/batches/:batchId/csv` — a CSV file download; `GET /api/apps/batches/:batchId` has the same data as JSON.
- `GET /a-api/:slug`, `POST /a-api/:slug/run`, `GET /a-api/:slug/run/:id` — a public page's own calls for its visitors (told apart by a cookie; runs start only from the page itself). A visitor's run has `partial` (as in a Run) while it works.
- `POST /api/sites` — publishes at once, without asking. Agents publish with their own publish_site tool, whose card lets the user decide and pick a private or public link.
- `POST /api/sites/requests/:id` — the user's answer on a publish card (Publish or Not now, and the kind of link).
- `GET /pg/:token`, `GET /pg/:token/html`, `GET /pg/:token/manifest.webmanifest`, `POST /pg/:token/storage` — a saved page's own address, for the browser that opens it (no sign-in; its storage calls are taken only from that page). Agents read and change a page's data with `POST /api/pages/:id/storage`.
- `GET /site/:token`, `GET /site/:token/*` — the published sites themselves, served to visitors.
- `GET /htmlframe` — the sandboxed frame every page renders in.
- `POST /hooks/:secret` — a webhook task's address, for outside services: no sign-in (the secret in the path is the permission), and it may require a signature. An agent starts a run with POST /api/tasks/:id/run.
- `GET /api/automations/proposals/:id`, `POST /api/automations/proposals/:id/confirm`, `POST /api/automations/proposals/:id/decline` — the card for a task an agent proposed with schedule_task or update_task: only the user answers it, and the agent hears the answer as a message.
- `POST /api/connections/secrets`, `PATCH /api/connections/secrets/:name` — these set a key's value, hosts, whether commands may use it, or how it is sent. Agents never set a key's value or where it may go: the user does, in Settings → Connections → Keys or on the key card.
- `POST /api/connections/:id` with `key` or a secret field — this sets a key's value.
- `GET /api/signatures/:id/image` — a saved signature's picture (raw PNG), for the app's own pages: agents never see it.
- `POST /api/signatures`, `PATCH /api/signatures/:id`, `DELETE /api/signatures/:id` — keep a signature drawn, typed or uploaded on the pad (Body: `{ name: string, kind: 'signature'|'initials', image: string — a base64 PNG with a see-through background, keep?: false — kept for a day }`), rename it, draw it again (`image`), keep one given for a day for good (`keep: true`), or delete it: the user's own, in Settings → App → Signatures, the documents editor and the cards. Agents ask for one with ask_signature.
- `POST /api/signatures/:id/asset` — puts a saved signature into a kept document as one of its pictures (the editor's **Insert → Signature**). Returns: `{ src: "naget-asset:<sha>", width, height }`. Agents place one with place_signature, which the user allows on a card.
- `GET /api/signatures/previews/:token` — a page as an agent would sign it, for the card that asks the user (raw PNG).
- `POST /api/connections/requests/:id` — the user's answer to a key or sign-in card; it carries the values.
- `POST /api/connections/requests/:id/items/:item` — the user saves one tab of a keys card; it carries the values (or `use: true` for the saved ones, or `reuse: <id>` to copy the ones the card offers from elsewhere). On a web app's card they are kept as that app's own, for that place. A live payments key is refused for an app inside Hydor.
- `POST /api/connections/requests/:id/items/:item/skip` — the user's **Skip for now** on one tab.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/keys/card` — puts a web app's keys card up on its page (or in a session). Body: `{ env?: string — default hydor; a place on a server, names?: [string], replace?: boolean, skippable?: boolean, session?: string }`. Agents use their `webapp_keys` tool, which puts it in their own session. Returns: `{ card: Card | null }`.
- `GET /api/webapps/services` — the catalog of outside services for a web app's needs, as the cards show it: for each need (database, mail, run, search, files, model, payments, signin, sms, visits, errors, uptime, domain, bots, maps) what an app does without one, the services offered (one, or at most three, the first recommended), others offered beside the card, and what is left out and why. Prices read more than ninety days ago are not given (`checkPrice`). Returns: `{ needs: [{ id, name, what, row?, builtIn?: { say, until }, note?, services: [{ id, name, recommended, why, free?, paid?, checkPrice, catch?, notFor?, page, keys: [name] }], also: [same], leftOut: [{ name, why, url? }], readOn? }] }` (`url`: the page that says why a service cannot be had for now).
- `GET /api/webapps/:id/services` — what a web app chose, with what happened to its settings. Returns: `{ chosen: [{ need, needName, service, name, keys, at, applied: { state: agent|outside, say }, missing: [string] — its keys not saved yet, by name }], needs: [{ id, name, what, services, builtIn }] }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/services/cards` — puts up the card to choose a service for one need, in a session or on the app's page (the one waiting there is given back). Agents use `webapp_services`. Body: `{ need: string, session?: string }`. Returns: `{ card: { id, app, need, chosen?, keysCard?, createdAt } }`; 400 for `run` (where an app runs is the Launch tab's).
- `GET /api/webapps/services/cards/:card` — a choice card as it stands. Returns: `{ card }`.
- `POST /api/webapps/:id/services/:need` — the choice: kept with the app, and the service's keys asked for. The person's: an agent that was told the name uses `webapp_services`. Body: `{ service: string, card?: string, session?: string }`. Returns: `{ chosen, keys: keys card | null, say }`.
- `DELETE /api/webapps/:id/services/:need` — forgets a choice (its keys stay until they are removed). Returns: `{ ok: true }`.
- `DELETE /api/webapps/:id/keys/:name` — removes a web app's key for one place (`?env=`, default hydor). The user's **Remove** on the app's Keys tab.
- `POST /api/connections/requests/:id/dismiss` — the user's "Not now" on another session's card, which tells that session's agent.
- `POST /api/connections/:id/oauth`, `GET /api/connections/flows/:flow` — a browser sign-in; it needs the user's browser.
- `GET /connections/callback` — the OAuth callback.
- `POST|GET|DELETE /connections/mcp/:token/:app`, `GET /connections/launch/:token/:app`, `POST /connections/elicit/:token/:app` — how running agents reach their connected accounts. These use run-token addresses and work from this machine only.
- `POST /api/setup/mcp/login` — opens a terminal for the server's sign-in.
- `GET /api/push`, `POST /api/push/subscribe`, `POST /api/push/unsubscribe` — they need the browser's own push subscription: only the device itself turns Notify this device on or off.
- `POST /api/accounts/:agent/login` — starts a Claude Code, Codex, Grok Build (`grok`) or Antigravity CLI (`antigravity`) sign-in; the user must open the link and approve in their browser. Grok Build's flow returns a link and a one-time code to confirm there, and nothing is posted back to `/login/:flow`: the flow finishes by itself. Antigravity CLI's flow returns Google's sign-in link; the user signs in with their Google account and pastes back the code the page shows, or the whole address of the page they land on.
- `POST /api/accounts/:agent/login/:flow` — finishes a Claude Code, Codex or Antigravity CLI sign-in with the code or localhost address the user copies from their browser (not used for `grok`). Body: `{ code: string — for `claude` and `antigravity`, the code or the whole address the page shows }`.
- `DELETE /api/accounts/:agent/login/:flow` — cancels a sign-in in progress (part of the user's sign-in dialog).
- `POST /api/accounts/:agent/logout` — signs a CLI (Claude Code, Codex, Grok Build or Antigravity CLI) or an added account (`?account=`) out for every terminal and editor; the user's own call, from Settings → Accounts.
- `GET /api/accounts/sign-in` — the accounts Hydor found signed out when it needed them and could find nothing else to do the work (another account, or one of your Custom models), which it asks you to sign in to again: a prompt on every page, and a banner in a session it stopped. Returns: `{ needs: [{ agent: "claude"|"codex"|"grok", account?: string — an added account's id, name — the account as you know it, background: boolean — background work needed it, task?: string — what that was, sessions: string[] — the sessions it stopped (they continue by themselves once it is signed in again), at: number, hiddenUntil?: number — **Not now** hides the prompt until then }] }`.
- `DELETE /api/accounts/sign-in/:agent` — (`:agent` is `claude`, `codex`, `grok` or `antigravity`) **Not now** on the prompt: it stays hidden on every page for an hour (a session it stops shows it again). Query: `account?: string — an added account's id (none: the program's own sign-in)`. 404 for an unknown agent or account. Returns: `{ needs }` as GET.
- `POST /api/accounts/sign-in/:agent/check` — (`:agent` is `claude`, `codex`, `grok` or `antigravity`) reads the account's sign-in now, as **Settings → Agents → Accounts** does; when it is signed in again the prompt goes and the sessions it stopped continue. Query: `account?: string — an added account's id (none: the program's own sign-in)`. 404 for an unknown agent or account. Returns: `{ signedIn: boolean, needs }`.
- `PUT /api/models` with `openrouterKey`, `hfToken` or `endpoint.apiKey` — key values; the user types them in Settings.
- `POST /agent/run/:token/ensure`, `/describe`, `/review`, `GET /agent/run/:token/custom`, `/context`, `/assistant` — Custom's own calls during a run (start its vLLM model, describe images, Auto mode's check, settings, instructions), allowed only with that run's token.
- `/api/fs/*` (list, read, stat, raw, view-link, write, mkdir, rename, delete, exists, search, find, diff, recent, made, upload) and `/view/:token/*` — files: agents read and change files with their own tools, with the user's approvals; raw and view serve files to the browser.
- `/api/git/*` (repos, clone, clone/:id, repo, fetch, sync, pull, push, fetch-all, remote, diff, show, log, commit, message, discard, discard-hunk, ignore, revert, undo-commit, continue, abort, branches, switch, branch-delete, branch-rename, merge, compare, branch-only, tag, push-tags, stash, stash/show, stash/:action) — the Git page's own calls; agents use git through their own command tool, with the user's approvals.
- `/api/terminals*` (list, create, rename, close, context, complete, ask), `/ws/term/:id` and `/api/terminal-history` — the user's shells and their typed history; the socket streams.
- `/api/kernels*` and `/nbframe` — Jupyter kernels, run from the notebook page.
- `/api/uploads` — attachments sent with messages.
- `POST /api/access-tokens` and `POST /api/mcp/tokens` — each returns a secret token, shown once to the user (`{ id, token }`); the user makes them in Settings.
- `POST /api/preview/link` — a one-time link (valid 2 minutes) that signs the user's browser in to the preview port.
- `POST /api/preview/live` — opens a dev server's page in the app's own browser and streams it to the Preview page, where the user's browser can't reach the preview port: the user drives it from there (its frames and input go through the browser's live-view calls above, under the key it returns).
- `POST /api/auth/password` and `POST /api/auth/logout` — the password, and this browser's sign-in.
- `POST /mcp` and `POST /mcp/run/:token` — the MCP endpoints themselves (agents elsewhere with a token; each session's own tools).
- `/g/:token` and `/guest-api/:token` — what a guest's browser reads through a read-only link.
- `/p/:token` and `/p-api/:token/*` — what a person's browser reads and sends through a shared project's link.
- `/m/:id/*` — the app's pages forwarding to another machine's API and sockets.
